# Decisions to review

The maintainer asked the work to go on without waiting for answers: "if anything depends on my answers, put it per your recommendation and generate a report which we can revisit later". This file lists every question that came up and the answer chosen in the meantime. Change any answer, and the affected work is adjusted.

Status: **open** means not yet reviewed by the maintainer; **confirmed** and **changed** record the review.

## How the work is run

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R1 | What counts as "phase 2 finished" before its report goes to the phase 3 agent? | Plan phases A to E (protocol, simulator, firmware radio, controller, GM console). Phase F needs the radio boards, so it waits for the parts. | A to E can be built and tested without hardware; F is the field test itself. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| R2 | When is work committed and pushed? | After each plan phase, once the main session has verified it independently from a clean copy (builds, the CI test commands, sanitizers, a Wokwi run for firmware). | The maintainer asked for a commit and push after each phase; independent checks have caught several false "done" reports. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| R4 | Documentation | The README, the per-folder docs and the bilingual project page (site/i18n/pt.yaml and en.yaml, rebuilt with tools/build_site.py) are updated in every phase, as part of that phase (maintainer's instruction). | Docs kept drifting behind the work. | confirmed |
| R3 | Phases 3, 4 and 5 are being designed in parallel. When are they implemented? | Each stays design-only until the phase before it is implemented. Its design is updated with the earlier phase's report first, and committed as documentation as soon as it is reviewed. | Their plans depend on interfaces that are still being built. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| R5 | When is the Field Box build manual written (wiring diagram image, sourcing, assembly, first flash)? | Now, as a phase 1 hardware deliverable, alongside phase 2 plan phase E. The wiring diagram is generated from a text source and checked against the firmware's pin defaults. Phase 5 keeps the carrier PCB and the build guides for the other node types. English first; the Portuguese version goes with the phase 5 translations. (Maintainer asked for it on 2026-10-18; timing was the recommendation.) | The phase 1 gate and the phase 2 field test both need boxes built from the BoM; a manual at the end would arrive after they were built. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| R6 | When do the community-requested modes (design spec sections 2 and 15) ship as built-in modes? | A mode pack at the end of phase 2, after plan phase E, with the modes that need only what phase 2 provides: Hold-All Domination and Last Stand (moved from phase 3), per-team arming codes on Bomb, and the Codes modifier. Modes that need NFC, the player app, the target add-on or GPS stay in phases 3 and 4. Ideas only, from public forum posts; no vendor code or text. (Maintainer asked for them as defaults on 2026-10-18; the split was the recommendation.) | Many requests are a few lines of script once the field controller exists; the rest depend on later hardware and apps. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| R7 | The Wokwi CI token is on Wokwi's free plan, whose monthly CI minutes ran out on 2026-10-18 (during the phase 3 plan phase A merge check). How is firmware verified until then? | Until the quota resets or the maintainer upgrades the plan, firmware changes are verified with the firmware host tests (box_host, modem_host, screen reference images), the ESP-IDF builds of every variant with 0 warnings, and CI; each commit says that Wokwi did not run. No paid plan is bought without the maintainer. | Wokwi was the only runtime check of the real firmware image; host tests cover the same C code but not the IDF drivers or LVGL on the device. | maintainer (2026-10-21): a Heltec V4.3 is now on the bench: runtime checks of the firmware run on it (as on 2026-10-20) instead of Wokwi |

## Phase 2, plan phase C (firmware radio)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | How large should the OTA slots be? | 2 MB each (was 1.5 MB), plus a 4 KB nvs_keys partition; 9.9 MB of the 16 MB flash stays free. | The image was at 87% of 1.5 MB before the radio code. The agent reports that 3 MB slots broke LittleFS in Wokwi; that needs a root cause before going further. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| C2 | Which antenna gain does the EU868 power cap assume? | A conservative Kconfig default for the stock antenna, to be measured at bring-up. Conducted power = the limit minus the FEM gain minus the antenna gain, never above 25 mW ERP on the g bands and 500 mW ERP on 869.4-869.65 MHz. | The first version ignored the antenna gain and could exceed the legal limit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| C3 | How is the game key protected at rest? | NVS encryption with the ESP32-S3 HMAC (eFuse) scheme in production, and a clearly marked fallback for development boards without a burned eFuse key. The eFuse burn is a one-time provisioning step, documented in BRING_UP.md. | ADR-P2-12. Burning an eFuse cannot be undone, so it must not happen by accident on a dev board. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| C4 | The radio is disabled in the Wokwi build because probing it reportedly corrupted the emulated flash. Is that acceptable? | No (maintainer's answer). It must be fixed: the Wokwi build runs with the radio enabled, the probe finds no chip and carries on, and the root cause is found. A disable is only allowed with proof of an emulator defect and the maintainer's agreement. | The radio code has never run in a runtime test, and a real driver bug could look the same. | confirmed |
| C5 | How do boxes choose radio channels? | **Changed from the accepted design.** ADR-P2-13 and RFC 0001 had boxes rotate their transmissions across three 1% channels (868.1, 868.3 and 868.5 MHz), with time and commands on 869.525 MHz, but never said where a box listens. The SX1262 hears one channel at a time, so most packets would be missed. Chosen: one shared channel per game, by default 869.525 MHz (the 869.4-869.65 MHz sub-band: 10% duty cycle and up to 500 mW ERP), for everything; configurable per game to separate neighbouring games. RFC 0001 gets a dated amendment. | The standard approach for single-radio LoRa meshes (Meshtastic's EU868 default uses the same frequency). It gives more airtime than the three 1% channels together, and every box hears every packet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 2, plan phase D (field controller and USB modem)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | ADR-P2-4's modem frames are type + length + payload. How does a receiver find frame boundaries after junk? | Each frame starts with a sync byte `0xA5` and ends with a CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE over type, length and payload; the decoder resynchronises byte by byte. Specified in protocol/usb-modem.md. | The serial line carries the ESP32-S3 boot text, and the Pi may open the port in the middle of a frame; with a bare length prefix one lost byte desynchronises the link for good. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D2 | The RX frame (header + payload + RSSI + SNR) does not say which nonce epoch authenticated the packet. | It carries the epoch too (2 bytes after RSSI and SNR). | A box restarts its seq at 0 after every reboot, so the controller needs the epoch to tell new packets from old ones and to ACK per epoch. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D3 | ADR-P2-4 lists three TX_ACK statuses and no CMD opcodes. | TX_ACK statuses 3-7 added (not configured, invalid, nonce reuse, queue full, no radio); CMD opcodes PING, CONFIGURE, SET_CHANNEL, STATUS, SET_PEER_EPOCH, CLEAR, IDENT; every command answered by STATUS or ERROR. | The controller must know why a packet was not sent, and every command needs a reply for the watchdog. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D4 | ADR-P2-2's receiver tries only the last epoch and the next one. A box that reboots twice out of range, or joins with an epoch above 1, is never decrypted again. | The modem tries up to 16 epochs above the last known one (never below), and the controller can set a box's epoch (SET_PEER_EPOCH). The boxes' own receive code (`radio_protocol.c`) still tries one; a window function is ready for them. Recommend an RFC 0001 amendment. | Without it a twice-rebooted box is silently lost: the opposite of "zero lost captures". 16 extra AES-CCM checks per unknown packet are cheap on the ESP32-S3's AES hardware. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D5 | Who owns the controller's nonce counter, the Pi or the modem? | The Pi: it stores its epoch +1 in SQLite before the first packet of every run and at seq wrap, and sends the epoch with CONFIGURE. The modem refuses any (epoch, seq) not above the last one it sent. | The Pi has the persistent storage and outlives modem reboots; the modem's check stops a Pi bug from reusing a nonce. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D6 | ADR-P2-9's events table is UNIQUE on (game, node, seq). A box's event seq restarts at 1 after a reboot, so its new events would be dropped as duplicates. | UNIQUE on (game, node, seq, game time, type). Replays still match exactly. | A battery pull would otherwise lose every capture after it: the phase 2 gate. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D7 | ACK `ack_seq` is "highest contiguous seq", but a box's STATE and telemetry packets share the seq space and are never resent, so one lost STATE leaves a gap for ever. | Implemented as specified, per (box, epoch), with `ack_seq = 0xFFFF` meaning "seq 0 not received" and the bitmap for the next 256 seqs. Boxes must treat bitmap bits as ACKs. Recommend an RFC change (separate seq spaces for reliable traffic, or per-packet ACKs). | Fails safe (events are resent, never lost) but wastes airtime after a lost STATE. A protocol change needs an RFC. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D8 | The core's C payload codec disagreed with the schema, RFC 0001 and the shared vectors (HELLO capabilities as bytes, STATE counters and CLAIM acks as arrays, CMD params as text, required fields dropped when zero), and its vector tests skipped every vector it could not decode. | **Fixed in the core** to match the schema exactly; required fields and value ranges are now checked; the vector tests in C and Python are strict and also check the invalid vectors. | The controller (Python, schema-exact) could not have read a box's HELLO or STATE. The RFC is the accepted specification, so the C code was the bug. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D9 | CMD `params` is a "map"; the C struct keeps it as 128 bytes of JSON. | A CBOR map with text keys whose JSON form fits 127 bytes; the controller refuses bigger ones. | Matches the schema and what a box can hold. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D10 | Where does the controller keep the game key? (ADR-P2-4: never on the Pi's disk.) | In memory only by default (changed by OT-7, 2026-10-22: on by default now); `persist_game_key = true` keeps it in the SQLite file (mode 0600) so a game survives a controller restart. Without it, a game whose controller restarted can only be ended. | Follows the ADR by default; a field owner who prefers continuity over at-rest secrecy can choose it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D11 | Which REST endpoints make up "17"? | Spec section 11's 15 plus `GET /api/v1/games` and `GET /api/v1/games/{id}`; also `GET /api/v1/controller`, `POST /api/v1/controller/promote` (manual failover) and `GET /healthz`. Mode upload is JSON `{"files": {...}}` (1 MB cap, P5-6), not a zip. The node command endpoint takes `set_owner`, `message`, `reset` and `reboot`; the lifecycle goes through the game endpoints. | No multipart dependency; scripts are checked in the core before they are stored. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D12 | How do REST tokens work? | Read token for reading and the WebSocket, GM token for changes, as `Authorization: Bearer` (or `?token=` for the WebSocket). Unset tokens are generated on first start and kept in the store; `python -m atalaia_controller tokens` prints them. | Spec section 11's read and GM tokens; nothing to configure for a first run. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D13 | How are MQTT commands authorised? | By Mosquitto users and an ACL (`controller/deploy/`), not by a token inside the message. Events published while the broker is down are not queued; retained topics are republished on reconnect. | The broker already does authentication; the SQLite log is the record. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D14 | How many games can be live at once? | One per controller: the modem holds one game key. Other games can be listed and exported. | One radio channel and one key per game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D15 | Plan names: `controller/src/` and pytest. | Package `controller/atalaia_controller/` (like `atalaia_sim`), unittest like the simulator; dependencies from Ubuntu's packages in the dev image and CI (FastAPI 0.101, pydantic 1, paho-mqtt 1.6), with `requirements.txt` for pip. | `src` makes a poor import name; Raspberry Pi OS ships the same generation of packages. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D16 | Which serial port does the modem use? | The ESP32-S3's built-in USB-Serial-JTAG by default; UART0 as a Kconfig option. To be confirmed at bring-up: BRING_UP.md mentions `/dev/ttyUSB0`, which suggests a USB-UART bridge on the board. | The Kconfig comments list GPIO 19/20 as USB; both paths are compiled in every build. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D17 | Can a second Pi stand by as a backup controller in phase 2? | Not until the join flow (plan phase E) can give it the game key. The election code is shared and tested in the simulator (two and three controllers, priority, ties, manual promote, step-down); the real controller steps down on a higher term and can be promoted. | A standby needs the key to hear the game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| D18 | The node firmware starts the radio task but does not yet send its events and STATE or act on TIME, CMD and ACK (found while building the controller; plan phase C left it unwired). | To be wired before plan phase F, as a phase C follow-up; nothing on the controller side depends on it. | The controller and modem are tested against the protocol, but no box talks it yet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 2, plan phase E (GM console, join flow, scoreboard, boxes on the radio)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E1 | ADR-P2-12's join payload is a list of fields with no encoding, and lacks what a box and a standby controller turned out to need. | A versioned binary payload: version byte, a CBOR map with integer keys, HMAC-SHA256 tag; 448 bytes at most (fits an NTAG215). Beyond ADR-P2-12's list it carries `controller_id`, `channel_hz`, `max_hops` (the ADR-P2-6 clamp the ADR says the join carries), `game_seed`, optional `params`, and `kind` (box or standby controller); keys 13-31 are kept for optional fields. NFC: one NDEF MIME record `application/vnd.atalaia.join`; text and QR: `atalaia:join:` + base64url. Specified in protocol/join.md with shared vectors checked by C and Python; recorded as RFC 0001 Amendment 2. | Every other payload is CBOR with integer keys and shared vectors; the version byte and the reserved keys leave room for phase 3 (Android props) without breaking boxes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E2 | ADR-P2-2/P2-12 tag the payload with "the controller's long-term key", which a box cannot check unless it holds that key. | A 32-byte **field join key**, made once per field: generated by the controller on first start (`python -m atalaia_controller join-key` prints it, `[join] key` sets it, so a standby controller can share it), and given to each box once: the setup portal's new optional field, or `@joinkey <hex>` on the serial console (only while no game is joined). Stored in the same NVS namespace as the game key. A development key (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_DEV_JOIN_KEY`) is set only in the Wokwi build. | The tag must be checkable by every reader; a per-field key keeps another field's cards out without a public-key system. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E3 | ADR-P2-12's QR join has the controller push the payload to the box over Wi-Fi (`POST /api/v1/nodes/{id}/join`). Boxes have no HTTP server on the field network. | `POST /api/v1/nodes/{id}/join` (and `POST /api/v1/join`, which picks the id) return the payload as text, QR-ready, and as the bytes for an NFC card; they push nothing. Boxes join by NFC card (PN532 read path written, not yet run on hardware) or by the text on the serial console (bring-up, Wokwi). QR codes are for phones (phase 3) and for copying the text. | A box-side Wi-Fi endpoint means station mode during games and its own authentication; not needed for the phase 2 gate. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E4 | How does a second controller stand by (D17)? | It takes a `kind: controller` payload through `POST /api/v1/controller/join`: the game is stored with the payload's node id, channel and relay clamp, the modem gets the key, and it follows the TIME beacons (state, duration, scores, event log) without acknowledging anything until the GM promotes it. When the TIME beacons say the game ended it leaves the game and forgets the key. After its own restart a joined game comes back as standby (only with `persist_game_key`). Tested over the air with two C host modems: standby follows, the primary stops, the standby is promoted and continues the game with term 2. | Closes D17 with the same election code as the simulator. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E5 | The plan's field view uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles as a second option. | Not in phase E: the field view shows the uploaded field image (or a plain grid) with draggable node markers; positions are stored per game. No Leaflet is vendored and no tile server is contacted. | ADR-P2-10 makes the uploaded image primary and OSM online-only; leaving Leaflet out keeps the console small and offline. Add it when a field asks. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E6 | What does a box put in STATE? | `counters` = points **gained** per team (never decreasing, so the controller's merge by maximum works); `owner_team` = the team of the last `zone.captured` (0 after `zone.neutralized`); `phase` = the box's game state (0 waiting, 1 running, 2 paused, 3 ended), although the schema calls it "mode-specific phase"; `flags` bit 0 running, bit 1 paused. Sent every 15 s (Kconfig) and at once after a capture. | Penalties are not in `counters`: a box's `score.delta` events are logged but not subtracted from the totals. A per-team "lost" counter needs an RFC. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E7 | What do CMD `set_owner` and `message` do on a box? | They reach the mode as input events `cmd.set_owner` and `gm.message` with the CMD's params (`{"team": ...}`, `{"text": ...}`); `message` is also logged. The built-in Domination and Bomb do not handle them yet, so the console's owner override has no effect on those modes. | What an override means is mode-specific; scripts decide. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E8 | How does a box resend unacknowledged events? | Normal and critical events (and HELLO) ask for an ACK and wait in a 32-entry outbox; each resend (every 3 s) is a **new** packet with a new seq, because the modem and the controller drop an identical packet as a flood copy and would never ACK it. Normal events give up after 10 resends (STATE repairs scores), critical ones never. They are also written to the flash event log first, and resent after a reboot; the log is compacted as ACKs arrive. ACKs are read as D7 says (bitmap bits count). | Zero lost captures across a battery pull, as the phase 2 gate requires. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E9 | Box receive and relay. | Boxes use the 16-epoch receive window of D4 (not ADR-P2-2's one), drop packets of other games and their own relayed copies before any decryption, dedupe on (source, epoch, seq) for 30 s, and relay every authenticated packet once after the 10-50 ms jitter with hop_limit clamped to the join's `max_hops` and decremented (core flood.c: hop_limit counts the relays left, so 1 is relayed once more with 0). | The same rules as the modem; tested with a box out of the modem's range heard through another box. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E10 | A lost CMD (CMDs are not acknowledged). | The box also follows the game state in each TIME beacon: if it shows the game running, paused or ended and the box missed the command, the box acts as if it had received it (a late start steps its clock to the controller's). | CMD start, pause and end must not depend on one packet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E11 | Where does a box keep the join? | The whole verified payload (game key included) in NVS namespace `radio`, erased when the game ends (the copy in RAM goes once the last events are acknowledged, at most a minute later). NVS is encrypted only on boxes provisioned as BRING_UP.md step 0 says (C3); other builds now log a warning at boot that the key is in plain NVS. | ADR-P2-12's reboot survival, with the at-rest protection decided in C3. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E12 | GM console libraries. | Preact 10.24.3 (MIT), HTM 3.1.1 (Apache-2.0) and qrcode-generator 2.0.4 (MIT), vendored unmodified from their npm tarballs (checksums checked against the registry and pinned in `lib/README.md`). Class components only: Preact's hooks import a bare `preact` specifier, which needs an inline import map that the CSP forbids. | No build step (ADR-P2-10), a strict CSP, reproducible files. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E13 | Where do the console's browser tests run? | Headless Chromium through Playwright 1.49.0 in its own Docker image (`tools/dev/console/Dockerfile`, `python tools/docker_console.py`) and a new CI job `console`; not in `tools/check.sh`, so the dev image needs no browser. A missing browser or tool is an error there, never a skip. | The browser is 400 MB; keeping it out of the dev image keeps `check.sh` fast. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E14 | The console keeps the token in the browser's local storage. | Yes, per device, with a "leave" button that clears it. The read token can go in a scoreboard URL fragment (`#token=`), which is not sent to the server. | A GM's phone is a personal device; typing a 32-character token for every page load is not workable on the field. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E15 | The game task's stack on the ESP32. | 12 KB (was 8 KB): joins (CBOR, HMAC) and the radio link now run on it, and the Wokwi join scenario overflowed 8 KB before the preset list moved off the stack. | Found by the Wokwi run; 184 KB of DRAM stays free. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E16 | A game whose parameters do not fit in a join payload (over 200 bytes of JSON, or 448 bytes in all). | The controller refuses to make the payload (HTTP 409) instead of sending the box the mode's defaults. | A box playing different rules from the controller is worse than a clear error. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E17 | Which Heltec V4 revision does the firmware drive (the boards arriving may be V4.2 or V4.3)? | A Kconfig choice, *Board revision*: **V4.3 (KCT8103L FEM)**, the default, or **V4.2 (GC1109 FEM)**, for the Field Box and the USB modem. Both: GPIO 7 (the FEM's LDO) high, then GPIO 2 (chip enable) high; V4.3: GPIO 5 (CTX) low = receive LNA; V4.2: GPIO 46 (CPS) high = full PA; DIO2 drives the TX/RX switch on both. Driven only after the SX1262 answers its probe (Wokwi stays safe), in an order planned by `hal/board_fem.c` and tested on the host. Before this, GPIO 7 was held **low** ("RX path"), which would have left the FEM unpowered: the radio nearly deaf and weak on real boards. Pin facts from Heltec's public V4 pinout and Meshtastic's public V4 variant description (facts only, no code). | The revision cannot be read by software; BRING_UP.md tells how to read it off the FEM chip. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E18 | Button 2 was on GPIO 5, which is the V4.3 FEM's CTX pin. | Button 2 moves to **GPIO 39** (on the GNSS header, freed from the keypad matrix by the PCF8574). The radio logs an error at boot if any team button shares a FEM pin. The Wokwi build keeps button 2 on GPIO 5 (its diagram), where no FEM is driven. `hardware/field-box/` needs the same change (left to the build-manual work). | Other free pins are strapping, PSRAM, USB or console pins; 39 keeps the buttons on GPIOs (no I2C latency on the PCF8574). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E19 | Button 4 is on GPIO 34, the V4's GNSS power control (VGNSS_Ctrl). | Kept, with a caveat in Kconfig and BRING_UP.md: with no GNSS module plugged in, pressing button 4 only switches the empty connector's supply; bring-up checks it reads high at rest. | Moving it would take the last free GPIO; GPS is not used in this pin map (E21). | reopened by the maintainer (2026-10-21): GNSS must stay usable for future modes; see the pin-map revision (E21) |
| E20 | Does the 14 dB FEM-gain assumption keep both FEMs inside the 500 mW ERP cap? | Kept at 14 dB for both, **not verified**: the firmware sets the SX1262 to 13 dBm, which is legal (26.85 dBm ERP with a 2 dBi antenna) only if the real gain is at most ~14 dB. The FEMs saturate at 27-28 dBm, so the worst case is under 1 dB over the cap. BRING_UP.md step 10 now requires measuring the conducted power before any game transmission and setting `ATALAIA_RADIO_FEM_GAIN_DB` to the measured gain (C2). | The gains of the GC1109 and KCT8103L at our drive level are not known from public facts; a measurement settles it, and a too-low setting would cost range for nothing. | maintainer (2026-10-21): validate with the V4.3 and its antenna; needs a conducted-power measurement (RF power meter, or an SDR with an attenuator); until then the safe setting stays |
| E21 | The V4 ships with a GNSS module for its SH1.25 connector (GPIO 34, 38-42). | **GPS is not supported on the Field Box in this pin map**: the GNSS header pins stay with the display and buttons, and BRING_UP.md says not to plug the module in. Revisit the pin map in phase 4, when GPS-based modes (VIP, Payload) arrive. | The display needs those pins now; no phase 2 or 3 mode needs the box's position. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): plan ahead and keep GNSS usable (the module came with the board): revise the Field Box pin map so the GNSS header is free, before the TFT and keypad are wired |
| E22 | Vext (GPIO 36, active low) was never driven. | The firmware drives it low at boot, before probing peripherals, on the Field Box and the USB modem (`hal_esp_board_power_on`, `CONFIG_ATALAIA_VEXT_CTL_PIN`, -1 to leave it alone). | The OLED and the external peripherals are powered from it on the V4. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E23 | `python tools/docker_fw.py build` used `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig`, which a local Wokwi or menuconfig session could leave with emulator values (a keypad matrix on GPIO 1/2/7/39), so a "Field Box" image could drive the radio front-end pins as keypad lines. | `build` now keeps its configuration in `build/sdkconfig`, made from `sdkconfig.defaults` alone and remade when the defaults change, like the Wokwi and modem variants; `clean` also deletes a stray `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig`. CI's job is unchanged (a fresh checkout has no `sdkconfig`). | Found by the build-manual work. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| E24 | The Heltec V4's only USB port is the ESP32-S3's own USB-Serial-JTAG, but the shell read console input from UART0 only, so `@join`, `@joinkey` and serial keys could not reach a real box. | The Field Box reads console input from both: USB-Serial-JTAG (the driver installed at boot, `CONFIG_ATALAIA_CONSOLE_USB_INPUT`, default on) and UART0, each with its own line parser. IDF's driver drops console output after 50 ms when no USB host listens, so a box on battery does not block. The Wokwi build keeps UART0 only. Not yet run on a board: the parser is host-tested, the USB path is only compiled. | Bring-up and joining from a laptop need it. | confirmed on hardware (2026-10-20): keys typed over the V4.3's USB-Serial-JTAG reached the box (PIN, picker, game start) |
| E25 | The LED ring had no brightness control (WS2812 has none of its own); 24 LEDs at full white draw about 1.4 A. | The driver scales every colour at `show()`: 40% at boot (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_LED_BRIGHTNESS_PCT`), and `ring:set_brightness(percent)` from modes (0-100, clamped). The scaling is host-tested; the LED count fallback is 24, as in Kconfig. | Power budget of a battery box; 40% is still daylight-visible on a WS2812 ring (to confirm at bring-up). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

D17 (standby controller) and D18 (boxes on the radio) are done in this phase: see E4 and E8-E10.

## Phase 3, 4 and 5 designs

The open questions from those design runs are added here as each run reports, with the recommended answer used for now.

### Phase 5, community (design: docs/phase-5-community/)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5-1 | Which third language comes first, after Portuguese and English? | Spanish (es). | The largest neighbouring airsoft community, and close to Portuguese for translators. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): German and Traditional Chinese (Taiwan) next (Spanish speakers understand Portuguese well enough); a call for translators on the project page for French, Spanish and others |
| P5-2 | Is the visual mode editor a tab in the simulator or a separate page? | A separate page at `/editor/` that uses the simulator to test a mode (ADR-P5-2). | Keeps the editor and the simulator independent while reusing the simulator to run the blocks. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P5-3 | Create the separate `atalaia-modes` registry repository now, or later? | **Not yet.** The design recommends creating it now with a placeholder index, but creating a GitHub repository changes the maintainer's account, so it waits for the maintainer's go-ahead. | Outward-facing action; the rest of the registry work (schema, signing tools, tests) can proceed in this repository first. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P5-4 | Do mode packages live in the registry repository or in authors' own repositories? | Both; the registry hosts packages for authors without their own repository. | Lowers the barrier for first-time mode authors. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P5-5 | Is a partial translation acceptable? | Yes for modes and the editor, with English as the fallback; the project page must stay 100% translated (its build check enforces it). | Community translations arrive gradually. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P5-6 | Maximum size of a mode package? | 1 MB per package. | Modes are scripts and small assets; a cap limits abuse of the registry. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P5-7 | The carrier PCB: when? | Deferred until at least three perfboard prototypes work (ADR-P5-6); KiCad 8, CERN-OHL-S-2.0. | A PCB before the hand-wired boxes are proven would likely need a respin. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

### Phase 3, Android and players (design: docs/phase-3-android-players/)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P3-1 | How are chrono readings signed? | **Changed from the design's choice.** Ed25519 per marshal in both phase 3 and 4. ADR-P3-7 updated (2026-10-18 handoff). | Ed25519 survives password changes and lets other fields verify records without a shared secret. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-2 | Screen templates: code or data? | Declarative YAML templates in each mode's `assets/screens/` (ADR-P3-4), as spec section 12 describes, with a minimal YAML parser on the ESP32. The firmware's current C-function templates (ADR-P1-2) get rewritten to read them. | Modes can ship their own screens and look the same on the box and the app. It costs a firmware parser and a renderer change. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-3 | New standard events? | IDs 25-27 and 33-41 for phase 3 (kill.confirmed, kill.denied, team.eliminated, money.collected, money.deposited, chrono.checkin, chrono.spotcheck, visibility.granted, visibility.expired, node.tamper, pod.connected, pod.disconnected). IDs 28-32 (holdall.*, laststand.*) owned by the phase 2 mode pack (R6, K9). Data keys 29-30 for phase 3; Money Factory reuses `amount` (key 6). ADR-P3-8 updated. | Events are protocol changes (CONTRIBUTING.md). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-4 | MQTT on an Android controller? | A minimal embedded Dart MQTT 3.1.1 broker, QoS 0 and 1 only (ADR-P3-12); the Pi keeps Mosquitto. | No external broker on a phone. Worth checking whether a maintained Dart broker exists before writing one. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-5 | Chrono limit templates (Portugal, UK, Germany, generic CQB)? | Ship inside the app; Portuguese values to be checked against the current national rules before release. | Props may be offline. The figures came from research, not an official source. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): Portuguese law caps airsoft at 1.3 J: the Portugal limit template follows it |
| P3-6 | Smaller choices: the yaml package in Flutter (custom parser only on the ESP32), no dormant FFI bridge on iOS, cirruslabs/flutter:stable for CI, pod firmware sharing the modem framing (now includes sync+CRC), a 247-byte minimum BLE MTU, and plan phase A first. | As the design chose (M-1 to M-8 in its report), updated for modem framing change. | Standard tooling, code reuse, no BLE fragmentation. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-7 | Phase 2's USB modem framing and join format are now implemented. Do the phase 3 assumptions hold? | **Resolved (2026-10-18 handoff).** B4: modem framing changed to sync+CRC; pod framing updated (ADR-P3-2). B10: join payload is CBOR+HMAC, max 448 bytes; CA cert replaced by fingerprint + Wi-Fi fetch (ADR-P3-9). B6/B7: REST/WS exist; phase 3 extends them. B13: time sync holds. All assumptions re-checked in PLAN.md section 4 and ADR.md handoff section. | Phase 2 A-E complete; interfaces verified against implemented code. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-8 | The self-signed CA certificate does not fit in the 448-byte join payload. How is it distributed? | SHA-256 fingerprint in join payload reserved key 13; app fetches full cert from `GET /api/v1/controller/ca` over plain HTTP and verifies the fingerprint. Needs an RFC 0001 amendment for key 13. ADR-P3-9 updated. | 448-byte limit from protocol/join.md. A DER cert is ~800-1200 bytes. The fingerprint is 32 bytes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-9 | Player tokens and a position-reporting endpoint are not in the phase 2 API. How do they arrive? | Phase 3 adds `POST /api/v1/positions` (authenticated with player token), `GET /api/v1/controller/ca`, and player token issuance. The player token is HMAC-SHA256-signed JSON with player_id, team, role, game_id, exp. Controller URL comes from the Wi-Fi network (mDNS or the join's controller IP, not in the payload). | The join payload has no room for a controller URL; phase 2 boxes find the controller by its TIME beacons. Phones find it by the Wi-Fi AP or mDNS. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P3-10 | How does an Android prop join a game? | Over Wi-Fi: scan a QR code (text form `atalaia:join:...`) shown on the GM console, or enter the text manually. The app decodes the join payload, verifies the HMAC with the field join key (which the app must hold, entered once at setup), tunes the pod (if any) to the game channel, and connects to the controller's API. QR does NOT push to the device (E3); the phone reads and acts. | QR pushes nothing to boxes (decision E3). The phone is the active reader, like a box scanning an NFC card. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

### Phase 4, site server and advanced play (design: docs/phase-4-site-server/)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P4-1 | Where does the custom-scenario compiler (YAML objectives to field.lua) run? | **Changed from the design's choice.** The design puts it on the site server with a CLI fallback (ADR-P4-6). Chosen instead: a shared Python library at `controller/atalaia_controller/scenarios/` that runs on the field controller (the GM console calls it there), and the site server imports and reuses the same library. ADR-P4-6 and PLAN.md Phase E updated (2026-10-18 handoff). | The site server is optional and the platform is local-first (spec sections 3 and 11); a field without a site server must still build custom scenarios. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4-2 | How are chrono records signed? | Ed25519 key per marshal (ADR-P4-5), not HMAC. | Survives password changes and lets other fields verify a record without a shared secret. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4-3 | How are clubs isolated in hosted mode? | A PostgreSQL schema per field, with row-level security as defence in depth (ADR-P4-4). | Export and deletion are a single schema dump or drop; RLS catches query mistakes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4-4 | Where do geo functions (distance, inside-zone) live? | In the C core (geo.c), exposed to node and field scripts (ADR-P4-9). | Trigonometry in Lua is slow on the ESP32, and one implementation keeps every platform identical. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4-5 | Is two-factor login mandatory? | TOTP is mandatory for admin, GM and marshal accounts, optional for players (ADR-P4-2). | These roles can change games and penalties. Field staff may find TOTP awkward on game day, so worth confirming. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4-6 | GDPR: is a legal review needed? | Yes, before hosted mode goes live. The design ships templates and defaults (anonymise on erasure, keep chrono signatures, retention 12 and 24 months, daily purge, PT/EN notices) that a Portuguese lawyer should review. | Hosting other clubs' player data makes the project a data processor. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): the templates in `docs/templates/` go to a lawyer before hosted mode goes live |
| P4-7 | Which database for the site server? | PostgreSQL 16 in docker-compose with Caddy for TLS and daily pg_dump backups (ADR-P4-1, ADR-P4-10). | Multi-tenant hosting and JSONB event data; it runs on a mini PC or a small VPS. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): move to a newer PostgreSQL to be future-proof (18) |
| P4-8 | How does the site server authenticate to the controller for sync? | The site server uses the controller's GM token for write operations and read token for reads. The controller authenticates to the site server with a 256-bit API key (Argon2id-hashed, stored in `fields.api_key_hash`). Auth is asymmetric: controller-to-site-server uses an API key, site-server-to-controller uses bearer tokens. | The controller already has bearer token auth (read/GM tokens, decision D12). The site server needs GM-level access for sync writes (game state, score push) and read-level for pulls (event log, device health). Adding a separate API-key path on the controller would duplicate auth. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

### Spec questions found along the way

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | The spec (section 13) says ESP32 firmware images are "signed with Ed25519", and phase 5 signs modes with Ed25519 to match. The ESP32-S3's hardware secure boot only supports RSA-3072 or ECDSA. | Keep Ed25519 for modes and for an application-level check of OTA images, and decide separately whether to also enable hardware secure boot (RSA-3072 or ECDSA) when the firmware update path is built. | Ed25519 in software is fine for modes and update packages; hardware secure boot is a separate, one-time eFuse decision. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 2 mode pack (modes/holdall, laststand, codes; Bomb team codes)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| K1 | How do modifiers run alongside a main mode? | One id `main+modifier` (≤63 bytes) used by games, presets and joins. The scripts are concatenated, each in `do…end`, in that order. Parameters are merged into one schema (names must differ). Join encoding is unchanged; `protocol/join.md` documents the meaning. | No protocol change; one rule in the simulator, the controller and the box. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K2 | Where do mode translations go? | An `i18n.<lang>` block in `mode.yaml` (name, description, parameter labels). English stays the base text. pt-PT added for all modes. Not the spec's `i18n/` folder. | Travels with uploads (only `mode.yaml` and the scripts are accepted); simple for the console. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K3 | Codes semantics | Format `digits:kind[:points][:team]`, at most 120 bytes. Each code works once per box (same list on every box). `code.used` reports the code's place in the list, not its digits. A points code goes to the code's team, else the box holder, else it is refused. Hint and unlock only notify. No lockout after wrong codes. | Join parameters are capped at 200 bytes; read-token holders can see the log; boxes have no shared state. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K4 | Bomb per-team codes | `team_codes` turns the keypad into the only arm/defuse input. A team's code arms; any other team's code defuses. The explosion is credited to the arming team. Events add `reason: code`. Hold mode is unchanged. | Matches the forum request; backwards compatible. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K5 | `field.lua` points across restart and failover | They are controller counters, one per run, merged by maximum and saved with the game, so they survive a restart. They are not replicated to a standby: after a takeover the new controller's run starts from 0, and earlier field-script points are missing from its totals. | Fixing it needs controller counters on the radio (an RFC). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K6 | Restarting `field.lua` | After a controller restart or a takeover, the script runs again from the current game time. Last Stand's countdown restarts from its full length. Last Stand's countdown can start only once the defence is set up (defenders seen above `last_zones`) or after 30 s, because box owners come from STATE, which lags. | Avoids a spurious countdown at the start; keeps the scripts stateless. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K7 | Last Stand rules | Defenders claim every zone at game start (on the boxes). The countdown runs while they hold `last_zones` or fewer (losing all zones does not end it at once). `min_zones` default 2. `reset` default true. `end_game` → `game.win(attackers)`. | Spec section 2: "countdown to defeat while holding 1 of 3". | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K8 | Hold-All rules | Owners come from STATE (E6), checked every 1 s and on captures. The first points come one full tick after the last box falls. `min_zones` default 2. Boxes are not told the hold-all state (no CMD messages: airtime, and no screen for it). | Simple and robust to radio lag. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K9 | Event ids | Took 28–32 and data key 28 from ADR-P3-8. `holder` is new at key 31. `code.used` adds `amount` and `holder`; bomb events add `reason`. Ids 25–27, 33–39 and keys 29–30 stay with phase 3. | Keeps the phase 3 numbering. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K10 | How modes reach a box | Every mode in `modes/` is built in as a preset. At boot a box adds missing built-in modes and refreshes their scripts (the firmware owns built-in ids). There is no upload path yet, so a new mode needs one flash; no mode needs its own C code. | The only path that exists today. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K11 | Preset script size | 16 KB (was 8 KB), so main mode + modifiers fit: Bomb + Codes is 9.3 KB. Costs about 16 KB more static DRAM (two buffers), plus a 3.7 KB static list. | Measured; DIRAM still has 96 KB free. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K12 | Screen for codes | New C template `code_entry` (title, dots, optional clock, message); English text comes from the Lua scripts. Not waiting for P3-2's YAML templates. | Minimal change; P3-2 can replace it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K13 | Reduced-tier memory | Bomb + Codes loads in about 50 KB of the 64 KB heap; it runs in tests. Full tier recommended for that combination. | Measured in the simulator with a 64 KB limit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K14 | Simulator event order | Events at the same time keep their arrival order (was node id, then seq). With `field.lua`, time moves in 100 ms steps. | A controller's reaction must come after its cause. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| K15 | Hold-All and Last Stand in a box's own picker | Listed, but standalone they only capture: scoring and the countdown need the controller. | The presets must exist for joins. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 3, plan phase A (event schema, single-node modes, player tags, capability proxy, screen templates)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | What does plan phase A include? The plan disagrees with itself: its goal and file list name three modes and put the chrono data model in plan phase B, while constraint 20 and AC-2 also list Money Factory under phase A. | Kill Confirmed, Respawn and Lives, Player Check-in, the events (Money Factory's included), player tags, the capability proxy protocol and the screen templates with the LVGL renderer. Money Factory's mode waits: as one-box modes its "collect, then deposit elsewhere" needs state shared between boxes (the controller's `field.lua`, or tags rewritten by the boxes), which no document decides yet. Chrono stays in plan phase B (Dart); the Flutter renderer in plan phase D. | The goal text and file list are the plan's statement of the phase; Money Factory's rules are not designed anywhere beyond its two events. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A2 | The data of the new events (ADR-P3-8 lists `[player, team]` for the kill events and `[team, text]` for visibility). | `kill.confirmed` and `kill.denied`: `team` (common field) is the team credited or recovering the tag, `player` and `owner` (the tag's team) say whose tag. `visibility.granted`: `see_team`, `duration_s`, `text`; `visibility.expired`: `see_team`, `text`. `chrono.spotcheck` adds `tolerance_pct`. `team.eliminated` carries only `team`. ADR-P3-8's "phase 4 starts at ID 40" corrected to 42. A draft RFC records the additions (`docs/rfcs/0000-phase-3-events-tags-and-pods.md`). | `team` cannot mean both the scoring team and the victim's; `see_team` (key 29) exists for exactly this. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A3 | Player tag details ADR-P3-9 leaves open. | `version (1) \| player (1-32 bytes of letters, digits, - _ .) \| team (1-based id, 0 none) \| game_id (2) \| HMAC-SHA256(game key)[:16]`, NDEF MIME record `application/vnd.atalaia.tag`; the game id is checked before the MAC (so an old game's tag says "game"). The key is the game key itself, as the ADR says, although it also keys AES-CCM; a key derived with HKDF would keep the two uses apart (open question in the draft RFC). | A version byte lets the format change; a fixed character set keeps the JSON simple; separate reasons tell a player what is wrong with a tag. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A4 | Who checks a tag: the script or the platform? | The platform: the host checks the tag with the game's id and key (core `atalaia_tag_event_json`) and gives the script `nfc.tag` with `valid`, `player` and `team`, or `reason`. Scripts never see the key. A game picked from a box's own list has no game key, so its tags are refused (`no_game`): the tag modes are for controller-run games. | Same rule on every platform, no key in Lua, and the Wokwi/serial path (`@tag`) reuses it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A5 | Kill Confirmed rules. | A team presses its button at the station, then scans the tags it collected within `turn_in_s` (20 s; each counted tag keeps it open). An enemy's tag: `kill.confirmed`, `confirm_points` (1) to the team, `penalty_points` (1) off the tag's team. A teammate's tag: `kill.denied`, no points. The same player's tag counts again after `retag_after_s` (60 s). Each box counts on its own. The penalty is local to the box: STATE carries only gains (E6), so the controller's totals do not subtract it, although the log has the `score.delta`. | Matches the spec's "scanning a team's tag removes their point" and the plan's +1/-1; the penalty gap is E6's, to be closed by an RFC for a "lost" counter. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A6 | Respawn and Lives rules. | Lives per team (default 20) or per player (`per_player`); a tag respawn waits `respawn_cooldown_s` (30 s) per player; a team button press is a respawn for that team with a 2 s guard (it carries no player). A team with no lives left is refused and `team.eliminated` is sent once; the game does not end by itself. Lives are counted per box: one respawn point per team, or one shared. The plan named the parameter `lives_per_team`; it is `lives` with `per_player`. | A box cannot know a team's roster, so per-player mode cannot tell when a whole team is out; ending the game is left to the GM. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A7 | Player Check-in rules. | Counts check-ins per team on the box; `points` (1) per check-in for the player's team; a player counts once per game (`repeat_after_s` 0) or again after that many seconds; tags without a team are refused. | The plan's "duplicate scan within 10 s rejected" generalised into a parameter. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A8 | The Field Box never sent `team_input.press`, although modes/README.md documents it. | The button tracker now sends it when a team button goes down (before the holds). No existing mode reacts to it. | Kill Confirmed and Respawn count presses. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A9 | Capability proxy details ADR-P3-2 leaves open or gets wrong. | Added RADIO_ERROR (`0x0A`, the modem's ERROR + 3); a RADIO_CMD IDENT is answered with the proxy IDENT; GATT service `4174616c-6169-6100-0000-000000000000` with Pod TX/RX/Status at `...0001`-`...0003` (the ADR gave the service Pod TX's UUID); Pod Status is 3 bytes (battery, RSSI, flags); keys 6-23 are skipped for forward compatibility; required fields and limits fixed; an NFC event carries the card's player-tag record (up to 52 bytes), not the whole card (would not fit 256 bytes). The C codec lives in the core (`core/src/proxy.c`, next to the CBOR helpers and under the sanitizers); the frames are tested with the modem framing in `firmware/esp32/test` (`ctest -R proxy_codec`), and a Python reference codec in the simulator. | Every radio command needs a reply; one parser for both transports; frozen means fully specified. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A10 | Screen template format beyond ADR-P3-4. | Kept the ADR's widgets and style properties, and added: comparisons, `!`, `&&` and `\|\|` (giving the deciding value, as in Lua, so `{a && 'x' \|\| 'y'}` chooses a colour) in expressions; text filters `\|clock`, `\|upper`, `\|int`; `{{`/`}}`; a screen-level `style: { background }`; colours `contrast` and `{expression}`; one-line flow mappings (the ADR's own example uses them; the plan said "no flow collections"). The simulator checks templates with the core's parser through ctypes rather than a second parser. | Without these the migrated Bomb screens could not keep their M:SS clock, red last seconds and team-coloured results. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A11 | Where the Field Box reads templates from. | From the firmware image: every `modes/*/assets/screens/*.yaml` is compiled in at build time (`builtin_screens.cmake`), like the scripts (K10), not from LittleFS (no upload path yet). The node runner announces the running mode to the LVGL task with an `@mode` message in the screen queue. `qr_code` draws its text on the box: the QR library stays out of the image until a template needs it. | The only path that exists; ordered with the screens, so no race between tasks. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A12 | Which screens move to templates. | Bomb (standby, countdown, defused, exploded) and Domination (standby, captured, final_score; Domination now draws a screen while it runs, it drew none) and the three new modes. Bomb never draws `final_score`, so the plan's `bomb/assets/screens/final_score.yaml` was not made. Hold-All, Last Stand and Codes keep the C screens (`final_score`, `code_entry`). | The plan's list, minus a screen nothing draws. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| A13 | The plan runs mode tests with `pytest modes/`. | The project's runner: `python -m atalaia_sim test` (in `tools/check.sh`); it now also counts each mode's screen templates as a test. New test step `scan` for player tags. | There is no pytest in the project. | answered by the maintainer's question (2026-10-21): kept `unittest` and the simulator's runner (no new dependency, CI runs them as they are); pytest can run them unchanged locally if wanted |

Found and fixed along the way: `score.add(team, -n, reason)` (a penalty with a reason and no squad) put the event's data table inside itself, so the event could not be encoded and the handler failed with `script.error` (core `script_api.c`; a core test now covers it). No built-in mode had used that form.

## Phase 3, plan phase B (chrono logic, player tags and player tokens in Dart)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | The plan's file list still says `hmac_signer.dart` (HMAC-SHA256 chrono signing) and lists `pointycastle`. | Followed ADR-P3-7 as changed by P3-1: Ed25519 per marshal in `app/lib/chrono/record_signer.dart` (test `record_signer_test.dart`). Ed25519 from the `cryptography` package 2.9.0, using its pure-Dart `DartEd25519` explicitly; HMAC-SHA256 (tags, tokens) from the Dart team's `crypto` 3.0.7. No `pointycastle` (it has no Ed25519) and no `ffi` yet (plan phase C). The Dart Ed25519 is checked against RFC 8032's three known answers and refuses non-canonical signatures (S ≥ L), as OpenSSL does on the Python side. | The ADR is the decision; the file list was not updated with it. `cryptography` is maintained, widely used and pure Dart (the same code on Android, iOS and the test runner); `crypto` is the standard HMAC. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B2 | How is Dart built and tested, and does `tools/check.sh` run it? | `app/` is the Flutter project (`atalaia_app`, Flutter SDK dependency, no platform folders yet) so plan phases C and D add to it. Image `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/flutter:3.44.0` pinned by digest (`sha256:46691e31...`; Flutter 3.44.0 / Dart 3.12.0, built 2026-05-20, the newest stable tag; P3-6 said `:stable`). `tools/docker_app.py` (check, test, format, lock; volume `atalaia-app-pub-cache` + `ATALAIA_VOLUME_SUFFIX`) and CI's "Player app logic (Dart)" both run `app/tool/check.sh` on a copy of the read-only checkout: `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`, `flutter analyze --fatal-infos`, `dart format --set-exit-if-changed`, `flutter test`. Dependencies pinned exactly, `pubspec.lock` committed. `check.sh` does not call it (atalaia-dev has no Dart and the image is 2.3 GB); it does run the Python side of the shared vectors (controller tests). | One script for local and CI runs; a moving `:stable` tag would change the analyzer and formatter under the code. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B3 | ADR-P3-7's canonical JSON says only "signature removed, keys sorted, no whitespace": not enough for two languages to sign the same bytes. | `protocol/chrono-record.md`: members sorted, no whitespace, strings in UTF-8 escaping only `"` and `\` (control characters and lone surrogates refused in every field), numbers as the shortest round-trip binary64 decimal in plain notation (`100.0`, `0.2`). Every value is kept between 1e-4 and 5000 by the field ranges and decimals (velocity 0.01 m/s, weight 0.001 g, energy 0.001 J, rounded half away from zero), where Dart's `double.toString()` and Python's `repr` agree. It equals ADR-P4-5's `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)`, so phase 4 can use its snippet as written. Defined over values: a verifier re-serialises after parsing. | Floats are the usual way canonical JSON breaks across languages; restricting the values avoids implementing RFC 8785 twice. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B4 | What is one chrono record: a shot or a check-in series? How are energies compared? | One record per shot (the ADR's record has one velocity). A series is judged by its highest energy. The comparison uses each record's `energy_j` as stored (rounded to 0.001 J), so the result matches what the marshal sees and anyone can recompute it from the signed records. | Every measured shot stays signed; a 0.0005 J rounding is far below a chronograph's own error. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B5 | Record fields beyond ADR-P3-7. | Added `ref_reading_id` (the ADR says a retest references the original reading without naming a field): required for `retest`, optional for `spotcheck`, absent for `checkin`. `game_id` is optional (a morning check-in has no game; ADR-P4-1's records allow none) and a string, like the other ids. | Append-only records need the link; a 16-bit controller game id is reused over a year of records. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B6 | Field formats and checks. | Lowercase UUIDs for reading and replica ids; other ids 1-64 of `A-Za-z0-9._:-`; `timestamp` `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` (UTC, whole seconds, a real date); `marshal_name` 1-64 characters, not normalised; velocity 0-1000 m/s, weight 0-10 g (paintballs fit); unknown fields refused. Reasons `format`, `unsigned`, `signature`, checked in that order. | Strict parsing on both sides means a record either verifies everywhere or nowhere. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B7 | Where does the Python verifier live? | `controller/atalaia_controller/chrono.py` and `player_token.py` (AGPL), with `controller/tests/test_chrono_records.py` and `test_player_token.py` against the shared vectors (in `check.sh` and CI's controller job). Not wired into the API: the chrono and position endpoints are plan phase E. The site server (phase 4) can copy them. | The controller receives readings and issues tokens in phase 3; `cryptography` is already its dependency. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B8 | ADR-P3-9 signs player tokens with "the controller's game key". | The format takes any 16-64-byte key, and `protocol/player-token.md` says the controller should use a token key of its own: 32 random bytes per game that never leave the controller. **Changed from the ADR's choice.** | The game key is in every box, every Android prop and every join card and QR code; with it anyone could mint a `gm` or `marshal` token and see every position, which ADR-P3-6's server-side filtering is meant to prevent. Only the controller issues and checks tokens, so no one else needs the key. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B9 | Player token details ADR-P3-9 leaves open. | `apt1.<base64url(canonical JSON)>.<base64url(HMAC-SHA256 over "apt1.<payload>")>`, full 32-byte MAC, at most 512 characters. Claims exactly `exp` (Unix seconds), `game_id` (the 16-bit integer, not the ADR example's `"g-001"`), `player_id` (the tag's id rule), `role` (player, squad_leader, vip, marshal, gm, spectator: ADR-P3-6's table), `squad` (1-255, 0 none), `team` (1-8, 0 none). An authentic but non-canonical payload is refused. No clock leeway. Reasons `format`, `version`, `mac`, `game`, `team`, `expired`. | A versioned prefix like the join text's; canonical payloads keep the two implementations byte-identical; the integer game id is what the controller has. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B10 | Limit template files and format. | Files named by country: `portugal.yaml`, `uk.yaml`, `germany.yaml`, `generic_cqb.yaml` (the plan's `portugal_apda.yaml` and `uk_ukara.yaml` named associations as if they were the source). Format 1 adds `id`, `status: unverified`, a `warning` shown with the template, `notes`; `verified_date: null`; `med_m` may be null; `max_fps_020` must be within 1% of `max_energy_j` with 0.20 g. Every file and `app/README.md` say the figures are unverified research, not the law (P3-5); a test fails if a built-in template stops saying so. The research's German figures (0.5 J full-auto, 7.5 J semi-only) are legal ceilings, not game limits, and the file says so; the research attributed the UK figures to UKARA, a retailers' registration scheme, and the file says that too. Field overrides keep the template's status. | Decision P3-5; nothing here was checked against a primary source, so the files must not look authoritative. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B11 | Spot-check edge cases. | At the limit, or at exactly check-in + tolerance, passes (`<=`, as the ADR's PASS rule). Without a check-in, only the limit counts. Tolerance 0-100%, default 5%. A check-in or retest is PASS or FAIL only. | ADR-P3-7's rules, with the boundaries made explicit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B12 | GDPR retention period unit. | Days: 365 by default, 1 to 3653 (10 years), in `app/lib/chrono/retention.dart`, with erasure by player. | "12 months" in days avoids calendar arithmetic that two languages could do differently; the operator can set 366. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| B13 | Plan phase B's scope beyond the file list. | Also in Dart: the `nfc.tag` event data a prop gives its mode (checked against `tag.json`'s events), the NFC Type 2 tag memory helpers (join cards use the same record layout), a strict `Replica` (NFC UIDs as 4, 7 or 10 uppercase hex pairs, QR labels as 1-64 printable ASCII, `class` as a template class name) and the retention rules (constraint 18 of the plan). | The Android prop (plan phase D) needs the tag event exactly as the core writes it; the rest is small and keeps the data model complete. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 3, plan phase C (dart:ffi bridge, game loop isolate, FFI stub, Android NDK build)

The "PC" prefix keeps these apart from phase 2 plan phase C's rows C1-C5.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PC1 | ADR-P3-1 and constraint 2 say `NativeCallable.listener` for every callback. | **Changed from the ADR's choice.** `NativeCallable.isolateLocal` (`app/lib/ffi/callbacks.dart`). The core calls its callbacks synchronously, inside `atalaia_node_input`/`_advance`, on the game loop isolate's own thread; the handlers copy everything into Dart objects there and the isolate forwards them to the UI through its `SendPort`. `Pointer.fromFunction` is still not used. | A `.listener` runs later, after the core has returned: the event struct and the JSON strings it points to are gone by then (they live on the core's stack or in Lua). It also cannot return a value, and the field operations (`nodes`, `owner`, `send`) must; and it could deliver output after the reply to the call that caused it. The ADR's goal, nothing on the UI isolate, is kept: only the game loop isolate calls the core. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC2 | Where do the Dart tests get a real core, when the Flutter image has no CMake? | The core and the FFI stub are built as shared libraries before the Dart tests and passed in: by `tools/docker_app.py` in the `atalaia-dev` image (into the volume `atalaia-app-core`), by CI's `flutter-test` on the Ubuntu 24.04 runner. Both are Ubuntu 24.04 with glibc 2.39, as the Flutter image is, so the libraries load there. `ATALAIA_CORE_LIB` names the real core (as for the simulator) and `ATALAIA_CORE_STUB_LIB` the stub (the plan used `ATALAIA_CORE_LIB` for the stub). New CMake option `ATALAIA_MBEDTLS` (default ON) builds without mbedtls, which the Flutter image and Android lack. Not a derived image: the Flutter image does have GCC 13 (the task's premise that it has no compiler is wrong) but no CMake, and an image with CMake added would duplicate `atalaia-dev` and need its own pins. | One build recipe for the core everywhere; no new image to pin and maintain. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC3 | The plan's test list has only `bindings_test` and `game_loop_isolate_test` against the stub. How are modes tested through the Dart bridge? | Every mode's `modes/*/tests/*.yaml` (all 8 mode folders, 36 files), each twice: nodes called directly through `CoreNode`, and each node in its own game loop isolate with a manual clock. `app/test/support/mode_runner.dart` is a port of the simulator's `testing.py`, `sim.py` (without the radio model) and `mode.py`: the same steps, expectations, parameter checks, modifiers and screen-template checks. Every event is checked against `protocol/schema/events.yaml` and must fit a LoRa payload, through the core's own C codec (the simulator uses its Python codec). A mode's `field.lua` always runs directly with field operations. | Python and Dart run the same YAML files, so a mode behaves the same in both. `field.lua` needs synchronous answers about the field, which the test runner holds; the Android controller (plan phase E) will run it the same way, inside its own isolate. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC4 | "Prefer generating or checking layouts rather than hand-copying." | Checked, not generated. The stub exports `atalaia_stub_layout()`: the size and every field offset of the six structs the bindings mirror, compiled from the real headers. `app/test/ffi/layout_test.dart` compares the Dart structs with it and `simulator/tests/test_ffi_layout.py` the simulator's ctypes structs (in `tools/check.sh` and CI's core job, which now build the stub). Dart lays out `size_t` and pointers per ABI, so a match on x86-64 holds on armeabi-v7a. | `ffigen` needs libclang, which neither image has; a check catches drift in both bindings with no generator to pin. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC5 | What does the FFI stub do? ADR-P3-11 says "success/default values". | `core/tests/stub.c` (`-DATALAIA_STUB=ON`, `libatalaia_core_stub.so`, built with `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`): accepted inputs echo back as an event with the same type and data, a `ui.screen` command and a log line; `game.pause`/`resume`/`end` change the state; `advance` emits `stub.tick` per 100 ms crossed; an empty script halts; `stub.field` calls every field operation; each input scores a point for team 1; plus the layout report. Header comment lists it all. | A stub that only returns defaults cannot test callbacks, ordering or the isolate's clock. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC6 | The game loop isolate's clock and API. | `GameLoopIsolate.spawn(spec, clock:)`. Real time (the app): a 10 ms `Timer.periodic`; inputs are queued and applied at the next tick, then game time moves by the `Stopwatch` time since the last tick; game time starts at the first accepted `game.start` and stands still while paused, ended or halted. Manual (tests, replays): inputs apply at once, `advanceTo` moves time. Outputs (events, commands, log lines, status changes, handler errors) arrive in order on one port, before the reply to the call that caused them. The core's default memory limit is used (256 KB, as the ADR asks). The game's length timer is not in the isolate: the shell sends `game.end`, as the Field Box's shell does. | As ADR-P3-1 describes, plus a deterministic clock so the mode tests can run through the isolate. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC7 | How are iOS and the web kept from the core (ADR-P3-10, P3-6 "no dormant FFI bridge on iOS")? | `lib/core/node_runner.dart` picks `node_runner_android.dart` where Dart has `dart:ffi` and `node_runner_stub.dart` elsewhere (the web). The UI only sees `lib/core/node_types.dart`, which has no `dart:ffi`. iOS has `dart:ffi`, so the FFI runner is compiled in there but reports itself unsupported and loads nothing. Fully leaving the bridge out of the iOS build (flavours or a compile-time define) is for plan phase D, with the iOS build itself. | A conditional import can only tell platforms apart by library, and `dart:ffi` exists on iOS. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC8 | The Android project. | Written by hand from Flutter 3.44.0's template: `org.atalaia.app` (the plan's Kotlin plugins live in `org/atalaia/app/`), min SDK 26, compile and target SDK 36, ABIs arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a and x86_64, `externalNativeBuild` on `core/CMakeLists.txt` with `-DATALAIA_SHARED=ON -DATALAIA_MBEDTLS=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=OFF` and the target `atalaia_core_shared`. Versions written out instead of `flutter.*` defaults: Gradle 9.1.0 (bin, with `distributionSha256Sum`), AGP 9.0.1, Kotlin 2.3.20, NDK 28.2.13676358, CMake 3.22.1. No template PNG icons: a placeholder adaptive icon drawn in XML. Release builds are still signed with the debug key (template default; signing is plan phase G). `lib/main.dart` is a placeholder screen with the core's version, so `flutter build apk` has an entry point. The template's two Gradle flags (`android.newDsl=false`, `android.builtInKotlin=false`) are kept: AGP 9 warns they are deprecated, and Flutter 3.44's plugin still needs them. Gradle's other warnings come from Flutter's own Gradle plugin. | Pinned tools that match the Flutter version in use; the files stay small and owned by the project. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC9 | "Commit lockfiles": is the Gradle dependency graph locked? | No Gradle lockfile. `pubspec.lock` is updated (`ffi` 2.2.0 became a direct dependency; it was already locked as a transitive one). Gradle, its plugins, the NDK and CMake are pinned by exact version, and the Gradle download by checksum. | Gradle's dependency locking fails a build that resolves fewer modules than the lock lists, and Flutter's plugin adds one engine artifact per target ABI: `flutter run` on one phone (one ABI) would fail against a lock made for three. Gradle dependency verification (checksums) could be added later without that problem. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC10 | Which ABIs and what is checked on the Android builds? | arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a and x86_64 (the plan's list), 64-bit Lua numbers on all three (no `LUA_32BITS`, as on the ESP32 since phase 1), no warnings allowed (`-Werror` on the core). `app/tool/check.sh ndk` (CI `core-android-ndk`, from `core/CMakePresets.json`) and `apk` (CI `flutter-build-android`) check each library: right machine, the `node.h` entry points exported, nothing needed beyond libc, libm and libdl, and 16 KB-aligned segments on the 64-bit ABIs (Google Play requires it for Android 15+ devices). | The plan's ABIs; checks that would otherwise only fail on a device. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PC11 | The plan puts the `flutter-build-android` CI job in plan phase D. | Added now, with `core-android-ndk` and `flutter-test` (which replaces the "Player app logic (Dart)" job). The emulator job (`flutter-integration`) stays in plan phase D. | Plan phase C writes `build.gradle.kts`; ADR constraint 3 wants CI to build it with `flutter build apk --debug`. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

Found and fixed along the way: the core's `field.common_owner()` kept the string from one `owner()` call and compared it after the next call, so it read memory the host may already have freed (both the Python and the Dart bindings keep only the latest answer). The Dart mode tests showed it as Hold-All results that changed from run to run. The core now copies the first answer onto the Lua stack, `field_ops.h` says how long an answer must live, and a core test with a host that rewrites one buffer catches it (it fails on the old code). Also: `tools/docker_app.py` crashed on Windows when its output was redirected and Flutter printed a character the code page lacks.

## Phase 3, plan phase D (Flutter UI, Android prop shell, Kotlin plugins, emulator job)

The "PD" prefix keeps these apart from phase 2 plan phase D's rows D1-D9.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PD1 | What does plan phase D include, given what exists? The plan lists all four roles, five Kotlin plugins, positions-ready plugins (`geolocator`) and an iOS build. | Built: the role selector (prop on Android only, player, marshal, GM), the Android prop shell, the screen-template renderer, join by QR code, NFC card or text (P3-10), the player's view and the GM console, the marshal's chrono check-in, replica registry and tag writer, European Portuguese and English, and the Kotlin plugins: device (vibration, tones, screen, battery, permissions), kiosk, foreground service, NFC, QR scanner, USB pod, BLE pod. Left for plan phase E: the controller role (it needs the embedded MQTT broker and the controller's API), player tokens with roles and the role-filtered feed, positions (no location permission or plugin yet), the prop's link to the controller. iOS is not built (PD10). | The controller role and positions are plan phase E's files and depend on API extensions that do not exist yet; everything else the plan lists for D can be built and tested now. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD2 | ADR-P3-4 says the Flutter renderer parses the templates with the `yaml` package. | **Changed from the ADR's choice.** `app/lib/ui/template/` ports the core's YAML-subset parser, its checks and its expression evaluator to Dart, line for line, with the same error messages. Tests compare the two through `dart:ffi`: every template the modes ship, 2,500 mutated templates (accepted or refused alike, same messages), 4,000 generated expressions, 4,000 texts with filters, 3,000 `visible:` and progress values, the colour names. The renderer lays the widgets out on the Field Box's 320x240 canvas with its LVGL renderer's rules (centred column 4 px apart, rows 6 px, `fill`, defaults 20 pt, 200x12 bars, 120 px QR codes, `team`, `contrast`) and scales it to the phone. Two limits differ only where C would cut a UTF-8 character in two (the 256-byte scratch, the 159-byte text): Dart drops the whole character. | The templates are a YAML subset that the core defines (A10); a full YAML parser accepts templates the Field Box refuses and reads plain values differently (`1e3`, `0x10`, `~`, `null`), so a template could show differently on a box and a phone. A port plus differential tests keeps them identical. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD3 | The differential tests found that `{x\|clock}` on a value beyond a 64-bit integer gave different texts on x86 and ARM. | Fixed in the core: `core/src/screen_template.c` clamps the seconds at 1e15 before converting (`(long long)` of a larger double is undefined behaviour in C); the Dart port does the same; a core test covers it. Core tests under ASan and UBSan, the firmware host tests and both ESP-IDF builds (node, modem) were rerun: all pass, no warnings. | Undefined behaviour in shipped C code, reachable from any mode's data. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD4 | The modes' screens are in English; the app is Portuguese by default. | The mode screens stay as the modes write them (English, as on the Field Box: K12, modes/README.md "Screens"); the app's own screens are translated. The Field Box's C-only screens that modes call (`code_entry` for Codes and Bomb's team codes, the older `final_score` of Hold-All and Last Stand) are written as YAML templates for the Flutter renderer (`app/lib/prop/shell_screens.dart`), with the same data and text, checked by the core's parser. | Translating mode screens needs a template i18n mechanism the format does not have yet; one look on box and phone matters more for now. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD5 | QR scanning: `mobile_scanner` (the plan's choice) uses Google's ML Kit on Android, a proprietary SDK. | **Changed from the plan's choice.** The app's own scanner (`QrScanActivity.kt`): CameraX 1.6.2 (AndroidX, Apache-2.0) for the picture and ZXing `core` 3.5.4 (Apache-2.0, pure Java) for the decoding, started from Dart over a method channel. `python tools/docker_app.py apk` (CI `flutter-build-android`) fails if the APK holds any Google Play services or ML Kit class. iOS will need its own (AVFoundation) when it is built. | No proprietary SDKs; phones without Google Play services (de-Googled, some Chinese markets) must scan join codes. ML Kit decodes better in poor light; ZXing with `TRY_HARDER` is enough for the large codes the GM console shows. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD6 | Where the settings and secrets live. | JSON files in the app's private folder (Android's `filesDir`, through the device plugin), written to a temporary file and renamed; `android:allowBackup="false"`. The field join key, the marshal's Ed25519 seed and the player's read code are protected by Android's app sandbox, not a hardware-backed keystore. No `shared_preferences` or `path_provider` (the latter's Android side now builds native code through `jni`). | Few settings; no extra native code. Wrapping the secrets with the Android Keystore is a follow-up before a public release. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD7 | ADR-P3-5's foreground service "holds the Dart game loop isolate (via FlutterEngine)", and ADR-P3-9's tamper check runs in it. | The service keeps the process in the foreground (notification), the CPU awake (partial wake lock `atalaia:game_loop`, at most 24 h) and Wi-Fi up (`WIFI_MODE_FULL_LOW_LATENCY`); the game loop stays in the activity's Flutter engine, in the same process. The tamper check is the prop session polling lock-task mode every second while a game runs; a loss is shown, logged as `node.tamper kiosk_broken` and vibrated, once. Sending it to the controller comes with the controller link (plan phase E). Service type `connectedDevice` only; `location` comes with positions. | A second engine in the service would duplicate the game state; one process with a foreground service is what keeps Android from killing it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD8 | ADR-P3-5's screen states. | Active: keep-screen-on, full brightness. Dimmed (30 s idle): 10% brightness, keep-screen-on still set, so Android's own timeout cannot turn the screen off before 5 minutes. Off (5 min idle): outside kiosk mode the flag is cleared and Android turns the screen off by its timeout (`goToSleep` needs a system permission); in kiosk mode it stays on at 1% brightness.
