Any touch, team button, tag or pod event makes it active. | Matches the ADR's intent with what an app may do. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD9 | Theme. | The prop's game screen is always dark (the template palette); the rest follows the system, with a light "sun" theme (darker amber on white for readability in sunlight). Fonts: the system's (Roboto). Team colours: the template palette. | Props at night and in bunkers, players in the sun. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD10 | The plan asks for an iOS build. | **Not built.** Flutter builds for iOS only on macOS; this project has Docker on Windows and Ubuntu CI runners. There is no `ios/` folder: a generated one could not be checked. The Dart code stays iOS-tolerant (PC7): the role selector leaves out the prop on iOS (a widget test checks it), the node runner reports no core, every channel answers "not available" without its plugin. `app/README.md` lists what a Mac build needs (`flutter create --platforms=ios`, `Info.plist` camera and NFC keys, the NFC capability, Swift sides of the files, QR and NFC channels). A macOS CI job would cost a paid or limited runner. | Nothing unverifiable is committed. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): an iOS build is needed (the maintainer has no Android phone): an iOS build in CI on GitHub's macOS runners (free for a public repository); TestFlight needs an Apple developer account (maintainer's call). The GM console already works in Safari |
| PD11 | The field controller serves plain HTTP on the field's network. | `android:usesCleartextTraffic="true"` for now (the player's feed, the GM console). The controller's own CA, its fingerprint in the join payload and HTTPS are plan phase E (ADR-P3-9, P3-8). | Without it Android 9+ refuses the controller. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): prefer HTTPS: drop the app's cleartext permission wherever the controller's HTTPS (field CA, since plan phase E) covers it |
| PD12 | What a prop does after joining with a join payload. | It checks the payload with the field join key (entered once in the settings), takes the game's id and key (player tags are then checked), its node id, mode (with modifiers), role, teams, parameters and seed, and prepares the game; the game's length is set on the phone (the payload has none); the admin starts it on the prop. Being started and followed by the controller needs the prop's link to it, plan phase E (Wi-Fi) or F (a pod's radio). | The link does not exist yet; the rest of the join can be used and tested now. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD13 | How a player connects before player tokens exist (plan phase E). | With the controller's address and its read code, typed or from the scoreboard's address (`http://<controller>:8080/scoreboard#token=...`, as a QR code the GM makes); the app follows `WS /api/v1/stream` as the scoreboard page does and shows the time, points, objectives, lives, eliminations, the GM's messages and the events in words. A "share with players" QR code in the GM console is a follow-up. | Uses only what the controller already serves; roles and filtering need plan phase E. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD14 | Who writes players' tags in the app. | The marshal role, for a game picked with its join card or code (protocol/nfc-tag.md: "the field controller, or the GM's phone"); the game key comes from the payload, so writing tags needs the field join key too. Each tag is checked by reading back what was written before it goes to the tag. | The GM console cannot write tags yet; the marshal is already at the safe zone with the players. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD15 | Who may start, pause, end or leave a prop's game. | Whoever knows the admin PIN (4-8 digits, stored as a salted SHA-256); with no PIN set, anyone. The back button asks for it too. | Like the Field Box's admin PIN. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD16 | The I/O pod before its firmware exists. | The Dart codec passes `capability_proxy.yaml`; the pod client (IDENT on connect, ACK, dedupe of the last 16 seqs, 500 ms retries x3, then the link is broken) is tested against a simulated pod; the USB (CDC ACM, 921600 8N1, DTR) and BLE (service UUID, MTU 247, Pod TX notifications, Pod RX writes cut to the MTU) Kotlin transports compile into the APK but have met no pod. A script's commands for pod capabilities become CMD args `{"team"}` (fill, show), `{"freq", "ms"}` (tone) or `{"args": [...]}`: a proposal for plan phase F to confirm. | Plan phase F builds the pod; the phone's side is ready to meet it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD17 | The emulator job. | `flutter-integration`: Ubuntu 24.04 runner with KVM, `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` v2.38.0 and `subosito/flutter-action` v2.23.0 (Flutter 3.44.0), both pinned by commit, Android 11 (API 30) x86_64 with the **AOSP** image (the ADR said `google_apis`), `flutter test integration_test`: the roles and languages, a Bomb prop on the APK's core armed by holding a button and exploding, a joined game, the player's view over a WebSocket served on the phone. Flutter runs on the runner, not in the pinned image, because the emulator and the Flutter tool must share a machine. **Not run locally**: Docker on Windows has no KVM. | The AOSP image proves the app needs no Google Play services. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD18 | Golden images. | Drawn and compared in the pinned Flutter image (`python tools/docker_app.py goldens`, then `check` and CI compare), with Roboto and the Material icons loaded from the Flutter SDK. The mode-template images use the Field Box's reference data and match its images in layout and colour, not pixel for pixel (Roboto vs Montserrat, Material icons vs LVGL symbols, a real QR code vs the box's text). Two of the screen images are in `app/README.md`, three in `README.md` and the project page (each language's on its page). | Real fonts make the images reviewable; the same image everywhere keeps them stable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD19 | Packages. | `qr` 4.0.0 (kevmoo.com, maintained) with the app's own painter instead of `qr_flutter` (last release 2023); `webview_flutter` 4.14.1 (flutter.dev) for the GM console; `flutter_localizations` and `integration_test` from the SDK. Not added: `geolocator`, `flutter_blue_plus`, `usb_serial`, `nfc_manager`, `mobile_scanner`, `path_provider`, `shared_preferences`: the app's Kotlin plugins do what is needed (PD5, PD6). Kotlin: CameraX 1.6.2, `androidx.activity` 1.13.0, ZXing `core` 3.5.4, pinned in `build.gradle.kts`. | Few, well-maintained, open dependencies; every version exact and locked. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD20 | How the app is translated. | String tables in Dart (`app/lib/l10n/strings_pt.dart`, `strings_en.dart`): flat keys and `{name}` placeholders like the GM console's, no code generation. Portuguese as players say it, "tu", inclusive ("quem joga", "participante"), the site's terms (marshal, réplica, cronografagem, organização). Tests: both languages have the same keys and placeholders, every key the code names exists, the run-time key families are complete. Decimal comma in Portuguese; team colours named in the language in the app's own screens. | Matches the console and the site; nothing generated to keep in step. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD21 | How the app gets the modes. | `tool/gen_modes.dart` writes `lib/modes/builtin_modes.g.dart` with every file of `modes/` (manifests, scripts, screens) as Dart strings (`python tools/docker_app.py gen`); a test fails when it is out of date. The embedded SPDX headers sit between `REUSE-IgnoreStart` and `REUSE-IgnoreEnd`. | Flutter assets cannot come from outside `app/`; the Field Box compiles the modes in the same way (A11). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD22 | Kiosk mode without prompts. | A device-admin receiver with no policies (`AtalaiaAdminReceiver`), so a prop phone can make the app its device owner (`adb shell dpm set-device-owner org.atalaia.app/.AtalaiaAdminReceiver`); then lock task needs no confirmation. Otherwise Android asks to confirm screen pinning. The provisioning guide is plan phase G's. | The standard way for a dedicated device. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD23 | The battery-optimisation exemption. | Asked once, the first time the prop role opens (`REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS`); refused, a warning and the guide by maker (Samsung, Xiaomi, Huawei, OnePlus, others) stay, and it is never asked again. Google Play restricts that permission to some app kinds: to review if the app goes to Google Play. | ADR-P3-5. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PD24 | Changes to plan phase C's code. | `RunningNode` gained `startupOutputs` (what a script's top level emitted before anyone listened); the bindings gained `atalaia_tpl_truthy`, `_fraction`, `_color` and `_expr_ok` (for the differential tests); `checkScreen` decodes a cut error message safely. | Needed by the prop session and the tests. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

Found and fixed along the way: the core's `{x|clock}` conversion (PD3).

## Phase 3, plan phase E (field check-in, player tokens, chrono readings, Wi-Fi props, positions)

The "PE" prefix keeps these apart from phase 2 plan phase E's rows E1-E16.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PE1 | What plan phase E delivers in this round. | Built: field check-in (poster, identities, tokens, declarations, the replicas-to-chrono list, retention), player tokens with the role re-check and the role-filtered stream, signed chrono readings to the controller, the stream's history for players, Android props on Wi-Fi, HTTPS with the field CA, player positions with server-side visibility, the GM console's Check-in tab and poster, the app's screens for all of it, and for the Android controller role its MQTT broker and visibility engine. **Not built:** the Android controller role itself (`controller_shell.dart`: `field.lua` on the phone and the controller's API served by the app; PE24), BLE beacon positions indoors, a map of positions in the app. | Items 1-5 of the brief first, then positions and the controller role's parts that can be tested on their own. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE2 | ADR-P3-9 sketches an Ed25519 field CA. | **Changed from the ADR's choice.** ECDSA P-256 for the CA and the server certificate (`controller/atalaia_controller/tls.py`): CA valid 10 years, server certificate 1 year, made again under 30 days left or when its names change; the CA is never replaced on its own. | Browsers (the GM console runs in one) do not accept Ed25519 certificates in TLS; P-256 works in every browser, Android and Python. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE3 | HTTP or HTTPS for the field API? | Both: the same app on `[api] port` (8080, HTTP) and `[api] tls_port` (8443, HTTPS). Posters and the join payload's addresses are `https://` only; the app's check-in, player token and prop link use HTTPS trusting only the pinned CA. The GM console, the scoreboard and the read-only player view still work on HTTP, so the app keeps `usesCleartextTraffic` for them (PD11). | Existing consoles and scoreboards keep working; everything that carries a player secret or token goes over TLS. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE4 | Where does an Android prop learn the controller's address and CA? | Optional join keys 13 `ca_sha256` (P3-8) and 14 `urls` (1-3 https addresses), RFC 0001 Amendment 4 (proposed); boxes skip them (keys 13-31 were reserved). The old `optional_key_skipped` vector used key 13 for a text and now uses key 20 (the C test header regenerated, core tests rerun); a new `controller_link` vector section is checked by Python and Dart. A payload too long with them is made without them. | P3-9 left the address to mDNS or the Wi-Fi; a payload the GM shows anyway is the simplest carrier. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE5 | Where is the per-game token key kept (B8, F5)? | In the controller's SQLite file (table `token_keys`), made at the first token of a game. The file is now created 0600 before SQLite opens it, so its WAL and shared-memory files are 0600 too (they were created with the default mode before). | The store already holds the GM and read tokens; one protected file is simpler than a second one. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE6 | Who gets which stream message? | Routing marks on the engine's messages (`_staff`, `_player`, `_position`), applied per connection and stripped before sending: check-in and chrono-list messages to the GM token and the game's marshal and gm player tokens, and to the player whose row it is; positions by the visibility table; a player token's connection gets only `hello`, `game`, `score`, `node`, `event`, `field_state`, positions and its own rows (no radio, scan, join or controller internals); its `hello` has a trimmed `status`. A GM change to a player's team, squad or role closes that player's streams with 4409 after telling them. | ADR-P3-13's rules, with the read token unchanged. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE7 | What may an older token still do after the row changed? | Its own role if the row still says it, else the lesser of the two (spectator < player < squad leader = VIP < marshal < GM; squad leader against VIP gives player); team and squad always the row's; `stale` reported, and the app refreshes. | ADR-P3-13: "the old token keeps only what the row still allows". | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE8 | Token expiry at the end of the field's day. | As F5, but never less than 60 seconds from issue; the field's day is the controller's local time zone. | A check-in a minute before midnight should not get a token that is already dead. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE9 | What is a chrono series, and who judges it? | One `POST /api/v1/chrono/readings` request: the same replica, player and type. The controller judges it with the field's limit template (`[checkin] limit_template`, read from `app/lib/chrono/limit_templates/`) and the class of the matching declaration (else the field registry's replica): check-in and retest pass at or below the limit; spot checks fail above it and warn above the last passing check-in plus 5% (the tolerance is not configurable yet). Without a class: stored, `unjudged`. Refusal reasons add `marshal` (no key registered) and `field` (another field's record) to chrono-record.md's; a request that mixes stored and new readings is refused (409); one fully stored is a duplicate (no change). | The marshal's own judgement is not trusted: the list changes only on what the controller verifies and computes (constraint 27). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE10 | ADR-P3-12's embedded broker: topics and retained messages. | **Changed from the ADR's choice.** The Pi's topics (`field/v1/{game}/...`) instead of `atalaia/game/...`, and retained messages are supported (the Pi bridge publishes the score and node topics retained). Clients may publish only where the controller allows (default nowhere, like the read-only Mosquitto user). QoS 2, wills, sessions, auth and TLS are not supported, as the ADR says. | The ADR's own aim is the same topics on both controllers; scoreboards that connect late need the retained score. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE11 | The prop's Wi-Fi link format. | WebSocket with JSON messages (HELLO, STATE and EVENT fields), not the radio's AES-CCM packets: a challenge answered with HMAC-SHA256 over the game key proves the prop holds it; the controller encodes the messages with the radio's codecs and feeds the same FieldController (dedupe, scores, log); privacy comes from TLS with the pinned CA. The node id must be assigned in the game (the join payload does that). The prop follows the controller's start, pause, resume and end, and TIME's state when a command was missed; it does not slew its clock to the controller's game time yet. | Reuses the radio's logic without a Dart AES-CCM; WebSocket is what the stream already uses (protocol/prop-link.md). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE12 | `node.tamper` from a prop's shell. | Sent on the link as an event with the shell's own seq counter (the type keeps it apart from the mode's events in the log's duplicate key), priority critical, `reason: kiosk_broken`. | ADR-P3-9; the console already alerts on critical events. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE13 | The player view showed no events from before it connected. | The stream's `hello` now carries the live game's latest 100 events (oldest first); the app rebuilds its list from it on every (re)connect. | One message, no extra request; the console still loads the full log. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE14 | Positions: source, permission, endpoints. | Android's own LocationManager (the fused provider where there is one, else GPS, else network), no Google Play services; the location permission asked when the player turns sharing on; the foreground service gets the location type only then (`FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION`); reports accepted only while the game runs or is paused, the latest per player kept and deleted at the game's end; `GET /api/v1/positions` (not in the ADR) returns what the caller may see. BLE beacons (indoors) not built. | ADR-P3-6 and PD5; GPS first, as most fields are outdoors. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE15 | The marshal app's hand check-in. | Name only from the app (replicas are declared by the console's form, or chronographed from the list later); the console's form takes the federation number, team preference and replicas. | Keeps the phone flow short at the desk. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE16 | A declared replica the field has not registered. | The marshal taps it in the list; the app registers it on the phone with a QR label text `ATL-xxxxxxxx` and the declaration id is kept until the readings that link it are sent (`declaration_id` and `replica` in the upload). Binding an NFC sticker at that moment is a follow-up. | ADR-P3-13's "the marshal picks the player's pending declaration and registers the replica". | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE17 | Rate limits on the open endpoints. | `POST /api/v1/checkin`: `[checkin] rate_per_minute` (10) per client address; `GET /api/v1/checkin`: six times that (at least 60). | ADR-P3-13 limits the check-in; the info endpoint is cheap but open too. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE18 | Personal data on leaving or removal. | Leaving (`DELETE /api/v1/checkin/me`) or a GM removal erases the name, federation number and team preference at once and withdraws pending declarations; chronographed declarations stay for the list's history until the retention purge. The CSV export protects cells from spreadsheet formulas. | Minimal data (F11) and the player's right to leave. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE19 | The console's Check-in tab and poster page. | A tab after *Game*; the poster is its own page (`poster.html`, CSP-safe module), QR error correction M, the Wi-Fi password field cleared once drawn and never sent; starting a game with pending declarations asks with the count (the API also answers `pending_declarations`). | ADR-P3-13. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE20 | The field's day for "valid for the day". | The controller's local time zone; readings' UTC timestamps are converted to it. | A field is in one place; no time-zone setting to get wrong. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE21 | How the app's check-in is tested without a controller. | A fake controller in Dart (`app/test/support/fake_field.dart`) with the same paths, bodies and codes; on the device, the same fake behind a real HTTPS server on 127.0.0.1 with a test-only CA committed in `app/integration_test/test_certs.dart` (its private key public on purpose, protecting nothing). The real controller's rules are tested in Python. | Running the Python controller in the emulator job would need a second toolchain there; the Python tests already cover its side. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE22 | A series the controller refuses for good during "send pending". | Marked done on the phone (kept there, not resent), counted in the message; network failures keep it queued. | A 422 will never succeed; retrying it forever would hide new series. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE23 | The coordinator's fix for notifications after dispose. | Kept (`PropSession.notifyListeners` does nothing once disposed). Its test, as written, still failed here with `GameLoopException: closed` from `advance()`'s own call to the core; `PropSession.advance` now returns quietly when the session closed meanwhile. A new test (`app/test/prop/prop_link_session_test.dart`) holds a refresh's snapshot until after dispose: it fails without the notify fix ("used after being disposed") and passes with it. | Both paths (the tick's refresh and a manual advance) are harmless now. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE24 | The Android controller role (ADR-P3-12's controller shell). | Not built in this round: it means `field.lua` with `CoreNode`'s field operations, the controller's REST/WebSocket API, check-in and token issuing served by the app, and the same contract tests. Its parts are: the MQTT broker (`app/lib/controller/mqtt_broker.dart`, tested over real sockets) and the visibility engine (`visibility_engine.dart`). | Plan phase E lists it; it is a controller port of its own and comes after the Pi controller's API settles. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PE25 | Teams and squads (maintainer, 2026-10-19). | A team ("equipa") always exists; a squad is a sub-group inside a team that exists only when the game defines it: `POST /api/v1/games` takes `squads` (0, the default, to 16 per team; a new games column). The console's game form asks how many; the Check-in tab and the app show squad pickers, squad columns and the squad-leader role only in a game with squads, as team › squad; the controller refuses a squad or a squad leader in a game without squads, and a squad without a team. "Squad" stays "squad" in Portuguese ("esquadra" is a police station). | Small games have teams only; milsims and big games split teams into squads. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 3, plan phase F (I/O pod firmware)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PF1 | What plan phase F delivers, and how its files are laid out. | The pod variant (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_MODE`), the proxy, the USB and Bluetooth links, the capabilities on the Field Box's HAL, the radio passthrough, `node.tamper` from an enclosure switch, the host pod for tests, and on the phone the radio link through the pod and the choice between it and Wi-Fi. **Changed from the plan's file list:** the logic is plain C apart from ESP-IDF, as for the modem (`main/pod/pod.c` the proxy, `pod_caps.c` the capabilities, `pod_ble.c` the GATT logic, `pod_json.c`), with thin ESP-IDF files (`pod_task.c` for the task, USB, audio and switch; `pod_ble_nimble.c`; `pod_main.c` with its own `app_main`, as `modem_main.c`, instead of a dispatch in `shell.c`). The device-mode Kconfig bool became the three-way choice ADR-P3-3 shows (node, modem, pod); `sdkconfig.modem` still selects the modem. | Host-testable code, the modem's pattern; one task owns the proxy. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF2 | MEASURE through the pod. | The modem's MEASURE (RADIO_CMD opcode 8), its NOISE answer as RADIO_NOISE `0x0B` (NOISE + 3) and its error codes 7 (busy) and 8 (no_radio) reach the phone as the other radio frames do; added to `protocol/capability-proxy.md` (dated refinement), the C, Python and Dart codecs and two shared vectors (`cmd_measure`, `noise`). No protocol version change: a phone that does not know `0x0B` ignores it, as the spec says of unknown types. | The pod runs the modem's code, which gained MEASURE after the proxy spec was written (CH10); a future Android controller can scan channels with a pod. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF3 | USB: USB-Serial-JTAG or TinyUSB CDC? | USB-Serial-JTAG, as the USB modem: the Heltec V4's only USB port, a CDC ACM device to Android, no extra component, flashing over the same port keeps working; the baud rate is ignored; the log console stays on UART0 only. The phone's plugin sets DTR and RTS together, which the USB-Serial-JTAG does not treat as a reset (RTS with DTR clear is): to check on the board (BRING_UP, the pod's step 2). | One USB path in the firmware, already used; TinyUSB would take the port from the ROM's download mode. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF4 | Bluetooth LE security. | LE Secure Connections, Just Works (the pod has no display or keys), bonded (NVS, up to 4 bonds): the pod asks to pair on every connection; Pod RX takes only encrypted writes and frames go out only on an encrypted link with Pod TX notifications on (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_BLE_ENCRYPTED`, on by default); then the pod sends IDENT unasked. Pod Status is readable without pairing. The app waits 30 s for IDENT over Bluetooth (Android's pairing question) instead of 3 s. | RADIO_CMD CONFIGURE carries the game key: without encryption anyone listening at the field would have it. Just Works stops listening, not someone who pairs first (an OLED passkey would, PF11). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF5 | Links, ACKs and the game key on the pod. | A link is active once the phone has sent a valid frame on it (Bluetooth: once ready); EVTs go to every active link, an ACK on either clears them, replies go back where the request came from. CMD seqs are remembered per link (16) and forgotten on a new connection and on every RADIO_CMD IDENT (a new client numbers from 0). An EVT sent 4 times without an ACK makes the links inactive and drops what waits. The game key is forgotten (CLEAR) when the link that configured the radio goes or breaks; RADIO_RX, RADIO_TX_ACK and RADIO_NOISE go to that link. | The protocol's own rules, made precise for two links; a key that outlives its phone would let the next one transmit as that node. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF6 | What the pod lists in IDENT. | Only what is fitted (buttons with a pin; the keypad and the NFC reader if they answered; the ring and the amplifier if their pins are set; the enclosure switch if its pin is set), as `btn.1`-`btn.4` (button), `keypad`, `ring` (led, count), `audio`, `nfc`, `case` (tamper). Button N stands for the game's N-th team, as on a Field Box; a colour set in Kconfig (`ATALAIA_POD_BTNn_TEAM`, empty by default) goes into the descriptor and the phone uses it when the game has that team. Everything fitted fits in 256 bytes with a 23-byte `fw_version`; when colours would not fit they are left out. No battery events: the level is in IDENT and Pod Status. `fw_version` is the image's version cut to 23 bytes. | IDENT's 256-byte limit (a first layout with colours did not fit); positional teams are what a box does. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF7 | PD16's command shapes. | Confirmed: `fill {"team"}` (also `{"args": [team]}` or `{"r","g","b"}`), `tone {"freq","ms"}`, other commands `{"args": [...]}`. The phone now also sends a script's semantic targets to the pod by type: `team_display:show` to the pod's `led` as `fill`, `audio.*` to its `audio`. The pod plays the mode's clips (explosion, armed, defused, captured, alarm, tick; others a beep) as tone patterns: it has no sound files. A CMD for a part or method the pod lacks is acknowledged and counted. Not supported yet: `nfc` `write`, button lights, `ring` `pattern`. | The app's shapes work for every built-in mode; no app change needed beyond the semantic targets. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF8 | The enclosure switch (`node.tamper`). | A switch from `ATALAIA_POD_TAMPER_PIN` (GPIO 47 by default: the Field Box's backlight, free on a pod) to GND, closed while the lid is shut, pull-up on, 50 ms debounce; armed only once it has read closed, so a pod without one never alarms. Events `opened` and `closed` of a new capability type `tamper` (draft RFC 0000, design specification section 7); `opened` is reported again to a phone that links later. The phone sends `node.tamper` (critical, `reason: pod_opened`) once while its game runs and shows a warning. No IMU: the HAL has none. | The brief's "enclosure switch or IMU"; the switch needs one GPIO. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF9 | How a phone with a pod picks between Wi-Fi and LoRa. | `DualLink` (`app/lib/controller/dual_link.dart`): while the Wi-Fi link is up everything goes on it and the radio stays quiet; while it is down, events and STATE go on the radio too, and TIME and commands come from the radio; every event still goes to the Wi-Fi link, which resends it after a reconnect (the controller logs it once: node, seq, game time, type). HELLO's `transports` bit 0 is set when a pod with a radio is linked (3 with Wi-Fi, 1 without). The radio link (`app/lib/radio/pod_radio_link.dart`, a box's field link in Dart over the pod's RADIO_* frames): CONFIGURE from the join payload with a new epoch stored first (per game and node, in the app's store), HELLO until acknowledged, events with ACKs resent every 3 s (normal 10 times, critical until acknowledged), STATE at most every 15 s, the controller with the highest term followed, no relaying; plain-text codecs for the header, HELLO, STATE, ACK, CMD and TIME in Dart against the shared vectors, the EVENT payload by the core through `dart:ffi`. A joined game without the controller's addresses uses the radio alone. | The field's airtime is shared and duty-cycled; Wi-Fi is cheaper when it is there. Plan phase E's handoff named the dedupe and the HELLO bit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF10 | How the phone's side is tested against the real pod. | `firmware/esp32/test/pod_host`: the pod's own C code with mbedtls AES-CCM on Linux; USB as a TCP socket of raw bytes, Bluetooth as a TCP socket of length-prefixed notifications and writes (cut to the MTU), the radio as UDP, the hardware as stdin lines, its outputs as stdout lines. mbedtls is linked statically (also into `modem_host` now), so both run in the Flutter image. Dart tests: the pod client over both links (`app/test/pod/pod_host_test.dart`), the radio link and `DualLink` with the host modem as the controller's radio (`app/test/radio/pod_radio_link_test.dart`), a prop session on the pod's radio (`app/test/prop/prop_pod_session_test.dart`); the controller with its modem and the pod (`controller/tests/test_pod_e2e.py`). | The brief asked for the Dart client against the real C pod; the controller test checks the radio passthrough against the real controller. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF11 | What the pod does not use. | No Wi-Fi, no screens: the Heltec's 0.96" OLED stays off (a pairing passkey on it would make Bluetooth pairing MITM-proof: a follow-up). GPS not supported (E21). The NFC poll (every 300 ms) can hold the pod's loop for the PN532's timeout, as on the box. | Scope; the passkey needs an OLED driver and an app flow. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF12 | Which packet an ACK names (found by the tests). | The phone's radio link counts an ACK for any of the last 16 packets that carried an event or HELLO (a box's field link keeps only the last one); before this, a HELLO sent again before the ACK of its first copy arrived waited for another ACK. | The controller may answer an earlier copy. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF13 | A pod client closed from inside one of its events (found by the tests). | `PodClient.close()` now yields once before it reports the link closed: its events are delivered synchronously, and a listener that closed it hit "Cannot fire new event". | The prop session closes the client from its listeners' futures. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF14 | The `bt` component in the build. | Listed in `main`'s REQUIRES for every variant: ESP-IDF expands requirements before it reads the sdkconfig, so a conditional one did not work. Without `CONFIG_BT_ENABLED` it builds nothing: the Field Box image is the same size as before (0x16db40 bytes); the modem's grew by 80 bytes, from the modem's new `frame_out` hook. NimBLE is ESP-IDF's own (v5.3.2): no managed component, `dependencies.lock` unchanged. | The documented way; a variant-only REQUIRES failed to compile the pod. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| PF15 | Not built in this round. | The pod's configuration over CMD (`config`: ADR-P3-3's admin PIN, LED count; Kconfig instead), NFC writing, button lights, the app's automatic reconnect to a pod (it reports the lost link; linking again is by hand), the OLED passkey (PF11), Wokwi (no pod in the emulator; Wokwi's CI quota is used up, R7). | Each needs a design or hardware first. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Field Box build manual (hardware/field-box/BUILD.md)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | Licence of the wiring diagrams? | CERN-OHL-S-2.0 for the YAML sources and the rendered images (the `hardware/` default, via `.license` sidecars); CC-BY-SA-4.0 for BUILD.md. | The diagram is the hardware design itself. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M2 | Battery? | 1S: two protected 18650 cells in parallel, with an inline fuse, a Schottky crowbar diode, a main switch and a 5 V boost converter for the LEDs, buttons and amplifier. The kit's 3000 mAh LiPo is fine for a first prototype. | The board's charger and the MAX17048 gauge are single-cell parts; the old BoM's 2S pack would have destroyed the board. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M3 | Peripheral power: Vext or 3V3? | The board's 3V3 rail for the I2C modules and the display. The firmware now switches Vext on at boot (E22), but the standard build does not depend on it. | Fewer moving parts; Vext's current limit is not documented. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4 | LED ring data line? | A 74AHCT125 level shifter (3.3 V to 5 V) as standard, on a small interconnect board. | WS2812 data at 3.3 V from a 5 V supply is unreliable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5 | One diagram or two? | Two: signals and power. | A single diagram rendered 18,000 px wide and was unreadable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6 | Where do the diagram's GPIO numbers come from? | From Kconfig, filled in at render time; `tools/check_wiring.py` (in check.sh and CI) fails if the diagrams, the README pin table and Kconfig disagree, or if a board-internal, FEM or strapping pin is wired. | Pins changed twice in one day; a hand-drawn diagram would drift. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7 | Should pins on the GNSS connector fail the check? | No, a warning: the Field Box uses GPIO 34 and 38-42 with the GNSS module unplugged (E21). | It is a documented trade-off, revisited in phase 4. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8 | How does a Windows user flash a box? | Build in Docker, flash with esptool 4.8.1 in a Python venv on the host (Docker Desktop cannot reach USB); a browser flasher as an alternative. | The maintainer works from Windows cmd. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9 | Display backlight? | The builder measures its current; above 10 mA, wire it to 3V3 and set `ATALAIA_DISP_BL_PIN=-1`. | A GPIO can source only a few mA safely. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M10 | When may a box transmit in a game? | Only after BRING_UP's conducted-power check (step 10). | The 14 dB FEM gain behind the legal power cap is assumed, not measured (C2, E20). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Field radio channel (channel choice, MEASURE, limits that follow the channel)

The maintainer approved choosing the field's channel from the GM console (keep 869.525 MHz, scan for a quiet one, random, or by hand), a modem command to measure noise, limits that follow the channel, and the whole-channel edge rule. RFC 0001 Amendment 3 has the regulatory figures and their sources.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CH1 | Which channels can a GM pick? | 14 candidates: 868.1, 868.2, 868.3, 868.4, 868.5 MHz (868.0-868.6, 1%, 25 mW); 868.8, 868.95, 869.1 MHz (868.7-869.2, 0.1%, 25 mW); 869.475, 869.525, 869.575 MHz (869.4-869.65, 10%, 500 mW); 869.775, 869.85, 869.925 MHz (869.7-870.0, 1%, 25 mW). Every one keeps its 125 kHz channel at least 12.5 kHz inside its sub-band. 863-868 MHz is left out. The API also accepts any other legal centre. | A short list keeps a scan short; the margin leaves room for the crystal's frequency error. 863-868 MHz is not in RFC 0001's table, and ERC 70-03 warns that RFID interrogators use parts of 865-868 MHz at higher power. The three 869.4-869.65 MHz channels overlap; 869.475 and 869.575 MHz separate two neighbouring full-power games best. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH2 | What about the 0.1% sub-band (868.7-869.2 MHz)? | Listed, with a warning, but never scanned, drawn at random or suggested. | 0.1% is about 3.6 s of airtime per device per hour: a few dozen packets, too little for a game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH3 | Which channel does a scan suggest? | Ranked: channels where more than 5% of the CAD tries found LoRa preambles last, the rest by 90th-percentile noise, then activity, then mean. Suggested: the best-ranked 869.4-869.65 MHz channel if it is quiet, otherwise the best-ranked of all (marked "every channel is noisy" when even that one is not quiet). The GM accepts or overrides; a scan changes nothing on its own. | The maintainer's rule: the quietest full-power channel when it is quiet enough, otherwise the quietest overall. The 90th percentile catches intermittent traffic that a mean hides. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH4 | What counts as quiet? | 90th-percentile RSSI at or below `[radio] quiet_threshold_dbm` (default -105 dBm) and LoRa activity in at most 5% of the CAD tries (one 2-symbol CAD every 100 ms of dwell). | A guess to be calibrated at bring-up: the SX1262's noise floor at 125 kHz is around -117 dBm, but the V4.3's front-end LNA may shift the readings. Noise at -105 dBm costs roughly 10-15 dB of link budget. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH5 | How is a random channel drawn? | Uniformly from the 11 scan candidates (CH1 without the 0.1% channels), with the system's random generator. Shown as a suggestion with its limits and the odds (3 of 11 give 500 mW); applied only when the GM accepts it. | "Random" must still be legal and usable; the console says plainly that it usually lands on a 1%, 25 mW channel. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH6 | How long does a scan listen? | `[radio] scan_dwell_s`, default 60 s, split equally across the candidates (about 5.5 s each), at most 10 s and at least 10 ms per channel (one MEASURE command each); the API can ask for 1-600 s. | One minute is short enough to wait for at setup and long enough to catch traffic every few seconds; the 10 s cap keeps one command's dwell bounded. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH7 | Where is the choice kept, and what if the config says otherwise? | In the controller's store (`radio.channel_hz`, `radio.channel_source`, `radio.channel_confirmed`), so it survives a restart. `[radio] channel_hz` is the default until the GM first chooses; after that the stored choice wins (the controller logs it at start). Keeping 869.525 MHz counts as a choice. An illegal stored value falls back to the config. The last scan's results are kept too. | The maintainer asked for a field setting chosen in the console; a config edit that silently overrode the GM would be confusing. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH8 | When can the channel change? | Only while no game exists on this controller (ready, running or paused) and no scan runs: a game keeps the channel it was created with, its boxes got it in their join payloads. A scan likewise only runs with no game, and no game can be created during a scan. The console shows a first-setup prompt until the channel has been chosen once. | Changing the channel mid-game would strand boxes on the old one; the modem leaves the game channel while it measures. Moving a running game is left open (RFC 0001 open questions). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH9 | Edge rule details. | Legal when centre - 62.5 kHz and centre + 62.5 kHz both lie in one sub-band, edges included, at the game's fixed 125 kHz bandwidth. One function per language (`atalaia_band_of_channel()` in the core, `eu868.py` in Python), used by the controller config and API, the modem (CONFIGURE, SET_CHANNEL, MEASURE), the join codecs, the radio HAL and the simulator. Two join vectors moved from 869.65 and 870.0 MHz to 869.5875 and 869.9375 MHz; four invalid vectors added. | The maintainer's bug report: 869.40 MHz was accepted although its channel starts at 869.3375 MHz. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH10 | MEASURE command. | CMD opcode 0x08 (channel, dwell 10-10000 ms, CAD flag), answered by a new NOISE frame (0x08: min, mean, median, 90th percentile, max in dBm, samples, CAD tries and detections); ERROR busy (7) while a game is configured or another measurement runs, no_radio (8) without a radio chip; IDENT gains `measure`, STATUS `measuring`. The RSSI is read about once a millisecond in continuous receive, into a 1 dB histogram. | A separate reply frame keeps STATUS within its size; refusing during a game keeps a measurement from taking the radio off the game channel. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH11 | How do the limits follow the channel? | The radio HAL (boxes and modem) takes the duty-cycle budget and TX power from the channel's sub-band through `radio_limits.c`: the core's per-sub-band sliding hour, LoRa time on air for SF7/125 kHz (was a rough `len + 20` ms), and a packet refused when its whole time on air does not fit (was: sent whenever any budget was left). Power is set before the frequency when it drops, after it when it rises. The host modem and the simulator's radio model use the same rules; the simulator now refuses to transmit over budget. | Before this, a field on 868.x MHz would have used g2's 10% budget and 500 mW power: up to ten times the legal airtime and 13 dB too much power. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH12 | The SX1262 sync word. | Changed to the private-network value 0x1424; the firmware wrote 0x3444 (LoRaWAN's public sync word) with a comment saying "private". | Found while checking what shares 869.525 MHz: with the public sync word, boxes and LoRaWAN devices on the same channel and spreading factor would receive each other's packets (the game drops them at authentication, but the radio is busy meanwhile). No boards are deployed, so nothing breaks. To be checked at bring-up with two boards. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| CH13 | The brief calls 869.4-869.65 MHz LoRaWAN's "g2". | Not repeated in the docs: LoRaWAN Regional Parameters do not name EU sub-bands; ERC 70-03 now calls it h1.7, and its older lettering (followed by many LoRa documents) called it g3. The code keeps the project's labels (g, g1, g2, g3, RFC 0001 section 8); user-facing text gives frequencies. | Accuracy; renaming the labels in the code would touch the wire-format RFC for no gain. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Field check-in, registration and scheduling (maintainer request, 2026-10-19)

The maintainer asked for field check-in by a printed QR code, per-game replica declaration so marshals see in advance which replicas still need the chronograph, game scheduling and registration, and publication on the field's page and on shared or third-party platforms such as jogarairsoft.pt. Split agreed with the maintainer, local-first: the game-day part is phase 3 plan phase E (ADR-P3-13 in `docs/phase-3-android-players/ADR.md`), the site-server part is phase 4 (ADR-P4-11 and ADR-P4-12 in `docs/phase-4-site-server/ADR.md`, plan Phase I). Design only; nothing is built.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | Is the check-in poster per field or per game, and is it signed? | Per field, printed once (optionally pinned to one game). Not signed and no MAC. It holds no secret: no join key, game key, token key or Wi-Fi password. The app pins the field's CA certificate per `field_id` on the first scan (fetched over Wi-Fi and checked against the poster's fingerprint, P3-8) and refuses a later poster for the same field with another fingerprint. | A player's app holds no key that could check a signature, and the poster must be safe to photograph. The controller answers only on the field network, so reaching it already means being at the field. A forged poster can only fool a phone that never saw the real one, and a check-in asks for no password or payment. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F2 | The poster's format. | `atalaia:checkin:` + base64url of a version byte (1) and a CBOR map: 1 `field_id`, 2 `field_name`, 3 `urls` (1-3), 4 `ca_sha256`, 5 `wifi_ssid` (optional), 6 `game_id` (optional); at most 192 bytes; keys 7-31 skipped. Specified in `protocol/checkin.md` with shared vectors for Dart and Python. A field whose Wi-Fi has a password can print a standard `WIFI:` QR code beside it; the console draws it and does not store the password. | The same shape as the join text, so the CBOR codecs and the tests are reused. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F3 | Who a player is without an account. | Per field, the app makes a `player_id` (`p-` and 12 random base32 characters) and a 32-byte secret, kept in its private storage; the controller keeps the id and the secret's SHA-256. The same id comes back on every game day at that field; a known id with the wrong secret is refused. A phase 4 account adopts the id when the player links it. | The replica and chrono history continue at one field, two fields cannot link a player's ids, and nobody can take over a known id. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F4 | Which role does a self check-in give? | Only `player`. Marshal, GM and the other roles come from the GM, with a new token. The controller also checks every token's role and team against the player's current check-in, so a demotion, a team change or a removal takes effect at once. | Player tokens cannot be revoked one by one (protocol/player-token.md). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F5 | The player-token key. | The controller's own per-game key (decision B8, now a requirement), kept in the store (mode 0600) so tokens survive a controller restart; `exp` is the game's planned end plus 2 hours or the end of the field's day, whichever is first; the app refreshes. | The game key is printed on every join card, so it must not sign tokens. The token key only opens the field API, never the radio, so keeping it on disk does not weaken ADR-P2-4. Not keeping it would make every player check in again after a restart. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F6 | A new event for field check-in? | No. The controller emits the existing `player.checkin` (15) as its own node with the player id only (and the team, once assigned). Staff see check-ins and the chrono list through the stream messages `checkin` and `chrono_list`, not events. | No protocol change, and names stay off the event path. The other option is a new id from 50 (phase 4 holds 42-49), to keep field check-ins apart from the Player Check-in mode's checkpoint scans in reports. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F7 | Does a check-in need staff approval? | `[checkin] confirm = "auto"` by default; `"staff"` keeps check-ins pending until a marshal or the GM confirms them. | Some fields check payment, a waiver or a wristband at the desk. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F8 | How long does a chrono pass count? | For the rest of the field's local day (`chrono_valid_for = "day"`); `"game"` asks for a chronograph every game. A replica refused earlier that day is shown as such to marshals. | Spec section 12 already says a pass shows "for the rest of the day"; one queue per day, not per game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F9 | What happens to check-ins when a standby controller takes over? | They are not copied to the standby in this version. After a takeover the app checks in again by itself with its field identity and resends its declaration, and the marshals' apps resend their signed readings, which rebuilds the list. | Check-ins never travel on the radio; copying them over the network is extra work for a rare case, and the phones hold copies. To revisit if takeovers turn out to be common. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F10 | The federation number (many Portuguese games require players federated with the FPA, per the maintainer). | An optional field per check-in and registration, set per field: `off` (default), `optional` or `required`, with a label such as "FPA". Free text, **never verified automatically**; `required` only stops the form from being sent empty; staff tick "card seen". **Open:** does the federation offer any way to verify a number, and should the project use it? No such service is assumed; the maintainer would have to find out. | Fields need to record it; the project has no verification source and must not pretend to have one. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F11 | Check-in personal data and its retention. | Kept: a display name, the optional federation number, a team preference, the declared replicas, the time, the per-field id and its secret's hash; nothing else. Never in events, MQTT, logs or read-token views. Erased 30 days after the game (ADR-P4-8's guest rule); a field identity unused for 365 days is deleted (the chrono retention, B12). Proposed lawful basis: legitimate interest (running the game, replica safety); for the federation number, the field's rules for that game where it requires one. A short default notice in pt and en ships with the controller. All of it goes to the legal review of P4-6. | Data minimisation; consistent with the phase 4 retention table. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F12 | A player without the app. | A marshal (app) or the GM (console) checks them in by hand with the same fields; the controller makes the id, which the tag writer can put on a player tag (PD14). Linking a hand check-in to an app later is not designed. | Not every player will install the app; the list and the chronograph must work for everyone. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F13 | Who schedules and publishes, and what the field page shows. | `admin` and `gm` on a field schedule and edit; only `admin` publishes and cancels. The public field page is off by default, rendered on the server, and shows published games and places left, never who registered. | Publishing is an outward-facing act of the field; names on a public page are not needed. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F14 | Does online registration need an account? | Yes (ADR-P4-2). Registrations imported from elsewhere have no account and follow the 30-day guest rule. | An account delivers the check-in ticket, keeps the history and gives the player the GDPR rights in one place. A name-only guest registration would need an email to deliver the ticket, which is more data, not less. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F15 | Pre-declared replicas and the check-in ticket. | Declared at registration (on by default per game), sent in the roster and shown as pending in the controller's list before anyone arrives. Each registration gets a 32-byte ticket secret: the site server keeps its hash and puts the hash in the roster; the app (or a printed `atalaia:ticket:` QR code shown to a marshal) presents it at check-in. | Marshals can prepare in advance; the ticket matches an arrival to a registration offline, and is no game credential. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F16 | Who is in charge on game day, and what goes back? | The controller pulls the roster until the game starts (additions and cancellations apply to players not yet checked in); after the start nothing from the site server changes that game; staff changes on the day win. After the game it pushes each registration's outcome and the declarations' final states. Walk-ins go back as a count unless the field sets `[sync] push_walkins`. | Local-first (spec sections 3 and 11) and ADR-P4-3's "the site server never pushes game state to the controller during a game"; walk-ins never agreed to anything on the site server. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F17 | A waiting list when a game is full? | Not in this version: registration closes at `max_players`. | Keeps the first version small; easy to add if fields ask. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F18 | iCalendar feed details. | Public `.ics` per field and per game (when the field page is on); RFC 5545 with UTC times (no `VTIMEZONE`), stable `UID`, `SEQUENCE` raised on changes of time, place or status, cancelled games kept for 30 days as `STATUS:CANCELLED`; drafts never; no personal data. | Every calendar app can subscribe; UTC avoids shipping time-zone definitions. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F19 | JSON feed format. | The project's own versioned `atalaia.games` version 1 with a JSON Schema, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; the game page also embeds schema.org `Event` JSON-LD. | No shared format for airsoft game listings is known to the project; a small documented one is easy for clubs' sites and platforms to read. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F20 | What the registration import reads. | CSV (RFC 4180) or JSON with only `display_name`, `external_ref`, `team_preference`, `federation_number` and `replicas`; any other column (an email, a phone number) is ignored and named in the preview; rows update by `(source, external_ref)`; dry run first. | Minimisation even when another platform's export carries more; re-importing an updated export is safe. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F21 | Rules for platform adapters. | Only through the platform's documented API or a written agreement. No scraping, no automated form posting, no use of someone's login beyond what the API offers. Off by default, per field, credentials encrypted, every call audited, outbound from the site server only; registrations go through the import rules. | Clean-room policy (spec section 17), the platforms' own terms, and legal safety. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F22 | jogarairsoft.pt | **Needs the maintainer.** No public information or API about it was found (web search, 2026-10-19), so no adapter is designed and nothing about it is assumed. The maintainer would contact its operators to ask whether they offer an API or a registration export, or would read the iCalendar and JSON feeds. Until then a field can use the feeds and the CSV import (if the platform lets it export a list). No scraping. | Nothing about a third party is invented. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| F23 | Importing a registration list without a site server. | Not now: the import is a site-server feature (phase 4). A GM-console import of the same CSV straight into the controller's check-in list, for fields that take registrations elsewhere and have no site server, is a small follow-up if the maintainer wants it. | The agreed split puts the import in phase 4; the format is shared, so the controller can take it later without a new design. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Medic Kit (Ligaduras), queued after phase 3 plan phase E

Maintainer request, 2026-10-19: "bandages": a hit player asks for medic assistance, touches and holds the button (as when arming the bomb) for a time the organisation sets per game, to get healed; the kit has a set number of heals, shows how many remain, and updates the GM console and the players' view. Then: "in order to activate the heal, the player must identify himself with his NFC tag (so we know who is the player being healed and can also define a max number of healing per players)".

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MK1 | Is the player tag required? | **Yes (maintainer).** A heal starts only with the player's signed NFC tag (protocol/nfc-tag.md); forged tags, another game's tags and players of a team the kit does not serve are refused, and the screen says why. | Each heal names the player, and per-player limits need to know who it is. | confirmed |
| MK2 | Flow | Scan the tag, then hold the team button for `heal_hold_s` (per game, default 10 s); letting go early cancels; the screen shows the progress, then "Restam N ligaduras". | The Bomb's hold-to-arm, which players already know. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK3 | Limits | `charges` per kit (default 5, refused when empty, the GM can refill from the console), `max_heals_per_player` per game (default 2), optional `cooldown_s` per player; `team` = the team the kit serves or any. | The maintainer's two limits, plus what a field would tune. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK4 | Where the per-player limit is counted | Game-wide on the controller: it sees every heal (its field.lua keeps the count and tells every kit); each kit also counts its own heals, so it keeps working out of contact, at the risk of one extra heal before the count arrives. Android kits on the field's Wi-Fi get the count almost at once. | A per-kit count would let a player with a limit of 2 be healed twice at every kit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK5 | Events | `medic.heal` {team, player, remaining, player_heals_used} and `medic.empty`, ids 50 and 51 (phase 4 already plans 42-49), to the controller, the GM console feed, the players' view and the scoreboard; no personal data beyond the player id. | Next free ids; the check-in reused event 15 (F6), so 50 is free. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK6 | Running alongside a main mode | A field game assigns the medic role to its kit nodes, and a node runs only the parts for its role (not, say, Domination's capture rules). This needs a small change to how a game composes scripts per node role. | Medic kits sit next to any game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK7 | Hardware | A Field Box with its PN532 NFC reader, or an Android phone's own NFC. | The tag is required (MK1). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK8 | Names | "Medic Kit" in English, "Ligaduras" in Portuguese; "squad" stays "squad". | Players' own words. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK9 | How does a node run only its role's part (MK6)? | A new manifest kind, `kind: role` (a **role package**), next to `mode` and `modifier`. In a composed game (`org.atalaia.domination+org.atalaia.medic`) the game's roles are the main mode's and the role packages'; a node with a role package's role runs that package's `node.lua` alone, with its parameters only; the other nodes run the main mode and the modifiers as before; every part's `field.lua` runs on the controller. The controller makes each box's join with **what that box runs** (`org.atalaia.medic` and the kit's parameters for a kit; the main mode, its modifiers and their parameters for a zone box; the whole game for a standby controller). Join encoding, the Field Box's and the app's composition are unchanged; `protocol/join.md` and `modes/README.md` say so. | The smallest change consistent with K1: boxes and phones need no manifest knowledge (their stored presets have none), and each join's parameters stay well inside 200 bytes. Alternatives: the composed chunk dispatching on a node-role global (a core API change on every platform), or a modifier flag (a modifier runs on the main mode's nodes by definition). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK10 | In which language are the kit's screens? | A string parameter `lang`, `pt` (default) or `en`. The templates carry both languages' texts (`visible: "{params.lang == 'en'}"`); the refusal reasons come from the script in that language. The other modes' screens stay English (PD4). | The maintainer asked for the Portuguese texts ("Lê a tua etiqueta", "Mantém premido para tratar", "Restam N ligaduras"); there is no template i18n mechanism, and a game parameter reaches both the box and the phone. Default `pt` because the kit is the maintainer's request for a Portuguese field; `en` is one setting away. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK11 | How do the GM's refill and the controller's counts reach a kit? | A new CMD opcode **8, `mode`** (RFC 0001 Amendment 5, proposed): params `{"name": ..., ...}`, delivered to the script as `cmd.mode` with the params (name included). The GM's commands are declared in the manifest (`commands: { refill: { roles: [medic], label } }`, labels under `i18n.<lang>.commands`); the console shows a button per command on boxes of those roles and the controller refuses undeclared names or a box of another role. A `field.lua` sends `field.send(node, "mode", { name = "heals", ... })` (any valid name, no declaration). The Field Box, the Android prop (radio and Wi-Fi) and the simulator deliver it; older boxes ignore opcode 8. | Opcodes 0-7 are all used, and `set_owner` and `message` mean something else on every box (`message` is shown to players); one generic opcode stops new modes from needing new opcodes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK12 | What does a refill do, and is it reported? | It sets the kit back to `charges` and its idle screen. No event: the scope names only `medic.heal` and `medic.empty`; the console says the command was sent. A `medic.refilled` event (id 52) would let the console show a kit's charges after a refill. | Smallest scope; easy to add if the GM wants it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK13 | Hold details (MK2). | Any team button heals: the tag says who the player is. The heal comes when the button has been held `heal_hold_s` after the scan; a button already down when the tag is read counts from its first hold step after the scan; the hold must start within 15 s of the scan; one heal per hold (holding on does nothing more). | A medic kit is not a team's objective; the tag already identifies the team. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK14 | How are refusals shown? | `medic_refused` with the reason (`mac`, `game`, `no_game`, `team`, `not_tag`, `wrong_team`, `limit`, `cooldown` with the seconds left, `cancelled`, `scan_first`) for 4 s, then the idle screen; a hold during those 4 s does not replace the reason with "scan first". An empty kit shows its empty screen instead. Refusals send no event (the kit logs them). | The player has to read why; refusals are not game events and would cost airtime. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK15 | The per-player count across kits (MK4) in detail. | The controller's `field.lua` counts each player's heals: on each `medic.heal`, the larger of its count + 1 and the kit's `player_heals_used`; it tells every other kit, and the kit that healed only when that kit was behind (`heals` with `player`, `used` and `at`, the controller's game time in whole seconds, which the cooldown uses too). A kit out of reach heals on its own count (one extra heal possible); its heals arrive later and the totals add up. A kit that rebooted (`node.rebooted`) is told every count again. A kit's own charges do not survive a reboot (no mode's script state does yet). | MK4, made concrete; whole seconds keep a command well inside a box's 127 bytes of parameters. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK16 | The Field Box's fonts have no accented letters. | Its template renderer folds label texts first: accented Latin letters lose the accent ("Le a tua etiqueta"), dashes, quotes and the ellipsis become ASCII, LVGL symbols stay, anything else becomes `?` (`screen_template_fold_text`, tested). The app draws every character. Fonts with Latin-1 glyphs would cost flash; to revisit if a field wants Portuguese boxes to look right. | The Montserrat fonts in `lv_conf.h` are ASCII only; without folding "Lê" showed a missing-glyph box. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK17 | Parameter edges. | `max_heals_per_player` 0 means no limit (1-100 otherwise); `team` is optional: empty means every team, and then a player tag without a team is healed too; the GM console and the app's prop setup offer "every team" for optional team parameters (they always picked a team before). | A field without limits, and kits shared by both teams, are the common cases. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK18 | Event data (MK5). | `medic.heal`: `team` is the healed player's team (the common field), with `player`, `remaining` and `player_heals_used`; `medic.empty`: `team` is the team the kit serves, if one. Both normal priority. New data keys `remaining` (32) and `player_heals_used` (33); `player` (14) reused. Vectors for both in `protocol/vectors/events.yaml`, one for opcode 8 in `cmd.json`, a JSON example. | The scoreboard and the players' view group by team; the kit's team is a parameter the GM already knows. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK19 | How are a kit out of reach and the per-kit fallback tested? | In the simulator's Python tests (`simulator/tests/test_medic.py`, `Simulation.set_reachable`: events wait for the link, commands to the kit are lost), not as a YAML step, so the Dart runner (which runs every YAML test too) needs no new step. Two kits sharing a limit are a YAML test (Python and Dart), a controller test and an end-to-end test with host boxes. | Keeps the two test runners identical. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| MK20 | On-device test without NFC. | The emulator has no NFC reader, so the on-device test gives signed tags to the prop's session as the phone's reader does (`PropSession.tagRead`), and holds the on-screen button with a real gesture. Real tags on a real phone, and the Field Box's PN532, are not tested. | The same call the NFC subscription makes; nothing else is faked. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Field and team branding (maintainer request, 2026-10-19)

Maintainer request: "another nice addition to the mobile app would be each field (or team) running it at their own server be able to set a logo and/or a wallpaper to identify them". The agreed scope: field branding (name, logo, optional wallpaper, accent colour) on the controller a field or club runs, shown in the app, the GM console, the scoreboard and the check-in poster; team branding per game (a display name and a crest next to the colour) with saved team presets; club profiles with accounts left for phase 4.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BR1 | Where does a field's branding live? | On the controller the field or club runs: set in the GM console's *Definições / Settings* with the GM token, kept in its SQLite file (schema 4: an `images` table and a `branding.field` setting), so it survives restarts. A standby controller has its own branding (nothing is synced over the radio or between controllers); a field with two controllers uploads it to both. | The field's own server is what the request names; the radio has no room for images. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR2 | Who can read it? | Anyone who can reach the field's API, with no token: `GET /api/v1/field/branding`, its images and the crests (players need it before or right after checking in, a prop has no read token). Nothing in it is personal data. Reads are rate-limited to 240 a minute per address. | It identifies the field to everyone on its network, like the poster on the wall. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR3 | Endpoints and caching | `GET /api/v1/field/branding` (the field id and name, the accent, each image's id, size and type) with an ETag; `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/field/branding/{logo,wallpaper}` (raw body; GET with the image's SHA-256 as ETag and `Cache-Control: no-cache`, so clients revalidate with `If-None-Match` and get 304); `POST /api/v1/crests` and `GET /api/v1/crests/{sha256}` (immutable, cached for a year). The stream's snapshot carries the branding and a `branding` message announces changes, so the console, the scoreboard and the app update live. Served next to the existing `/api/v1/field/image`, which stays the console's map. | The ETag makes the app's refresh one small request when nothing changed; content-addressed crests never need revalidation. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR4 | Is the field name a new setting? | No: the branding's name is the check-in poster's field name (`[checkin] field_name` until changed), one setting shown in both places; changing it in the branding form changes the poster (with the poster's checks: 1-48 characters). | Two names for one field would drift apart. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR5 | Does the poster, the check-in answer or the join payload need a branding pointer? | No protocol change. The app already knows the controller's addresses (the poster's, or the join payload's keys 14 and 13 for a prop) and asks the branding endpoint there; `protocol/checkin.md` says so in a line. | Keeps the poster's QR code and the join payloads as they are (both have size limits). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR6 | Exact limits | Logo: PNG, JPEG or WebP, at most 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes), 16 to 2048 px a side, at most 8:1. Wallpaper: at most 4 MiB (4,194,304 bytes), 320 to 4096 px a side, at most 8:1. Crest: at most 512 KiB (524,288 bytes), 16 to 1024 px a side, at most 4:1. The type is read from the file's signature and must match the declared Content-Type. SVG (scripts), GIF and anything else: 415; over the size: 413; damaged, animated (APNG, animated WebP) or outside the pixel limits: 422. | The request's ~1 MB / 4 MB / 512 KB, with pixel caps that bound what a phone or a Pi has to decode. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR7 | Pillow is not a controller dependency. Decode and re-encode, or validate? | Validate without an image library (`controller/atalaia_controller/images.py`, standard library only): PNG chunk by chunk with every CRC, a valid header, the pixel data inflated in 1 MB steps and checked to be exactly as long as the header says (truncated files and decompression bombs refused), IEND last; JPEG segment by segment with exactly one frame header and its size, the scans walked to the end marker; WebP's RIFF size and its VP8, VP8L or VP8X header with the size. No pixel is re-encoded; only browsers and the app decode pixels, with their own decoders, and the app falls back to the default look if one fails. If the maintainer prefers a full decode, `python3-pil` (Debian/Raspberry Pi OS package) could re-encode uploads; not added. | No new dependency on the Pi; Pillow's decoders have a long CVE history and a full decode of a 4096 px image is slow on a Pi. The residual risk is a malformed entropy-coded stream that only a full decoder would notice; it reaches only the GM's own uploads and the clients' decoders. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR8 | Metadata and storage | The controller rewrites each container without its metadata: PNG text, time, EXIF and unknown ancillary chunks; JPEG APP1 (EXIF, XMP), APP3-APP13 (IPTC), APP15 and comments, and anything after the end marker; WebP EXIF and XMP chunks with their VP8X flags. Colour profiles stay. Images are stored once by the SHA-256 of the result. A replaced or removed logo or wallpaper is deleted at once; a crest no game and no team preset uses is deleted a day after its upload; at most 500 crests are kept. | Photos from phones carry the place and the device; the hash makes uploads idempotent and gives the ETag. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR9 | Served images | `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, `Content-Disposition: inline`; the console's own CSP already allows images from itself only. | An image URL opened as a page stays inert. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR10 | Image rights | A line in the console next to every upload (field images and crests): upload only images you have the right to use; the uploader is responsible. No takedown process in the controller (it is the club's own server). | The maintainer's requirement; the project hosts nothing. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR11 | Accent colour | One `#rrggbb` colour or none. In the console it colours the header (black or white text by luminance); in the app, BR17. | A single colour is enough to tell fields apart and is easy to keep readable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR12 | Team branding model | Per game: `team_info` `{team: {display_name, crest_id, team_preset_id}}` on `POST /api/v1/games` (a new `games.team_info_json` column). The team's name stays its colour (red, blue...): the boxes, their LEDs and buttons, the radio, join payloads, events and MQTT are unchanged and keep the colour names. A display name is 1 to 32 characters without control characters. | Colours are what boxes can show; names and crests are for people. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR13 | When can a game's team names and crests change? | Until the game ends: `PUT /api/v1/games/{id}/teams/{team}` (GM token; `null` clears a field), from the console's game card too; an ended game keeps them for its results and refuses changes (409). | Teams are often named on the day, after the game was created. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR14 | Team presets | Saved teams on the controller for teams that come back: a display name (unique), the colour they usually play (informational) and a crest; `GET/POST /api/v1/team-presets`, `PUT/DELETE /api/v1/team-presets/{id}`. Picking one for a game copies its name and crest into the game (later changes to the preset do not change past games). Club profiles with accounts are phase 4 (site server). | The request's "teams that come back"; copying keeps game history stable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR15 | How does an Android prop get the team names and crests? | `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/teams`, no token, over the join payload's pinned HTTPS. Game ids are 16-bit and could be guessed, but the answer is only names and crests, which are public on the field by design. | A prop has no read token, and a new prop-link message would be a protocol change. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR16 | Where does the app show a field's branding? | On that field's screens only: the check-in page, the player's view (also with a read-token link: whichever field answers there) and an Android prop in that field's game. The home screen, settings, the marshal's tools and other fields keep the default look. A field that set nothing (no logo, wallpaper or accent) keeps the default title and look. | A phone visits several fields; one field's colours should not follow the player around. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR17 | Accent and readability in the app | The accent becomes the theme's primary colour on the branded screens (filled buttons, switches, section titles), lightened on the dark theme or darkened on the light one in 12% steps until it reads at 4.5:1 on the screen's background; the text on it is black or white, whichever reads. | The primary colour is also used for text; WCAG AA for normal text. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR18 | Wallpaper dimming | Blurred a little (sigma 3) under a veil of the theme's background: 80% on the dark theme, 86% on the light one; decoded at the screen's width (at most 2048 px). Tested with bright stripes behind the text (golden images). | Text and team cards keep their contrast whatever the photo; decoding at screen size saves memory on cheap phones. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR19 | The wallpaper and the YAML game screens | Never behind the mode's own screen: on the Android prop the wallpaper shows only on the idle screens (loading, ready to start, ended), with the field's logo and name; during the game (running, paused) the mode's 4:3 screen, the team buttons and the clock stay on the plain dark background, and the logo stays small in the top bar. The mode's screens keep showing the colour name, as the Field Box does. | The game screens are read at a glance in the field; a photo behind them would cost contrast for no gain. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR20 | What the app keeps | Per field id (ADR-P3-13's field identity): the name, accent, ETag and the two images, in the app's private files (`blobs/`); crests by their hash (checked on download), the last 64. Refreshed when the check-in page or the player's view opens and when the stream says the branding changed; out of reach, the last kept branding shows. Removing the field in the app removes its branding. The app checks the size and the signature again and draws the default look if an image does not decode. | Shows at once at the next visit and without a connection; a stale branding is harmless. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR21 | Prop team buttons | Each button keeps its team's colour and gets the crest above the name ("Manter premido / LOBOS"); without a crest it is exactly as before. | Colour is what the box next to it shows. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR22 | Scoreboard | The field's logo and name above the game's name, the wallpaper behind a veil of the page's colour (84% light, 82% dark) with the team cards opaque, each team's crest and display name; the winner by display name. | Read from a distance. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR23 | Check-in poster | The field's logo above its name, outside the QR code; the QR code is unchanged (error correction M). Tested by reading the code back with `zbarimg` from the code alone and from the whole poster with the logo. | A logo inside a QR code costs error correction for no benefit. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| BR24 | Left for later | The field's logo on the Field Box's TFT (needs a conversion to the box's LVGL image format and flash space); club profiles with accounts (phase 4, site server, see its PLAN.md); team names in MQTT and on the boxes' screens; syncing branding to a standby controller; the console's own PWA icon and theme colour (they stay Atalaia's). | Outside the agreed scope or needing hardware work. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Project page polish (site/, 2026-10-19)

The maintainer approved showing the real status on the project page "so people checking it can see the real status/progress", and asked for wider intros, a screenshot viewer and more app captures.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SP1 | Status tags of the roadmap | Phase 0 "Done" (gate met); phases 1 and 2 "Software done" (their gates wait for the hardware); phase 3 "In progress", the lit one; phases 4 and 5 "Designed" (research, architecture and plan written, no code), a dashed tag like the planned parts of the diagrams.
