The tags follow `docs/phase-*/STATUS.md`. | Phase 2 was still lit as the current phase although phase 3 has been built since, and phases 4 and 5 had no status at all. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| SP2 | Portuguese for "Designed" | "Planeada". | It is what a Portuguese reader says of work that is researched and planned but not started; "Desenhada" reads as drawn (a sketch or a drawing), and "Prevista" is already the hub's "Planned". | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| SP3 | A script on the page | One small inline script, on the main page only, for the screenshot viewer: progressive enhancement (each screenshot is a link to its image, which opens without JavaScript), nothing loaded from other sites, no storage, no cookies. site/README.md no longer says "no JavaScript". | The maintainer asked for click to enlarge; a `<dialog>` with focus handling needs a few lines of script, and CSS alone cannot give Esc, focus return and arrow keys. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| SP4 | Which app captures | Added: the role picker and a field's branded player view (each language its own), and three Medic Kit screens (one copy: the kit's screens are Portuguese by default). Not added: the check-in screen after scanning the poster and the marshal's "replicas to chrono" list, which have no golden images; the branded prop (its Portuguese image mixes the English mode screen with Portuguese buttons) and the setup form (cut off at the bottom). | Only images that read well at their size, taken from the committed golden images so they stay reproducible; making new golden images is app work. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| SP5 | Statements that were plans | The page now says "still to come" for: sending a new mode to a box without flashing (K10: a new mode needs one flash), firmware updates over the air (pending in firmware/esp32/README.md), the Android field controller (PE24), Arduino-class thin nodes (firmware/thin-node: planned), iOS (PD10); LoRa's 0.5-2 km and the ESP32's one to three days on a battery are marked as estimates. The hero's lead (a hacking terminal on an old phone, a 20-box weekend milsim) stays as the project's aim, under the pre-alpha status line. | Accuracy on a public page; the lead describes what the platform is for, not what runs today. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 design refresh after phase 3 software (2026-10-19, design only)

The phase 4 design (`docs/phase-4-site-server/`) was written before most of phase 3 was built. This refresh checks it against the code as built (the controller, the app, the modes, the protocol files) and re-sequences the plan so that everything that needs no hardware can be built while the Heltec V4 boards are on their way. Each answer below is the recommendation used in the refreshed RESEARCH.md, ADR.md (dated amendments) and PLAN.md. Nothing here is built yet.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P4R1 | Which event ids and data keys does phase 4 take? | Events: 42 `scenario.objective.completed`, 43 `scenario.objective.failed`, 44 `scenario.objective.revealed`, 45 `scenario.started`, 46 `vip.extracted`, 47 `hacking.solved`; 48-49 stay free for phase 4; no `satellite.*` events (P4R2); `target.hit` is already 16 (`window_open`, key 16). Data keys: 34 `objective`, 35 `scenario`, 36 `puzzle`; `zone` (5), `player` (14), `code` (10) and `reason` (3) are reused. 50-51 are the Medic Kit's, so 52 and up are free for later. Each id is added to `protocol/schema/events.yaml` (with `tools/protogen.py`, a shared vector and the draft RFC) in the plan phase that first emits it, not all at once. | The old design reserved 42-49 for eight events, two of which are not needed and one of which (`target.hit`) already existed. Adding ids only with the code that uses them keeps every commit's schema, codecs and tests in step. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R2 | How do satellites report captures in Master and Satellites? | With the standard `zone.captured` and `zone.neutralized`, as the design specification's own example says (section 15). One main mode, `org.atalaia.master_satellites`, with the roles `master` and `satellite`; its `node.lua` is capture-only (as Hold-All's: boxes do not score) and its `field.lua` tells master from satellites with `field.nodes("master")` and `field.nodes("satellite")`, scores the master's owner and resets the satellites (`field.send(id, "set_owner", { team = "none" })`) when the master changes hands. Points are whole numbers: `score.add` takes integers only, so the "x1.5 per satellite" bonus is `points_per_tick * held // 2` added to the base points (rounded down), not a fractional multiplier. | `field.owner()` follows each node's last `zone.captured`, the console, scoreboard, MQTT topics and the app already show it, and no node needs to know its role (a main mode's roles share one `node.lua`). `score.add(team, 1.5)` raises an error in the core. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R3 | Engineer-only satellites? | Deferred: not in the first version of the mode. Player tags carry the player and the team, not the squad (`protocol/nfc-tag.md`), and plain team buttons carry no squad, so nothing tells a box who is an engineer. Candidate later designs: a per-check-in `engineer` flag known to the controller (its `field.lua` refuses a capture by tag from a player without it), or a squad or skill in a version 2 player tag (an RFC). | The old plan assumed "NFC squad identification" from phase 3; phase 3 built player tags without squads. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R4 | How does a target addon's hit reach a script, and what can be built before the addon exists? | The spec's `hit` capability reaches scripts as the input event `hit.hit` with `strength` (0-255), and `cap("hit"):arm()` / `:disarm()` are its commands; written into the draft RFC 0000 with the capability-proxy type `hit` (so a pod or an addon can lend it later). Target Windows (`org.atalaia.target_windows`) is written and tested now in the simulator (the tests' `input` step injects `hit.hit`) and through the core in Dart; it uses `target.hit` (16) with `window_open`. It is not a Field Box built-in preset until the addon and its driver exist, and Android props do not offer `hit`. | The core's `cap()` already forwards any capability to the host, and the test runner already injects any input event, so the logic needs no new core code; only the addon itself needs the hardware. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R5 | The Hacking Terminal: touch puzzles on Android only, or something else? | Built on the inputs that exist: the keypad (`keypad.key`), team input and screen templates, not on a `display.touch` event (no such event exists in the core, the app or the firmware, and spec section 7 notes that touch is weak outdoors). Puzzles: code cracking (guess a 3-6 digit code with right-place and wrong-place counts), wire order (cut numbered wires in the right order on the keypad) and a sequence to repeat; a wrong answer locks the terminal for `lockout_s`; `hacking.solved` (47) with `team`, `puzzle` and optionally `code` (the code it reveals). Runs on a Field Box with a keypad and on an Android prop's on-screen keypad, so it is a built-in preset like the other modes. A touch-only puzzle needs a `display.touch` input (an RFC) and stays for later. | Everything here is testable now in the simulator, the Dart bridge and the Field Box's host tests; a touch event would need new work in the core, the firmware and the app, and the spec warns against touch on the field. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R6 | VIP / Payload: how does a position reach the mode's script? | Two parts. **VIP player:** the controller hands its `field.lua` a controller-only input `position.fix` (`player`, `team`, `role`, `lat`, `lon`, `acc`, `src`) for each new report from a player whose check-in role is `vip` (`POST /api/v1/positions`, phase 3); it is never logged, never emitted, never sent on the radio, MQTT or the stream, and never replicated to a standby. Scripts cannot leak coordinates through events because no event has `lat` or `lon` data keys. **Payload prop:** an Android prop with a `gps` capability gets `gps.fix` (`lat`, `lon`, `acc`) in its `node.lua` from the app's location service, checks the extraction zone itself with `geo.in_zone()` and emits only `vip.extracted` (46). | The phase 3 positions are kept on the controller and filtered by role (ADR-P3-6, constraint 9); a `gps.fix` event on the radio, as the old design had, would broadcast a player's position to every box. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R7 | How does a custom scenario run on boxes that only have built-in scripts? | A new manifest kind, `scenario`: a package with a `field.lua` and no `node.lua` and no roles of its own, which leads the composed id (`org.club.foxhound+org.atalaia.objectives`) and is never named in a box's join. The nodes run a new built-in role package, `org.atalaia.objectives` (kind `role`), with two roles: `objective` (capture and hold without local scoring, a code typed on the keypad, tags scanned) and `bomb_site` (arm, defuse, explode without local scoring); the scenario's `field.lua` configures each node at the start, and again after a reboot, with mode commands (`field.send(id, "mode", { name = "setup", ... })`, as the Medic Kit's `heals`). The compiler generates the scenario package (manifest, `field.lua`, translations); the box side never changes per scenario. | A Field Box refuses a join for a mode it does not have (`BOX_JOIN_NO_MODE`), and a new built-in mode needs a firmware flash (decision K10), so per-scenario node scripts would never reach the boxes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R8 | How does the scenario DSL name teams, squads and nodes? | Teams by the game's team names (the colours the boxes and the radio use; display names and crests come from branding); squads by number (1 to the game's `squads`, as the controller has them); nodes by names the GM assigns to roles in the console (as the mode tests' `nodes:`), mapped to node ids at join time; objective ids `[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,23}` so that every scenario event fits a LoRa packet; texts (objective names and descriptions) in the scenario package in English and `i18n.pt`, never in events. | The old example used free squad names (`alpha-1`) and team names that the controller does not have. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R9 | Does the site server check a scenario's Lua the way the controller does? | Yes: the site server's image builds the C core (`libatalaia_core.so`) in a build stage and runs the same load check as the controller's `modes.install()`; the final image has no compiler. The controller checks again when the scenario is installed, and stays the authority. | One check everywhere; a scenario that compiles on the site server and fails on the controller on game day is the failure to avoid. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R10 | How are scores synced, given that a GM's adjustment can be negative? | Per node counters (the max each node reported, per team, as the controller keeps them) merge by `GREATEST`; the GM's adjustments and the field script's points arrive as `score.delta` events and controller counters; the site server derives the totals the same way as `FieldController.total_scores`. No `GREATEST` on totals. | A total can go down (a GM's -50), so a grow-only merge of totals would keep a wrong, higher score for ever. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R11 | How does the site server identify a controller game? | By `(field, game_id, game_seed)`: the 16-bit radio `game_id` repeats over the months, and `game_seed` (32-bit, random per game, carried in the join) is the same on a standby controller. The events' unique key on the site server is the controller's, `(game, node, seq, game_time, type)`. The controller's push cursor is its own `events.id` per game, acknowledged by the site server, kept in a new `sync_state` table (store schema 5). | No controller schema change to the games table; replays stay idempotent; a standby's events land in the same game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R12 | Which side opens the connections? | The controller, always: HTTPS requests and one WebSocket to the site server. The site server never connects to the controller and never holds the controller's GM or read token. Remote GM commands (and remote setup) ride the controller's WebSocket, are off by default (`[sync] remote_gm = false`), are limited to a list (start, pause, resume, end, message, mode commands), and are logged on both sides. This changes the answer to P4-8 (the site server would use the controller's GM and read tokens). | A field controller sits behind a phone's 4G link, usually behind carrier-grade NAT: it cannot be reached from the internet. Holding a field's GM token on a server is also a larger risk than needed. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R13 | How is the controller's API key stored? | On the site server as its SHA-256 (it is a 256-bit random value: hashing it with Argon2id adds nothing and needed a cache); compared in constant time; shown once; rotatable with two keys valid during the change. On the controller in `[sync] api_key` or `ATALAIA_SYNC_API_KEY`, or stored in its settings with `python3 -m atalaia_controller sync-key` (mode 0600, like the tokens). | Slow hashes protect low-entropy passwords; a random 256-bit key cannot be guessed. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R14 | Marshal signing keys: generated on the site server with an encrypted copy for recovery (ADR-P4-5), or as built? | As built: each marshal's phone makes its Ed25519 key (`app/lib/chrono/record_signer.dart`) and registers the public key with the controller (`PUT /api/v1/checkin/me/marshal-key`, `POST /api/v1/marshals`). The site server stores public keys only, by `marshal_id`, with their history (old keys stay so that old records verify), and links them to marshal accounts. No private key, encrypted or not, on the site server; a lost phone means a new key. `protocol/chrono-record.md` still says "per ADR-P4-5, encrypted on the site server for recovery" and is to be corrected when this is confirmed. | A server that can sign as a marshal breaks the non-repudiation ADR-P4-5 chose Ed25519 for, and phase 3 works without it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R15 | How does the site server keep chrono records? | One row per signed reading (the format of `protocol/chrono-record.md`), the record kept exactly as received and verified on arrival with the controller's own verifier (`atalaia_controller/chrono.py`, the shared vectors); the series and its outcome from the controller; the player is the per-field `player_id` until an account adopts it; sharing between fields only for accounts that consented (ADR-P4-5, P4-8), never by matching per-field ids. | ADR-P4-1's `chrono_records` (a readings array, a server-made signature) does not match the signed records phase 3 produces. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R16 | Which Python stack does the site server use, and in which image? | Its own image, `python:3.12-slim`, with pip dependencies pinned with hashes: FastAPI with pydantic 2, SQLAlchemy 2 (async) with asyncpg, Alembic, argon2-cffi, PyJWT, pyotp, cryptography, PyYAML, httpx (tests). Not the atalaia-dev image's Ubuntu packages (FastAPI 0.101 with pydantic 1.10, no SQLAlchemy 2). The code it shares with the controller (the scenario library, the chrono verifier, the player-token codec, the image checks, `atalaia_sim.mode`) stays free of FastAPI and pydantic, so both import it. | The site server is deployed as a container (ADR-P4-10), so it can pin its own versions; the controller keeps the Raspberry Pi OS generation (decision D15). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R17 | How are the site server's tests run, and with which database? | With `unittest` like the rest of the repository (`IsolatedAsyncioTestCase`, httpx's ASGI transport), against a real PostgreSQL 16: in CI a `postgres:16-alpine` service container in a new `server` job, locally an ephemeral container started by a new `tools/docker_server.py` (Docker Compose, `server/docker-compose.test.yml`). No SQLite stand-in for the database layer (JSONB, `ON CONFLICT`, `GREATEST`, schemas and row-level security differ). Layers without a database (the iCalendar and JSON feeds, the import parser, batch validation, the chrono check) also run in the atalaia-dev image with `tools/check.sh`. | One test runner in the repository; a database-specific schema tested on another database would hide its bugs. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R18 | What does the ATAK feed show, and from which coordinates? | Props and zones (owner, state) over UDP multicast on the field Wi-Fi, off by default (`[atak] enabled`). Prop coordinates come from the field image, which the GM georeferences by placing two of its corners on the map (lat and lon), since Field Boxes have no GPS (E21) and their positions are 0-1 on the image; Android props with GPS give their own. **Player positions are never multicast** (anyone on the field Wi-Fi would see every team); they go only to staff ATAK endpoints the GM lists (TCP), off by default, and only for the roles allowed to see them. | Constraint 9 of phase 3: positions are filtered on the server side; multicast cannot filter. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R19 | Club profiles and branding through the site server? | A club on the site server has a name, logo, crest and colour, edited by its own accounts (same image rules as `controller/atalaia_controller/images.py`, which the site server imports); a field's controllers pull the field branding and the saved teams in `GET /api/v1/sync/field` (images by SHA-256, fetched only when changed). The controller's own edits win unless the field sets `[sync] branding = "site"`. Public club pages need moderation (a report button and an admin who can hide a page); designed in plan phase 18. | Phase 3 built branding per controller (BR1-BR24); a club that runs several controllers, or a team that plays at several fields, otherwise uploads the same images many times. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R20 | In which order is phase 4 built, and what comes first? | Software that uses proven tooling first: the four modes and the geo functions (plan phases 1-5), the scenario system (6-8), the ATAK emitter (9), then the site server chain (10-22: skeleton and its test tooling, accounts, personal data, sync, chrono, scheduling and registration, club profiles, devices and remote GM, history, hosted mode, deployment). Hardware work (HW1-HW4) waits for the boards. First: plan phase 1, Master and Satellites. | The maintainer asked for as much code as possible before the Heltec V4 boards arrive; the modes reuse the simulator, Dart and Field Box tooling that phase 3 proved, and give quick, separately verifiable commits while the site server's new tooling (PostgreSQL in CI) is set up. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R21 | Licences of the controller additions? | AGPL-3.0-or-later (`controller/**` in REUSE.toml) for `sync_client.py`, `atak.py` and `scenarios/`; GPL-3.0-or-later for modes and the core; AGPL-3.0-or-later for `server/`. The old plan said GPL for the controller additions. | REUSE.toml is the source of truth. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R22 | Mode folder names and translations? | Folders with underscores like the existing ones (`master_satellites`, `target_windows`, `vip_payload`, `hacking_terminal`, `objectives`), ids `org.atalaia.<folder>`, translations in `mode.yaml` under `i18n.pt`, screens in `assets/screens/`, tests in `tests/*.yaml` run by `python3 -m atalaia_sim test` (and through the core in Dart); no `i18n/` folder and no pytest, as the old plan had. | The repository's conventions (modes/README.md). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R23 | Which new modes become Field Box built-in presets? | Master and Satellites, Hacking Terminal and the objectives package: added to the Field Box's preset list (`firmware/esp32/main/CMakeLists.txt`, `builtin_presets.c`), the app's catalogue (`python3 tools/docker_app.py gen`) and the GM console. Target Windows waits for the addon (P4R4); VIP / Payload's payload role is Android-only (it needs `gps`). Each is checked against the 16 KB preset buffer, the host tests and the three ESP-IDF builds with no warnings; Wokwi does not run (decision R7). | A mode that a box cannot run should not appear in its preset list. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R24 | How do scenario objectives reach the player app? | On the stream, routed like positions: `scenario.objective.revealed` and the objective events go to the team (and squad) they are assigned to, the GM and marshals see all, and a hidden objective is never sent before it is revealed (`ws.py` routing, `PLAYER_TYPES`). The app shows each objective with its translated name from the scenario package (fetched from the controller with the game's setup). | Visibility is part of the game (spec section 15) and must be enforced by the controller, not by the app. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R25 | Does the site server receive player positions? | No. Positions stay on the controller and are deleted when the game ends (phase 3); the sync carries none. ADR-P4-8's "positions deleted on game archive" is replaced by "never stored on the site server". A remote GM sees positions only live, through the controller's WebSocket, if `remote_gm` is on. | Data minimisation; the controller already deletes them. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| P4R26 | Target Windows picks a random team, but a node script cannot list the game's teams (the core has `field.score()` for the field script only; no `game.teams()`). | Add `game.teams()` to the core (the game's team names in order, for node and field scripts), with C, simulator and Dart-through-the-core tests, in plan phase 2. | One line of API that any mode choosing among teams needs; a parameter listing the teams again would drift from the game's real teams. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 10 (site server skeleton and its test tooling)

Choices made while building `server/`, `tools/docker_server.py` and CI's `server` job (2026-10-19).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S10-1 | The settings' names: ADR-P4-10's Compose file uses `DATABASE_URL`, `SECRET_KEY`, `LOG_LEVEL`. | `ATALAIA_*`: `ATALAIA_DATABASE_URL` (required, `postgresql+asyncpg://` only), `ATALAIA_DB_POOL_SIZE`, `ATALAIA_DB_MAX_OVERFLOW`, `ATALAIA_DB_TIMEOUT_S`, `ATALAIA_METRICS_TOKEN`, `ATALAIA_LOG_LEVEL`, read by plain Python (`config.py`, no pydantic-settings); a value that is present but wrong stops the start, never falls back to its default; the password is redacted in logs and `repr`. Plan phases 11 and 22 add theirs (`ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY`, ...) the same way. | The controller already uses `ATALAIA_<SECTION>_<KEY>`; one prefix avoids clashes in a shared environment; plain Python keeps the settings testable in tools/check.sh. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-2 | `GET /api/v1/metrics` is "admin auth" (ADR-P4-10), but there are no accounts before plan phase 11. | Until then it needs `Authorization: Bearer` with `ATALAIA_METRICS_TOKEN` (32+ characters, compared in constant time); with no token set it answers 404. It reports only what exists: version, uptime, the pool, the numbers of fields and users, the database's state (503 when down). Plan phase 11 puts it behind admin accounts and later plan phases add their counters (games, events, sync). | Public metrics leak the size of a club's player base; a fixed token is the smallest safe stand-in. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-3 | Where do the tests' own dependencies (httpx) go, given the hash-pinned requirements (P4R16)? | `server/requirements-test.in` and `requirements-test.txt` (hash-pinned, constrained by `requirements.txt`), installed only in the image's `test` target; the server image does not have httpx, and its health checks use `urllib` (ADR-P4-10's amendment). | The deployed image carries only what it runs. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-4 | How are the versions pinned and checked? | `uv pip compile --generate-hashes --exclude-newer <two weeks back>` (uv 0.9.30, in `python:3.12.14-slim`; the command is in `requirements.in`), here `--exclude-newer 2026-10-05`: FastAPI 0.142.2, pydantic 2.13.5, Starlette 1.7.0, uvicorn 0.54.0, SQLAlchemy 2.1.3, asyncpg 0.31.0, Alembic 1.20.0, PyYAML 6.0.3, cryptography 50.0.2 (httpx 0.28.1 for the tests). Every hash in both files was checked against PyPI's JSON API (each is a file of that release, every file of the release is listed, the newest uploaded before 2026-10-05). Installed with `pip --require-hashes --no-deps --only-binary=:all:` and `pip check`. Base images pinned by digest: `python:3.12.14-slim` (pushed 2026-09-25) and `postgres:16.15-alpine` (2026-09-21); the current `python:3.12-slim` (3.12.15, 2026-10-09) is under two weeks old. | Reproducible builds with nothing compiled from source in the image; the two-week rule leaves time for a bad release to be yanked. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-5 | What does `0001_base` hold, against ADR-P4-1's SQL? | `fields`, `users`, `roles` (seeded with ADR-P4-1's seven), `user_field_roles`, `sessions` and `audit_log` with ADR-P4-1's columns, indexes and constraints (named by a naming convention so downgrades can find them), except `fields.api_key_hash`: controller keys get their own table in `0003_sync` (two active during a rotation, P4R13). `user_game_roles` waits for `games` (0003). | Each plan phase adds its own reversible migration (ADR-P4-1's amendment); a column that a later table replaces would only have to be dropped again. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-6 | UUIDv7 keys (ADR-P4-1) on Python 3.12, which has no `uuid.uuid7`? | `atalaia_server/ids.py`: RFC 9562 section 5.7 with a 12-bit counter in `rand_a` (section 6.2, method 1), so keys from one process sort in creation order even within a millisecond; checked against RFC 9562 Appendix A.6's example. The ORM sets the key; `gen_random_uuid()` (version 4) stays the database's default for rows written by hand. | No extra dependency; time-ordered keys keep the indexes compact; the known answer is the RFC's. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-7 | How is the app started? | `uvicorn --factory atalaia_server.app:create_app` (the image runs `alembic upgrade head` first, then that, as an unprivileged user), instead of the amendment's `uvicorn atalaia_server.app:app`. | With a factory, importing the module reads no environment and builds nothing, so tests make apps with their own settings. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-8 | Security headers and API pages? | ADR-P4-10's headers on every response, errors included, from a plain ASGI middleware (WebSockets pass untouched); the CSP as written but with `connect-src 'self' wss:` for `wss://*`, plus `frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'`. FastAPI's `/docs` and `/redoc` are off; the OpenAPI document is at `/api/v1/openapi.json`. | The interactive pages load scripts from a CDN, which the CSP forbids; the added directives cover what `default-src` does not. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-9 | How do the tests get a database, and how are migrations checked? | Each test class creates its own database on the test server (`CREATE DATABASE atalaia_test_<random>`, migrated to head, `DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE)` afterwards); each test gets its own engine without a pool. `tests.test_migrations` steps through every revision (upgrade, downgrade, upgrade) comparing the whole schema from `information_schema` and `pg_catalog`, and checks that the migrations build exactly the ORM models (`compare_metadata` with types and server defaults); `docker_server.py migrations` runs `alembic upgrade head`, `downgrade base`, `upgrade head` and `alembic check`. | Isolation between classes without slow per-test migrations; a downgrade that leaves something behind, or a model changed without its migration, fails a test. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-10 | How does CI mirror the local commands? | One entry point in the image, `sh tests/run.sh test` or `sh tests/run.sh migrations`. Locally `tools/docker_server.py` runs it with Docker Compose (`server/docker-compose.test.yml`: the test image and `postgres:16.15-alpine` in a tmpfs, removed afterwards); CI's `server` job builds the same target with `docker build -f server/Dockerfile --target test .` and runs the same two commands with `--network host` against a `postgres:16.15-alpine` service container (same digest, user, password, tmpfs). The plain-Python tests (`test_config`, `test_ids`, `test_shared_imports`) also run in tools/check.sh and in CI's `controller` job on the Ubuntu packages. | A command that passes locally passes in CI for the same reasons; the CI job could not be run here, so its commands were run by hand against a container started the same way. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-11 | `docker_server.py up` and `down`, before plan phase 22's deployment? | A local trial: `up` builds the server image, makes a random database password and starts `server/docker-compose.yml` (server on `127.0.0.1:${ATALAIA_PORT:-8000}`, waits for both health checks); `down` stops it and deletes its volume. The Compose file has no default password (`${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?}`). Projects and images are `atalaia-server-test`, `atalaia-server-trial` and `atalaia-server`, each with `ATALAIA_VOLUME_SUFFIX`. | AC-1 needs a running server; a trial must not leave a database whose password nobody knows, nor collide with a second checkout. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S10-12 | Does the skeleton already build the core into the image (P4R9)? | Yes: stage `core` compiles `libatalaia_core.so` without mbedtls with Debian's cmake and gcc (build stage only; the final image has no compiler), and the test target copies `modes/` so `tests.test_shared_imports` loads every built-in mode through the controller's own `modes.check_scripts` in the image. The build context is the repository root, narrowed by `server/Dockerfile.dockerignore` (BuildKit's per-Dockerfile ignore file) instead of a root `.dockerignore`. | The scenario check of plan phase 20 then only adds an endpoint; a root `.dockerignore` would also affect other builds from the root. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 1: Master and Satellites (2026-10-19)

Made while building `modes/master_satellites` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 1). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M1-1 | How does the master box show its satellites and bonus (the plan's `master_status` screen), when the master and the satellites run one `node.lua` and no box knows its role? | `field.lua` tells the master box with a mode command of its own, `status` (`{ name, team, held, of, points }`), sent only when one of those changes; the box shows `master_status` when the status is for the team holding it, and `captured` otherwise (satellites, and a box picked from its own preset list). A new holder of the master shows 0 satellites and the base points at once, before the controller's next status. | Mode commands (CMD opcode 8, the Medic Kit's `heals`) already reach boxes and Android props; sending only changes keeps radio traffic to a few commands per capture. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M1-2 | When exactly are the satellites freed? | Whenever the master leaves a team's hands: another team captures it, or the GM sets it to another team or to none. The first capture of a free master frees nothing (a team that took satellites first keeps them). A satellite that is already free ignores `set_owner none`, so it does not log a second `zone.neutralized` (Hold-All's `node.lua` would). | Spec section 15 says "when the master changes hands"; freeing on the GM's "none" keeps the GM's reset of the master a full reset. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M1-3 | What does `min_satellites` mean? | The master scores nothing while its holder has fewer than `min_satellites` satellites (default 0: the master scores alone). The bonus is counted at each tick, with the satellites held at that moment. | The plan named the parameter without a rule; this reading gives organisers a "power plant must be up" variant and keeps the default the plan's game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M1-4 | The Field Box's keypad picker lists at most nine presets (keys 1-9), and Master and Satellites is the tenth built-in one. | Added as the tenth preset (P4R23): a box joined by the controller runs it (its script is in the preset store), but it cannot be picked on the box itself; on its own a box of this mode only captures anyway. Recommended: a second picker page (for example `0` for the next nine) in a later plan phase, before Hacking Terminal and the objectives package add more presets (built in plan phase 2: M2-1). | Changing the picker is firmware UI work outside this plan phase; the mode needs the controller for its scoring. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M1-5 | More than one master box, and where owners come from? | Only the first master (the lowest node id) counts. Owners come from `field.owner()` (the boxes' STATE reports), checked on every capture event and every second, as Hold-All and Last Stand do; no events of the mode's own (no `satellite.*` or `master.*`, P4R1, P4R2): the GM sees `master` and `held` in the console's field state. | One master is the spec's game; STATE is what the console, scoreboard and MQTT show, so the bonus follows what the boxes say. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M1-6 | The project page's roadmap tag for phase 4, now that it has code (SP1 had "Designed") | "In progress" on the main page and the roadmap page, with a line saying what is done in software (Master and Satellites, not yet on real boxes); phase 3 stays the lit phase. The hero status line says "phase 4 started". | "Designed" means no code (SP1), which is no longer true; phase 3's gate is still the next one to reach. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 11 (accounts, sessions and roles)

Choices made while building the site server's accounts, sessions and roles (2026-10-19).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S11-1 | Refresh tokens: ADR-P4-2's JWT signed with a `REFRESH_SECRET`, kept in `sessions.refresh_token`? | Opaque 256-bit random values (`secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`), stored only as their SHA-256 in a new `refresh_tokens` table (one row per token issued, `used_at` once rotated, `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `sessions`); `sessions.refresh_token` is dropped in `0002_accounts` (the table was always empty). A refresh marks the presented token used and issues a new one; a used token presented again revokes the whole session (ADR-P4-2's replay rule) and is logged as `refresh_replay`. The lookup is by hash, with the token's and session's rows locked (`FOR UPDATE`), so two refreshes cannot both pass. | Replay detection needs a record of every token a session was given; a database that leaks holds no usable token; a JWT adds nothing when the database is read anyway, and a random 256-bit value needs no slow hash (as P4R13). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-2 | Are access tokens stateless, as ADR-P4-2 says ("no database lookup on every API call")? | No: every request re-reads the session (it must exist, be the token's, not revoked, not expired) and the user's roles from `user_field_roles`; the token's `roles` claim is kept for the client's display only. JWT HS256 only, 15 minutes, `iss`, `typ`, `jti` and `sid` required. | A logout, a revoked session or a removed GM role must count at once, not up to 15 minutes later; two indexed queries per request are cheap at a field's scale. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-3 | How long does a session live? | A refresh moves its expiry to 7 days from now (ADR-P4-2's refresh lifetime), never past 30 days from the login; then the user logs in again (with TOTP where they have it). Two refreshes racing with the same token (two browser tabs) count as a replay and end the session, as ADR-P4-2 has it, with no grace period. | A sliding week keeps active users logged in; the cap bounds what one stolen session can do. A grace period would let a thief's replay pass too. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-4 | Which "top 100,000 breached passwords", under which licence? | The UK NCSC's list of the 100,000 passwords most seen in breaches (from Have I Been Pwned's Pwned Passwords), as published in SecLists: `Passwords/Common-Credentials/100k-most-used-passwords-NCSC.txt` at commit `1a7bb9127eca9e6ff2fc0301c597fe6e16a0cb56` (2025-11-19), shipped unmodified as `server/atalaia_server/data/breached-passwords.txt` (835 KB, SHA-256 `c2e5696882c603b76bb67a47ee970897e5a76fc4c3f5547abe3d0ca340c576e0`, checked by a test), annotated MIT in REUSE.toml as part of SecLists (MIT, 2018 Daniel Miessler). Compared case-insensitively after NFKC; only its entries of 10 characters or more (about 9,100) are held in memory. **The maintainer should confirm the licence**: the NCSC's terms and Have I Been Pwned's terms for Pwned Passwords may also apply to the data. | The ADR asks for exactly this list and no external call; SecLists is the common, stable source; an unmodified file can be checked against its source by its hash. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): ship the list as hashes, not in clear (done: `data/breached-passwords.sha256`, `tools/breached_hashes.py`) |
| S11-5 | Password policy and hashing details beyond ADR-P4-2? | NIST SP 800-63B: 10 to 1024 characters counted after Unicode NFKC normalisation (the hash is of the normalised form too), not the username, not on the list, no character classes. Argon2id with ADR-P4-2's parameters (t=3, 64 MiB, p=4, 16-byte salt, 32-byte tag); a hash with other parameters still verifies and is replaced at the next login; at most 2 hashes at once (a thread semaphore, so at most 128 MiB), in worker threads; an unknown username is checked against a dummy hash so the answer takes as long. | Normalisation keeps one password one password across keyboards; the cap stops a burst of logins from taking the server's memory (ADR-P4-2 says 256 MB minimum for the app); equal timing hides which usernames exist. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-6 | Per-game roles (ADR-P4-1's `user_game_roles`) in plan phase 11? | Not yet: the table needs `games` (migration `0003_sync`, plan phase 13), so it comes there, with the same role checks (`auth.Principal`). Plan phase 11 builds the per-field roles; PLAN.md says so in its "Built" note. | A foreign key to a table that does not exist cannot be made; S10-5 already moved the table to 0003. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-7 | Who administers the site server itself (creates fields, reads metrics), since ADR-P4-1's `admin` role is per field? | A new `users.server_admin` flag, set only by `python -m atalaia_server create-admin`, which enrols TOTP and creates the account only after a code from the authenticator app is typed back. A server administrator counts as `admin` on every field, creates fields (`POST /api/v1/fields`, with `GET /api/v1/fields` listing the caller's fields: the smallest API that makes per-field roles usable) and reads the metrics; only in a session that passed TOTP. There is no recovery command yet for an administrator who loses their authenticator (a database edit); a `reset-totp` command is left for later. | A field's own admin should not create other fields (hosted mode, plan phase 21); an administrator who could log in without TOTP would break ADR-P4-2's rule. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-8 | Who may grant or revoke which role? | On a field: its `admin` (and a server administrator) any role; its `gm` `player`, `squad_leader`, `vip` and `spectator`; nobody else. `admin`, `gm` and `marshal` go only to accounts with TOTP on (409 `totp_required`), count only in a session that passed the TOTP step (`sessions.mfa`, set by the TOTP login or by confirming an enrolment), and TOTP cannot be turned off while the account holds one of them (or is a server administrator). Every grant and revoke is in the audit log, with the field and role. | ADR-P4-2 makes TOTP mandatory for these roles; a GM granting marshals or GMs would let one compromised GM account spread; checking `mfa` covers a session that started before the role was granted. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-9 | `GET /api/v1/metrics` once accounts exist (S10-2)? | Server administrators with a TOTP session, as S10-2 planned; `ATALAIA_METRICS_TOKEN` stays as an optional credential for a monitoring system (which cannot log in with TOTP every 15 minutes), unset by default. Without either: 401; another account: 403 (no longer 404 when no token is set). `sessions_active` joins the counters. | A scraper needs a long-lived credential; keeping it optional and off by default leaves the admin-only default S10-2 asked for. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-10 | TOTP details? | RFC 6238 through pyotp: HMAC-SHA1, 6 digits, 30-second steps, one step either side accepted, all candidates compared in constant time; each code accepted once (`users.totp_last_step`, updated atomically, per RFC 6238 section 5.2); 160-bit secrets; enrolment needs the password, shows the secret and the `otpauth://` URI once, and is on only after a code is confirmed; turning it off needs the password and a code. The TOTP login step is a 5-minute JWT under its own key, single-use (in memory). Tested with RFC 6238 Appendix B's 18 vectors and RFC 4226 Appendix D's 10, copied from the RFCs. | What authenticator apps use by default; a replayed code (shoulder-surfed, phished) is refused. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-11 | Keys and settings? | One required `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY` (32 characters or more; plain Python settings as S10-1); the access-token key, the TOTP-step key and the Fernet key for TOTP seeds are derived from it with HKDF-SHA256 under separate labels. Changing it ends every session and makes stored TOTP seeds unreadable (their owners enrol again; administrators need a database edit until a `reset-totp` exists); no key rotation yet. Alembic reads only `ATALAIA_DATABASE_URL`, so migrating needs no secret. `docker_server.py up` makes a random key with the database password. | ADR-P4-2 names one `SECRET_KEY`; separate derived keys keep one kind of token from being read as another. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-12 | Where is rate-limit state, and whose address counts? | In memory in the one worker process (ADR-P4-2): 5 failed logins per address in 15 minutes lock that address out of logging in for 15 minutes; 5 wrong TOTP codes per account, the same; 10 registrations an hour per address (not in the ADR); 100 API requests a minute per address (health excluded); each table bounded to 100,000 keys. The address is the ASGI client's: behind Caddy (plan phase 22) uvicorn must take `X-Forwarded-For` from the proxy only (`--proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips`), or everyone shares Caddy's address. **For review:** players on a field's 4G link share one public address, so 5 failed logins there lock everyone at the field out of logging in for 15 minutes; a per-account-and-address key, or a higher limit for known field addresses, may be better. **Changed in plan phase 12 (S12-1):** the login lockout is now per (address, username) at 5 failures in 15 minutes, plus a per-address ceiling of 50 failures in 15 minutes against password spraying; the TOTP lockout per account, the registration and request limits and the 100,000-key bound per table are unchanged. | As the ADR chose; the registration limit stops scripted sign-ups; bounded tables keep a flood of addresses from growing the memory. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-13 | CSRF, with a refresh cookie? | Access tokens only in `Authorization: Bearer`, never in a cookie, so the API needs no CSRF token. The one cookie, `__Secure-atalaia_refresh`, is HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict and limited to `Path=/api/v1/auth/refresh`; that endpoint also needs the header `X-Atalaia-Refresh: 1`, which a cross-site form cannot send and a cross-site script cannot send without a CORS preflight the server never grants (no CORS). Logout uses the bearer token and clears the cookie. A native client (the app) keeps the cookie in its own cookie jar. | Defence in depth around the only credential a browser sends by itself. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-14 | Error answers and what is logged? | Errors are `{"detail": "<code>"}` (`invalid_credentials`, `password_breached`, `totp_required`, ...) for clients to translate; FastAPI's 422 answers lose their `input` and `ctx` (they would echo a password back). One answer for a wrong password and an unknown user; one 409 code for a taken username or email. The audit log never stores a password, a code, a token or the username typed in a failed login (only the account's id when it exists). Usernames are stored lower-case (a check constraint), 3-64 of `a-z 0-9 . _ -`; email is optional and unverified (the server sends no mail yet). | Data minimisation (ADR-P4-8); a password typed into the username box must not end up in a log. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S11-15 | Personal data added in this plan phase (ADR-P4-8's rule)? | Registration needs `consent: true` and stores `gdpr_consent_at` (`create-admin` sets it for the account it makes). No new personal data table: `refresh_tokens` holds hashes only; `sessions` (address, user agent) and `audit_log` (address) came with plan phase 10. Access, export, erasure and the purge of all of them (and of expired sessions with their refresh tokens) are plan phase 12. | ADR-P4-8: consent is the lawful basis for accounts; plan phase 12 builds the rights for every table at once. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 2: Target Windows, and the Field Box picker's pages (2026-10-19)

Made while building `modes/target_windows` and `game.teams()` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 2), and the second page of the Field Box's preset picker (M1-4). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M2-1 | How does the Field Box's keypad picker reach more than nine presets (M1-4), on a 3x4 or 4x4 keypad and a 320x240 screen? | Pages of nine: keys `1`-`9` pick an entry of the page shown, `0` turns to the next page (the first again after the last) and selects its first entry; `#` and `*` keep their meaning. The list screen numbers the entries 1-9 on every page, and with more than one page its header says `CHOOSE A GAME 1/2` and its footer `0 more`; with nine presets or fewer nothing changes (same data, same screens, `0` does nothing). `preset_select`'s data gains `page` and `pages` only when there are pages. The pick survives the timer screen and back. Up to 32 presets (the store's slots) make four pages. | `0` is the only key both keypads have that is free on that screen (`A`-`D` exist on the 4x4 only); the key shown next to each name is the key to press, so the box never needs two-key numbers or a scrolling cursor. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M2-2 | The rules of a window, which the plan leaves open | The target is dark for `interval_s` (default 20) after the game starts and after each window closes, then lit for `window_s` (5) in the colour of `game.teams()[rand(1, #teams)]`. A hit of at least `min_strength` (40) in the window scores `hit_points` (5) for its team and logs `target.hit` (team in the envelope, `window_open: true`); the first scoring hit closes the window at once unless `multi_hit`. A hit while dark logs `target.hit` with `window_open: false` and no team, and scores nothing. A hit with no `strength` counts as 0. The sensor is armed at `game.start` and disarmed at `game.end`; the ring is turned off between windows (`cap("ring"):off()`, the pod's command). | "Interval" as the dark time needs no rule tying it to `window_s`; closing on the first hit shows the shooter the hit counted and makes "at most one score per window" visible; logging the misses lets the GM see shots at a dark target without scoring them. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M2-3 | `game.teams()`: what exactly, and does the core's version change? | A new Lua table with the game's team names in order, on every call (a script that changes it changes nothing else), for node and field scripts alike; C tests on a node and on the controller, a simulator test through the ctypes bindings, Dart through the mode tests. The core stays 0.2.0: the version is reported in HELLO and checked against the shared vectors, and no tier or manifest asks for a core version yet. | P4R26's one line of API. Raising the version would touch the HELLO vectors and every fixture without anything reading it; a manifest's minimum core version, if ever needed, is the moment to raise it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M2-4 | Target Windows is not a Field Box preset (P4R4, P4R23): what of its screens and the checks that every mode is in the image? | Its script is not in the image and not in the preset list; the simulator's firmware checks name it as the one folder not on the box, with the reason (`NOT_ON_THE_BOX` in `simulator/tests/test_mode_pack.py`). Its two screen templates are in the image anyway, because the image carries every mode's templates (`builtin_screens.cmake`), and the host tests draw them (three reference images), as the app's goldens do. | Leaving the templates in costs about 2 KB of flash and keeps the generator simple; drawing them on the box's renderer now catches a layout that does not fit 320x240 before the addon exists (one did). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M2-5 | Does the app understand a lent `hit` sensor? | Yes, in the pod client only: an EVT `hit` of a descriptor of type `hit` becomes `hit.hit` with its `strength` (0 when missing), and `arm`/`disarm` go to the pod as commands without arguments. No phone feature offers `hit`, so an Android prop offers Target Windows only with a pod that lists a hit sensor, and no pod built so far does. | The draft RFC's amendment says a pod may lend `hit`; without the mapping a pod that lists one would make the role look playable while its hits went nowhere. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 12 (personal data)

Choices made while building the site server's personal data rights, consent records and the retention purge (2026-10-19).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S12-1 | How should failed logins be locked out, given that a field's players share one 4G/NAT address (S11-12)? | Two lockouts count every failed password check (login, and the password asked for by TOTP enrolment and removal, a profile change, a password change and erasure): 5 failures in 15 minutes for one **(address, username)** lock that username at that address for 15 minutes, and **50 failures in 15 minutes from one address**, whatever the usernames, lock the whole address for 15 minutes. A correct password clears its (address, username) count; the per-address count is never cleared early. The username in the key is the lower-cased typed name's SHA-256 cut to 128 bits, so a password typed into the username box is never held as text. The lockout audit row says which one tripped (`scope`: `username_address` or `address`). Unchanged: 5 wrong TOTP codes per account, 10 registrations an hour and 100 requests a minute per address, at most 100,000 keys per table. No per-account login lockout. | One player's typos lock only that player; the field keeps logging in. 50 failures in a quarter of an hour is far above a field's honest typos but keeps spraying from one address to a few thousand guesses a day, against Argon2id and the breached-password list. A per-account lockout would let anyone lock a named player out from anywhere. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-2 | What do access (`GET /api/v1/me/data`) and the export (`GET /api/v1/me/export`) contain? | Every table that holds personal data today: the account (not the password hash or the TOTP secret: credentials, not information about the person), roles per field with the field's name, sessions (address, user agent, times, which is the current one), the audit entries the person made (with addresses) and the entries others made about the account (a role granted: action, details and time, never who or from where), and the consent records; with `format: "atalaia-personal-data/1"`. The export is a ZIP of the same data as `export/profile.json`, `field_roles.json`, `sessions.json`, `audit_log.json`, `audit_log_about_you.json`, `consents.json` and a bilingual `README.txt`, built in memory. ADR-P4-8's `games.json`, `chrono_records.json` and `events.json` come with their tables (plan phases 13, 14, 20) under the ADR's rule; `consents` is a list of records, not ADR-P4-8's object. A bearer token is enough to read them. | The person's own data, including the security records about them; another person's identity (the GM who granted a role) is not theirs to receive. Sections for tables that do not exist yet would be empty promises. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-3 | What does rectification (`PATCH /api/v1/me`) change, and what protects the account's changes? | `PATCH /api/v1/me` changes the username and/or the email (`null` or `""` removes the email), with the registration's rules (`deleted-` names are reserved, S12-4) and one 409 for a taken name or address; `POST /api/v1/me/password` sets a new password under the password policy and ends every other session of the account; `DELETE /api/v1/me` erases. All three need the **current password**, checked under S12-1's lockouts, and erasure of an account with TOTP also needs a code (each code once). The audit log records `profile_update` with the names of the fields changed (never their values), `password_change` with the number of sessions ended, `gdpr_erasure`. | A stolen 15-minute access token must not be able to rename, lock out or erase its owner; ending the other sessions is what a person changing a leaked password expects. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-4 | Erasure (`DELETE /api/v1/me`): what exactly happens, and to the audit log? | ADR-P4-8's anonymisation: the `users` row stays as a tombstone named `deleted-` + 16 random hex characters, with no email, the password hash `DELETED` (never accepted), no TOTP, no consent time, no last login, not a server administrator; its field roles, sessions (and so their refresh tokens) and consent records are deleted; the audit rows the person made stay (pointing at the tombstone) but lose their addresses; rows others made about the account stay as they are (the other person's record); the erasure is logged as `gdpr_erasure` with no account and no address, its `resource_id` the tombstone's id. The username and email are free again. The last server administrator cannot erase their account (409 `last_server_admin`). Later plan phases unlink their own rows (adopted `player_id`s, per-game roles, replicas) in `personal_data.erase` per ADR-P4-8's amendment, keeping chrono signatures. | Rows that point at the account (the audit log now; games and signed chrono records later) keep their integrity while nothing leads to the person; addresses are personal data the security log no longer needs once the account is gone. Without a server administrator nobody could create fields or recover. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-5 | How are consents recorded? | A new `consents` table (migration `0003_personal_data`): purpose, the version of the privacy notice shown, given and withdrawn times, at most one live consent per account and purpose (a partial unique index). Registration and `create-admin` record the `account` consent with `ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION` (default `template-2026-10-19`; the operator raises it when the notice changes); `users.gdpr_consent_at` stays as ADR-P4-1 has it. Accounts from before the migration get an `account` record with the version `before-0003`. Withdrawing the account consent is erasure; the records are deleted with the account (the `gdpr_erasure` row remains). `chrono_sharing`, per field and withdrawable, comes with the chrono registry (plan phase 14). The server has no flow yet to ask existing accounts to consent again to a new notice version. | GDPR Art. 7(1): the controller must be able to show what was consented to; a timestamp alone does not say which notice. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-6 | The retention purge: what, how long, and who runs it? | Sessions 30 days after they expire or are revoked (their refresh tokens with them), the audit log after 36 months (1,095 days), server-wide because audit rows have no field (ADR-P4-8 says per field); each run is logged as `retention_purge` with its counts. The server runs it itself every day at `ATALAIA_PURGE_HOUR` (UTC, default 3, `off` to disable) in its one worker process; a failed run is logged and the next day's tries again; `python -m atalaia_server purge [--dry-run]` runs it once. Chrono records (365 days), game history (24 months) and guest registrations (30 days after the game) join with their tables, using `fields.default_retention_days`; `users.data_retention_days` is not used yet. | No deployment exists before plan phase 22, so a server that purges by itself needs no cron; the command serves a deployment that schedules it elsewhere, and a check. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-7 | Migration numbering: PLAN.md had `0003_sync` and `0004_chrono`. | This plan phase's migration is `0003_personal_data`; the controller sync becomes `0004_sync` and the chrono registry `0005_chrono` (PLAN.md updated with a dated note). | Revisions are numbered in the order they are built. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S12-8 | The privacy notice and DPA templates (`docs/templates/`): content and form. | `privacy-notice-pt.md` (pt-PT, informal "tu" like the controller's check-in notice) and `privacy-notice-en.md` cover ADR-P4-8's eight points with placeholders: the operator, the data and lawful basis per category (ADR-P4-8's, plus legitimate interest for sessions and the security log, a legal obligation for consent records; the federation number of F10/F11 left to the legal review), retention, the rights and how to use them on the server, the CNPD (its address to be checked), positions never on the site server (P4R25), check-in data on the controller only (F11). `dpa-template.md` follows GDPR Art. 28(3) with ADR-P4-8's 48-hour breach notice; English only until the legal review. All marked as templates for a lawyer's review (P4-6). **For review:** ADR-P4-8 makes consent the basis for accounts; a lawyer may prefer the performance of a contract (Art. 6(1)(b)). | ADR-P4-8 lists the contents; the project cannot give legal advice, so every file says so. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 3: geo utilities in the core (2026-10-20)

Made while building `geo.distance()` and `geo.in_zone()` (`core/src/geo.c`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 3, ADR-P4-9). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M3-1 | How do the geo functions give the same answer on the desktop, the ESP32 (64-bit Lua, software doubles) and Android (arm64, armv7, x86-64), so replays agree? | Bit for bit, by construction: `geo.c` uses only `+ - * /` and `sqrt` (IEEE 754 rounds them correctly on every target), its own sine, cosine and arc tangent (Taylor polynomials on at most 45 degrees with exact hexadecimal coefficients; above 45 degrees the complement is exact) instead of the C library's, coordinates subtracted in degrees before radians; compiled with `-ffp-contract=off` in the core's, the Field Box component's and the firmware host tests' CMake (plus `#pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF` for clang), and `#error` when `FLT_EVAL_METHOD != 0` (x87 extended precision). Tests: known answers within a tolerance from their sources, and every distance's exact bits (determinism pins) in C and through the shared library from Python. Checked once by hand: the same bits on arm64 and armv7 (gcc and clang, under QEMU) and on the ESP32 and ESP32-S3 (an ESP-IDF app in Espressif's QEMU). Lua's own `math.sin` etc. stay the C library's (modes are told to use `geo`). **For review:** a CI job running `ctest` under QEMU on arm64 would keep the cross-platform claim checked; it is not added yet. | The C libraries' `sin`, `cos` and `atan2` are not correctly rounded and differ in the last bit between glibc, musl, bionic and newlib; at a zone's edge one bit decides inside or outside, and a replay that disagrees with the box breaks the game log. Tolerances alone would not catch that; fused multiply-adds (arm64, and clang contracts by default) would change bits too. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M3-2 | What exactly do `geo.distance` and `geo.in_zone` accept, and what is on the edge? | WGS 84 degrees; latitude -90 to 90, longitude -180 to 180, Lua numbers only (a numeric string, NaN, infinity or a missing value is a script error, `script.error`, and the script runs on). Zones `{ type = "circle", lat, lon, radius_m }` (radius 0 or more, finite) and `{ type = "polygon", points = { {lat, lon}, ... } }` with 3 to 32 points; `type` is required. Distance by haversine on 6,371 km (ADR-P4-9). A point on a circle's edge, on a polygon's edge (as the arithmetic finds it: exact for vertices and for edges along a parallel or meridian) or on a vertex is inside. Polygons are tested in plain latitude and longitude (ray casting, even-odd for a self-intersecting one) and must not cross the 180th meridian; circles work across it. The C functions return NaN or false for what the script API refuses. | "On the line counts as in" is what players expect and matches the circle's `<=`; plain ray casting gives arbitrary answers on edges (Rosetta Code's sample says (0,5) is outside its square and (10,5) inside). Strict types stop a typo from becoming a point at (0, 0). Zones of a few hundred metres do not need the antimeridian; the scenario validator (plan phase 7) can reject such polygons. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M3-3 | Which known answers, with which tolerances? Does the core's version change? | `protocol/vectors/geo.json`, each value with its source: Rosetta Code's haversine task (BNA-LAX on 6,371 km, 2886.44444283798... km, 1 um); GeographicLib's GeodSolve manual (JFK-Changi on WGS 84, 15,347,628 m; the sphere within ADR-P4-9's 0.5 %); arcs along a meridian and the equator (R x angle) for 1 m and 100 m (1 um); the same point (exactly 0); antipodes (pi R; 1 um at the equator and poles, 1 m elsewhere, where haversine is ill-conditioned); Lisbon-Porto (274 km +/- 1 km, ADR-P4-9's own test); a short arc across the 180th meridian; Rosetta Code's ray-casting square, hexagon and concave "strange" polygon (the Ada solution's answers, points off the edges only); the edge cases of M3-2 marked as this decision's rule, not a reference. Generated into `core/tests/geo_vectors.h` by `tools/protogen.py` as hexadecimal doubles. The core stays 0.2.0, as in M2-3. | Values from published sources, not from the code (the plan's rule after the AES-CCM vector); the tolerances are the sources' own precision or the documented model error; exact bits are a separate, labelled check. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 13 (controller sync)

Choices made while building the controller sync on both sides (2026-10-20).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S13-1 | Where does the batch check that both sides share live, and where does it run? | `controller/atalaia_controller/sync_format.py` (AGPL, plain Python, no third-party import): the controller's client checks every event before sending, the site server checks every body it receives with the same functions; the site server imports it as it imports `chrono.py` (P4R16). Its tests (`controller/tests/test_sync_format.py`, against `protocol/vectors/sync.json`: 7 valid bodies, 30 invalid ones with code and path, 3 merges worked out by hand from the rule) run in tools/check.sh; the site server runs the same vectors through its endpoints. | One checker cannot drift from itself; the controller cannot depend on the server's package, the reverse is the established pattern. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-2 | Wire details ADR-P4-3's amendment leaves open. | Every body has `sync_version: 1`, `controller: {id, version}` (a random UUID made once per controller store, so a standby is a controller of its own) and `game: {game_id, game_seed}`; times are seconds since 1970 (numbers, the controller's own clock); at most 500 events per batch, 1 MiB per body, 1,024 bytes of JSON per event's data, 8 KiB of params; unknown fields are errors (except in a node's `info`, which is filtered); errors are `400 {"detail": "invalid_body", "error", "path"}`, `401 invalid_api_key`, `404 game_not_found`, `413 body_too_large`. Endpoints `/api/v1/sync/games`, `events`, `scores`, `nodes` (the amendment's list; marshals, replicas, chrono, check-ins, field, presets, schedule and roster come with their plan phases). | Strict bodies make "nothing private is sent" checkable; seconds since 1970 are what the controller stores. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-3 | Score counters per "node", but a controller's field script has counters too (`field-1`, `field-2`: one per run) that two controllers would both call `field-1`. | The counter source is `node-<id>` or `field-<run>@<the sending controller's node id in the game>`; the table `game_node_scores` has a `source` column (not ADR-P4-1's `node_id`), key `(game, source, team)`, values 0 to 2^31-1 merged by `GREATEST`. Only positive counters for the game's teams are sent (the controller's own merge ignores the rest). | Without the qualifier a standby's run 1 and the primary's run 1 would merge by maximum and lose points. Coordinator note (2026-10-20): totals sum the runs, which is right only while a standby's field-script run counts its own points from zero; check this in the failover field test (HW1) that no run starts from the primary's counter. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-4 | How strict is the event check? | Types by their form (a standard `a.b` name or a custom `x.club.name`, under 48 bytes), not against today's list, so a newer controller's events still reach an older site server; priorities `telemetry`, `normal`, `critical`; `data` at most two levels deep; a coordinate key anywhere in `data` (`lat`, `lon`, `x`, `y`, `position`, ...) is refused (P4R25); `score.delta` needs an integer `amount` (-1,000,000 to 1,000,000, negative allowed) and one team. The controller skips (and counts in `sync_state.skipped`) an event that fails, so it never blocks the queue. | Forward compatibility; positions must not reach the site server even by a bug in a mode. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-5 | Two controllers push the same game's metadata (primary and standby): which state wins, and does a standby that leaves end the game? | The state only moves forward (`ready` < `running` = `paused` < `ended`, last one wins between running and paused); `started_at` keeps the earliest, `ended_at` the latest, the rest is the last sent. A joined controller's `ended` counts as `running` (it may only have left); a `game.end` event, which only the controller that ends a game logs, ends it on the site server. | A standby's `_leave_standby` marks the game ended in its own store; ending the field's game on that would be wrong. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-6 | Names in migration `0004_sync` against PLAN.md. | `field_api_keys`, `controllers`, `games`, `game_events`, `game_node_scores`, `game_nodes` (PLAN.md's `nodes`, named after the game since a field-level node registry may come with plan phase 19), `user_game_roles`; child tables point at a game with a column `game` (UUID), because `games.game_id` is the radio's 16-bit id (P4R11); the controller's creation time is `games.controller_created_at` beside the site server's own `created_at`. Events keep the controller's key as the unique constraint. | Two different things called `game_id` would invite a wrong join. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-7 | Who manages API keys, and how? | The field's `admin` in a TOTP session (and a server administrator): `POST /api/v1/fields/{field}/sync-keys` (the key shown once, `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`), `GET` (fingerprints, never keys), `DELETE .../{key}` (revoked, the row kept); a third live key is refused (409 `too_many_keys`, the field's row locked so two requests cannot both pass); both in the audit log with the fingerprint. The fingerprint, on both sides, is the first 8 hex digits of SHA-256 of the key. The key is looked up by its SHA-256 and compared with `hmac.compare_digest`. No server command makes keys yet. | P4R13; a GM should not be able to connect a controller to the field's history. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-8 | The WebSocket `WS /api/v1/sync/stream`: how is it authenticated, and does the controller use it now? | The site server serves it: the API key in `Authorization: Bearer` on the upgrade (not ADR-P4-3's first message: refused before accepting, close code 4401, and the key re-checked per message so a revoked key ends the socket), messages `{"type": "games"|"events"|"scores"|"nodes", ...the POST body}` answered with `<type>.ack` or `error`, `ping`/`pong`. The controller's client uses the HTTP endpoints only in this plan phase, with the standard library's urllib: no new controller dependency (the atalaia-dev image and tools/dev/Dockerfile are unchanged); its stream client comes with the remote GM in plan phase 19, which needs the socket the other way. | ADR-P4-3 calls the stream optional; the controller's packaged websockets 10.4 has no thread-friendly client, and HTTP every 30 s is enough for the history. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-9 | The client's behaviour beyond ADR-P4-3's intervals. | Per game: metadata when it changed (SHA-256 of what was sent, in `sync_state`), events above the cursor in batches of 500 (at most 20 per game and cycle), counters and nodes when they changed; `acked_id` must be the batch's last id. A `404 game_not_found` sends the game and retries once; a `400` is logged, kept in the game's `last_error` and treated as sent, so it goes again only once it changes; `401`/`403`, network errors and `5xx` end the cycle and back off (doubling from the interval up to 5 min; `Retry-After` honoured, up to an hour); an ended game with everything acknowledged is `final` and skipped (`sync-key --resend` clears `sync_state`). A game's start, pause or end wakes the thread unless it is backing off. Redirects are refused (the key never goes elsewhere); TLS 1.2 or later against the system CAs or `[sync] ca_file`; `http://` only to loopback addresses. `GET /api/v1/controller`'s `sync` shows the URL, the key's source and fingerprint, the last success and error, failures and pending events, to the read token too. | A malformed body must never stall the queue for ever, and a refused key must not hammer the server. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-10 | Personal data in the new tables (ADR-P4-8's rule, S12-*). | `user_game_roles` joins access (`games` in `GET /api/v1/me/data`), the export (`games.json`) and erasure (deleted, not ADR-P4-8's "anonymous user" sentinel, as S12-4 did for field roles). Synced games hold no account: their events name players only by pseudonymous `player` ids (ADR-P4-8's amendment), linked to accounts only from plan phase 16, which then adds them (and ADR-P4-8's `events.json`) to access, export and erasure. The purge deletes a game (events, counters, nodes, roles by cascade) 24 months after it ended (or was made, if it never ended), server-wide and fixed until fields get a setting; `controllers` and `field_api_keys` hold no personal data (no addresses are kept). | Data minimisation; the 24 months are ADR-P4-8's game history. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-11 | Per-game roles (S11-6) and reading the synced games. | `user_game_roles` with the per-field rules (the field's admin grants any role, its gm the four player roles; admin, gm and marshal need TOTP and a TOTP session). `GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/games` for any role on the field; `GET /api/v1/games/{game}` (totals, counters, nodes) for a role on the field or the game, with `params` only for the field's admin and gm (a Bomb's arming codes travel in params: the controller sends them, they are not in ADR-P4-3's never-sent list); `GET /api/v1/games/{game}/events` for the field's admin, gm or marshal or the game's gm or marshal. The history views are plan phase 20. | The smallest API that makes the synced data and per-game roles usable and testable. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S13-12 | The site server's per-address limit (100 requests a minute, S11-12) also counts sync requests. | Kept: a cycle is about 4 requests per game with changes, and a backlog of 10,000 events is 20 batches; a refused request gets `429` with `Retry-After`, which the client honours. **For review:** a hosted server (plan phase 21) with several fields behind one address may want a separate limit per API key. | Simple until there is a real deployment to measure. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 4: Hacking Terminal (2026-10-20)

Made while building `modes/hacking_terminal` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 4, decision P4R5). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M4-1 | What is the mode called in Portuguese, and what does its screen say? | "Terminal de Hacking" (id `org.atalaia.hacking_terminal`), as the project page already says ("terminal de hacking"): what Portuguese players say is "hackear o terminal", and the English word is the one they use. The solved screen says "ACESSO CONCEDIDO" / "ACCESS GRANTED", the puzzles "DECIFRA O CÓDIGO", "CORTA OS FIOS", "REPETE A SEQUÊNCIA" (the informal "tu", as the Medic Kit's screens). "Pirateado" was not used: nobody says it on a field. Nothing names or genders a person: the texts speak of the team ("O terminal já é da tua equipa") or of the terminal. | Inclusive pt-PT: what players say, gender-neutral; the same register as the other modes. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): the mode is called "Hackeia o terminal" (pt-PT, the informal tu, as the screens speak) |
| M4-2 | The puzzles' exact rules (P4R5 names them only). | `code`: `digits` different digits (0-9, no repeats, so the counts are easy to read); a guess is `digits` digits and `#` (`*` clears; `#` too early only says how many digits the code has and is not a wrong answer); the screen shows the last three guesses with the digits in the right place and the digits in the code but elsewhere (the usual "Mastermind" counts). `wires`: wires 1..`digits` in a random order; pressing the next wire cuts it, a wrong one is a wrong answer and every wire is joined again; a wire already cut and keys that are no wire do nothing. `sequence`: `digits` keys from 1-9 (repeats allowed); any key shows them, one a second, then the player types them back, each key checked at once; `*` while typing shows them again from the start; 0 and `#` do nothing there. An unknown `puzzle` plays `code`. | Each is playable on a 3x4 keypad and a 320x240 screen with no touch, needs no texts in events, and is solvable by reasoning (code), memory (sequence) or trial under a time cost (wires). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-3 | What does a wrong answer do? | Every wrong answer locks the terminal for `lockout_s` (default 10 s, 0 to 600; 0: no lock): keys do nothing, the screen shows the seconds left (updated every second), and the puzzle stays the same, so what a team learned (the counts, the wires found) still counts. Wrong answers are not events (the plan's rule): the screen shows them. | A lock is the cost of guessing; a new puzzle after each mistake would make the code puzzle unsolvable by reasoning. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-4 | Who is the solving team? | The team that pressed its button last (`team_input.press`) since the last solve, or else the terminal's `team` parameter; with neither, nobody known: `hacking.solved` has no team, nobody scores and nothing is captured. A press is not forgotten after a while (an idle timeout was tried and dropped to keep the script within the Reduced box's memory, M4-8). | The plan's rule; "the last press" is what the screen shows (its header takes the team's colour) and what players can see. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-5 | What happens after a solve, and what may the GM do? | `points` (a new parameter, default 10, 0 to 1,000) for the team, so a box picked on its own plays a game; `reveal_code` (up to 16 bytes) on the solved screen and in the event. Without `capture` the terminal stays solved for the rest of the game. With `capture` it emits `zone.captured` (the box's owner, the team display), shows the solved screen for 10 s and then sets a new puzzle (the next from `rand()`) that its holder cannot solve ("O terminal já é da tua equipa"), so another team can take it over; the GM's owner override then works as on a zone, and is ignored without `capture`. A mode command `reset` ("New puzzle" / "Novo enigma" in the console's Boxes tab) sets a new puzzle at once, unlocked and unsolved; the holder stays. | "Capture the terminal for the solving team" (the plan) reads as a zone that can change hands; a holder solving its own terminal again would farm points. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-6 | Puzzles from the game's seed, on every platform. | Each puzzle is drawn with the scripts' `rand()` (Fisher-Yates for the code and the wires), seeded with the game's seed and the node's id, so a replay sets the same puzzles and two terminals in one game have their own. The mode tests pin the puzzles of seeds 1, 2 and 7 and two nodes; the Field Box host test runs the same script through the firmware's own runner and gets seed 2's code (8517), as the simulator and the Dart bridge do (one core). A game picked on a Field Box used the fixed seed 42, so a stand-alone terminal would set the same puzzle every game: `shell.c` now gives each such game `esp_random()`. A stand-alone game resumed after a reboot restarts its script (no script state survives a reboot yet) with the runner's seed, so it sets a new puzzle. | Replays (the game log) must agree with the box; a box on its own needs a different puzzle each game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-7 | The revealed code in the event: who sees it? | As the plan says, `hacking.solved` carries the code itself (`code`, key 10).
