| FU1-4 | What happens after a controller restart, on a takeover, and does `field.lua` see these? | After a restart (with `persist_game_key`), the seq continues, each box's link is what its last stored link event says (a box that was online has the threshold from the restart to be heard, then gets `node.offline`; one that was offline gets `node.online` at once when heard) and a `battery.low` already logged is not repeated. A standby that takes over has no such history and logs `node.online` for the boxes it hears (a duplicate of the old controller's, harmless for device health, which reads the last link event). A game's `field.lua` gets a box's own events (its `battery.low` included) but not the controller's health events. | The log should not repeat itself after a restart; scripts were written for box events, and a script that wants link state can be given it later on purpose. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU1-5 | What does the GM console show? | The *Boxes* list (already showing battery and "not heard" after 90 s) also flags a box the controller logged `node.offline` for, and marks the battery "low" ("fraca") under 20 % or with `battery.low` logged. `GET /api/v1/nodes` and the stream's node messages carry `link` (`online`, `offline`, null) and `battery_low`; a node message also carries `seen_ms_ago`, so a `node.offline` message does not reset the box's "last heard" in the page; MQTT's `nodes/{node}/health` carries both and the real last-heard time. The sync's node info is unchanged (the site server reads the events). | Small and in the place the GM already looks; no new screen. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU1-6 | How does the Field Box send `battery.low`? | In a joined game (waiting or playing), `box_game.c` reads the gauge every 10 s (`BOX_BATTERY_CHECK_MS`) through the shell's `battery_pct` (-1 without a MAX17048, as before), and below `CONFIG_ATALAIA_BATTERY_LOW_PCT` (Kconfig, default 20, 0 = never) sends one `battery.low` with `percent` from the link (event seq 0, as `node.rebooted`; the schema's telemetry priority: one packet, no ACK, not in the flash log); again only after the level has been 5 points back above the threshold (`BOX_BATTERY_HYSTERESIS`). A new game or a reboot rearms it. HELLO now carries the level at the join (it was always "unknown" on the ESP32). One small packet per crossing is nothing next to STATE every 15 s; the radio's own duty-cycle limit applies to it as to any packet. | Once per crossing and hysteresis keep a level that wobbles around the threshold from sending a packet every 10 s; telemetry is what the schema says, and the controller's backstop (FU1-3) covers a lost one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU1-7 | Does the protocol change? | No: `node.online` (19), `node.offline` (20) and `battery.low` (22) and their data fields were already in `protocol/schema/events.yaml`; nothing new on the radio or in the sync format. The shared event vectors gain three valid events (a box's `battery.low` with seq 0, the controller's `node.offline` and `node.online`), bytes worked out by hand from RFC 8949 and checked by the C codec, the Python codec and the app's FFI test. | The schema had them since phase 2; only the emitters were missing. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU1-8 | Why did `test_poster_renders_a_qr_code_with_the_poster_text` sometimes decode 4 extra characters ("…bw\nC3$B")? | `zbarimg` runs every decoder by default and, with `--raw`, prints each symbol it finds on its own line: the extra text came after a newline, so it was a second symbol, and "C3$B" is made only of Code 39 characters, so most likely a 1D decoder read a short false symbol in the QR code's modules (each run's poster QR is different: a new field CA each time). Not reproduced on demand: 400 random poster-like QR codes drawn with the console's own `qrcode.module.js` in the console's Chromium and read both ways gave no extra symbol, so the cause is inferred from the output, not observed. The browser tests now read QR codes through `read_qr()`: `zbarimg -Sdisable -Sqrcode.enable --raw -q`, only the final newline taken off, so the decoded text must still equal the expected text exactly (one symbol, nothing more; the four QR reads in the file use it). | A false 1D read is noise, not the QR code's content; turning the 1D decoders off removes it without weakening the check. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU1-9 | What is left. | Real boards (HW1-HW2): a Field Box with a MAX17048 fitted (the bench board has none), the silence threshold on a real field's radio; the console's *Boxes* list does not show `node.online`/`node.offline` history (the log does); a hardware id in HELLO (S19-9) and OTA (S19-8) stay as they were. | Outside this follow-up or needing hardware. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 4 follow-up: the site server's staff pages (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building the field staff's web pages for the APIs that had none (S19-11's "a GM page on the site server", S20-8's "a console or web page for the history and the library", plan phase 19's device health): `server/atalaia_server/web/staff_pages.py`, `staff_i18n.py`, `templates/staff/`, `static/staff/`, their tests (`server/tests/test_staff_pages.py`, `server/tests_browser/`). Tested in Docker (headless Chromium against the real API and PostgreSQL), not yet on a real deployment. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| FU2-1 | How do staff pages log in and get their data, when the API is bearer-token based and the public pages have no login and no JavaScript (S15-8, S16-9)? | Pages under `/staff/` rendered on the server (`string.Template`, every text escaped, pt-PT or English like the field pages) that hold **no data**: each loads one same-origin ES module (`/static/staff/<page>.js`) that calls the existing API with the access token in `Authorization: Bearer`, as the app does. Login is the existing `POST /api/v1/auth/login` then `/login/totp`; no endpoint, cookie or session type is added. Pages: `/staff/` (fields), `/staff/login`, and per field `history`, `games/{game}`, `scenarios`, `scenarios/new`, `scenarios/{id}`, `presets`, `devices`, `remote-gm`. | The alternative, a server-side session cookie with server-rendered data pages, would be a second way to authenticate, with its own CSRF tokens, and every page would repeat its API's role checks (and could drift from them). This reuses the audited auth path whole. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-2 | How does each page enforce its API's role rules? | By being its API: the page's HTML is the same for everyone and holds nothing of the field (a test checks a game's and a field's names never appear in it); the data and every action come from the API, which checks the roles as before (field admin or gm for the library, presets and devices; any role for the history, with its delays; `gm` in a TOTP session for the remote GM's view and commands, admin or gm for its log). The navigation hides the sections the account's roles do not reach in this session (privileged roles only after TOTP; a server admin counts as admin), as a hint only. A refusal is shown in words, naming the role and, when the session did not pass TOTP, why it does not count. | One place for the rules. Hiding links alone would be no protection, and none is relied on. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-3 | CSRF? | Nothing to forge: no request is authorised by a cookie alone. Every state-changing call (settings, scenario save and archive, presets, remote GM commands, logout) carries the bearer token, which another site can neither read nor send; the refresh cookie (HttpOnly, Secure, `SameSite=Strict`, path `/api/v1/auth/refresh`) is honoured only with the header `X-Atalaia-Refresh: 1`, which a cross-site form cannot send and a cross-site script cannot without a CORS preflight the server never grants (unchanged from ADR-P4-2). The pages' forms are `method="post"` with their fieldsets disabled until the script runs, so without it nothing is submitted (a password never lands in a URL). A test reads the scripts: their only `fetch` calls are the API client's (bearer), the login steps and the refresh. | The API's design (S11) already chose bearer tokens for this reason; the pages keep it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-4 | Where does the page keep the access token between page loads? | In the tab's `sessionStorage`, with its expiry (15 minutes, less 30 s), and the account's name for the header; a page without one (a new tab, an expired token, a 401) gets a new one from the refresh cookie. Refreshes are serialised in the page (one promise) and across the site's tabs (`navigator.locks`), because a reused refresh token ends the session (ADR-P4-2's replay rule): two tabs refreshing at once would otherwise log both out. Logout calls `POST /api/v1/auth/logout` (the session ends for every tab) and forgets the token. | Memory only would refresh, and rotate the refresh token, on every navigation, and multiply the race. `sessionStorage` is readable by a script on the origin, but so is the refresh endpoint itself; the defence is that no other script runs there (FU2-5, FU2-6). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-5 | Escaping in the pages' scripts? | One DOM helper (`el()`) sets text with `textContent` and values as attributes; an `href` must be a path on this site; nothing is ever parsed as HTML. The texts reach the script as a JSON data block (`<script type="application/json">`, with `<`, `>` and `&` escaped as in the field pages' JSON-LD). A test reads every script for `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `eval`, `new Function`, string timers and any `http(s)://` address, and a browser test shows a game named `<img src=x onerror=...>` as text, with no image made and nothing run. | XSS is the one way around FU2-3 and FU2-4. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-6 | Does the content security policy change? | No. `script-src 'self'` already allows same-origin module scripts; the pages have no inline script (the JSON block is data, which the policy does not run), no inline event handler and no `style` attribute (the chart's legend colour is set through the DOM); nothing comes from elsewhere. No nonces needed. A test checks every page's policy header is the unchanged one and that each page has exactly one script, `/static/staff/<page>.js`. | The strictest option that works. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-7 | A framework? | No: plain ES modules, no build step, about 1,500 lines in 13 files (`staff.js` is the shared client: texts, session, API, DOM helpers). The console's vendored Preact and HTM (controller/console/src/lib) were not reused. | The server's image carries no controller console files, and these pages are forms and tables; a build step or a second copy of vendored code is not worth it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-8 | The remote GM page: push or poll, and how often? | Polling `GET .../remote-gm` every 5 s while the tab is visible (stopped while hidden), the log every 30 s and after each command; about 14 requests a minute, within the 100-a-minute per-address limit a field's 4G connection shares. Buttons follow the live state (start when getting ready, pause when running, resume when paused, end when running or paused) and are off when the controller's `remote_gm` is off or there is no live game; ending asks to confirm; pause and end take an optional reason (64 characters), a message up to 100 characters, a mode command only from the live game's `mode_commands`, to one box or every box. The controller's own answer is shown (`done`, or its code and detail). Input already typed is kept across refreshes. | S19-7 chose polling for this API; push to the browser stays open in S19-11. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-9 | Do the staff pages count towards the per-address request limit? | Yes: `/staff` joins `/api/`, `/f/` and `/c/` in `LIMITED_PREFIXES`; `/static/` does not (scripts and the style sheet, revalidated with ETags). | Each page load is one page, then API calls that count anyway. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-10 | How are the pages tested in a browser, when the server's test image has no browser? | A new image, `tools/dev/server-browser/Dockerfile`: Playwright's own image and version (1.49.0, Chromium, as the console's), the server's hashed requirements, then Playwright's Python client and `pyee` without their pins (`--no-deps`; Playwright 1.49 pins greenlet 3.1.1, the server 3.5.6, and only its async API is used); the repository is mounted read-only. `python3 tools/docker_server.py browser` runs `server/tests_browser/run.sh` in it next to the same ephemeral PostgreSQL (Compose profile `browser`); a new CI job `server-browser` runs the same command. The tests serve the app with uvicorn over HTTPS with a certificate made for the test (Chromium keeps a `__Secure-` cookie only from an https:// page, as in a deployment) and Chromium told to accept it. | Playwright and Chromium are not in the server's image and must not be; the console's pattern, with the server's own packages. The two unhashed packages are test-only, like the console image's Playwright. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU2-11 | Languages and words? | Texts in `web/staff_i18n.py` (pt-PT and English, the same keys and `{names}`, checked by a test, and every text a script asks for exists). pt-PT inclusive and impersonal: roles as the app names them (`administração`, `GM`, `marshal`, `jogador/a`, `espectador/a`, `líder de squad`); the remote GM is "GM à distância" (no gendered adjective); "Nome da conta" for the username. The scenario validator's problem messages exist in English only: they are shown as they are, labelled "(em inglês)", beside their code and path. | The project's language rules; translating the validator's messages is a change of the shared compiler, not of these pages. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): "GM remoto" everywhere (the staff pages now match the project page) |
| FU2-12 | What is left for later? | Pages for the other APIs that still have none (scheduling and registrations, the registration import, roles and per-game roles, sync keys, branding and clubs, platform links, the firmware catalogue, server administration, an account's own data and TOTP enrolment, which is in the app); a field admin's view of the remote GM's live view (the API allows only `gm`, S19-7); pushing the live view (S19-11); positions (S19-7); a game page under another field's address shows that game (the timeline API is per game and its answer has no field id); the pages on a real deployment and a real phone. | Outside this follow-up's scope (the history, the library, device health and the remote GM). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 4 follow-up: hosted mode's deployment (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building the deployment S21-4 and S21-12 left for later (a hosted Compose file with on-demand or wildcard TLS, the two roles set up properly, a hosted hub's backup restored and tested): `server/docker-compose.hosted.yml`, `Caddyfile.hosted`, `hosted-roles.sh`, the `--hosted` paths of `setup.sh`, `backup.sh` and `restore.sh`, `atalaia_server/tls_ask.py`, `hosted check`, `tools/server_deploy_hosted.py` and `tests/deploy_probe_hosted.py` (`python3 tools/docker_server.py deploy-hosted`, CI's `server-deploy-hosted`). Tested in Docker on one computer, not yet on a real hosted server with a real domain; hosted mode still waits for the legal review (P4-6).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| FU4-1 | A Compose profile or an override file? | An override, `server/docker-compose.hosted.yml`, always used on top of `docker-compose.yml`: Caddy mounts `Caddyfile.hosted`; the server gets `ATALAIA_HOSTED=on`, `ATALAIA_HOSTED_DOMAIN` (= `ATALAIA_DOMAIN`), an empty public URL, the server role's URL and a command without `alembic upgrade`; two services are added (`tls-ask`, `hub`). `setup.sh --hosted` writes `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml:docker-compose.hosted.yml` into `.env`, so `docker compose` from `server/` uses both files; the scripts read `ATALAIA_HOSTED` from the env file and pass both `-f` files themselves (which take precedence over `COMPOSE_FILE`). | A profile cannot change an existing service's command, environment or mounts; one base file keeps the single-field deployment exactly as tested (its test passes unchanged) and the hub shares everything else (networks, PostgreSQL, the backups' tool). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-2 | On-demand TLS or one wildcard certificate? | On-demand TLS with Caddy's `ask` (`on_demand_tls { ask http://tls-ask:8001/check }`, the site `*.{$ATALAIA_DOMAIN}` with `tls { on_demand }`, or `tls internal { on_demand }` with `ATALAIA_TLS=internal`), validated on the pinned Caddy 2.11.7 with no warning. The wildcard certificate (DNS-01) is documented in DEPLOY.md as an alternative the operator builds (xcaddy with their DNS provider's module, pinned by them), not shipped. | DNS-01 needs a provider plugin, so a custom Caddy build per provider and an API token with DNS rights; no one provider fits every hub and such a build is not an image pinned and tested here. On-demand works with the stock pinned image and HTTP-01/TLS-ALPN-01, and Caddy's internal issuer lets the test exercise exactly the same path. Its cost (one certificate per field, the CA's per-domain weekly limit for very large hubs) is documented. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-3 | The `ask` endpoint: where, as whom, and what does it allow? | A separate container, `tls-ask` (`atalaia_server/tls_ask.py`, a dependency-free ASGI app under uvicorn on port 8001, the server's image, read-only file system), on the internal `app` and `db` networks only and never proxied by Caddy. `GET /check?domain=NAME`: 200 only for `<slug>.<ATALAIA_DOMAIN>` (case and one trailing dot ignored, one DNS label, the slug's pattern) whose slug is registered and `active`; 404 for anything else (the hub's own name, deeper names, other domains, a suspended field) and names that are no field's never reach the database; 400 for a bad query; 503 when the registry cannot be read (no certificate: the safe side); one token bucket for every check, 5 a second with bursts of 20, 429 past it before any lookup. It reads the registry through `tenancy.lookup` as `atalaia_app`. | A path in the server's app would be reachable through Caddy on every field's name and would need exemptions in the tenant middleware; its own container has neither problem. `atalaia_app` already may read the registry and nothing else of the hub; a third role only for this would add a password and a grant to keep in step for no isolation gain (the server process holds the same role). Caddy itself no longer rate-limits on-demand issuance (its `interval` and `burst` options are deprecated), so the limit lives in the endpoint. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-4 | Which roles, and how are they made without printing or logging a password? | Three: PostgreSQL's superuser `atalaia` (`POSTGRES_PASSWORD`; the image makes it) used only by the scripts inside the database's container (roles, `pg_dump`, `pg_restore`, `DROP`/`CREATE DATABASE`); `atalaia_owner` (owner of the database, the schemas, the hub's tables; the migrations and the operator's commands) and `atalaia_app` (the server and `tls-ask`), both `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOINHERIT`, passwords `ATALAIA_OWNER_DB_PASSWORD` and `ATALAIA_APP_DB_PASSWORD` (32 random bytes each in `.env`). `hosted-roles.sh` creates them if missing and sets their passwords from `.env` on every run (so a new machine or a rotated password needs no other step), then `ALTER DATABASE atalaia OWNER TO atalaia_owner`; the SQL goes to `psql` on standard input with `log_statement`, `log_min_error_statement` and `log_min_duration_statement` off for that session. The test fails if any secret appears in any output or any container's log. | S21-5's two roles, plus a bootstrap superuser that no long-running process holds. Passwords on a command line would show in the process list; in a statement log, in the container's log. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-5 | Who migrates, and when? | `hub`: the server's image with `python -m atalaia_server` as entry point and `hosted migrate` as its command, the owner role's URL, on `db` only, `restart: "no"`. The server `depends_on` it with `service_completed_successfully`, so every `up` migrates every field (and applies the grants and policies again) before the server starts, and a failed migration keeps the server down. The same service is the operator's command runner: `docker compose run --rm hub hosted create|list|suspend|...`. Each start writes one `tenant_migrate` entry per field to the hub's audit log. | The single-field image migrates as it starts; the hosted server must not (S21-7: `alembic upgrade` refuses hosted mode, and the server's role must own nothing). One service for both keeps the owner's credentials in one place. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-6 | What does `setup.sh --hosted` do, and can a deployment change mode? | It writes `.env` (no `ATALAIA_PUBLIC_URL`, `ATALAIA_HOSTED=on`, `COMPOSE_FILE`, six secrets), builds the image, starts PostgreSQL, runs `hosted-roles.sh`, `hosted init` as the owner (idempotent DDL; one `hub_init` audit entry per run) and `up -d --wait caddy server tls-ask`. A single-field `.env` is refused with `--hosted` and a hub's without it: one deployment is one mode (exit 1, the file unchanged). | Moving a field between modes is `hosted export` / `import-field` (S21-8), never a flag on a live database; a mixed `.env` would point the scripts at the wrong path. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-7 | What does a hub's backup hold? | The same encrypted `pg_dump -Fc` as a field's server (S22-3), through both Compose files: PostgreSQL's superuser reads past the row-level security, so every field's schema, the registry and the hub's audit log are in it with their owners, grants, `ENABLE`/`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and policies. The roles are cluster-wide and not in a database dump; `.env` has their passwords and `restore.sh` makes them again. | One file is the whole hub; nothing about the isolation has to be re-derived from code on restore. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-8 | How is a hub restored, and how are modes kept apart? | `restore.sh` reads the backup's table of contents (`pg_restore -l`) after opening it in full: `TABLE public atalaia_tenants` is a hub's, `TABLE public fields` a field server's; a backup of the other mode than `.env`'s, or of neither, is refused with nothing changed. A hub: `hosted-roles.sh`, stop the server and `tls-ask`, `CREATE DATABASE atalaia OWNER atalaia_owner`, `pg_restore` **without** `--no-owner` in one transaction, then `hosted check` (new: every registered field's schema present and every `field_*` schema registered, every schema and table owned by the owner role, `ENABLE` and `FORCE` and exactly one policy `atalaia_tenant` for its schema on every table, the server role no superuser, no `BYPASSRLS`, owner of nothing, exactly `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on each field table, nothing on `alembic_version` or the hub's audit log, only `SELECT` on the registry); if it fails the server stays stopped. Then `up` (`hub` migrates: an older backup is brought up to date and the grants and policies applied again) and `hosted check` again (on failure the server and `tls-ask` are stopped). A single-field restore is unchanged (`--no-owner`). | The brief's "no `--no-owner`, or re-apply grants and RLS": both, and checked, since a hub restored with the wrong owner or without `FORCE` would serve and leak. `hosted check` is also the operator's tool after any manual change. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-9 | How is it tested, and what exactly is compared? | `python3 tools/docker_server.py deploy-hosted` (`tools/server_deploy_hosted.py`; CI's `server-deploy-hosted`, which also runs ShellCheck v0.11.0 pinned by digest): the real files with `ATALAIA_TLS=internal`, the hub `hub.test`, three fields made and one suspended with `docker compose run --rm hub hosted ...`; the `ask` endpoint asked from Caddy's container; `tests/deploy_probe_hosted.py` on the public network checks each field's own certificate (SAN = that name only), page, key and WebSocket sync, and nothing of the other field, no certificate for an unknown field, the suspended one and the hub's own name, 404 `unknown_field` for an unknown `Host`, closed ports. Isolation as `atalaia_app` (`SET LOCAL ROLE` from the superuser): with each field's setting only that field's rows in every one of the 114 tables, with none nothing, refusals of `TRUNCATE`, the audit log, the registry, `NO FORCE`, `alembic_version` and `CREATE TABLE`. After `down --volumes`, a fresh hub and the restore: every table's rows by digest (the hub's audit log up to the backup's last entry, since starting the restored hub adds `tenant_migrate` entries), ownership, effective ACLs (an ACL that is the default counts as the default: `pg_dump` leaves a default ACL out, so a table's explicit owner-only ACL comes back as NULL, the same privileges), policies, row-level security and the roles' attributes and memberships, line by line; then the isolation and the probe again with a new CA. The single-field `deploy` test is unchanged and passes. | The brief's list, plus the failure modes a real restore would hit first (owner, `FORCE`, grants). `SET ROLE` from a superuser is how a test can act as the server's role without its password; it is checked by role attributes and membership that the server role cannot `SET ROLE` itself. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU4-10 | What is left? | A real hosted server with a real domain, a wildcard DNS record and public certificates (on-demand ACME over HTTP-01/TLS-ALPN-01 is untested; only Caddy's internal issuer ran); the DNS-01 wildcard alternative (documented, not built or tested); custom domains per field; restoring one field alone from a hub's backup (documented: restore on a spare machine, then `hosted export`); `hosted export` from the `hub` container to a host folder (documented, not in the test); the CA's per-domain limits for very large hubs; S21-13's per-field role; the legal review (P4-6) before hosted mode goes live. This closes S21-12's "hosted Compose file with wildcard or on-demand TLS" (on-demand) and "restoring a hosted hub's backup". | Scope; nothing here weakens the isolation. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 4 follow-up: over-the-air firmware updates (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building firmware updates for the Field Box over the field's Wi-Fi, after plan phase 19's catalogue (S19-8: "when OTA is built, the box must verify the image itself"). Format and flow: `protocol/firmware-update.md`; RFC 0001 Amendment 6 (proposed). In software and in host tests (the C update job with fakes, the shared vectors in C and Python, the controller's API, the host box end to end, the console in Chromium; every firmware variant builds with no warning); the over-the-air path has not run on a real board yet (`firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`, step 10c). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| FU3-1 | How does an update reach a box, and who decides? | The GM starts it at the controller; the controller sends the box **CMD update** (opcode 9, `{"id", "version"}`) over LoRa; the box fetches the signed release text and the image over the field Wi-Fi by HTTPS from the controller, and **decides alone**: it checks the offer, the release (exact text, Ed25519 signature with keys built into its firmware, platform, version, size), the image's own descriptors before it opens the slot, and the size and SHA-256 of everything written before it sets the boot partition (`main/fw_update.c`). Nothing of the image travels over LoRa. Any failure before activation aborts the slot's write (`esp_ota_abort`): the running image and the boot choice are never touched. | Boxes hear the controller over LoRa during and between games, but LoRa is far too slow for an image; a short command wakes the update and the safety checks do not depend on any server (S19-8). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-2 | Which Ed25519 implementation on the box? (ESP-IDF 5.3.2's mbedTLS 3.6.2 has no EdDSA: checked.) | **Monocypher 4.0.2**, vendored unmodified as an ESP-IDF component (`firmware/esp32/components/monocypher`): `monocypher.c` and its optional `monocypher-ed25519.c` (RFC 8032 Ed25519 with SHA-512, `crypto_ed25519_check`), pinned by the release tarball's SHA-512 as published on monocypher.org (the GitHub release asset is the same file) and each file's SHA-256 in the component's README; CC0-1.0 or BSD-2-Clause (REUSE.toml, both texts in `LICENSES/`). The host tests build the same files with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` and check them against RFC 8032 section 7.1's five vectors. | A small, single-file C99 library with no allocation or dependencies, audited (Cure53, 2020) and widely used in embedded code; libsodium (an ESP-IDF registry component) is much larger and brings its own build and random-number glue for one signature check; TweetNaCl-derived code is slower and unmaintained. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-3 | Where do boxes get images: the controller or the site server? | **The controller**, from a GM's upload in the console (*Boxes* tab: the `.bin` and its `.release.json`). It keeps a release only if a key of `[firmware] release_keys` signed it, the image fits a 2 MiB slot and its descriptors agree (FU3-6); at most 4, in `[firmware] dir`, mode 0600; a key removed from the setting withdraws its releases. The **site server stays a list** (no image storage): fetching images from it is deferred. | A field runs with no internet (the controller is the field's server); storing and serving images on the site server needs storage and bandwidth planning and a pull path in the sync; the catalogue already lets a GM check that a release is a real one (same fields, same keys). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-4 | When may an update go out, and what may send it? | Only at a GM's request (`POST /api/v1/firmware/{id}/install`, one box or all), only while the live game is `ready` (409 when running, paused or ended), only with HTTPS on, and only to boxes whose HELLO says the release's platform, a box's tier (full or reduced) and a lower version (per-box results otherwise: `not_newer`, `wrong_platform`, `wrong_tier`, `unknown`...). The game cannot start while an update is on its way (until a report, a HELLO or `[firmware] update_timeout_s` = 600 s); the GM can stop waiting. A box refuses CMD update while a game is played and stops a download when one starts. A mode's `field.lua` can never send `update` (the engine drops it), nor can `POST /nodes/{id}/cmd`. | "Never during a running game": refused on both sides, and the game waits for the boxes rather than starting with one rebooting. Firmware is the GM's decision, never a script's. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-5 | How does a box trust the controller it downloads from? | It pins the **field CA of its join payload**: key 13 (its SHA-256) and key 14 (the addresses), which the C core now decodes too (RFC 0001 Amendment 4's note; the `controller_link` vectors in the C tests; `atalaia_join_url_ok` is the Python rule). The HTTPS client gets a placeholder CA list and an mbedTLS verify callback that clears "not trusted" only on a certificate of the chain whose SHA-256 is key 13 (never on the server's own), so the chain must end at the field CA, which signs the server certificate for the address's name (the controller sends the CA after its certificate); no clock is needed (mbedTLS's expiry check is off by default). A join without keys 13/14 (too long for them, or HTTPS off) cannot be updated (`no_link`). | The phone already pins the field CA this way (P3-8); the join is signed with the field join key, so the fingerprint is as trustworthy as the join itself. The signature, not the channel, makes the image safe; the pinned channel keeps strangers from feeding the box garbage. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-6 | How does a box know an image was built for it? | Every Field Box image carries an **Atalaia descriptor** right after `esp_app_desc_t` (`main/fw_desc.c`, `.rodata_custom_desc`, byte 288): `ATFW`, format 1, the variant (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_VARIANT`: `box`, `box-bench` for the bench build, `box-dev` for Wokwi) and the board (`heltec_v4_3` or `heltec_v4_2`). Before it writes anything, the box requires the image's `esp_app_desc_t` version to equal the signed version, its project `atalaia_esp32`, and its variant and board to equal its own (`wrong_board`). The controller accepts only `[firmware] variants` (`["box"]`; `["box-bench"]` for a test on the desk), and the signing tool refuses an image whose descriptors are missing or disagree. | The signed platform (`esp32s3`) does not tell a V4.2 from a V4.3 (their radio front ends differ: the wrong one leaves the receiver deaf, BRING_UP) nor a bench or Wokwi build (serial keys, a public join key) from a Field Box; the descriptor is covered by the signature through the image's hash. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-7 | What is a firmware's version, and what is anti-rollback? | The firmware's version is `PROJECT_VER`: the core's (`core/include/atalaia/version.h`) or `ATALAIA_FW_VERSION` given to the build (`tools/docker_fw.py --fw-version=`), MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH with an optional pre-release, at most 31 characters (`esp_app_desc_t`); HELLO's `fw_version` now reports it (`core_version` stays the core's). A box installs only a version **strictly above** its own, ordered as Semantic Versioning 2.0.0 section 11 (`fw_version_compare` and the Python `version_key`, one set of vectors; numeric identifiers by value whatever their length). | A version check in software stops a validly signed old image (with a known bug) from being installed again; eFuse anti-rollback is FU3-8. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-8 | What confirms a new image, and what about hardware anti-rollback? | `CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ROLLBACK_ENABLE` (`sdkconfig.defaults`; a new bootloader, flashed once by USB). The new image, once its radio and storage have come up, runs `fw_update_self_test` (SHA-256 and Ed25519 known answers, at least one release key, a join round trip, a Lua node running a script) and only then marks itself valid; a failed check rolls back at once, a crash or reboot before rolls back through the bootloader. It does **not** wait for the controller's ACK of its HELLO. ESP-IDF's eFuse secure version (`CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ANTI_ROLLBACK`), with secure boot and flash encryption, is noted as the production option and **not enabled**. | Waiting for the controller would roll back a good update whenever the controller is off or out of range at that moment; a local self-test catches what would stop the box from playing or from ever updating again (no keys, broken crypto, no radio). eFuse settings are irreversible and belong with the production provisioning (BRING_UP step 0), not a software follow-up. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-9 | How does the controller hear how an update went? | The box's next HELLO (`done`, or `rolled_back` after `installed`), and a **report** the box posts over the same HTTPS link once it reached the controller: five fixed lines (game, node, release, result, version) with `X-Atalaia-Mac` = HMAC-SHA256 with the live game's key; accepted only for an update the controller sent that box. The box-facing `GET /api/v1/firmware/{id}/release` and `.../image` need no token (a release is public and every box verifies it). Update attempts are kept in memory (after a restart the boxes' HELLOs tell the truth again) and streamed to staff (`{"type": "firmware"}`). A failure before Wi-Fi (no Wi-Fi set, refused at once) shows only on the box and ends as `timeout`. | No new event type on the radio (no schema change, no app strings); the game key is what a joined box can prove; an attacker on the field network could at most send a false report for a game whose key it has. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-10 | Release keys: where, how many, and the ceremony? | Public halves only in the repository: `CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_RELEASE_KEYS` (hex, up to 4 for rotation), `[firmware] release_keys` (base64, up to 8), `ATALAIA_FIRMWARE_KEYS` (the site server's). **The default is empty**: until the maintainer makes the project's release key (`firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`), every build refuses every update, and an image without a key fails its self-test (rollback). `tools/firmware_release.py`: `keygen` (encrypted PKCS#8, mode 0600, refuses a path inside the repository, never overwrites), `sign` (checks the image's descriptors, writes `.release.json`), `verify`, `pubkey`; `docker_fw.py --release-keys=` for bench builds. | Fail closed until a real key exists; a private key in a repository or on a field machine would let anyone sign firmware for every box. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-11 | Where does a box get the field Wi-Fi? | The setup portal's new optional fields (name; password 8-64 characters or none) or `@wifi <ssid> <password>` on the serial console, stored in NVS (`wifi_ssid`, `wifi_pass`; encrypted once NVS encryption is provisioned, BRING_UP step 0); the station runs only during an update and is stopped afterwards. | The box needs no Wi-Fi in a game; the radio and the battery are better off without it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-12 | One implementation of the manifest or several? | One Python module, `controller/atalaia_controller/firmware_release.py` (manifest, versions, signatures, image descriptors, reports), which the site server's `firmware.py` now re-exports (in its shared-imports test) and `tools/firmware_release.py` imports; the C side checks the same vectors (`protocol/vectors/firmware.json`: RFC 8032's vectors copied from the RFC; signed manifests made with TEST 1's public test key; Semantic Versioning's own ordering example; this project's cases), generated into `firmware/esp32/test/firmware_vectors.h`. The core gains a streaming SHA-256 (`atalaia_sha256_init/update/final`, FIPS 180-4's two-block example cut every way in its test). `cmd.json` gains a vector with opcode 9 (bytes worked out from RFC 8949 and checked against the encoder). | P4R16 and "shared vectors for anything with more than one implementation". | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-13 | Does it fit the slot? | The Field Box image grows by about 153 KB (the Wi-Fi station, the HTTPS client, Monocypher) to 1,735,824 bytes, **17 % of the 2 MiB slot free**; bench 1,711,584, Wokwi 1,695,648; the modem and the pod are unchanged in function (no OTA: they are updated by USB). | Room is left, but the slot is now 83 % full: the next large addition should look at trimming (for example the mbedTLS features the box does not use) before the partition table. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU3-14 | What is left. | Running it on the real board (BRING_UP step 10c: the bench procedure with one Heltec V4.3, `@update` from the serial console, refusals, a power pull, a rollback); a controller and a box with a radio together; the project's real release key and its ceremony; images through the site server (FU3-3); secure boot, flash encryption and eFuse anti-rollback for production (FU3-8); updating the modem and the I/O pod (USB only for now); a rate limit on the box-facing endpoints; an update started on the serial console is reported but not followed by the controller. | Needing hardware, the maintainer, or later plan phases. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## The Field Box pin map with GNSS (2026-10-21)

Choices made while revising the Field Box pin map after the maintainer changed E19 and E21 ("plan ahead to be as future-proof as possible; future game modes will need GNSS and we already have the module with the Heltec we received"), before the TFT, keypad and expander arrive. Pin facts from Heltec's public sources: the V4 pin map (resource.heltec.cn/download/WiFi_LoRa_32_V4/Pinmap/V4_pinmap.png), the V4.2 schematic (.../Schematic/WiFi_LoRa_32_V4.2.pdf: VGNSS_Ctrl drives a P-MOSFET with a 10 kohm pull-up, so low = on; GPIO 26 is the in-package PSRAM's chip select; GPIO 1 sits on the battery divider's 100 kohm to GND; GPIO 35 drives the white LED through 330 ohm; nothing but the header on GPIO 43/44) and the WiFi LoRa 32 V4.3.1 datasheet (Rev 1.1.0, Sep. 2025, tables 2.2.1-2.2.3: 38 GNSS_RX, 39 GNSS_TX, 40 GNSS_Wakeup, 41 GNSS_PPS, 42 GNSS_RST); the wiki's V4 / V4 R8 table (VGNSS_Ctrl on GPIO 34, GNSS_RST on 42); cross-checked with Meshtastic's public `variants/esp32s3/heltec_v4/variant.h` (PIN_GPS_EN 34 active low, GPS_TX_PIN 38 towards the GPS, GPS_RX_PIN 39 towards the CPU, standby 40, PPS 41, reset 42 active low; L76K, 9600 baud). Facts only, no code. In software and in the builds; not yet on hardware. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PIN-1 | Which pins does the GNSS module keep? | All six of its connector's: GPIO 34 (VGNSS_Ctrl, active low), 38 (the ESP32's UART TX to the module's RX), 39 (the module's TX to the ESP32's RX), 40 (wake-up), 41 (PPS), 42 (reset, active low). They are now in the Kconfig header's board-internal list, so `tools/check_wiring.py` fails if anything else is wired to them (the M7 warning is gone). New Kconfig menu *GNSS*: `ATALAIA_GNSS_PWR_PIN` (34), `ATALAIA_GNSS_POWER_ON` (default **off**), `_TX_PIN` 38, `_RX_PIN` 39, `_WAKE_PIN` 40, `_PPS_PIN` 41, `_RST_PIN` 42, `_BAUD` 9600; the firmware drives GPIO 34 high (module off) at boot on the Field Box, the modem and the pod (`hal_esp_board_power_on`). No driver. | The maintainer's change of E19/E21. The connector is wired on the board, so a pin used for anything else would fight the module even when no mode uses it. Off by default: nothing reads the module and it draws current. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-2 | Where does button 2 go? (the maintainer's bench board has it on GPIO 39) | **GPIO 48** (the LED ring moves to 35, PIN-3). **The bench board must be rewired: GPIO 39 -> GPIO 48** (BRING_UP.md and BUILD.md say so in bold). Button 1 stays on GPIO 4. | GPIO 39 is the module's TX output: a button there would short it to ground when pressed, and the module would hold the line. 48 is one of the eight GPIOs no board part uses. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-3 | The ESP32-S3R2 has only 8 GPIOs free of any board part (3, 4, 6, 15, 16, 33, 47, 48) for 12 signals (display MOSI, SCK, CS, DC; LED ring; 3 I2S; 4 buttons). Which costs are cheapest? | Display **RESET and backlight to 3V3** (`ATALAIA_DISP_RST_PIN` and `_BL_PIN` = -1; the driver already sends SWRESET; a 2.8" backlight draws more than a GPIO should give anyway, M9); display **MOSI 33, SCK 47, DC 3** (unchanged), **CS on GPIO 1** (the board's battery-voltage ADC input: an output does not mind the divider's 100 kohm to GND; the firmware reads the battery from the MAX17048, so the board's own divider stays unused); **LED ring data on GPIO 35** (the board's white LED, through 330 ohm: it flickers faintly while the ring changes). `check_wiring.py` allows GPIO 1 only for display outputs and GPIO 35 only for the ring data. | Each cost is cosmetic or gives up something unused: no software backlight dimming (no GPIO left for it), no hardware panel reset, no reading of the board's own battery divider (a later fallback for boxes without a gauge would need another pin), a faint LED flicker inside the enclosure. Audio, all four buttons and the radio keep working. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-4 | Where does button 4 go? | **The PCF8574's spare pin P7** (`ATALAIA_KP_P7_BUTTON` = 4, `ATALAIA_BTN4_PIN` = -1). The keypad scan already reads the whole port each row; it now keeps P7's level (`hal_esp_keypad_p7_pressed`), and the button HAL debounces it like a GPIO. The pod reports button 4 when its expander answers. A button both on a GPIO and on P7 does not build. | No GPIO is left; P7 costs no extra I2C traffic and no new part. Reaction time is the keypad scan's (every loop on the box, every key-scan interval on the pod), well inside the 30 ms debounce. Rejected: GPIO 45 (strapping: a pull-up added against noise, as BUILD.md's troubleshooting suggests for buttons, would stop the box booting), GPIO 0 (a button held at power-on enters download mode), a second expander (a new part and a second address the keypad's address scan would have to skip). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-5 | Where does button 3 go? | **GPIO 44, UART0's RX.** When a team button is on GPIO 44, the box no longer reads console input on UART0 (`shell.c`; logs still go out on GPIO 43); the USB-C console, the only one the V4 has a socket for, keeps `@join`, `@wifi` and the bench's keys. The pod's UART0 log is read with an adapter on GPIO 43 only. | The Heltec V4 has no USB-UART bridge on 43/44 (schematic), so nobody reads UART0 input without an adapter on the header. A button keeps a GPIO (no I2C), and at reset GPIO 44 is only an input, so a press at power-on is harmless. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-6 | What about the Wokwi build? | `diagram.json` is unchanged; `sdkconfig.wokwi` keeps its display on MOSI 40, SCK 41, CS 42, backlight 47 and its ring on 48, and leaves GPIO 34 alone (`ATALAIA_GNSS_PWR_PIN=-1`). Buttons 3 and 4 stay off there. | The DevKitC-1 has no GPIO 33 on its headers and the Wokwi keypad matrix uses GPIO 1; Wokwi cannot run now (R7) to check a rewired diagram. The emulator still runs the same display and ring code. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-7 | Anything else moved? | Nothing: button 1 (GPIO 4), display DC (3), I2C (17/18), I2S (6, 15, 16), the radio and its front end, Vext. `check_wiring.py` also checks that the GNSS Kconfig pins match the connector, that BTN*n* of `ATALAIA_KP_P7_BUTTON` goes to the PCF8574's P7, and the README's pin table (a GNSS block, and "PCF8574 P7" for button 4). | Fewer rewires for what is already on the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-8 | What is left. | A GNSS driver (UART1 on 38/39 at 9600 baud, PPS on 41, power through 34) with the first GPS-based mode; measuring the module's current (BUILD.md's power budget says "verify"); on the bench: button 2 on GPIO 48, the display on the new pins, the ring on GPIO 35 and button 4 through P7 when the TFT, keypad and expander arrive; whether the white LED's flicker is visible through the enclosure. | Needs hardware or a later phase. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## The first radio transmission on the bench (2026-10-21)

Choices made after the bench board (Heltec V4.3, 0.2.0 bench firmware) joined a game and every packet ended in `TX timeout`, while an RTL-SDR on the maintainer's Raspberry Pi (dongle stx:0:28, `radio2` paused) was watching 868.0-870.6 MHz. Facts from Semtech's SX1261/2 datasheet (SetDIO3AsTCXOCtrl 0x97, GetDeviceErrors 0x17, ClearDeviceErrors 0x07, OpError bit 5 XOSC_START_ERR), RadioLib's SX126x driver and Meshtastic's public Heltec V3/V4 variants (`SX126X_DIO3_TCXO_VOLTAGE 1.8`). Verified on both bench boards (Heltec V4.3, KCT8103L), RF-6.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RF-1 | Why did no packet go out? | The Heltec V4's SX1262 is clocked by a TCXO powered from its DIO3, and the firmware never sent SetDIO3AsTCXOCtrl (the command was defined but unused). Now, right after STDBY_RC: SetDIO3AsTCXOCtrl at 1.8 V with a 5 ms start-up (320 x 15.625 us), ClearDeviceErrors, then the existing calibration; after it GetDeviceErrors, and any error refuses the radio at boot (`SX1262 device errors 0x.... after calibration`, "(TCXO did not start)" for XOSC_START_ERR) instead of timing out every packet. A fixed 1.8 V (no Kconfig): every board the firmware supports is a Heltec V3/V4. | The chip answers register reads on its RC oscillator, so it passed detection; the PLL needs the TCXO, so SetTx never finished. Fail-closed at boot beats a radio that looks alive. After the fix every packet sends, at about 103 ms of budget per 53 bytes (SF7/BW125's time on air). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RF-2 | What does a TX timeout cost the duty-cycle budget? | The packet's full time on air, as if it had been sent (`radio_limits_record` on the timeout path too). | Before, a timeout recorded nothing and the radio task retried every ~2 s; if the chip did transmit and only the DIO1 interrupt was lost, that airtime was missing from the budget. Conservative is the legal side. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RF-3 | How does a bench check see the radio work? | Kconfig `ATALAIA_RADIO_LOG_PACKETS`, off by default and on in `sdkconfig.bench`: `TX <n> bytes, <ms> ms budget left` and `RX <n> bytes, RSSI <dBm> dBm` from the radio task. BRING_UP.md steps 2 and 3 say what to look for. | BRING_UP step 3 already asked for "the RSSI of received packets (log ...)", which no log printed; successful sends were silent too, so only failures were visible. Off in a game: a busy field would flood the log. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RF-4 | The default game channel is busy. What now? | Nothing changed yet; **for the maintainer.** At the Pi, 869.525 MHz carries frequent 250 kHz bursts of 1-2 s even with our board off, strong enough to clip the dongle's 8-bit ADC at gain 30: Meshtastic's EU_868 LongFast preset uses exactly 869.525 MHz at 250 kHz. Our private sync word (0x1424) keeps their packets out of our demodulator and ours out of theirs, but the airtime is shared (collisions, and listen-before-talk if it is ever added). Options: keep 869.525 (the only 10 %/500 mW channel centre that fits 250 kHz), move the default to another 125 kHz slot inside 869.4-869.65 MHz (869.4625 or 869.5875 MHz: still g2 but overlapping LongFast's edges), or have the GM console/controller measure the channels before a game (the modem's MEASURE exists) and pick the quietest. | Measured, not assumed; the choice trades range, interference and setup steps. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RF-5 | Did the SDR measure the transmit power (E20)? | No. `rtl_power` (1 s bins) dilutes a ~100 ms packet by 10 dB or more, and in a raw IQ capture the board's packets could not be told from the Meshtastic traffic, with the dongle's antenna path faulty and its ADC clipping. E20 still needs a conducted measurement: an attenuator (30-40 dB at least: the FEM can give 27-28 dBm, an RTL-SDR takes about +10 dBm) and a power meter or a calibrated receiver on the LoRa port. | Honest about what the bench showed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RF-6 | Do two boards hear each other? | Yes (BRING_UP step 3 met): both Heltec V4.3 boards, 1-2 m apart on the desk, the 8b26124 bench build, joined to one test game (nodes 7 and 8, `max_hops` 1, no controller): in 60 s each sent 43 packets with no TX failure and received 29 and 33, at **-37 to -40 dBm** (expected -30 to -50). Each relays the other's packet once about 180 ms after receiving it (`hop_limit` 1 -> 0, as `atalaia_flood_should_relay` intends), and both resend HELLO about every 3 s with no controller answering: about 7 % of g2's 10 % duty cycle while idle. Packets lost are half-duplex overlaps (one board sending while the other's packet arrives). | Facts from the bench. Still open: a controller with the modem board (usbipd to WSL), range outdoors, E20's conducted power. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PIN-9 | Is GPIO 35 (the LED ring's data, PIN-3) usable? ESP-IDF warns "GPIO 35 is not usable, maybe conflict with others". | Yes, the warning is cosmetic: ESP-IDF 5.3.2's `esp_mspi_pin_reserve()` reserves every MSPI line up to `ESP_MSPI_IO_MAX`, which on the ESP32-S3 includes the octal-only DQS and D4-D7 (GPIO 33-37) even with quad PSRAM (`CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_QUAD`, as on the S3R2); the RMT driver only warns and goes on (`rmt_tx.c:353`). The same applies to the display's MOSI on GPIO 33. To confirm when the LED ring and the TFT arrive. | Read in ESP-IDF's source; on the S3R2 those pins are not wired to the PSRAM. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 4 follow-up: the remote ATAK relay (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building the remote ATAK relay through the site server that M9-10 deferred to plan phase 19 and S19-10 declined there (ADR-P4-7's amendment, P4R12, P4R18): `[atak] remote` on the controller's stream, `server/atalaia_server/atak_relay.py` with per-field client-certificate enrolment (`atak_pki.py`, `services/atak.py`, `api/atak.py`, the staff page `/staff/fields/<field>/atak`, `python -m atalaia_server atak`), migration `0012_atak`, the deployment's published port; FU5-13 to FU5-20 from an independent review of it. All open but FU5-14 (accepted by the maintainer, 2026-10-21).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| FU5-1 | Where does the TLS listener run, and how does the deployment reach it? | In the site server's own process: a small asyncio TLS server started with the app when `ATALAIA_ATAK_RELAY=on` (off by default: no listener, no certificate issued), on `ATALAIA_ATAK_RELAY_PORT` (8089). Caddy's pinned image has no layer-4 proxy, so the server's container publishes 8089 itself (`${ATALAIA_BIND}:${ATALAIA_ATAK_PORT:-8089}:8089`) and terminates the TLS. Docker publishes no port from an internal network, so the server also joins `atak`, a bridge network with its outbound NAT turned off (`enable_ip_masquerade: "false"`): on a Linux host packets leaving through it get no answer from the internet; Docker Desktop routes them anyway (checked), so this is not a firewall, and the server's code opens no outbound connection. The network and the published port exist even with the relay off (nothing listens then). | One process keeps the links, the pictures and the revocations in one memory, as the remote GM's links are (ADR-P4-2, S19-4); a sidecar would need a channel to the server and its own database access for every check. A TCP forwarder in front would have kept the server on internal networks but lost the devices' addresses (no PROXY protocol in the stack) and was not what the brief asked. **For review:** whether to add an egress rule (`DOCKER-USER`) to the documented install, and whether `atak` should exist only when the relay is on (a Compose override). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-2 | How do the events reach the site server? | On the sync's WebSocket the controller already opens (P4R12), with `[atak] remote = true` (needs `[atak] enabled` and the sync): the stream is then opened even with `[sync] remote_gm` off, but sends no `live` view and refuses every `command` unless `remote_gm` is on. `hello` gains `atak: true` (sent only when on, so an older site server, whose check refuses an unknown field, is never sent it while the feature is off); `hello.ack` gains `atak_relay`, true only when the controller asked and the relay is on; the controller sends `cot` messages (1-100 events) only after that, queues at most 500 events while the socket is up, drops them otherwise, and asks its emitter for the whole picture again on each new socket. A `cot` from a controller that did not ask, or while the relay is off, is dropped silently. | The brief's rule (never the site server connecting in), and the stream's existing authentication (the field's API key, the host's tenant). Dropping rather than buffering: CoT carries its own stale time, and the emitter resends everything every `interval_s`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-3 | Who holds a certificate, and who gets player positions? | A certificate issued through the API names its **holder**, an account with a role on the field (the issuer unless `holder` names another; a server administrator with no role must name one). Positions only if it was issued with `positions: true`, which is refused unless the holder has the field's `gm` or `marshal` role (as `positions.py` lets the GM and marshals see every player), and the relay checks the holder's roles again at every connection and every 30 s: a holder who loses gm/marshal stops getting positions, one with no role left (or erased) is refused and disconnected. The field's `admin` alone does not count for positions. And positions leave the controller only with `[atak] remote_players = true` as well (off by default): two switches, one per side. The operator's `atak issue` makes certificates with no holder (`issued_via = 'command'`), props and zones only; an API certificate whose holder row is gone is refused. | P4R18: "only for the roles allowed to see them", never by default; the field (controller) and the site server each keep their own say. **For review:** whether admins should count, and whether a field wants positions remote at all. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-4 | Keys, lifetimes and the field's CA? | Per field one CA (`atak_cas`), RSA 3072, 3 years, `CA:TRUE, pathlen:0`, `keyCertSign`/`cRLSign`, made on the field's first certificate; its private key stored only sealed (Fernet under an HKDF-SHA256 key of `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY` labelled `atak ca key`, the field's id sealed in, so a sealed key copied to another field's row does not open), never exported, opened in a thread only to sign. Remade (old certificates revoked `ca_rotated`) when its key no longer opens (the secret key changed) or it expires within 31 days. Device certificates RSA 2048, SHA-256, random serial, `clientAuth` only, the device's name as CN, valid 7 days by default and 30 at most (never past the CA), backdated one minute; their private keys are made on the server, go into the package once and are never stored. | Short lifetimes keep a lost device's exposure small even before it is revoked; RSA 2048 is what TAK clients take everywhere. Making the device's key on the server is what a one-step import needs; a CSR path was not built. **For review:** the 7/30-day defaults for a weekend milsim, and a CSR option. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-5 | What is in the data package, and where is the password? | A ZIP as TAK's data packages are laid out: `MANIFEST/manifest.xml` (`MissionPackageManifest` version 2, `onReceiveDelete`), `certs/atalaia.pref` (`cot_streams`: one connection `host:port:ssl`, its description; `caLocation`/`certificateLocation` under `cert/`, the trust store's password), `certs/truststore.p12` (the relay's CA as a Java trusted-certificate entry) and `certs/client.p12` (the device's key, certificate and the field's CA). Both PKCS#12 with PBES2 AES-256-CBC, PBKDF2-SHA256 200,000 rounds. The client file's password is random (20 characters), returned once beside the package and **not** in the preference file; the trust store's is a fixed `atalaia` (it guards only a public certificate). The API answers JSON with the ZIP in base64 (`no-store`); the staff page turns it into a download in the browser. | The ZIP alone then holds no usable key. **Open for HW3:** whether ATAK asks for the missing client password on import (else the password would have to go in the preference file), whether its Android key store reads AES-256 PKCS#12 (older ones wanted 3DES), and the `cert/` paths; the layout follows TAK Server's documentation and has been checked only by reading it back with `cryptography`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-6 | What certificate does the relay present? | Its own identity, the same for every field and tenant: a CA whose P-256 key is derived from `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY` (HKDF label `atak relay ca`), self-signed with RFC 6979 deterministic ECDSA and fixed dates (2026-2046), so it is the same certificate byte for byte after every restart and on every machine with the secret; it signs a leaf made at each start (a fresh P-256 key, 397 days) for the relay's name: `ATALAIA_ATAK_RELAY_HOST` or the public URL's host, or `*.DOMAIN` and `DOMAIN` in hosted mode. Every package's trust store holds that CA. Changing the secret key means every device needs a new package. | Nothing to store or back up, no SNI needed to choose a certificate (TAK clients' SNI support is unknown), and a hosted hub's fields share the hub's one TLS identity as they share its HTTPS name. Caddy's certificates live in Caddy's volume, out of the server's reach. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-7 | How is a device checked, and how does its certificate pick the field (and tenant)? | In the handshake, OpenSSL (`CERT_REQUIRED`, `VERIFY_X509_STRICT`) checks the chain against every field's CA in the context's trust store (loaded at start for every active tenant, added when a CA is made, reloaded every 15 s for one the operator's command made; it only grows, so the next step decides), the dates and `clientAuth`. Then the relay finds the CA by the certificate's authority key identifier and checks the signature again; that CA names the field and, in hosted mode, the tenant (refused if suspended), and in that tenant the certificate must be in `atak_certs` by the SHA-256 of its DER, of that field, not revoked, not expired, its holder still on the field. Anything else: closed, logged (`atak_refused`, once a minute per certificate and reason). The host name the device connected to is not used. | "The certificate's field selects the tenant", as a host name does for HTTP (tenancy.py): a CA signs only its own field's certificates, so the choice cannot be forged, and the list in the database (an allow-list, not a revocation list) is the authority, so a deleted row is refused too. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-8 | What does the relay accept from a controller and from a device? | From a controller: each event checked by the shared `sync_format.cot_event` (a narrow form: the declaration then one `event` 2.0, no `<!`, no other processing instruction or character reference, Atalaia's `atalaia.` uids, types `b-m-p-s-m` and `a-f-G-U-C` only, the emitter's detail elements only, a point in range, at most 2,048 bytes), then serialised again by the site server; a `b-m-p-s-m` placed by hand is the field's map (every device of the field), anything else is a position (allowed certificates only). The current picture is kept in memory until stale; a drop goes out and is forgotten; nothing is stored. From a device: read and thrown away (64 KiB at most without an `</event>`), never parsed as XML; only TAK's ping (`t-x-c-t`) is answered with a `t-x-c-t-r` pong. Nothing a device sends reaches a controller or another device. | The site server must not become a way to push arbitrary CoT (file transfers, chat) to staff devices even if a controller's key leaks; a parser for untrusted XML from the internet is avoided altogether. Shared vectors: 2 valid and 11 invalid stream messages. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-9 | How fast do revocation and expiry act? | Revoking through the API or the page closes the certificate's connections at once (the answer's `closed`) and refuses it from then on; an expired certificate is refused by OpenSSL in the handshake, and a connected one is closed at its expiry by a timer; every connection is checked against the database again every 30 s (a revocation by the operator's command, a role removed, a field suspended). No CRL or OCSP. | "A revoked or expired certificate is refused at once" for the paths the staff use; the operator's command runs in another process, which the 30 s check covers. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-10 | Limits? | Per address 16 connections open and 30 new a minute, 64 per field, 256 in all (amended: FU5-15, FU5-16), 10 s for the handshake, 30 s for a write, 1,000 events queued per device (a slower device is closed); 20 certificates an hour per account (each makes RSA keys, off the event loop). | An internet-facing port: a flood must not exhaust the one worker. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-11 | What is logged, and what is personal data? | Audit log: `atak_cert_issued` and `atak_cert_revoked` (who, which device, positions, expiry; `via: command` for the operator's), `atak_connect` (the holder, the certificate, positions, the device's address) and `atak_refused` (why, with the certificate's SHA-256); `GET .../atak/log` for the field's admin and gm. A holder's certificates are theirs (`atak_certificates.json` in the export; never a key); erasure revokes them (`erasure`), replaces the device's name and unlinks every account; a row goes 30 days after it expired. | ADR-P4-8's rule for every new table; addresses only in the audit log, which already has its retention. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-12 | What is left? | A real ATAK device (HW3): the package's import, the password prompt, the PKCS#12 encryption, the `cert/` paths, whether ATAK checks the relay's host name and sends SNI; a real deployment (port 8089 through a real firewall, the devices' real addresses); an egress rule for the server's `atak` network (FU5-1); a CSR option and the defaults (FU5-4); renewing a package before it expires; TAK Server federation or data packages over the TAK Server API; more than one server worker; a console screen on the controller for the remote relay's state (the API has it). Tested: `server/tests/test_atak.py` (26 after the review's fixes, FU5-13 to FU5-20), `test_atak_e2e.py` (2: the real controller through the real site server under uvicorn to devices enrolled through the API), `tests_browser` (2), the deployment tests through the published port, single-field and hosted; controller `test_atak.py`, `test_remote_gm.py`. | Outside a software-only follow-up. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-13 | Who may issue a certificate that sees player positions? (independent review, 2026-10-21) | Besides the holder's `gm` or `marshal` role (FU5-3, checked again at every connection), the **issuer** must hold `gm` or `marshal` on the field in the session that issues it (`403 positions_need_gm_or_marshal`). The field's `admin` alone, or a server administrator with no such role, can still issue certificates for any member, but without positions. | The issue's answer hands the client certificate and its password to the caller, not to the holder: before this, an admin with no gm/marshal role (or a server administrator with none) could name a marshal as holder and get every player position on their own device, which FU5-3 ("the field's admin alone does not count for positions") meant to exclude. Tests: `test_atak.EnrolmentTests.test_positions_need_an_issuer_with_gm_or_marshal`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-14 | May an admin or gm issue a certificate in another member's name at all? The audit attributes the device's connections to the named holder. (independent review, 2026-10-21) | Yes, kept: a field's admin or gm may name another member with a role on the field as holder (staff hand out tablets). The issuer is recorded in `atak_cert_issued` (its account) and now in every `atak_connect` row of the certificate too (`issued_by`, `issued_via`), so a connection is attributed to both the holder and who issued the package. | A command post's tablets are issued by the GM for the marshals using them; the holder rule keeps positions tied to the role of who uses the device, and the issuer in the connection log keeps who actually received the package visible. Tests: `test_atak.BoundsTests.test_the_issuer_is_in_the_connection_log`. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — they can issue, and the issuer is recorded in the connection audit log too |
| FU5-15 | How much may a controller (or a leaked controller key) make the relay keep? (FU5-10 amendment, independent review 2026-10-21) | Per field's picture at most **1,024 uids** (`PICTURE_MAX`; past that the least recently updated goes), each kept at most **2 hours** after it arrived (`KEEP_MAX_S` = 7,200 s, the emitter's longest stale time: 2 x `atak.interval_s`'s 3,600 s) whatever its stale time says; stale entries are dropped at every publish as well as when a device connects, and a field whose picture is empty keeps no entry. At most **120 `cot` messages a minute per field** (`Limits.atak_cot`, all its streams together; past that `429 too_many_requests` on the stream, the events dropped). | Before, the picture was pruned only when a device connected and had no cap, so unique uids or stale times far ahead grew memory without bound. 1,024 is far above a field's boxes, zone and players; 120 messages a minute is ten times what the emitter needs at its fastest (positions every 5 s, at most 5 messages of 100 events a cycle) and bounds a leaked key to 12,000 events a minute. Tests: `test_atak.BoundsTests.test_the_pictures_are_bounded`, `test_a_controllers_cot_messages_are_limited`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-16 | How are connections still in their handshake kept from starving enrolled devices? (FU5-10 amendment, independent review 2026-10-21) | Connections in their TLS handshake or not yet authorised have their own caps: **64 in all** (`MAX_PENDING`) and **4 per address** (`MAX_PENDING_PER_ADDRESS`); `MAX_CONNECTIONS` (256) now counts authorised devices only, checked at authorisation. Every per-address limit (16 open, 4 pending, 30 new a minute) takes an IPv6 address by its **/64** (an IPv4-mapped one as IPv4), an IPv4 address whole. | Before, the 256 counted handshakes too: a few dozen IPv4 addresses (or one IPv6 /64, every address a new key) holding handshakes for 10 s each kept every enrolled device out. Now held handshakes fill only their own 64, and filling it takes 16 address groups; new devices can still be held off by a distributed flood of handshakes (no layer-4 defence here: the deployment's firewall is the place for that), never the connected ones. Tests: `test_atak.BoundsTests.test_handshakes_have_their_own_cap`, `test_address_groups`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-17 | What may a controller put in a relayed event's details? (FU5-8 amendment, independent review 2026-10-21) | Only what the emitter (`atak.py`) writes, attribute by attribute: `event` only `version`, `uid`, `type`, `how`, `time`, `start`, `stale` (no more `access`, `qos`, `opex`); each detail element at most once and without text but `remarks`; `contact` only `callsign` (1-48 characters, whitespace folded); `remarks` no attribute, at most 160 characters starting `Atalaia: `; `color` only `argb` (a signed 32-bit integer); `usericon` only `iconsetpath` = `COT_MAPPING_SPOTMAP/b-m-p-s-m/<argb>`; `__group` only `name` (ATAK's 14 team colours) and `role` = `Team Member`; `status` only `battery` (0-100); a player's event only `contact`, `__group`, `remarks`. Anything else: `bad_detail` at `events[i].detail[.<element>]`. | Element names alone let a leaked key relay `<contact endpoint="attacker:4242:tcp"/>` (a TAK client may connect back to it), about 1.8 KB of arbitrary remark text, or any icon path to every staff device. Shared vectors: 8 more invalid stream messages (19 in all for the relay); a controller test checks the emitter's widest output passes and the constants are the emitter's. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-18 | What does erasure do to the relay's audit rows? (FU5-11 amendment, independent review 2026-10-21) | As to the certificate's row: in `atak_cert_issued`, `atak_cert_revoked`, `atak_connect` and `atak_refused` details, the device's name of the person's certificates is replaced (`erased`), and the account's id is taken out of `holder_id` and `issued_by` (set to null); what happened, the certificate's id and the times stay, and the addresses go as for every audit row of the person (ADR-P4-8). | The device's name (free text, often a person's name) was scrubbed from `atak_certs` but kept 36 months in the audit details, and `holder_id` in them survived the unlinking FU5-11 promised. Tests: `test_atak.BoundsTests.test_erasure_scrubs_the_log_and_closes_at_once`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-19 | Do the other revocations close connections at once too? (FU5-9 amendment, independent review 2026-10-21) | Yes: a CA's rotation (`ca_rotated`, when the field's next certificate is issued) and an account's erasure (`erasure`) close the revoked certificates' connections as soon as their transaction commits (`AtakRelay.drop_cert`), as the API's revocation does; only the operator's command (another process) still waits for the 30 s check. The 30 s check and the CA reload now run in separate tries (and the check per connection), so a failure loading one tenant's CAs never stops revocations, role removals and suspensions from reaching live connections, and one tenant's failure does not stop the others' CAs loading. | Before, those two kept connections up to 30 s, and a failing `load_cas` (one hosted tenant's schema) skipped the check forever. Tests: `test_atak.BoundsTests.test_a_ca_rotation_closes_connections_at_once`, `test_the_check_runs_when_loading_cas_fails`, `HostedRelayTests.test_one_tenants_failure_does_not_stop_the_others_cas`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| FU5-20 | What happens when two first certificates of a field are issued at once? (independent review, 2026-10-21) | The CA is made under a transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory lock on the field (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, the field id's first 8 bytes), taken only when no usable CA is found; the CA is then looked up again, so the second request uses the CA the first one committed. | Both requests used to make a CA and the second failed on `uq_atak_cas_field_id` with a 500. The lock costs nothing once the field has its CA (it is not taken then). Tests: `test_atak.EnrolmentTests.test_concurrent_first_issues_share_one_ca`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Adopting boxes (2026-10-21)

Choices made while designing the three ways a Field Box is adopted by its field (gets the field join key, which the sealed radio invites make the field's master secret: RJ decisions) and building two of them, the NFC field card and the USB cable ([protocol/adoption.md](../../protocol/adoption.md); `firmware/esp32/main/adoption.c`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_card.py`, `controller/atalaia_controller/adoption.py`, the console's *Adotar / Adopt* tab). The pairing code over LoRa is designed, not built: it lands on the re-invite work's message ids and key derivation. All open but AD-1.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AD-1 | How does a box get the field join key at a field, now that `@joinkey` and the setup portal are the only ways? | All three: a **pairing code over LoRa** (the box shows a short code, the GM types it, CPace over the radio, the key sent sealed), an **NFC field card** (its own NDEF type, written from the console, the field's master secret), and the **USB cable** from the controller (a dedicated serial command, the GM confirming which box). Cards and cable built now; the pairing code after the radio invites. | The maintainer's decision. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — all three adoption methods |
| AD-2 | Who may adopt a box, and move it to another field? | An **unadopted** box (no key stored) takes the first field that offers itself, by any way (trust on first use: whoever holds it or, with the code, sees its screen). An **adopted** box takes the same field again (its name may change: `same`) and another field's key (or this field's new key after a rotation) only with its **admin PIN**: on the keypad after the card (a `pin_entry` screen, "PIN to change field", `*` cancels, a minute without a key cancels), or as `@adopt <key> <PIN>`. Five wrong PINs lock re-adoption until the box restarts (changed by AD-28: a persistent count with waits). Otherwise only a **factory reset** (erasing the flash) frees it. | A stranger with their own card or a laptop must not take a field's boxes, nor adopt them away over the radio; the field's staff, who know the PIN, must be able to move a box. The PIN is what already guards the box's admin menu. Five per boot slows guessing a 4-digit PIN to reboots; with physical access an attacker can erase the flash anyway, which takes our key with it. **For review:** whether the lock should persist across restarts (an NVS counter), and a longer PIN for production. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-3 | Is a box running on `CONFIG_ATALAIA_DEV_JOIN_KEY` adopted? | No: it counts as unadopted, and the first field to adopt it replaces the development key in RAM and stores its own. | The development key is the Wokwi build's and the bench's, the same on every board: it identifies no field. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-4 | What is on a field card? | One NDEF MIME record of type `application/vnd.atalaia.field` (distinct from the join card's `application/vnd.atalaia.join`, so neither reader takes the other's card), payload `version (1) = 1 \| name_len (1) \| name (0-48 bytes UTF-8, no control characters, double quote or backslash) \| join_key (32) \| check (8)`, `check = HMAC-SHA256(key, "atalaia-field-card" \| version \| name_len \| name)[:8]`. 42-90 bytes: an NTAG213 holds it, NTAG215/216 as join cards.
