No NTAG password. Reasons refused: `length`, `version`, `name`, `check`. | Binary and fixed, as small as a key and a name can be, readable in a few lines of C without CBOR. The check catches a card written wrong and ties the name to the key; it cannot be a signature (whoever makes a card has the key), which is why AD-2's PIN guards adopted boxes. An NTAG password (32 bits) stops no copier and the reader would need it too. Vectors made by hand from this spec with Python's `hmac`, not by either codec: 4 valid, 18 invalid. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-5 | How do people compare which field a box belongs to? | The key's **fingerprint**: `HMAC-SHA256(key, "atalaia-field-fingerprint")[:4]` as `XXXX-XXXX`, shown by the box (ADOPTED screen, OLED, `@adopt-info`), the console (*Adopt* tab, scan) and `join-key --fingerprint`, with the field's name. | A name alone can be copied by anyone; 32 bits are enough to tell a few fields apart by eye and say nothing of the key. It identifies, it does not authenticate. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-6 | How does the controller find and adopt a box by USB, and how does the GM confirm it? | Only when the GM presses *Look for boxes*: every port with Espressif's USB-Serial-JTAG ids (303a:1001), never the modem's (`[modem] port`, after resolving `/dev/serial/by-id`), plus `[adopt] usb_ports` (`/dev/...`, `COMn`, `socket://...`, at most 8); opened without DTR/RTS, `@adopt-info` sent (again after half the 3 s timeout, for a box that reset), replies read as `ATALAIA-ADOPT {json}` among log lines. The GM picks a box by name and MAC (the chip's factory MAC, also the USB serial number); `POST /api/v1/adopt/usb` reopens the port, checks that the same MAC answers, then sends `@adopt <key> <PIN\|-> <field name>`. GM token; `[adopt] usb = false` turns it off. | No background probing of serial devices; nothing is ever written to the modem. Re-checking the MAC on the port stops a swapped cable between scan and adoption. A JSON line with a prefix survives the ESP-IDF log on the same console. The modem board answers no `@adopt-info`, so if `[modem] port` names it differently it is still only listed as silent, never adopted. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-7 | What happens to `@joinkey` and the setup portal's "field join key"? | Kept, under AD-2's rules without a PIN: they adopt an unadopted box and refuse another field's key (`join key: refused (adopted to another field: "@adopt <key> <admin PIN>")`). A behaviour change: `@joinkey` used to overwrite any key. | One set of rules, whatever the way in; the bring-up instructions still work on a new box. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-8 | How is the join key rotated after a card is lost? | `python3 -m atalaia_controller join-key --rotate`: a new random key in the store, the old key's fingerprint kept (`join_key.previous_fingerprint`), both fingerprints printed (never the key); refused when `[join] key` is set (change it there). Then restart, adopt every box again (boxes on the old key are listed as *adopted to this field's old key* and need their PIN, like a stranger's), new cards, destroy the old, give a standby controller the new key. No remote revocation. | The old key may be in the wrong hands, so it cannot authorise anything, not even the move to the new key; the PIN can. Keeping only the old fingerprint lets the console show which boxes still need it without keeping the old secret. **For review:** a rotation the boxes take over the radio when sealed with the old key and confirmed with the PIN would save walking to every box, but proves nothing against whoever holds the old key. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-9 | Can a box be adopted during a game? | No: refused (`busy`) while a field game is joined or a game is being played, by any way; a field card tapped during a game is logged (`field card: ignored during a game`) and never reaches the mode as a player tag (`nfc.tag` would hand the master secret's card to a script). | A key change mid-game would break the game's joins; a mode must never see the field's master secret. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-10 | Which PAKE for the pairing code, with which libraries? | **CPace**, `CPACE-X25519-SHA512` (draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-14). Box: Monocypher 4.0.2 (already vendored) `crypto_x25519`, `crypto_elligator_map` (fed `min(r, p - r)`, since it reads 254 bits and CPace 255; checked: it gives the draft's B.1.1 generator) and `crypto_sha512`; mbedtls HKDF and AES-CCM. Controller: `cryptography` X25519 and `hashlib`, Elligator 2 in a dozen lines of Python (checked against B.1: `g`, `Ya`, `Yb`, `K`, `ISK_IR`). Known answers from the draft's Appendix B.1, copied into the design. Not SPAKE2 (RFC 9382). | Both sides already have every primitive; SPAKE2's RFC vectors are for P-256 only and `cryptography` has no P-256 point addition, so the controller would need hand-written curve arithmetic; SPAKE2 also needs fixed M and N points. CPace's messages are 32-byte u-coordinates, small for LoRa. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-11 | The pairing code's size and limits? | 6 random digits, a new one each run and after each failure; on the screen 5 minutes, then after a key press; a failed confirmation burns the code, 3 failed runs wait 60 s, 10 stop the offer until a restart; the controller allows one run per typed code, 10 s per message, 30 s per run; explicit key confirmation both ways before the key is unsealed. An adopted box offers a code only after its admin PIN. | One online guess per run against one in a million, and few runs; a passive recording learns nothing (CPace). Short enough to read off a 2.8" screen and type. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-12 | What does a production box need to keep the key? | NVS encryption (HMAC scheme, eFuse key: BRING_UP step 0), flash encryption in release mode, secure boot v2, the JTAG part of USB-Serial-JTAG disabled by eFuse (the serial part stays for USB adoption), and the admin PIN changed from 1234 at setup. None is set in the repository's sdkconfig files: they are one-way eFuse steps for a release process. Development boards keep the key in plain NVS and say so at boot. | Without flash encryption the master secret can be read off any box's flash; without secure boot a modified image could print it; with the default PIN AD-2 protects nothing. **For review:** whether the setup portal should refuse to finish with 1234 (answered by AD-28: no default PIN, 1234 refused). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-13 | Where does adoption live in the console, and who sees it? | A tab of its own, *Adotar / Adopt*, after *Join*: the field's fingerprint, *USB cable* first, then *Field card (NFC)* with its warning and what to do when one is lost, a note on the coming pairing code, the recent records. GM token only for everything (`/api/v1/adopt*`, `no-store`); a read token sees only "only the organisers can adopt boxes". Records: the last 100 in the store (`adoption.log`), method, box, MAC, port, result, whether a PIN was given; never the key or the PIN; each also a `WARNING` log line (`atalaia.adoption`). | Adoption is set-up work, not game work: the *Join* tab is for games. The card's bytes are the field's master secret: GM only and never cached, like a join payload's game key. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-14 | What does method 1 need from the radio invite work? | Message ids (and CBOR keys) for `PAIR_OFFER`, `PAIR_START`, `PAIR_REPLY`, `PAIR_KEY`, `PAIR_DONE`; the framing of a packet outside a game (no game key, never relayed, dropped unless adoption listens); the idle boxes' channel and schedule and the duty-cycle budget for `PAIR_OFFER`; one HKDF-SHA256 helper on both sides with one table of labels; a dispatch from `field_link.c`'s idle receive to a new `adoption_pair_*()` in `adoption.c`, and a pairing service beside the controller's invite flow; ideally the invites' AES-CCM sealing for `PAIR_KEY` (80 bytes of plaintext). The RFC 0001 amendment text is in adoption.md section 7, to be numbered after the invites' amendment. | Listed so the two pieces of work meet without either editing the other's files now. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Re-inviting boxes and the start guard (2026-10-21)

Found on the bench with real boards (a Heltec V4.3 Field Box joined a controller game over LoRa through a second Heltec running the modem firmware): a GM started a new game with no box joined (the box still showed the last game's end), and every box needed a fresh join (card, QR or serial line) for every game. RJ-1 and RJ-2 are the maintainer's decisions; the rest were chosen while building them (RFC 0001 Amendment 7, proposed; [protocol/field-frames.md](../../protocol/field-frames.md)).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| RJ-1 | May a game start with no box joined? | No: Start is refused while a role the game needs has no joined node (a box or a linked Android prop), with the roles named (`409`, `start_check` in the answer and on the game). The GM can start anyway after a confirmation in the console ("Start anyway?", which sends `{"force": true}`); a forced start is logged (`game N started anyway (forced) without nodes for: ...`) and told to every console (`system` `start.forced`, an alert). A mode's minimums are warnings, never a block. `force` does not skip the firmware-update guard. | The bench found a game started with nobody in it. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — refuse Start while a needed role has no joined node; "Start anyway" after a confirmation; minimums only warn |
| RJ-2 | How does a box get into the next game without a card? | The controller invites it over LoRa: a dialog on creating (or before starting) a game lists the free boxes, the previous game's ticked; each picked box gets its join payload (made as for a card, still HMAC-signed) in an INVITE sealed with AES-128-CCM under HKDF-SHA256(join key, "atalaia field frame v1"). Only an idle box takes it (no game, or its game over). Whoever has the field join key and records the radio can read later games' keys: the join key is a field secret (protocol/field-frames.md, join.md, SECURITY.md). | Joining every box again for every game was far too tedious. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — invites over LoRa sealed with a key derived from the field join key; the join key becomes a field secret |
| RJ-3 | Which roles does a game need before it starts? | Every role of the game's main mode and of its role packages (each has `requires:` capabilities), unless a manifest marks one `needed: false` (no built-in mode does); a modifier brings none. A scenario's game: the roles of the boxes its manifest names (`nodes`), or, with none named, as any game. A node has joined when the controller has heard it (its HELLO first; boxes and props over Wi-Fi alike) or stored it as heard before a restart; a pre-assignment alone does not count. Warnings: an integer parameter `min_<role>s` above the joined count of that role (Hold-All's and Last Stand's `min_zones`, Master and Satellites' `min_satellites`), and `last_<role>s` at or above it (Last Stand: the countdown would run from the start). | The manifests already say which capabilities a role requires; every built-in mode's roles are its reason to exist (Master and Satellites without satellites, a Medic Kit game without kits); a scenario names its boxes. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-4 | Does the remote GM (site server) get "Start anyway"? | No: its `start` is refused like the console's without force (`conflict`, the roles named); the sync's command format is unchanged. | The remote GM sees the live view, not the console's confirmation; widening the shared command format is the site server's change to make. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-5 | One message type or several, and which header? | One type, `FIELD` (9), with the kind in the body (ANNOUNCE 1, DISCOVER 2, INVITE 3); `game_id` 0 (game ids are 1-65534), `source` and `seq` 0, `hop_limit` 0 (never relayed); body at most 187 bytes after a 13-byte nonce, so a frame is at most 217 bytes like any packet. | One type keeps the schema's message table small and lets the modem pass every field frame up on its shape alone. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-6 | What identifies a box across games? | An 8-byte device id, random, made at the first start with a join key and kept in NVS (`device_id`), shown as 16 hex digits; in HELLO as the optional key 10 and in every ANNOUNCE. Not derived from the ESP32's MAC address. | The node id changes with every game. A random id cannot be linked to the board's Wi-Fi MAC address (which it shows when it joins Wi-Fi for updates), works the same for an Android prop, and goes with an NVS erase. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-7 | Nonces under a key that lives as long as the field? | Counter-based, never random (amended by RJ-15): `HMAC-SHA256(K_n, sender id)[0..7] \| boot (3) \| ctr (2)` in the clear after the header, `K_n` from the join key (HKDF, `atalaia field nonce v1`); AES-128-CCM with an 8-byte MIC, AAD the header without `hop_limit`. Sender ids: a box's device id (its boot count in NVS); a controller's fresh from `os.urandom` at every start, never stored (independent review 2026-10-21: a stored one could roll back with a restored backup or a copied database), its boot count only in that run. A box's NVS must not be cloned to another board. | A stored boot count and a frame count make one sender's nonces unique, distinct sender ids make senders' differ; no RNG quality is assumed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-8 | How is an old invite kept from pulling a box into an ended game? | Every announcement carries the box's token (8 random bytes, new at every start and every 10 minutes); both tokens are dropped whenever the box answers an invite (whatever the result), joins a game in any way (card, QR, serial, invite) and when its game ends (independent review 2026-10-21: a refused invite stayed valid for up to 20 minutes); an INVITE must carry the current or the previous one, or it is refused without an answer. The controller refuses an announcement that is not newer than the last it heard from that box (its nonce's boot and count), and forgets a game's invites when it ends. A box also refuses a join for any of the last four games it played to their end (answer "ended"). | A token works like a challenge without a clock: a recorded invite played back never matches again; the ended-games list covers a valid token with a stale game. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-9 | A join payload is up to 448 bytes; the modem's payload at most 200. | Split into 1-3 INVITE parts of up to 153 bytes (the other fields take at most 34 of the 187), put back together by part number on the box; a part of another invite, token or part count starts over. Nothing is left out of the payload (a box joined by invite keeps its field CA and addresses for firmware updates). A typical box's join (115-300 bytes) is one or two parts; "too big" stays as a console status for a payload that cannot fit, which the join's own limit rules out. | Leaving out the optional fields would make boxes joined by invite unable to update their firmware; refusing would refuse common games (parameters, HTTPS). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-10 | What does the USB modem do with field frames? | It carries them as they are and never holds the field key: FIELD_TX (0x09, id + frame), FIELD_RX (0x0A, frame + RSSI + SNR) and FIELD_ACK (0x0B, id + status), with or without a game configured; it checks only the shape, forces `hop_limit` 0, counts the duty cycle as for any packet, answers `busy` (new status 8) during a MEASURE; IDENT says `"field": true`. Its radio tag sets bit 31, so a FIELD_ACK never mixes with a TX_ACK. The I/O pod does not pass field frames to its phone. | The join key stays on the controller; the modem is configured per game and must carry field frames before the next game is configured, and between games. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-11 | Timings and the duty cycle? | A free box: its first announcement 2-20 s after its game ends (or after it starts), then every max(60 s, time on air / (2 % x the sub-band's duty cycle)) ±10 % (about 72 s on 869.4-869.65 MHz, 12 minutes on a 1 % sub-band, 2 hours on 0.1 %), a DISCOVER answered after 0-3 s unless it announced in the last quarter of that interval, an invite answered after 100-600 ms (once per invite in 10 s, the last eight invites remembered, at most six answers a minute in all). The controller: one DISCOVER per 10 s at most, invites one at a time paced by their time on air, resent after 6 s with the box's latest token, three attempts. Numbers: an 80-byte announcement is 144 ms on air, so 7.2 s an hour (2 % of 360 s) on 869.525 MHz; a two-part invite about 0.55 s, a three-part one 1.03 s; twenty boxes with two parts take about 11 s of the controller's 360 s an hour (3 %), 30 % of a 1 % sub-band's. | A box between games should leave its budget for its next game; the GM should not wait long. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-12 | What does a box show between games? | When it joins from an invite: "Invited", "game: role" (TFT and OLED). 30 s after its game ends, the final screen gives way to "Ready", "next game", unless the preset picker was opened meanwhile; new screen references `status_invited.png` and `status_ready.png` (TFT and OLED). `@lobby` on the console prints the device id, whether it is free, its channel and its invites. | The final screen left on for good was what made the bench look stuck; short words fit the OLED's head line. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-13 | The console's dialog? | Opens after a game is created when free boxes have been heard (else the "Invite boxes" button, also there until Start); the previous game is the most recent other game whose HELLOs carried device ids; its boxes are ticked unless they are already in this game; the role per box defaults to its previous role when the game has it (a scenario's game: one of its box names with that role); each row shows the box's progress (sending, invited, accepted, joined, refused with the box's reason, failed). Invites live in the controller's memory only. | What the maintainer asked for; a restart in the middle of inviting only loses the progress shown (the boxes' HELLOs still count). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-14 | What is left out? | Android props are not invited over the radio (they link over Wi-Fi; they count for the start guard; the app's HELLO codec reads and writes key 10). A box listens on its last game's channel: after the field's channel changes, its next game needs a card once. Field frames are not relayed: a box out of the modem's direct range is not heard between games. | Kept small; each can come later (a Wi-Fi invite for props, a channel hint in the announcement, a relay rule). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| RJ-15 | Is the ESP32-S3's `esp_fill_random` good enough for the nonces, the tokens and the device id? (coordinator, 2026-10-21: must be fixed) | No, so (1) **nonces no longer use any RNG**: RJ-7's layout, the box's boot count in NVS (`ff_boot`), raised and stored before the first frame of a run and when the frame count would pass 65535 (only "none stored yet" starts at 0; any other read error or a failed write stops its field frames); the controller's sender id fresh at every start, never stored; (2) **tokens and device ids** come from a seeded generator: `app_main` first collects 32 bytes with `bootloader_random_enable()` / `esp_fill_random` / `bootloader_random_disable()`, before any ADC or I2S driver starts, and each request is SHA-256 of that seed, the board's MAC, a counter and fresh `esp_fill_random` bytes. Shared vectors gained the nonce rule (`nonces` in `field_frame.json`), checked by C and Python; host tests check a box's nonces across a reboot, the wrap and a store that fails. | ESP-IDF calls the RNG truly random only while Wi-Fi or Bluetooth runs or between those calls; a nonce repeated under the field key breaks CCM. Still to check on the bench: the boot log's entropy step before the audio and battery drivers. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Pairing a box by its code (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building the third adoption method (AD-1), the pairing code over the radio, on the re-invite work's field frames (RJ-1 to RJ-15) as AD-14 asked; AD-10 (CPace) and AD-11 (the code's limits) are kept and made concrete here. RFC 0001 Amendment 8 (proposed); [protocol/adoption.md](../../protocol/adoption.md) section 7. All open but AD-28, the maintainer's decision on the admin PIN (2026-10-22), built as AD-29 to AD-32 (`main/admin_pin.c`).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AD-15 | How do the pairing messages travel? | **Message type 10, `PAIR`**, with field frames' header rules (game id 0, source 0, seq 0, `hop_limit` 0, never relayed) but **in the clear** (`version_flags` 0x28): header and a CBOR body of 1-200 bytes, five kinds (OFFER, START, REPLY, KEY, DONE). Not kinds 4-7 of type 9, which are sealed under the field key the box does not have. The USB modem carries type 10 in FIELD_TX / FIELD_RX with a shape check of its own (version 1, not encrypted, game 0, 10-209 bytes) and says `"pair": true` in IDENT; the controller tells the two types apart before anything else. | What the re-invite work's author suggested; the modem, the controller's receive path and a box's both stay one path for "frames outside a game". In the clear is honest: their security is CPace's, not a layer that would need a key. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-16 | Which boxes show a code, and when? | An **unadopted** box with a radio, at start (once the picker is up, over it: `*` gives the picker back, `#` a new code) and on `@pair`; not during the setup portal, not while a game is joined or played. **Not an adopted box**: it moves to another field by card or cable with its admin PIN (AD-2); "adopt to another field" by code from the admin menu is left for later. A box without a screen still logs its code on the serial console. | Showing the code is the box saying "anyone who can see me may adopt me": right for a new box in the GM's hands, wrong for a field's box on the field. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-17 | Who encrypts what, and with which nonces? | **Only the controller encrypts**: KEY's join key and field name, AES-128-CCM (8-byte MIC, AAD `"atalaia pair key v1"` \| pair id \| sid) under a key from the run's ISK, with field-frames.md's nonce rule: `HMAC(nonce key from the ISK, sender id)[0..7] \| boot 1 \| count 0`, the sender id drawn with `os.urandom` for every run and never stored. The box only computes HMAC tags (REPLY's and DONE's), so it needs no boot count in NVS for pairing. | The coordinator's hardening rule (RJ-15): no nonce from an RNG. The key is new to every run anyway (fresh scalars on both sides), so even a repeated nonce could not meet the same key; the rule is kept so nothing depends on that. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-18 | Which channel does an unadopted box listen on, and how does the controller reach it? | The box: `CONFIG_ATALAIA_RADIO_DEFAULT_CHANNEL_HZ`, **869.525 MHz** by default (the radio's channel until a game sets one), logged at start. The controller: *Look for boxes on the radio* keeps the modem on 869.525 MHz for five minutes: when the field's channel is another and no game is live, it tunes the modem there and back afterwards (or as soon as a game is live); with a game live on another channel it refuses (409) until the game ends. | A box that never joined a game knows no other channel. Retuning during a live game would deafen the controller to that game's boxes. **For review:** a field that never uses 869.525 MHz still pairs there (it is the 10 % sub-band, the cheapest for the offers). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-19 | What bounds the box? | The code shows 5 minutes; offers every max(15 s, time on air / (5 % x the sub-band's duty cycle)) ± 10 %, only while the code shows and not during a run or a wait (about 20 frames, 0.5 % of the hour's budget on 869.525 MHz); one run per START's sid, the last eight sids never run again; REPLY at most twice per sid, DONE at most twice again for a repeated KEY within 10 s; a run waits 10 s for KEY; every failed run (a low-order point, or no KEY with the controller's confirmation in time) burns the code; a KEY that does not authenticate is ignored, not a failure (AD-25); three in a row: a minute without runs or offers, said on the screen; ten: no pairing until a restart, said on the screen until a key; a run's scalar, ISK and keys wiped as soon as unused and on every outcome. | AD-11's limits, made exact: one guess per run (10^-6), at most three a minute and ten per start, and an attacker without the code can make the box send at most two answers per run. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-20 | What does the controller keep and allow? | One run at a time, GM token only (`GET /api/v1/adopt/pair`, `POST .../pair/listen`, `POST .../pair {pair_id, code}`); START and KEY each sent at most three times, 3 s apart, a step failing after 10 s (`no_answer`, `no_done`); the key is sent only after the box's confirmation matches (`wrong_code` otherwise: nothing more goes); DONE accepted only with its tag and, when the box says adopted, this field's fingerprint (`fingerprint` otherwise); offers forgotten a minute after the last; frames of runs that are over ignored. Every outcome logged and recorded with the other adoptions (method `radio`, the box's name and pair id; never the code, the key or the run's secrets). The console polls once a second while its pairing card is open (5 minutes at most). | The join key, the field's master secret, leaves the controller only for a box that proved it shows the code the GM typed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-21 | The exchange's parameters? | CPace `CPACE-X25519-SHA512`, initiator the controller: `PRS` the 6 ASCII digits, `CI` = `lv_cat("atalaia pair v1", pair id)`, `sid` 16 bytes from the controller, `ADa` = "atalaia controller", `ADb` = "atalaia box"; from the ISK, HKDF-SHA256 with the sid as salt and the labels `atalaia pair confirm a v1`, `... confirm b v1`, `... seal v1`, `... nonce v1`; 16-byte tags, HMAC-SHA256 over fixed labels (DONE's also over its result and fingerprint). Monocypher's Elligator 2 gets `min(r, p - r)` (AD-10). | Explicit key confirmation both ways before anything secret moves; the pair id in CI binds the run to the box that offered it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-22 | What does a box say about itself on the air before it is adopted? | A **pair id** (8 random bytes, new at every pairing: neither its MAC nor its later device id), its name and tier, in the clear. | Enough for the GM to pick it in the console; nothing that follows the box afterwards. The name is whatever the box was given (`node_name`), visible to anyone listening while the code shows. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-23 | How are the two sides checked? | `protocol/vectors/pairing.json` (`tools/pairing_vectors.py`, `--check` in check.sh and CI): the CPace draft's B.1 and B.1.10 **copied from the draft** (the tool refuses to write if `atalaia_sim.cpace` disagrees), a whole run with fixed scalars, sid and pair id (keys, tags, the sealed key, the five frames, a wrong code's answer), six generators found by a fixed search, four of them hashing to an r of 2^254 or more (the branch where Monocypher gets p - r; without it the box's generator differs, checked), and 19 bodies and 6 frames to refuse. C: `core/tests/test_pair_frame.c` (with mbedtls's AES-CCM: the sealed bytes) and `firmware/esp32/test/test_box_pair.c` (Monocypher against the draft; the run played against the box, its REPLY and DONE compared byte for byte; KEY re-sealed with the core's test stub because the firmware's host tests, like CI's firmware job, have no mbedtls). Python: `simulator/tests/test_pair_frame.py`. End to end: the C host box through the C host modem (`controller/tests/test_pairing.py`, a browser test). | Known answers from the source document for the primitive; this project's own cases for its framing, cross-checked by two implementations. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-24 | What is left? | On the boards (BRING_UP step 10a.5): the offers heard by the modem, a wrong code then the right one, range, the OLED's code legible; the bench log's entropy step (RJ-15) before the first code is drawn; "adopt to another field" by code on an adopted box (AD-16); the app's Android props are not paired this way (they link over Wi-Fi). | Outside the software. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-25 | Can a radio attacker without the code end a real run, or silently lock a box? (independent review 2026-10-21, finding 1, medium) | No longer the first; the second is now said. A **KEY whose confirmation does not match, or whose sealed key does not open, is ignored and counted**, and the run keeps waiting for the controller's own KEY until its 10 s run out; before, any KEY with the sniffed sid ended the run. A START while a run is on is still refused, not a replacement; the console's "no answer" now says that another pairing may be under way with the box (interference) and to try again. After three failed runs the screen says **PAIR PAUSED, for 1 minute** (no code shown); after ten **PAIR LOCKED, restart the box** until a key, instead of going back to the picker with only a log line. A START that got a REPLY still burns the code (its tag is one guess). adoption.md's threat model now says that a radio attacker can always deny pairing (jamming, taking the run, the pauses and the lock) and never adopt the box. | The run slot and the code's limits are what bound the guesses, so they stay; what an unauthenticated frame may no longer do is end a run it could not complete. Tests: test_box_pair.c (a forged KEY and a tampered sealed key mid-run, then the real KEY adopts; the wrong code fails only at the timeout; the paused and locked screens). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-26 | Can a fake box get extra guesses by offering under a real box's name? (independent review, finding 2, low) | Made visible: the box's pairing screen shows **Id: the pair id's first two bytes**, and the console shows each offer's short id, RSSI and how long ago it was heard; two offers under one name are flagged, and an OFFER reusing a known pair id under another name is ignored (the first name stays) and flags the entry; after a wrong code the console says to read the box's screen again (a real box shows a new code after any failed run; if it did not change, that entry was not the box). | A fake entry can still take the GM's guesses, one per run, but never the key, and the GM now has what it takes to tell the entries apart. Tests: test_pairing.py (duplicate names, a reused pair id), the browser test (the id beside the entry is the box's). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-27 | Can the console say "listening on 869.525 MHz" while the modem is elsewhere? (independent review, finding 3, low) | No: whenever the engine tunes the modem to the field's channel (the channel set, a scan starting or ending, the modem reconnecting), pairing clears its listening window, and a run under way fails as `channel_changed` unless the field's channel is 869.525 MHz itself. | The modem has one channel; the console must not claim what the radio is not doing. Tests: test_pairing.py (each path). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-28 | The admin PIN: how are guesses slowed, and may a box keep 1234? | **Maintainer (2026-10-22).** (a) Wrong admin PINs are counted in NVS and survive a restart; after five in a row each further try waits 1, 2, 4, 8 ... minutes, at most 60; a right PIN sets the count back; the wait is on the box ("PIN locked · try again in N min", in the box's language) and survives a restart; every place the PIN is asked uses it. (b) No default admin PIN on a Field Box: set at the first setup, 1234 and other trivial PINs refused with a clear message; bench builds (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_SKIP_SETUP`) keep 1234 and say so in the log. Built as AD-29 to AD-32 (`main/admin_pin.c`). | The maintainer's decision; AD-2's "five per boot" let a reboot reset the guesses. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted — a persistent failure count with waits of 1, 2, 4 ... 60 minutes after five wrong PINs, and no default admin PIN on a Field Box (bench builds keep 1234) |
| AD-29 | How does the wait count across a restart, with no wall clock? | The count of wrong PINs in a row is in NVS (`pin_fails`); at boot a count of five or more **starts its whole wait again**, never shorter (a box switched off for an hour still waits the full time after). The count is raised and stored **before** a PIN is compared, so cutting the power after a wrong guess does not undo it; a count that cannot be stored refuses the try; one that cannot be read waits the longest (60 min). During a wait a PIN is refused without being compared or counted. One check (`admin_pin_t`) for the game picker and for adoption by card and by `@adopt`; pairing never asks (an adopted box offers no code, AD-16) and the setup portal runs only on a box with no setup. The waits: 1 min after the fifth wrong PIN, then 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 60, 60 ... | Restarting cannot shorten a wait or win back a guess; trying all 10,000 four-digit PINs takes over a year. Tests: test_admin_pin.c (the five free tries, every wait to the cap, a correct PIN refused during a wait, a wrong guess then a reboot, four before and one after a reboot, a right PIN resetting it, a failing store, an unreadable count; the picker and adoption sharing one count; `adopt-info` saying `locked_min`). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-30 | Which PINs are too easy? | 4-8 digits, and not: one digit repeated (0000, 1111), a sequence one up or one down with wrap-around (1234, 0123, 4321, 9876, 7890, 12345678), two digits in turn (1212, 252525). Refused by the setup portal ("Choose another admin PIN: 1234, 0000, a sequence or a repeated pair is too easy to guess"), by `@admin-pin`, and a stored one counts as none on a Field Box. The portal's field allows 4-8 digits (it said 4-6 with 1234 as its placeholder). | The patterns people pick first, simple to explain on a form; not a dictionary of common PINs (2580 is allowed). Tests: test_admin_pin.c, test_wifi_setup.c. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-31 | A box set up before, with no PIN or a trivial one: how does it get one? | It has none (AD-28 b): the picker shows "No admin PIN set · USB console: @admin-pin", adoption to another field answers `no_pin` (the console says to set one), and the boot log says why. **`@admin-pin <PIN>`** on the USB (or UART) console sets one while none is set (like the setup portal: whoever holds the box); `@admin-pin <PIN> <new PIN>` changes it, the old PIN checked and counted as any other. `@adopt-info` also says `locked_min` and `pin_set`. Bench and Wokwi builds use 1234 when none is stored and log it. | Re-running the setup portal would rewrite every setting and take a box out of a stored game; the console is already how a box is adopted by cable. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| AD-32 | The wording of the new screens? | TFT: "PIN locked" / "Try again in N min", "PIN bloqueado" / "Tente daqui a N min"; "No admin PIN set" / "USB console: @admin-pin", "Sem PIN de administração" / "Consola USB: @admin-pin". OLED (16 characters, under ADMIN PIN / PIN ADMIN): "LOCKED" / "again in N min", "BLOQUEADO" / "daqui a N min"; "NO PIN" / "SEM PIN" over "@admin-pin". | **Tenta**, not the brief's "tente": the box speaks to the GM with *tu* everywhere else ("Mantém premido", "Tenta outra vez"). References `pin_entry_locked(_pt)`, `pin_entry_no_pin(_pt)`, TFT and OLED. | maintainer (2026-10-22): "Tente" (not "Tenta") for the PIN lock's wait; the rest as built |

## The re-invites on the bench boards (2026-10-22)

Choices made while running the re-invites (RJ) and the start guard on the two Heltec V4.3 bench boards, a controller in Docker and board 2 as its modem. Before these fixes, an invite never completed on real radios, while every software test had passed. All open but BN-14, whose wording the maintainer settled on 2026-10-22 (BN-22).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BN-1 | Why did invites time out on the boards? | Each `recv()` call set packet params, cleared IRQs and started a single 50 ms RX, then waited 100 ms for RX_DONE; a packet longer than that (an invite part of 217 bytes is ~330 ms at SF7) was abandoned and the next call restarted RX over it. Now the SX1262 stays in continuous RX (`sx_start_rx()` once, re-armed only after TX, CAD, standby/sleep or a channel change, tracked by `rx_armed`), and `recv()` waits up to 50 ms for a finished packet, clears the IRQ at once and leaves the chip listening. CRC and header errors are dropped (they were passed up before; the MIC still protected the content). `send()` goes through standby and drops a stale DIO1 edge before SetTx, so an RX_DONE cannot pass for TX_DONE. | Found on the bench: the box logged one 217-byte part in six, and none of the second parts. The same deaf gap explains the earlier lost STATE packets and a box left in STANDBY after Start (a START right before its TIME beacon). Applies to the box, the modem and the pod (one HAL). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-2 | Why did the second part never arrive even then? | The controller queued all of an invite's parts in one tick and the modem sent them back to back. The inviter now sends one part per tick, the next one `PART_GAP_MS` (150 ms) after the previous part's time on air; an invite counts as "invited" when its first part is out. | A receiver needs time after each packet before it hears the next preamble (~12 ms at SF7); with BN-1 the gap is smaller, but pacing costs nothing at this rate. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-3 | How fast does a box see Start now? | Not measured yet: the next bench game times it. Expected well under a second when the START is heard (its TIME beacon follows at once). | Before BN-1 the START was often lost and the box waited for a retry or the next beacon (the maintainer pressed the button while it still showed STANDBY). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-4 | Why did the box crash-loop after a reflash mid-game? | `box_game_resume()` kept the stored join's buffer (448 B), the decoded join (~750 B) and the saved state (~1.1 KB, with the mode's 1 KB snapshot) on the main task's 8 KB stack, then ran the mode's restore: a stack overflow on every boot with a saved running game. They are now static (resume runs once, at boot, on one task). | The first boot with a saved running game on the boards since the lobby and adoption were added. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-5 | A box that reboots more than 16 times in one game can no longer be heard: its epoch is past the receivers' window (RFC 0001: try the current epoch and the next; the modem tries 16). What should resync it? | **For the maintainer**; answered by BN-8 (2026-10-22, proposed: HELLO and TIME searched 256 epochs up). Seen after BN-4's crash loop (35 reboots: epoch 91 to 126): the modem counted 57 authentication failures and the game lost the box. Options: an unauthenticated-epoch hint in the header for HELLO (with a bounded search), a field-frame resync (the box asks, sealed under the field key, and the controller answers with the epoch it will accept), or accept that a new game is needed after a crash loop. | Rare (a crash loop), but the box then silently drops out of the game. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-6 | What does a free box show after a restart? | **Follow-up** (done in BN-10): today the box shows its admin-PIN lock (the picker) until its first game ends, and only then "Ready / next game". An adopted, free box should show "Ready" from boot too, so a GM does not take the PIN screen for a box that cannot be invited (the maintainer was misled by it on the bench). | UX found on the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-7 | A box that has booted or joined more than ~16 times since it was flashed is never heard in a new game (bench: the modem counted 863 authentication failures for a box at epoch ~127 in a fresh game). How should the nonce epoch work? | **Per game key** (RFC 0001 Amendment 9, proposed). At every join and resume the box takes that key's last epoch + 1, or **1** for a key it does not know, stored before the first packet. The key is named by a **tag**: the first 8 bytes of `HMAC-SHA256(game_key, "atalaia epoch tag")` (vectors `protocol/vectors/epoch_tag.json`): the same key always gives the same tag, the tag reveals nothing of the key, and a collision (2^-64) only makes the second key go on above the first (never a reused nonce). The box keeps its **last 16 keys** with their epochs and the **48 before them by tag alone** (retired, refused: BN-19) in one NVS blob (`radio/epochs`), MRU first, written whole by one `nvs_set_blob` + commit, so a power cut leaves the old store or the new one: never a new tag with an old epoch, never a key's epoch going back (tested: a cut before the write, after it, a new key's first write cut). Keeping keys, not 1, means rejoining an earlier game (an old card tapped after another game) goes on above that key's epochs instead of reusing them. **Migration:** the old single `u16 epoch` is read only while no blob exists, and only for the stored game the box resumes at boot (a box updated mid-game keeps working); any other game starts at 1. An unreadable or malformed blob stops the radio, as an NVS read error did. | Nonces only need to be unique per key, every game has a fresh random key, and within a key the epoch still only grows; receivers meet a new source at epoch 0 and look 16 above (unchanged). Fail-before: `test_many_games_heard_by_a_fresh_receiver` (test_field_link.c) failed 362 checks on the old code (unheard from game 8); `test_a_box_updated_from_the_old_epoch_store` and `test_a_box_in_many_games_in_a_row` (test_box_e2e.py, 18 games) failed (the old-store box unheard; epoch 2 in game 2). Left: see BN-18 and BN-19 (the independent review). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-8 | BN-5: a box whose epoch jumped more than 16 within one game (a crash loop) is never heard again in it. Recover, or make it visible? | **Recover:** every receiver (the box's field link, the modem, the pod) searches a **HELLO or a TIME** up to **256 epochs** above the source's last authenticated one (`ATALAIA_RESTART_EPOCH_WINDOW`); other packets keep the receiver's window. A rebooted box says HELLO first (resent until acknowledged), so the receiver learns its new epoch and hears the rest; a controller restarting many times in one game is found by its TIME the same way. Replay protection is untouched: a lower epoch is never tried, and an accepted packet's epoch is at or above the one already seen. The cost is up to 256 AES-CCM trials for a junk HELLO/TIME with a known source (a few ms on the ESP32-S3's AES), only for those two types, whose type is authenticated (in the AAD). Budgeted after the review: BN-20. | The simplest sound fix: no wire change, no new frame, no hint in the clear. Beyond 256 reboots in one game the box is still lost to that game (a new game fixes it); not made visible on the console yet. Test: `test_recv_after_a_crash_loop` (35 reboots: the EVENT is missed, the HELLO is heard, then the EVENT is). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-9 | Does the controller have the same problem? | **Yes, and it is fixed the same way:** its one `tx_epoch` in SQLite grew with every game and every restart, so after ~16 games the boxes (window 16 from epoch 0) no longer heard the controller's TIME, CMD and ACK. Now `tx_epochs` (one settings row) keeps `[tag, epoch]` for the last 8 game keys; a new game starts at 1, a restored game goes on above its own; the old `tx_epoch` serves only the game restored after the update. | Found while checking every node that keeps an epoch. The app's own radio link already keeps its epoch per (game id, node) (`app/lib/radio/pod_radio_link.dart`), so a new game id starts at 1 there; it is keyed by the game id, not the key (a reused game id with a new key goes on above, which is safe). The simulator has no per-node epoch model (its radio model uses one epoch for the whole air). Tests: test_store.py (per key, 40 games, older version), test_engine.py (a new game configures epoch 1), the 18-game e2e. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-10 | BN-6: what does a free adopted box show after a restart? | **"Ready / next game" from boot** (the shell's boot path, and the host box's): an adopted box with field frames on, no stored game and no pairing code shows `ready` with the hint that a key brings the game picker; the first key opens the picker (and its admin PIN) as after a game. An unadopted box still shows its pairing code over the picker; a box with no setup still shows the setup portal. | The maintainer took the PIN screen for a broken invite. Test: `test_a_free_box_is_ready_from_boot_and_speaks_the_fields_language` (test_box_e2e.py); references `ready.png`, `ready_picker.png`, `oled/ready_picker.png`; BRING_UP's bench and display steps. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-11 | (a) Domination's running zone with no owner showed the Bomb's words (STANDBY, "The bomb is not armed"). What should a neutral zone show? | A **`neutral`** screen: ZONE · NEUTRAL, "Hold to capture", the hold time, "Nobody holds this zone". Domination and Master and Satellites rename their `standby` template to `neutral`; Hold-All and Last Stand, which drew nothing until the end, now show the box's built-in `neutral` and `captured` C screens (with the hold time and the time of the capture). The OLED maps `neutral` to NEUTRAL / "Hold to capture" and `captured` to the team / ZONE HELD. The Bomb keeps `standby`, its header now READY (and "Hold to arm"); objectives and Target Windows were not reusing it for a zone. | Found on the bench. Tests: holdall/tests/screens.yaml, laststand/tests/screens.yaml, the renamed mode tests, references `neutral*`, `captured*`, `yaml_domination_neutral*`, `yaml_master_satellites_neutral`, OLED `neutral*`, `captured*`; the app goldens `domination_neutral(_pt)`, `master_satellites_neutral`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-12 | (b) A joined box looked the same as a running game with no owner. What should it show before the start? | Its own **`waiting`** screen from the join until the start, whatever time passes: WAITING · Game N · "The game hasn't started", and "Joined: zone" or "Invited: zone" below; on the OLED WAITING / Game N / "not started yet". | The maintainer twice thought a running game had not started. Test: `test_screens_follow_the_game` (test_box_game.c: no other screen for 20 s), the e2e invite test (`SCREEN waiting {...,"invited":true}`), references `waiting*`, OLED `waiting_invited*`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-13 | (c) The OLED kept "Invited / 3995: zone" after the start. How does it follow the game? | At the start the box shows **`playing`** (GAME ON · Game N, the role; OLED: the mode / GAME ON / the role) before the mode's first screen, so the joined screen never stays over a running game, even for a mode that draws nothing until a capture. The whole path: invited or joined (`waiting`) → `playing` → the mode's screens → its game over → `ready` 30 s later. | Tests: `test_screens_follow_the_game` (the shell's screens are exactly waiting; playing; ready, with neutral and final_score from the mode between), references `playing*`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-14 | (d) Box screens in the field's language, and better words than "standby". | **maintainer (2026-10-22): requested.** English and European Portuguese for every text the box draws itself (`main/box_text.c`: one static table by id; the TFT renderer, the OLED view, the lobby, the game, adoption, pairing, firmware updates), chosen from the joined game's `lang` (join key 15, RFC 0001 Amendment 10), kept in NVS (`radio/lang`) for the screens between games and after a reboot; English when no game gave one. Mode templates get the language as data `lang` (when a game gave one and the mode sets none) and choose their words with it: Domination, Master and Satellites, the Bomb and Target Windows now do; the modes that already had a `lang` parameter (medic, hacking terminal, VIP, objectives) keep it. Wording as the maintainer proposed, except where the screen needs shorter (LIVRE · "Mantém premido" / "3 s para capturar a zona": "Mantém premido para capturar" does not fit 320 px at 20 px). | The fonts gained the Portuguese letters (BN-17), so accents are drawn, not folded. Left: the Lua modes' own texts (the Bomb's code entry "ARM", "Enter your team code"; the Codes modifier) stay English (they would need the language in the script API), team names are the game's, and the app's prop screens do not use the join's `lang` yet (the app reads it: `JoinPayload.lang`). Tests: test_box_text.c (every text in both languages, OLED lengths, no unknown letters), `test_screen_language` (a pt join draws pt on the OLED and is kept across a reboot; a join without it gives English), 34 `_pt` reference images, the e2e test (the console's setting reaches the box). | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted — "LIVRE" for a zone nobody holds in Portuguese ("LIVRE · Mantém premido / 3 s para capturar a zona"); English stays "NEUTRAL · Hold to capture"; the rest as proposed |
| BN-15 | Which join key carries the box language? | Optional key **15 `lang`**: text, 2-8 bytes of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, the first two letters (`pt`, `en`, `pt-br`); anything else refuses the payload. Absent: English; an unknown language: English. RFC 0001 Amendment 10 (proposed). Readers before skip keys 15-31, so old boxes take new joins in English; readers now skip 16-31. Vectors: the `box_language` section of `protocol/vectors/join.json` (C, Python and Dart check them). | The next free key; field frames and adoption did not take it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-16 | What is the controller's "box language", and when does a game's language change? | A field setting `box_language`: `auto` (the default) gives a game the language of the console that created it (`console_lang` in `POST /api/v1/games`), `pt` or `en` fix it (`GET/PUT /api/v1/settings/box-language`, the console's Settings tab). A game keeps the language it was created with (`box_lang:<game id>` in the settings), and every join of it carries it (card, QR, text, radio invites, a standby controller's); changing the setting affects the next game. A game created through the API without `console_lang` gets no key 15 (English). | The language belongs to the people on the field, so it travels with the game's join. Tests: test_join.py (`BoxLanguageTests`), the console browser test, the e2e test. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-17 | How do the box's fonts draw Portuguese? | LVGL's own Montserrat Medium 14/20/28/48 regenerated with LVGL's command (`tools/gen_box_fonts.py`, lv_font_conv 1.5.2) plus ª º · and the 24 accented letters of Portuguese (`main/fonts/lv_font_atalaia_*.c`, OFL-1.1), with LVGL's line height and baseline kept, so every English screen is identical to the pixel (checked: all references matched before any text changed); an accent on a capital or a cedilla may reach a pixel past its line. The template text fold keeps those letters and still folds the rest. The builds use them instead of LVGL's (lv_conf.h). | The modes' Portuguese screens (medic, hacking terminal, VIP, objectives) had their accents dropped; they are now drawn (their references changed for that alone). Flash: the Field Box image grew from 0x1ada60 to 0x1b6590 bytes (+35.6 KB with the texts and screens; 14% of its slot free, was 16%), the bench (Heltec, reduced tier) image is 0x1b06d0 (16% free), the modem +6 KB (77% free); the 48 px font is most of it. RAM: the text table is in flash; nothing new on the heap. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-18 | (Independent review, finding 1, medium-high) Two devices on one (game key, node id) reuse nonces with certainty: the controller issued a join for a node id already given to a box (a dead box's spare, one card tapped on two boxes). | **Prevent and detect.** The controller issues a join for a node id once per game: a node id a join was issued for (kept in the store, `issued_nodes:<game>`), or a box was heard on, is refused (409: "a replacement box gets a new node id"), except to the same device invited again (`reinvite.py`, whose epochs go on above its own). When two device ids (HELLO key 10) say HELLO on one node id, the controller logs it, marks the node `conflict` and the console shows "Two boxes share node N: one join was used on two boxes… take one out and join it again". Not done: telling one of them to stop (a CMD reaches both) or not acknowledging the node (both would keep retrying on the air). | Left: a box reflashed with its NVS erased, tapping a card of the game it was in (protocol/adoption.md and join.md say to re-join it instead); two devices without a device id (an Android prop) are not flagged. Fail-before: `SharedNodeIdTests` (test_reinvite.py: the second issue of node 4 succeeded; two device ids raised nothing), the console browser test (the alert). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-19 | (Review, finding 2, medium) A key the store had forgotten (after 8 newer games) restarted at 1, and a card join did not check the games the box had played to their end. | **Refuse what cannot be safe.** The store keeps 16 keys with their epochs and the tags of the 48 before them (retired: blob version 2, still reading version 1); a retired key's join is refused before anything changes (`BOX_JOIN_ENDED`, by card, QR, text or invite), and `boot_epoch` refuses it too. Card, QR and text joins now also refuse a game the box played to its end (its last four), as invites did. **Floor considered and rejected**: starting new keys above the highest forgotten epoch turns into a lifetime counter (every new key's epochs are above the floor, so each forgotten key raises it by at least one), out of the receivers' reach after ~240 games, the bug this amendment fixes. **Legacy counter**: used only on the resume of the stored game, as before. | Left: a key older than 64 newer games; a card of a game played before the update tapped after it (the old counter cannot say which keys it covered). Fail-before: `test_boot_epoch_old_keys` (the forgotten key restarted at 1; the old test `test_boot_epoch_forgets_the_oldest_key` asserted it), `test_old_card_refused`, the ended-game card in `test_lobby_refusals`; plus a first resume whose write fails, and a too-long blob (NVS's invalid length) read as an error. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-20 | (Review, finding 3, low-medium) The 256-epoch search costs up to 257 decryptions per junk HELLO/TIME (source and type are in the clear). | The normal window first; then the wide search at most once per source every 2 s (`ATALAIA_WIDE_GAP_MS`) and 20 times a minute in all (`ATALAIA_WIDE_PER_MIN`), counted (`wide_searches`, `wide_refused`). A genuine HELLO is resent every 3 s until acknowledged, so it is searched within a retry or two. The CCM key is still set per trial (the core's crypto interface takes the key per call); the budget bounds the cost instead. | 1000 junk HELLOs from 50 sources in 10 s: 21 800 decryptions instead of 257 000 (`test_wide_search_budget`, a counting crypto backend). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-21 | (Review, finding 4, low) A receiver that no longer knew a source took an old recorded HELLO at a low epoch and then dropped the source's real packets more than 16 above it. | A source's first epoch is **provisional**: until a packet authenticates in the normal window, its packets of any type may get the (budgeted) wide search; a source the controller names (SET_PEER_EPOCH) is trusted. | Cheap and keeps replay protection (never lower). Test: `test_provisional_source`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-22 | Where else does BN-14's "neutral" read in Portuguese, now that it is LIVRE? | Everywhere it names a zone nobody holds: the box's own `neutral` screen and OLED (`box_text.c`), Domination's and Master and Satellites' `neutral` templates (and the app's copy of them, `builtin_modes.g.dart`), the console's *Box language* intro ("zona livre") and the app's event line ("{zone} ficou livre" for `zone.neutralized`); BRING_UP's screen table. Only the Portuguese references changed: `neutral_pt`, `yaml_domination_neutral_pt`, OLED `neutral_pt`, the app golden `domination_neutral_pt` (Master and Satellites has no Portuguese reference). | One word for one state, wherever players read it. Test: test_box_text.c pins "LIVRE". | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| BN-23 | (Bench, 2026-10-23) While a mode shows `captured`, the OLED showed only the team and ZONE HELD / ZONA OCUPADA; the screen's `points` (the team's on this box) and `since` (the game time of the capture, in seconds), sent by Domination and the registry's King of the Hill, reached only the TFT. What does the OLED show? | The team big, as before; its points at the right of the header (`12 pts`, `12 pts`, `12 Pkt.`, `12 分`; the mode's name on the left gets the rest of the bar and ends in "..." when both do not fit); below, **how long the team has held the zone**, the game's clock minus `since`, ticking: `held for 2:15`, `ocupada 2:15`, `gehalten 2:15`, `已佔領 2:15` (`1:00:05` past an hour). Without `since` the footer stays ZONE HELD, without `points` the header has no number, so another mode's `captured` with only a team looks as before. Two new texts (`TXO_POINTS`, `TXO_HELD_FOR`), no new letter: the fonts and their sizes are unchanged (`gen_box_fonts.py --check`). Reference images: OLED `captured`, `captured_pt`, `captured_de`, `captured_zh-tw` changed, `captured_taking` new; each looked at. | The maintainer asked for the owner big, the points in the header's corner and the held time in the footer. **Words for review:** the request suggested "since %s" / "desde %s" / "seit %s"; they were not taken because the same screen's TFT line already says "held since 1:35" / "desde 1:35" / "seit 1:35" for the game time *of the capture* (`TX_HELD_SINCE`), so the OLED's "desde 0:40" beside it would read as a second, different capture time. The OLED's words say a duration instead ("ocupada há 2:15" would be the natural Portuguese, but past an hour it no longer fits the footer's 124 pixels: `check_held_tick` checks 9:59:59 in every language); going back to "since" (or to the capture time, as the TFT) is one line in `box_text.c`. Tests: `test_zone_screens` (test_oled_view.c), `check_held_tick` (test_oled_screens.c), test_box_text.c (the 16 columns, the letters). | open; checked by the maintainer on the bench board's OLED in a Domination game (2026-10-23): the hold bar, the points and the held time read well |
| BN-24 | Where does the OLED get the game time, and what does ticking it cost? | `oled_view_set_game_clock()`: the shell hands the OLED view a reader of the node runner's `game_time_ms` (the time the core's `game.time()` gives the script, so `since` is on the same clock; time-synced in a field game, restored after a reboot; one 32-bit word the game task writes whole, read from the LVGL task). `oled_view_poll()`, called by the LVGL task every 5 ms, compares the game second with the last one drawn and, once a second, sets only the footer label: LVGL redraws the 128x64 frame in RAM and `hal_esp_oled` sends the pages that changed, one or two of eight 128-byte pages on the 100 kHz bus (~25 ms), as a Bomb countdown's tick. The TFT is not touched: no screen message, no template render (the 120-170 ms "slow redraw" lines are full frames, at boot and on screen changes). Nothing ticks while the bar shows, over another screen, or without a clock (the host tests set a fake one). | The LVGL task runs on core 1 at priority 4, below the game task (core 1, 5), which preempts it; the radio task is on core 0 (priority 3), so the I2C write cannot starve it. A pause stops the game time, and the box shows `paused` then anyway. A message from the script each second would cost the TFT a template render and the radio nothing, but the script nothing it needs. | open; checked by the maintainer on the bench board's OLED in a Domination game (2026-10-23): the hold bar, the points and the held time read well |
| BN-25 | While another team holds its button to take the zone (King of the Hill's `taker` and `progress`), what does the footer show? | The hold bar as before, and the taking team's name beside it (BLUE) instead of ZONE HELD; the held time comes back when the bar goes. `neutral` gains the same (the taker beside the bar instead of "Hold to capture"), the one cheap addition there: the same data, no new text. Without a `taker`, or without a bar, both screens are as before. | The bar alone does not say who is taking the zone; the generic mapping already shows a mode's `team` beside its bar (a medic's hold). Domination sends no progress, so only modes that do (the Hill) show it. | open; checked by the maintainer on the bench board's OLED in a Domination game (2026-10-23): the hold bar, the points and the held time read well |
| BN-26 | (Bench, 2026-10-23, after BN-23) Holding a team button on a built-in Domination box showed no progress bar on the OLED, on `neutral` or `captured`; the capture, the points and the held time worked. Where did the bar go? | **Cause:** the OLED's only hold bar was the screen data's `progress` (or Bomb's `defuse`): `oled_text_for` (`firmware/esp32/main/oled_view.c`, the `neutral` and `captured` branches: `out->bar = share_pct(data, "progress")`). Domination's `node.lua` has never sent `progress` (nor a `taker`), in no version, and nothing else in the firmware drew one (no overlay from the buttons, `button_tracker.c` only feeds `team_input.*` to the script), so 8560d54 did not remove a Domination bar: none existed. The bar seen before was the registry's King of the Hill (fetched and played on the same bench day; its script sends `taker` and `progress`, BN-25) or Bomb's arming bar (HB-4); BN-25 then wrote "Domination sends no progress, so only modes that do show it", which is the gap. **Fix:** the box draws the bar itself on a zone's screens. The shell hands the OLED view a reader of its team buttons (`oled_view_set_hold_reader`: which are down and for how long, from `btn_tracker`, while a game runs); the hold time comes from the `@mode` message's params (`capture_hold_ms`, the built-in zone modes; else `hold_ms`, the Hill; neither: no bar). `oled_text_hold` fills the bar in 250 ms steps (one per hold event, as a mode's own bar) and puts the holding team's name beside it, exactly as BN-25's taker line; only on `neutral` and `captured`, only when the mode sent no bar of its own (the Hill's wins), never for the team that already holds the zone (Domination ignores its holds), the longest-held button when several are down. While the bar shows, the footer is the team's name and the bar, the points stay in the header and the held time comes back when the button is let go: no new text, so the four languages' widths are unchanged. `oled_view_poll` redraws the footer only, at most four times a second, as the held time's tick. | A mode should not have to send a bar the box can draw from its own buttons, and every built-in zone mode (Domination, Hold-All, Last Stand, Master and Satellites) gained it with no script change; their TFT screens are unchanged (a script-sent `progress` there remains possible). Tests: `test_box_hold_bar` (test_oled_view.c), `check_button_hold` (test_oled_screens.c: a held button on Domination's `captured` and `neutral`, the bar fills, goes on release and the held time returns; fails without the fix), new references `neutral_holding`, `captured_holding` (looked at), no other image changed. | open; checked by the maintainer on the bench board's OLED in a Domination game (2026-10-23): the hold bar, the points and the held time read well |

## The invite dialog says when the game is ready, and starts it (2026-10-22)

The maintainer asked on the bench for the GM console's *Boxes for this game* dialog (`controller/console/src/components/box-invites.js`) to say when the boxes joined so far are enough, and to start the game from it. In the console only; tested in Chromium against the real controller.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GM-0 | The dialog should say clearly when every role has a box (counts per role, unmet minimums as warnings) and offer "Start game", asking "Review the game's other settings first?" (Review settings / Start now; Start anyway through the same force path when a role has no box). | Built as GM-1 to GM-4. | The GM could not tell when the dialog could be closed. | maintainer (2026-10-22): requested |
| GM-1 | Where does the dialog's "ready" come from? Does it need a new endpoint (`GET /api/v1/games/{id}/readiness`)? | No new endpoint: the live, ready game the controller pushes already carries the engine's `start_check` (`needed`, `joined` per role, `missing`, `warnings`), and the engine pushes the game again on every HELLO (and `GET .../start-check` gives the same). The dialog only words it: *Ready to start: every role has a box (1 × zone)* in a live region, or *Still waiting for a box for: …* with the counts, and each unmet minimum as a warning. A controller test pins that the pushed check carries the counts and the warnings. | One implementation of the rule, the engine's; the console computes nothing. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| GM-2 | What does the dialog's Start do? | *Start game* (or *Start anyway*, red, while a role has no box) opens a confirmation in the dialog: *Review the game's other settings first?* with *Review settings*, *Start now* (or *Start anyway*, naming the missing roles) and *Back* (Escape too). It calls the same `POST .../start` as the game card's Start, with `{"force": true}` only when a role is missing, so a forced start is logged and told to every console (`start.forced`) as before; if the controller refuses (a box left meanwhile, a firmware update on its way) the dialog stays open and the error shows. The dialog appears only for the GM token, on the field's leader, for a ready game; the remote GM is unchanged and cannot force (RJ-4). Undeclared chronograph checks show as a warning in the confirmation. | The maintainer's wording; the existing guard's rules and path, not a second one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| GM-3 | What is the "game's settings" view that *Review settings* shows? | A new *Game settings* section on the game card (read-only): the game's parameters as created, labelled in the console's language, next to the duration, teams and squads the card already shows. *Review settings* closes the dialog, opens that section, scrolls to the card and focuses it; the game stays ready. Settings cannot be changed once a game is created (end it and create another; the section says so); teams' names and crests still can, below. | There was no view of a created game's parameters; editing them after creation is a bigger change nobody asked for. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| GM-4 | Accessibility | Readiness in `role="status"` `aria-live="polite"`; the confirmation is a labelled group, focus moves to its first choice (*Review settings*, the safe one) and back to Start when it closes; Escape closes the confirmation, then the dialog. | Keyboard and screen-reader use, as the console's other dialogs. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## The first firmware update over the air on a board (2026-10-22)

Found running BRING_UP step 10c on the Heltec V4.3 bench board with a throw-away bench key, sent by the controller (in Docker on the maintainer's PC, HTTPS on the LAN) over LoRa. Each fix was checked on the board before the next; the last attempt installed 0.2.1-bench.1 and the controller recorded `done`. All open but OT-7, the maintainer's decision (2026-10-22), built as OT-8 to OT-11.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OT-1 | Why did the update fail with `no_wifi`? | The Wi-Fi driver could not start: its 16 static TX buffers (~26 KB) and then its RX buffers did not fit in internal RAM next to the game's tasks, the radio, the lobby, pairing and LVGL (47 KB of DMA-capable RAM free before it started). Now: `CONFIG_SPIRAM_TRY_ALLOCATE_WIFI_LWIP` (Wi-Fi and LwIP buffers in PSRAM), 6 static RX buffers (they must stay internal, DMA) with `RX_BA_WIN` 6, 8 static TX buffers, no Wi-Fi code in IRAM (`ESP_WIFI_IRAM_OPT`, `RX_IRAM_OPT` off, ~27 KB), and `SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL` 4096 (allocations above 4 KB in PSRAM). 63 KB of DMA-capable RAM free before Wi-Fi now. | Measured on the board; throughput does not matter for an image fetched between games. The reduced tier (no PSRAM) still needs its own check when an update is first tried on one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-2 | Why was the controller's certificate refused once Wi-Fi worked? | mbedTLS allocated only in internal RAM (ESP-IDF's default `MBEDTLS_INTERNAL_MEM_ALLOC`): the ECDSA check failed with `MBEDTLS_ERR_MPI_ALLOC_FAILED`, which mbedTLS reports as "not correctly signed by the trusted CA". Now `MBEDTLS_EXTERNAL_MEM_ALLOC` (PSRAM). | The usual choice on a board with PSRAM; slower TLS for one download. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-3 | Is the pin check right? | Rewritten after ESP-IDF's certificate bundle: wherever mbedTLS marks "not trusted", the flag is cleared only if that certificate is the field CA of the join (its SHA-256) or was issued and signed by it (name, CA bit, signature checked), the CA found among the certificates the server sent. The old callback cleared the flag only on the CA itself, which mbedTLS did not mark here. | Same guarantee (only the join's field CA is trusted), robust to where mbedTLS puts the flag. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-4 | How does a failed update explain itself? | The box logs the DMA-capable and internal RAM free before Wi-Fi starts, why the driver did not start, and mbedTLS's own reasons for a refused certificate; the result codes sent to the controller are unchanged. | Each of OT-1 to OT-3 took a guess before these logs existed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-5 | What did the bench show besides? | **Follow-ups:** an update that fails before the box has any network (`no_wifi`) never reaches the controller, which keeps it `in_progress` and refuses a retry for its 10-minute timeout (report the failure over LoRa, or let the GM retry at once); in Docker the controller advertises the container's own host name in the join's URLs (`26eeb750e075`, `.local`), which the box cannot resolve (harmless on a Pi; do not advertise names that only resolve inside a container); the console showed "Em uso" over the firmware version a box runs (reads as a status: "Versão instalada"), and the check-in poster's "HTTPS is off" problem untranslated. **Done (2026-10-22):** a failure the controller could not hear over HTTPS now goes over LoRa (`node.update_failed`, OT-8) and the GM may send an update again at once (OT-9); the joins' and the poster's addresses leave out what only resolves inside a container (OT-10); the column reads "Versão instalada" / "Installed version" (key `firmware.installed_version`, used only there); the poster's problems come as codes the console translates (OT-11); and the app's flaky "at most 20 connections" test was a race in the test's own client (OT-12). Not yet tried on the bench board. | Found on the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-6 | Does step 10c's step 8 pass? | Yes, on the bench board (2026-10-22): the same version again is refused by the controller (`box 1: not_newer`, nothing sent) and by the box itself (`@update`: `update refused: not_newer`, before any network); an image signed by a key the controller trusts but the box does not is refused before the image is fetched (`bad_signature`, the controller's record `failed: bad_signature`); a USB reset of the board about 10 s into the download (mid-download by the timings of the good run; the serial port could not log during the reset, and a reset is not a true power cut) left it booting the version it ran; an image built without a release key installed, failed its self-test (`core FAILED`), and the bootloader rolled back to the previous slot, the controller's record `rolled_back: self_test`. A reboot mid-game then resumed it (epoch 3 to 4 under the game key, 0 authentication failures; BN-4 and BN-7 on the board). The bench keys and images were deleted and a normal build flashed. | Facts from the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-7 | A controller restart strands the boxes of its live game. | **maintainer (2026-10-22): both.** (a) `persist_game_key` on by default (OT-14): the field join key, the master secret, is on the Pi's disk anyway, so a short-lived game key adds little; a restarted controller takes the game up again (tested end to end: the box still heard, the GM's end reaching it). (b) A sealed GAME_OVER field frame (OT-13, OT-15) for a controller that has no key for the game, and after every end besides. No silence timeout on the box: it cannot tell a controller gone from one briefly out of reach or a failover. Before: with `persist_game_key = false` (the default then) a restarted controller forgets the live game's key, so it can never send that game's end: its boxes stay in the game (answering invites `busy`) until a card, a reboot into the same game or an NVS erase; on the bench the box had to be erased and paired again. Options: a field-frame "leave game N" sealed under the field key (the controller still has it), a box timeout when it hears no TIME for a long time after the controller's beacons stopped, or `persist_game_key` on by default with its key-at-rest trade-off. | Found on the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted — persist_game_key on by default, and a sealed GAME_OVER field frame (kind 4) that ends a box's game; no timeout on the box |
| OT-8 | How does a box tell the controller about an update that failed where HTTPS cannot carry the report (`no_wifi`, `unreachable`, refused before any network, a report with no answer)? | A new standard event **`node.update_failed`** (id 52, critical: retried until acknowledged) with `release` (new data field, key 37), `reason` (the result code) and `fw` (the version it still runs), sent by the box's link in its joined game (`field_link_send_update_failed`; on the ESP32 the update task hands the result to the game task, which owns the link: `fw_update_esp_poll`). The report's `post` now says whether the controller answered 200 (`fw_job_t.reported`); anything not taken over HTTPS goes over LoRa, and so do the refusals before any network (`fw_update_esp_offer`) and a CMD update during a game (`busy`, box_game.c). The event's seq is the header seq of the first packet that carries it: the controller's event identity is (node, seq, game time, type) and the game time is 0 before the start, so a fixed seq 0 (as `node.rebooted`) would make a second failure look like a copy of the first. The controller marks only an update it sent to that box, still `sent` or `timeout`, and only with a code-shaped reason (`[a-z][a-z_]{0,23}`). RFC 0001 Amendment 11 (proposed); vector in `protocol/vectors/events.yaml` (CBOR made by hand from the RFC 8949 rules, checked by the C and Python codecs). Tests: `test_update_failed_over_lora` (test_box_game.c), `test_job_refusals` (`reported`, test_fw_update.c), `test_failure_said_over_the_radio` (controller), the console's browser test. | No existing message fits: HELLO and STATE carry no update result, and adding one to HELLO would change the codecs of four implementations and resend a HELLO mid-game. An event is sealed with the game key (as the HTTPS report's MAC), retried until acknowledged, logged in the game for the GM, and needs only the shared tables (protogen) and one vector. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-9 | "Let the GM retry at once after a failure, whatever the timeout": what may be retried? | A failed (or timed-out) update could already be sent again; now also an update still `sent` when the GM names the box (`{"nodes": [N]}`; the console's *Send again* next to "sent, waiting for the box"), since a box whose failure never arrived (an old firmware, the event lost) would otherwise wait 10 minutes. `"all"` still skips boxes with an update on its way, and an `installed` box (restarting into the new image) is never sent another. A box already running an update ignores a second offer (`s_busy`). | Re-sending is harmless on the box and the GM is the one who can tell a stuck update from a slow one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
