| OT-10 | Which addresses go into the joins (key 14) and on the check-in poster? | `[api] tls_names` first, in their order (only loopback and `localhost` left out), then the names and addresses found on the host, but only outside a container (`/.dockerenv`, `/run/.containerenv`, or a docker/containerd/kubepods/libpod/lxc cgroup of PID 1) and never loopback, link-local or unspecified addresses, `localhost`, or a host name shaped like a container id (12 or 64 hex digits, and its `.local`). In a container with no `tls_names` the controller logs a warning and the poster says it has no address; the poster's other settings (the field's name from the branding) are still checked and saved without one. The server certificate keeps every name, as before. (`tls.link_names`, `main.poster_urls`; tests in test_tls.py.) | In a container (bridge networking, as on the bench) the host name is the container id and the addresses are on the container's bridge: none of them reaches a box, and each cost the box seconds of failed lookups. With `--network host` the found names would work, but they cannot be told apart from the inside reliably; `tls_names` is the documented way to say where the field reaches the controller. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-11 | How does the console translate the poster's problems? | `GET /api/v1/checkin/poster` keeps `problem` (English, for scripts and logs) and adds `problem_code`: `https_off`, `no_address`, `too_long` (the poster does not fit a QR code) or `invalid` (any other refusal of the codec, its text as the detail); the console's check-in tab and the poster page show `checkin.problems.<code>` in pt-PT or English, else the English text. | Codes are what the console already does for other refusals (firmware reasons); the text stays for API users. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-12 | Why did the app's test "at most 20 connections" fail under load? | Reproduced under load (a 40-round copy of the test failed at once with `SocketException: Broken pipe` from the 21st client's CONNECT; it passed alone): the broker closes the 21st socket as it accepts it, and when that happens before the client's CONNECT is written the write fails, an error the test's client never listened for, so it surfaced as an uncaught error. The broker is right; the test's client now treats a reset, a broken pipe or the end alike as "closed" (`onError`, `socket.done`, `close()`), and its waits are 15 s instead of 5 s. The same 40 rounds then passed under load, and the test file passed 10 runs in a row under load. | Only the test was wrong: the timing decided which of two equally correct closings the client saw. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-13 | GAME_OVER: which frame, and what stops an old one ending a new game? | **Field frame kind 4** (kinds 4-7 were free; type 10 is pairing's): `{1: 4, 4: game_id (1-65534), 5: key_tag (8 bytes)}`, sealed under the field key with RJ-15's nonce rule. `key_tag` is the epoch store's tag of the game key, `HMAC-SHA256(game key, "atalaia epoch tag")[:8]` (BN-7), which the controller keeps beside the game, not secret. A box in a game (joined or playing) whose id and key tag both match ends it as on CMD end (game over, announcing, Ready); anything else changes nothing (another game's; its id under another key: counted `games_over_refused` and logged). **No time or counter**: a game's end is a fact that stays true, so one played back later can only end that same game, which the box no longer joins (played to its end, BN-19, or its key retired), and a later game with the same id has another key, so another tag. Binding to the join's MAC was considered: the box would need to keep it, and it says nothing a key tag does not. RFC 0001 Amendment 7, addendum of 2026-10-22. | The tag is the one thing a controller without the key can still name the game by, and a box can check without a clock. Vectors (field_frame.json: 2 valid frames, 7 bodies to refuse, the tags taken from epoch_tag.json and checked against them) for C and Python; box host test `test_lobby_game_over` (a stranded box leaves on it; another game's, the same id under another key, another field's: nothing; played back after the game: nothing; a new game with the old id refuses the old frame); sim `test_game_over_frees_a_box_whose_controller_lost_the_key`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-14 | What changes with `persist_game_key` on by default? | The default only (`config.py`, `controller.example.toml`); a `controller.toml` that says `false` keeps it off. The key is still erased when the game ends and kept in the SQLite file (0600). The game key's tag goes into the settings (`key_tag:<game>`) whenever a game is made live, whatever the setting, and is deleted when it ends or a standby leaves it. | The maintainer's choice; the tag is what OT-13 needs after a key is lost. Tests: test_engine (`test_the_default_survives_a_restart`, the old lost-key test now with the setting off), test_reinvite (`test_persisted_by_default`), test_box_e2e (`test_a_controller_restart_in_the_middle_of_a_game`: C box, C modem, a capture heard after the restart, the end reaching the box). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-15 | When does the controller send GAME_OVER, how often, on which channel, and what does the console say? | After **every** game it ran ends (the GM's End, the time, a field script), besides the game's own CMD end, and as the only way for a game whose key was lost; never from a standby (its game may still run on the primary). **Three times, 4 s apart** (47 bytes, 92 ms each, 0.28 s of air), through the inviter's pacing, on the game's channel: with no game live and the field's channel another, the modem is tuned there and back afterwards (pairing's listening window cleared, AD-27); with a game live on another channel it is dropped. `end` answers `game_over_frame`: `sent`, `no_radio`, `no_join_key`, `no_tag`, `standby`. The console's game card says when the key was lost, *End* asks "End the game and tell its boxes over the field radio?", and a toast says whether they were told. | Bounded airtime; a box that missed CMD end hears it too. Tests: test_reinvite `GameOverTests` (the frame and its tag, three in all, the channel there and back, no radio), test_box_e2e `test_a_restart_without_the_key_ends_the_game_over_the_field_radio` (C box through the C modem leaves and is listed free again), the browser test `test_ending_a_game_whose_key_was_lost`. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| OT-16 | What is left? | A game made live before this version and then ended without its key has no stored tag (`no_tag`): its boxes still need a card, a reboot into another game or an NVS erase, as before. A lost database or a replacement Pi with the same field join key has no game record to end (a console tool to end "game N" by id cannot name its key tag either). Boxes with older firmware refuse kind 4 and stay in the game. Not yet on the bench boards. | Outside this change, or rare. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |


## Multi-story fields: a floor plan per level (2026-10-22)

The maintainer asked for multi-story buildings in the GM console's field tab. Built in the controller (`field_levels.py`, `api.py`, `store.py` schema 9, `positions.py`, `atak.py`, `georef.py`, the scenario validator) and the console (`components/field-levels.js`, `field-view.js`, `georef-panel.js`); tested with the controller's unit and API tests and in Chromium against the real controller; not yet at a real field. ML-0 is the maintainer's; the rest are open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ML-0 | "At the GM console, where we load the field map, we need a checkbox to enable multi-story buildings (so it can have different floor maps), and when checked, you must specify the number of stories/levels and load a floor map for each." | Built as ML-1 to ML-12. | A field in a building needs a plan per floor and the boxes on the right floor. | maintainer (2026-10-22): requested |
| ML-1 | How many levels, how are they numbered, where are the plans kept? | Off by default (one image, as before). On: 2 to 10 levels, numbered 0 to n-1 in the order the GM lists them; level 0 is the field image of before. Each plan has the field image's types (PNG, JPEG, WebP) and limit (4 MB). Plans in a new table `field_levels` (store schema 9), the settings (`enabled`, `count`, `names`) in the settings key `field.levels`. API: `GET|PUT /api/v1/field/levels`, `GET|PUT|DELETE /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/image` and `/georef`; the old `/api/v1/field/image` and `/georef` are level 0; writes GM only, reads with the read token, plans `no-store` as the image was (it never had an ETag). | Ten covers any airsoft building; level 0 being the old image makes the migration and the old clients trivially right. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-2 | Names, the ground floor and basements. | Free names (one line, at most 32 characters, spaces folded); the console fills them in in its language when the GM ticks the box or adds levels ("R/C", "1.º andar", "2.º andar", ... / "Ground floor", "Floor 1", ...), and the GM can change them. No separate "ground floor" setting: a basement is a level the GM names so (e.g. "Cave", "R/C", "1.º andar"). Levels cannot be reordered (a plan can be uploaded again on another level). | Names already say everything a ground-floor index would, for the GM and for ATAK (ML-7); one concept instead of two. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| ML-3 | What happens when levels are turned off, or there are fewer? | Off asks first (*Only the plan of R/C stays in use, as the field image, and every box shows on it. The other plans, the names and each box's level are kept and come back if you turn levels on again*) and deletes nothing; while off only level 0 is used and every box shows on it (ATAK by level 0's points). Fewer levels delete the plans and own control points of the levels that go, asking first when one has a plan, and move the boxes on them to level 0 in every game. | Turning off by mistake must not cost the GM ten uploads; fewer levels is an explicit change of the building. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| ML-4 | Boxes' positions with a level. | `positions.level` (0 for every older row and every new box). `PUT .../positions/{node}` takes an optional `level` (a level in use; without it the box keeps its level); `GET` and the stream's `position` message carry it. A box on a level not in use (levels off) is shown on level 0 by the console and ATAK; the stored level is kept. In the console a box is moved between levels with *Each box's level* under the plan (a select per box, keyboard friendly), and a box dragged on a level's plan is put on that level; dragging a marker onto another tab is not offered. | One list works with a keyboard and on a phone; dragging onto a tab would be a second, mouse-only path. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-5 | A player's level: the phone may not know its floor. | A position report may carry `level` (0-9, or `null`: not known). A report without it keeps the level the player last gave in that game (so a phone asks "which floor?" once); never given, the position has no level (shown on no particular level). The level is not checked against the field's count (the phone's view may be stale; clients clamp). The console's field view does not show players (it never did: players report GPS or a BLE beacon, not a place on a plan; a beacon-to-plan map would be a follow-up), and the app does not send a level yet: a floor picker in the app is a follow-up for the app's work. | The last chosen level is what a player expects; unknown is honest, and never guessed as level 0. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| ML-6 | Georeference with several plans. | Every level shares level 0's control points unless the GM gives a level its own (its tab's *Map position (ATAK)* panel, `PUT /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/georef`, stored as `field.georef.<n>`); the panel says when a level shares level 0's. A new plan on a level clears that level's own points only; a new level-0 image clears level 0's (which the others share), as before. | Floor plans of one building are usually drawn to the same frame, so one set of corners serves all; a level drawn differently can be fixed alone. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| ML-7 | ATAK: how does a level show? Height? | The box's `<remarks>` end with its level's name (`Atalaia: Red (red), 1.º andar`; `L<n>` if unnamed), and a player's only when the phone gave its level. The remarks are already free text starting `Atalaia: ` in the form `sync_format.cot_event` checks, so the relayed form, `protocol/sync.md` and the vectors are unchanged. No height: `hae` stays unknown (`9999999.0`), since a height above the ellipsoid needs the ground's ellipsoidal height, which the GM does not have; a floor height setting was not added. | The name is what staff need to find a box; a wrong height is worse than none. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-8 | Scenario placements per level. | Scenario manifests have no map placements (positions are per game, set in the field view). A box node may say its level (`roof: { role: objective, level: 3 }`, 0-9; refused on a player node), kept in the compiled package's `scenario.nodes` and shown in the scenario builder's box list (*piso 3* / *level 3*); it is a note for the GM and for phase 5's visual editor, and places nothing by itself. | The smallest change that lets a scenario say where a box goes; the visual editor can write it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-9 | Live messages. | The stream's `position` carries `level`; `field_image` and `field_georef` carry the `level` they concern; a new `field_levels` message carries the levels' view when the GM changes them (not sent to player tokens). Positions never go on MQTT, as before. | The console and ATAK follow a change at once. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-10 | Site server sync. | The levels stay on the controller: the field image was never synced (the site server's "field images" are the branding's logo and wallpaper), and a building's floor plans can be sensitive. If the site server ever shows a field map, the levels go with it. | Nothing to keep consistent today; no new personal or sensitive data leaves the field. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| ML-11 | The store's migration. | Schema 9: the old `field_image` row is copied to `field_levels` level 0 and the table dropped in one transaction; `positions` gets `level INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`. Opening again changes nothing. A controller of an earlier version opening a schema 9 file would see no field image (its own empty table), not lose it. (Another change in parallel that also bumps the schema would need renumbering.) | In-place upgrades as for every earlier schema; tested with a hand-made schema 8 file. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| ML-12 | Accessibility, languages, phone width. | The tabs are an ARIA tablist (arrow keys, Home, End; the plan is its tabpanel); every input and select has a label; the confirmations are a labelled group with the question announced; every string in pt-PT and English; the tabs and the levels' names wrap at phone width (checked at 390 px). | As the console's other panels. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |



## Map features (2026-10-22)

The maintainer asked for more than the boxes on the field's plan. Built in the controller (`map_features.py`, `api.py`, `store.py` schema 11, `field_levels.py`, `ws.py`, `atak.py`, `protocol/vectors/cot.json`) and the console (`components/map-features.js`, `field-view.js`, `field-levels.js`); tested with the controller's unit and API tests (`tests/test_map_features.py`), ATAK output checked against cot.json and the relay's shared check, and in Chromium against the real controller (pt-PT, English, phone width); not yet at a real field nor on a real ATAK device. MF-0 is the maintainer's request and MF-1 and MF-3 carry the maintainer's answers; MF-5, MF-7, MF-12, MF-14 and MF-16 are **for the maintainer**; the rest are open. MF-18, the maintainer's idea of loading the field from a map with its real outline, was built on 2026-10-22 as MF-19 to MF-30 (`field_map.py`, `tests/test_field_map.py`, the console's `field-map.html` and its browser tests with the map services mocked); MF-21, MF-25, MF-27 and MF-28 are **for the maintainer** (the map viewer, the orthophoto's licence, the page's own Content-Security-Policy, a level that already has a plan). The out-of-bounds warning on players' phones that MF-30 left for later was built on 2026-10-22 as MF-31 to MF-40 (`app/lib/position/field_bounds.dart`, `player_screens.dart`, `position_reporter.dart`, `field_feed.dart`; `app/test/position/field_bounds_test.dart`, with the polygon cases of `protocol/vectors/geo.json`); in software only, not yet on a real phone at a real field; MF-33, MF-36 and MF-38 are **for the maintainer** (what the player feels and hears, telling the staff, a switch per game). The maintainer then asked (2026-10-23) to centre the field-map page on the GM's location and to type a place's name: built on 2026-10-23 as MF-41 to MF-49 (`components/place-search.js`, `field-map.js`, `tile-map.js` `fit`, `api.py`'s headers; browser tests with Nominatim answered from `tests/fixtures/nominatim.json` and the position from Playwright, no real network); in software only, not yet against the live Nominatim from a browser nor on a phone at a field; MF-42, MF-43 and MF-44 are **for the maintainer** (centring by itself on open, the `Permissions-Policy`, and Nominatim's usage policy, of which two points are not met and the policy asks for the maintainer's own informed decision). The maintainer then used the page for real (2026-10-23: a field near Corroios loaded with the DGT orthophoto) and found a half-empty wide window, a busy Overpass (502 "at capacity"), a request that never ended, a preview below the fold (nothing saved, twice) and no satellite view while outlining; built on 2026-10-23 as MF-50 to MF-64 (`components/field-render.js` `fetchOsm` and `orthoTileUrl`, `tile-map.js` sources, `field-map.js`, `field-view.js`, `field-levels.js`, `style.css`, `api.py`'s headers; browser tests with every outside service mocked, including a 502, a 502 without CORS, a request that hangs and the orthophoto tiles); in software only, not yet against the live services from a browser after the change; MF-51 and MF-57 are **for the maintainer** (the other Overpass instance, with older data, and VK's in Russia left out; imagery outside Portugal). The maintainer answered MF-57 (2026-10-23, "open source would be nice"): built on 2026-10-23 as MF-65 to MF-72 (`components/ortho-sources.js` and `ortho-coverage.js`, written by `tools/ortho_coverage.py`; `field-render.js`, `field-map.js`, `api.py`'s headers; browser tests with every service mocked, each country's source, its tiles, its credits and the border case; the services themselves checked by hand on 2026-10-23, not yet from the page in a browser); MF-68 to MF-70 are **for the maintainer** (each credit's wording). The candidates MF-72 left unchecked were checked on 2026-10-23 and built as MF-73 to MF-80 (Belgium by region, Luxembourg, Switzerland with Liechtenstein, eleven German Länder, Poland, Czechia and France overseas added to `ortho-sources.js`; `tools/ortho_coverage.py` reading Natural Earth's admin-1 too; `api.py`'s headers; browser tests with every service mocked: each new source, its tiles, its credits and the policy; the services themselves checked by hand with CORS requests, not yet from the page in a browser); MF-74 to MF-77 are **for the maintainer** (each credit's wording, Belgium's choice of sources, the years in credits); MF-80 lists what was left out and why.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MF-0 | "We should also be able to mark other stuff on the map besides the boxes locations, like: safe zone(s), respawn (per team), parking lot, first aid stations, objectives, headquarter (per team), etc." | Built as MF-1 to MF-16. | Players and staff need to know where the safe zone, the respawns and first aid are, on the same plan as the boxes. | maintainer (2026-10-22): requested |
| MF-1 | What is a feature? | A type (`safe_zone`, `respawn`, `hq`, `parking`, `first_aid`, `objective`, `other`; `other` needs a label), a free label (48 characters, one line: an ATAK callsign's length), one shape, a level, an optional team, a visibility and an optional note (200 characters, one line). Shapes, as the maintainer answered: a **point** (a pin at `x`, `y`), a **circle** (a pin and `radius_m`, 1-2000 m) or a **polygon** (3 to 12 `points` traced on the plan, refused if its area is empty); coordinates in the plan's own space (0-1 across and down), as the boxes' positions. At most 200 features on a field. | One model for every kind of mark; the plan's coordinates work with or without a map position and on every level. 12 corners keep a polygon's CoT event in one UDP datagram (MF-11); a zone's outline rarely needs more. | maintainer (2026-10-22): shapes as answered; the limits open |
| MF-2 | Field or game? | Field-level only, like the plans: features stay from game to game (store schema 11, table `map_features`). A game or a scenario carrying its own features is not built; if wanted, a scenario's manifest could list features in a later round, shown only during its games. | The safe zone, the car park and first aid belong to the field; per-game marks can come when a scenario needs them, with their own visibility rules (P4R24). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-3 | Who may see a feature? | Per feature, as the maintainer answered: `everyone` (any token, the read token's console and scoreboards included), `team` (that team's players, and the organisers) or `organisers` (staff only: the GM token and the marshal and gm player tokens, `visibility.is_staff`). Checked on the controller on every path: REST (`GET /api/v1/field/features`), the stream (MF-10) and ATAK (MF-12); never only in the page. | The maintainer's answer; one rule in one function (`map_features.can_see`), tested per path. | maintainer (2026-10-22): per feature, as built |
| MF-4 | Defaults by type. | A feature with a team is that team's (`team`), any other everyone's; the console gives a respawn or HQ the first team of the live game (else `red`) and so `team`, and a safe zone, the car park, first aid and objectives `everyone`. Objectives default to everyone. A respawn may also have no team (a shared respawn, everyone). The controller applies the same default when `visibility` is not given. | The maintainer's defaults; objectives that are secret belong to a scenario (P4R24), where visibility already exists, so a field's objectives are public by default. | maintainer (2026-10-22): defaults as answered; objectives: Claude's call, for review |
| MF-5 | **For the maintainer:** how is a feature's team named, and checked? | By the team's name as games name teams (`red`, `blue`...; 1-23 bytes, the boxes' limit), checked for its form only. The field has no team list of its own (teams are per game; team presets are display names), so a feature for a team no game has is seen only by the organisers until a game has that team. A player sees a `team` feature when their check-in's team in their game has that name. The console offers the live game's teams, else `red` and `blue`. | Games name sides by colour, and a field's respawns usually belong to a colour; tying features to one game's team list would lose them at the next game. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-6 | A circle's radius without a map position. | The radius is metres on the ground; the console draws it to the plan's scale from its map position (the corners' distances, so a stretched plan draws an ellipse); without one the GM types the radius and the circle is drawn dashed at a set size (4 % of the plan's width), and the editor says so. Pressing at the centre and dragging to the edge sets the radius only with a scale. | Metres are what a marshal measures and what ATAK needs; a scale guessed from nothing would mislead. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-7 | **For the maintainer:** Portuguese names. | *Marcações no mapa* (the panel), *Zona segura*, *Respawn* (untranslated: what Portuguese players say, "ir ao respawn"; the mode keeps its name *Reentradas e Vidas*), *Base* for HQ (players say "base" more than "QG"), *Estacionamento*, *Primeiros socorros*, *Objetivo*, *Outro*; shapes *Ponto*, *Círculo*, *Área*; who sees it *Todos*, *Uma equipa* / *Só {equipa}*, *Só a organização*. English: *Map features*, *Safe zone*, *Respawn*, *HQ*, *Car park*, *First aid*, *Objective*, *Other*. | What players say, gender-neutral; "a organização" is the console's word for staff. "Respawn" next to the mode's *Reentradas* is the one inconsistency to confirm. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-8 | Levels. | A feature is on one level's plan (a level in use when written). Fewer levels delete the features of the levels that go, with their plans (the console's confirmation now says so, and asks also when a level going has features but no plan); levels off keep every level's features but show only level 0's, everywhere (REST, stream, ATAK), and bring the others back when levels are on again. A new plan uploaded on a level keeps its features (they may need moving). | As the plans and the boxes (ML-3); a feature drawn on floor 2 means nothing on the ground floor's plan, so it is never moved to level 0. Keeping features over a new plan avoids losing them to a re-upload of the same drawing. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-9 | The API. | `GET /api/v1/field/features` (any token: filtered, MF-3), `POST /api/v1/field/features` (GM, 201), `PUT` and `DELETE /api/v1/field/features/{id}` (GM; PUT replaces the whole feature). Marshal and gm player tokens read everything but do not edit (as the field image). Errors 400 (with the field), 404, 409 at 200 features. | The field image's model (GM writes, every token reads), with the filter added. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-10 | The live stream. | A new message `{"type": "map_features", "features": [...]}`, the whole list each connection may see, sent to every connection whenever a feature is added, changed or deleted or the levels change (the routing mark `_map_features`, resolved per viewer in `api.make_route`; `ws.default_route` drops it). The hello is unchanged: the console and the app fetch the list over REST when they connect. | The list is small (200 at most) and whole lists cannot leak by a missed delete; an unchanged hello keeps the shared contract with the phone controller as it is. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-11 | ATAK: which CoT types? | A point: a spot marker (`b-m-p-s-m`, as the boxes). A circle: ATAK's drawn circle `u-d-c-c`, centre as the point, `<shape><ellipse major minor angle='360'>` with the radius in metres and the style link of TAK's example. A polygon: ATAK's drawn shape `u-d-f`, its corners as `<link point='lat,lon'/>` with the first again at the end (how ATAK writes a closed shape). Both with `strokeColor`, `strokeWeight`, `fillColor` (translucent), `contact`, `remarks` (type, team, who sees it, radius, level, note) and `labels_on`. Colours: the team's, else the type's (as the console's legend). Sources in `protocol/vectors/cot.json`: the TAK examples "Drawing Shapes - Circle.cot" and "Free Form.cot", `shape.xsd`, and ATAK's `DrawingCircleImporter` (major/minor read as the radius) and `EditablePolyline` (closed shapes repeat the first point). Placed by the feature's level's georeference (ML-6); a plan without one sends nothing, with the existing `no_georef` status; the status counts `features`. Sent only while a game is live, as the boxes. | ATAK's own shapes, so a safe zone shows as an area and not a dot; all citable. Without a live game the emitter sends nothing at all, and features follow it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-12 | **For the maintainer:** ATAK and visibility. | The field's Wi-Fi (multicast) gets only features for `everyone`; the staff ATAK devices (`staff_endpoints`, TCP) get every feature, a team's and the organisers' included, with "team only" or "organisers only" in the remarks. A feature deleted, moved off the map or no longer for everyone is dropped (stale before start) on the transports that had it. | Anyone on the field's Wi-Fi can read the multicast group, and the feed is not per team, so a team's respawn there would reach the other team; the staff's devices see what the organisers see, as player positions (M9-6). Per-team ATAK feeds (one endpoint list per team) could come later if wanted. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-13 | ATAK through the site server's relay. | The relay gets the pins and circles for everyone, a circle as a spot marker with its radius as the circular error (the extraction zone's form), never an area nor a team's or the organisers' feature; every event passes `sync_format.cot_event` unchanged, so the site server and `protocol/sync.md` are unchanged. | Widening the relay's narrow form (a security decision, FU5-17) for drawn shapes is its own change; the relay's devices are enrolled staff, but their certificates are not split by team. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-14 | **For the maintainer:** the Android controller. | Not on the phone controller yet: it has no field plan at all (no `/api/v1/field/*`; PE24-6), so map features join it with the field plan in slice S7, with shared contract cases then. No case is added to `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` now; the shared hello is unchanged (MF-10), so the two controllers' contract stays consistent. The app's player screens do not show features yet either. | A contract case for features would fail on a phone that has no plan to put them on; the plan and its features belong together. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-15 | Store migration. | Schema 11: a new table `map_features` (`id`, `level` 0-9, `data_json`, `created_at`, `updated_at`), created empty on a schema 10 file; nothing else changes. A controller of an earlier version opening a schema 11 file ignores the table. (A change in parallel that also bumps the schema would need renumbering.) | The JSON column keeps the shapes' different fields in one row; the level is a column so levels that go can be deleted in SQL. Tested by turning a schema 11 file back into a schema 10 one and opening it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-16 | **For the maintainer:** site server and privacy. | Features stay on the controller: not synced (as the plans, ML-10), not on MQTT, not in the remote GM's live view. | MF-17 | Found on the bench: the GM chose *Área*, then the type, and the safe zone's usual circle came back (a press on the plan then set a centre and radius). Which wins, the shape or the type's usual shape? | The shape the GM chose: the type picks the shape only until the GM chooses one or places something. | The form's order (type, then shape) is not the order a GM works in; a choice made on purpose must not be undone by another field. Tested in Chromium (an area chosen before a respawn). | Built |
| MF-18 | The maintainer's idea (2026-10-22, for later, mostly for milsim fields): pick the field on a map and load it as the field image directly; and draw the field's real outline, since few fields are a perfect four-sided area. | Built (MF-19 to MF-30). **Sources (the maintainer's choice):** in Portugal, the DGT orthophoto as the base with OpenStreetMap's paths, buildings and names drawn over it ("Ortofotomapas © DGT (CC BY 4.0)" and "© OpenStreetMap contributors" on the image); elsewhere, OpenStreetMap alone (ODbL allows keeping it offline with its attribution); more countries' open orthophotos can be added the same way. **A one-off fetch** while the GM sets the game up: the GM opens the map, finds the field and defines it; the controller fetches only that area, once (OSM's own tile servers forbid bulk or offline prefetching: a configurable tile server whose policy allows it, or the image drawn from OSM data for the area; to be chosen when built). **Not Google Maps:** its terms forbid storing or exporting its imagery. **Flow:** in the console, with internet while the GM sets up the game, *Load the field from a map*: find the field, draw its **outline corner by corner** (as an area, MF-0's editor); the controller fetches the outline's bounding rectangle once, stores it as the field image with its attribution, sets its map position (ATAK) from the rectangle's corners (no corners typed, GR-1), and keeps the outline as the **field boundary**: a new map feature type, one per field (or per level), for everyone, the outside dimmed in the console, a closed shape on ATAK, and later an out-of-bounds warning on players' phones. | The field has no internet during a game, so the image must be kept, and only openly licensed maps may be. An image is a rectangle (screens, ATAK), but a field is not: the boundary carries the real shape. | Built as MF-19 to MF-30 (2026-10-22); the out-of-bounds warning on phones is left for later (MF-30) |
| MF-19 | What was built of MF-18. | The field boundary as a map feature type, drawn by hand or loaded with the plan; and *Carregar o campo de um mapa / Load the field from a map*: find the field, trace its outline, the GM's browser draws the image from OpenStreetMap data (over the DGT orthophoto in Portugal), one request to the controller sets the plan, its map position and its boundary. In the controller (`map_features.py`, `field_map.py`, `store.py`, `api.py`, `atak.py`) and the console (`field-map.html`, `field-map.js`, `components/tile-map.js`, `components/field-render.js`, `map-features.js`, `field-view.js`); tested with unit and API tests (`tests/test_field_map.py`) and in Chromium with the three map services mocked (Playwright routes, fixtures in `controller/console/tests/fixtures/`). Not yet tried against the live services from a browser nor at a real field. | The maintainer's choices, as recorded in MF-18, with the coordinator's design direction. | Built |
| MF-20 | The field boundary type. | `boundary` (*Limite do campo* / *Field boundary*, lime `#84cc16`, a corners icon in the legend): a `polygon` only (3-12 corners), never a team's (a team or `team` visibility is refused), `everyone` by default (`organisers` allowed), at most one per level (POST or PUT of a second: 409; the console offers the type only when the level has none). The console dims the plan outside it (an even-odd path under the other shapes) and draws its outline dashed, unfilled. Like every feature it stays on the controller (MF-16) and goes with its level (MF-8). | One outline per plan is what "the field" means; a team's boundary has no meaning; the same model, API and checks as the other features. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-21 | **For the maintainer:** finding the field: which map? | OpenStreetMap's standard tiles (`tile.openstreetmap.org`), for interactive viewing only, in a **hand-written** slippy map (`components/tile-map.js`, about 250 lines: drag, wheel, + and −, arrow keys, a press for a corner, an SVG layer for the outline, the attribution link always shown) rather than a vendored Leaflet. Only the tiles of the current view are requested (no prefetching, no offline copy), the browser's cache is left to the tiles' headers, and each tile is requested with the page's origin as its Referer (`referrerPolicy` on the tile, since the console otherwise sends no Referer). No place search (a geocoder would be a fourth service): the GM pans, types "latitude, longitude" to go to, or the map opens on the level's current map position. (Place search and the GM's location added on 2026-10-23 at the maintainer's request: MF-41 to MF-49.) | The OSM tile usage policy allows "normal interactive viewing by a human where the client requests only the tiles needed for the current viewport", asks for visible attribution and a valid Referer from browsers, and forbids bulk download, prefetching and offline use (https://operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/tiles/, read 2026-10-22; a request without a Referer was answered with `x-blocked: Access denied`). Leaflet would have meant ~150 KB of vendored code, its CSS and images and a REUSE entry for what this page needs: no layers, no drawing plugin. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-22 | Who fetches the area, and how much? | The GM's **browser**, once, when the GM presses *Draw the field image*: the outline's bounding box plus a margin (10 %, at least 20 m on each side), refused above 4 km on its longer side; one Overpass API query (`https://overpass-api.de/api/interpreter`, `[out:json][timeout:25][maxsize:33554432]`, ways and relations of highways, buildings, natural, landuse, leisure, waterways and barriers and named nodes in the box, `out geom`). Errors in words: 429/502/503/504 or a timeout remark *busy, try again in a minute*; a maxsize or memory remark *too much map data*; no network *no internet*. No automatic retries. The controller never goes to the internet for this. | The field controller is usually offline at the field; the GM's phone or laptop has internet while setting up. The public Overpass instance asks for under about 10,000 requests and 1 GB a day per user and answers 429 when its rate limit is hit (https://dev.overpass-api.de/overpass-doc/en/preface/commons.html, read 2026-10-22); one query per field set-up is far below that. The endpoint sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (checked 2026-10-22). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-23 | What the image shows. | Drawn by the browser from OSM data on a canvas (not copied from OSM's tiles): woods, scrub, grass, farmland, built-up land, bare ground, wetland, water and buildings filled; roads (major and minor, cased), tracks and paths dashed, streams and rivers, fences, walls and hedges in widths in metres (with a floor in pixels); names of roads along their longest segment, of areas at their middle and of named points. Over the orthophoto only the lines, the buildings' and water's outlines and the names, in bright colours with dark edges, so the photo stays readable. The attribution is burned into the bottom-right corner on a light box: "© OpenStreetMap contributors", and with the orthophoto "Ortofotomapas © DGT (CC BY 4.0) · © OpenStreetMap contributors". The outline itself is not drawn into the image (it is the boundary, MF-20). PNG for a drawn map, JPEG (0.85, then 0.7) over the photo; smaller by 25 % until under the 4 MB field image limit. On ATAK the boundary is a `u-d-f` closed shape with a fully transparent fill (`BOUNDARY_FILL_ALPHA` 0), so the field inside stays readable. | What a marshal needs to find a place on the field: paths, buildings, cover, water, fences; ODbL allows a produced work like this with its attribution. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-24 | The image's projection, and how exact is its map position? | North-up and **equirectangular over the area**: latitude linear down the image and longitude linear across it, pixels square on the ground at the area's middle latitude (about 0.5 m per pixel, coarser to stay within 4096 pixels on a side). The map position is the existing two-corner georeference with the image's edges as its corners (`north, west` at 0, 0 and `south, east` at 1, 1), and georef.py's two-corner map is linear in latitude and longitude, so image and map position agree **exactly** (the tests check the outline's corners back through the map position to under a millimetre). The only distortion is in the pixels' shape away from the middle latitude: under 0.05 % across 4 km (about 1 m at the image's top and bottom edges at worst), in the picture, not in the positions. The DGT orthophoto is requested in EPSG:4326 for the same box, so it lands in the same frame. A Web Mercator image would put latitude off linear by about 0.4 % over 1 km at 40° N (about 1 m), which the two-corner map cannot represent. | Exact by construction, with no new georeference kind. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-25 | **For the maintainer:** the DGT orthophoto: which service, CORS, licence? | `https://ortos.dgterritorio.gov.pt/wms/ortosat2023`, layer `ortoSat2023-CorVerdadeira` (OrtoSat 2023, 30 cm, Pléiades Neo satellite images of mainland Portugal), WMS 1.3.0 GetMap in EPSG:4326, JPEG, at the image's size (its capabilities allow up to 4096 × 4096; "Fees: no conditions apply", "AccessConstraints: None"). It sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (checked 2026-10-22 on GetCapabilities and GetMap), so the browser fetches it with `fetch()` and the canvas stays exportable: no controller-side fetch was needed. Ticked by default when the area is inside the layer's bounding box (lat 36.7643-42.2796, lon -10.1934 to -5.70954); the GM can untick it; a picture that is over 90 % white (the WMS's answer outside its coverage, e.g. in Spain, which that box partly includes) is dropped and the image drawn from OSM alone, with a notice; a failed request does the same. **Licence:** DGT's open data page says its geographic data is under CC BY 4.0 with the DGT credited as owner (https://www.dgterritorio.gov.pt/dados-abertos, read 2026-10-22), and its older orthophoto datasets on dados.gov.pt are marked CC BY 4.0; no page found states the licence of the OrtoSat 2023 WMS itself (a 2018 third-party catalogue entry listed DGT's WMS services as CC BY-NC 4.0). The attribution used is the maintainer's "Ortofotomapas © DGT (CC BY 4.0)"; the maintainer should confirm OrtoSat 2023's terms with the DGT. The endpoint and layer are constants in `field-render.js` (and the origin in `api.py FIELD_MAP_ORIGINS`), so a newer orthophoto is a two-line change. | The maintainer's choice of source; CORS made the simplest design possible. | maintainer (2026-10-23): CC BY 4.0 fits (commercial use with attribution); the orthophoto stays on by default with DGT's credit on the image; confirm OrtoSat 2023's terms with DGT when convenient |
| MF-26 | One request, all or nothing. | `POST /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/from-map` (GM) with `{content_type, image (base64), bounds, outline, label?}`: everything is checked first (`field_map.check`: PNG or JPEG by its first bytes, at most 4 MB, bounds that make a valid two-corner map, 3-12 outline corners inside the bounds, the boundary as `map_features.check` takes it), then the plan, the level's own map position (`field.georef` or `field.georef.<n>`) and the boundary are written in **one SQLite transaction** (`store.set_field_from_map`, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` ... `COMMIT`, rolled back on any error, tested by failing its last write). The level's boundary is replaced, not added (MF-20); a new one past 200 features is 409. The stream then gets `field_image`, `field_georef` and `map_features`. The image's corners are not sent as typed points: the server derives them from `bounds`, and the boundary's plan coordinates from the same map (`x = (lon - west) / (east - west)`, `y = (north - lat) / (north - south)`). Base64 in JSON costs a third more bytes (at most about 5.6 MB) but keeps one plain JSON call. | Three separate calls (image, georef, feature) could stop half-way and leave a new image with the old map position (which the upload clears) or no boundary. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-27 | **For the maintainer:** the Content-Security-Policy, and a page rather than a dialog. | The console's policy is unchanged (`default-src 'self'`, same-origin only). The field-map page is **its own page**, `field-map.html` (opened from the Field tab like the check-in poster, back to `#field` when done), served with its own policy (`api.py FIELD_MAP_HEADERS`): the same, plus `img-src https://tile.openstreetmap.org` and `connect-src https://overpass-api.de https://ortos.dgterritorio.gov.pt` (and, since MF-45, `https://nominatim.openstreetmap.org`); `script-src` and `style-src` stay `'self'` (no inline code, no CDN), `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` for the page (the tiles alone send the origin, MF-21). `test_console.py` checks the exact policy, that every other console file gets the self-only one, and that the scripts name no other origin. A dialog inside the console was the coordinator's suggestion; a page was chosen because a CSP is per document: to allow those origins in a dialog, the whole console (the GM token, the live game, the check-in names) would have had to allow them. | Least privilege: the internet is reachable only from the one page that needs it, only while it is open, and only to three named origins; with scripts same-origin only, an injection elsewhere in the console cannot send data out. The same page could later move into an iframe of the console if a dialog is preferred. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-28 | **For the maintainer:** a level that already has a plan. | Loading from a map replaces the level's plan and map position (said before *Use as the field*: *The boxes and the map features keep their places on the image and may need moving*), as uploading a new plan does (MF-8); the boxes' positions and the other features are not moved. A level that shares level 0's map position (ML-6) gets its own. | Moving features from the old image to the new one would need the old image's map position, which a plan without one does not have; MF-8 already keeps features over a new plan. Moving them when both have one could come later. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-29 | Accessibility, languages, phone width. | The map is focusable (`role=application`, labelled, its help text linked), panned with the arrow keys and zoomed with + and − or labelled buttons; a cross marks its centre and *Add a corner at the cross* adds it; corners can be typed as "latitude, longitude"; the corner count is a live status; errors are alerts. Every string in pt-PT and English (*Carregar o campo de um mapa*, *Limite do campo*, *Ortofotomapa da DGT*, *Juntar um canto na cruz*...); the page checked at 390 px wide with no sideways scroll. Offline, the page says it needs internet and that the rest of the console does not. | As the console's other panels (ML-12, MF-16). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-30 | Later, and left out. | Recorded, not built: an **out-of-bounds warning on players' phones** (the app would test the player's position against the level's boundary, with the core's `geo.in_zone`, once the app shows the field plan; built on 2026-10-22 as MF-31 to MF-40, without the app showing the plan); the Android controller has no field plan yet (MF-14); place search (built on 2026-10-23 as MF-44 to MF-46); other countries' open orthophotos (a WMS with CORS added to `field-render.js` and to the page's policy); burning the outline or the boxes into the image. Features and plans are still not synced to a site server (MF-16). | The maintainer asked for the warning later; the rest has no request yet. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-31 | The out-of-bounds warning: where is the check made, and from what? | **On the phone**, from what the player's token may already read: `GET /api/v1/field/features` (the `boundary` features this token may see, levels in use only) and `GET /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/georef` for each level with a boundary (its map position's corners; georef.py's map is linear in latitude and longitude, so three corners give it exactly), fetched when the player turns position sharing on and again when the stream's `map_features` message changes the list (MF-10). The fixes are **the ones the phone already takes to share its position** (`PositionReporter.onFix`), at the interval the controller sets (10 s by default): no fix is taken for the warning alone, and a player who does not share gets no warning (while sharing on a field with a boundary, the switch says the phone vibrates). Inside and outside are the core's ray casting (`atalaia_geo_in_polygon`, geo.c) written again in Dart on the corners' latitudes and longitudes, checked against geo.json's polygon cases; the distance to the line in metres in a local flat plane (east = R cos(lat) dlon, north = R dlat, R = 6,371 km), as georef.py fits its map. Code: `app/lib/position/field_bounds.dart`. | No position or state goes anywhere new (MF-39); the boundary and the map position were already readable by any token (MF-3, ML-1). The player role does not load the core (FFI is for props), and one polygon test is a few lines, kept identical to geo.c by the shared vectors. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-32 | GPS error at the edge: margin, accuracy, dwell. | A fix is **judged** only when its reported accuracy is 50 m or better (a fix without one is taken as 20 m); it counts as **outside** only when it is further out than its own accuracy and than 10 m; the player is **out** when the fixes have stayed outside for 5 s (with the 10 s reports, the second fix in a row), and **back** on the first judged fix inside the boundary or on its line. Between the line and the margin nothing changes (the hysteresis band): an out player stays out, an in player stays in. A fix inside before the dwell starts it over. Constants of `BoundsWatch`, not settings. | A phone's fix near trees and walls is often 5-15 m off; warning only past the fix's own error and the margin, and only on two fixes in a row, keeps a player walking along the edge from being buzzed, while one fix back inside clears the warning at once. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-33 | **For the maintainer:** what the player sees, feels and hears. | A **banner** across the top of the player's view, in the error colour, with an icon: *Fora do campo* / *Out of bounds* and *Saíste do limite do campo. Volta para dentro.* / *You have left the field's boundary. Go back inside.*, a screen-reader live region (announced when it appears); a **vibration** of three long pulses (600 ms) when the player leaves, repeated every 60 s while still out, and one short pulse (200 ms) on coming back; the sharing **notification** (the foreground service's, shown on the lock screen) says *Fora do campo* while out, then *A partilhar a posição* again. **No sound**: a tone could give a player's place away. While sharing on a field with a boundary, the switch's subtitle adds *Este telemóvel vibra se saíres do limite do campo.* | A phone in a pocket is felt before it is seen; the notification tells a player who looks with the screen locked; words, colour and an icon, not colour alone. Silence is the airsoft norm; a field that wants a sound could ask for one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-34 | A player whose level is not known, on a multi-level field. | The player's level is the one the controller keeps for them (ML-5: their own position in the stream carries it once a report gave it; the app does not send one yet). With a known level, that level's boundary only (a level without a boundary: no warning there). With the level **not known**, every level's boundary, and the player is out only when **outside all of them**. A boundary whose level has no map position is left out (it cannot be placed on the ground). | A floor is never guessed (ML-5); a building's floors share their footprint and frame (ML-6), so "outside every floor's outline" is the honest test, and it is the same as one outline on a single-level field. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-35 | When the boundary changes during a game. | The stream's `map_features` (sent to players since MF-10) makes the phone fetch the map positions again with the new list; an empty list or no boundary left ends a warning without a buzz. A map position changed alone is not pushed to players (`field_georef` is not a player stream message) and is picked up the next time the features change or sharing is turned on; loading the field from a map sends `map_features` too (MF-26). | The boundary is what changes in practice; adding `field_georef` to the player stream would change the shared contract with the phone controller for a rare case. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-36 | **For the maintainer:** are the staff told? | **Not in this round.** An event such as `player.out_of_bounds` was considered and left out: events reach every player's stream (only `gm_only` data is hidden, M5-1), so the other team would learn that someone is out, and when; the game log keeps events after the game, while positions are deleted at its end (ADR-P3-6); and it would be a new standard event (an id in `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, protogen, the shared vectors) that the phone controller must serve too. Staff who need it already see every shared position (marshals and the GM, ADR-P3-6). If wanted later: the controller tests the reports it already receives against the same boundary and sends a staff-only stream message (`_staff`, not an event, not logged), shown once the console shows players on the plan. | Only if cheap and private enough, as asked; it is neither yet. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-37 | Who is warned, and when. | Players, squad leaders and VIPs (and a check-in whose role the phone does not know) while the game is **running**; not marshals, the GM's phone or spectators (staff walk outside the field: the car park, the safe zone). A game paused, ended or not started ends a warning without a buzz. | The warning is a rule of play; the staff are not playing. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-38 | **For the maintainer:** can a game turn the warning off? | **No new setting.** There is no per-game settings model (a game has its mode's parameters, checked by each mode's own schema, and the `squads` column), and adding one means a store change, the console's game form and the phone controller. The switch is the boundary's own **visibility**: a boundary for *everyone* warns the players; set to *only the organisers*, players do not receive it and are not warned (the staff and the staff's ATAK devices still see it). A field without a boundary warns nobody. The player has no switch of their own; turning sharing off ends it. | Uses what exists, with no new state to keep in sync; a per-game switch can join a game-settings model when one is made. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-39 | Privacy and battery. | Nothing about the warning leaves the phone: no event, no report field, no request (the widget test checks that only position reports are sent). No new position sharing: the phone tests its own fixes, which it sends only if the player shares, as before. No extra GPS: the warning reads the reporter's fixes (no second listener, no higher rate). Network: one request for the features and one per level with a boundary, when sharing starts and when the features change. | Constraint 9 and ADR-P3-6 unchanged; the phone already had both the boundary (MF-3) and its own position. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-40 | The Android controller, and what is not done. | On a field whose controller is a phone (PE24), `GET /api/v1/field/features` does not exist yet (no field plan on the phone, MF-14): the request fails, no boundary is known and nobody is warned; it works there once the field plan joins the phone controller (slice S7). The player's view still does not draw the plan or the features (MF-14). Found on the way: the player screen looked up the app's services in `dispose` (a debug assertion when leaving it while sharing); it keeps them from `initState` now. Not tried on a real phone at a real field (the fix's accuracy there, the vibration in a pocket, the notification with the screen off). | Nothing on the phone controller had to change; the warning follows the plan when it arrives. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| MF-41 | The maintainer's request (2026-10-23): "center the map on our current location, so we don't need to keep zooming a lot on OSM to locate where we are; would also be nice to be able to type a location". *Use my location*: how? | A button *Usar a minha localização / Use my location* beside *Search* on the field-map page asks the browser for **one fix** (`navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition`, `enableHighAccuracy`, a 20 s timeout, `maximumAge: 0`: a fresh position, no tracking) and moves the map there at zoom 17 (where a field is recognisable; clamped to the tile server's 19), saying how accurate the fix was. Only in a **secure context**: `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` on the controller's own computer and the field's HTTPS address are; plain HTTP on the field's Wi-Fi is not, and the page then says so in words (open it over https:// or on this computer, or search by name). A denial, no position and a timeout are each said in words (pt-PT, en, de, zh-tw), each pointing to the search and typed coordinates. | Browsers give the location only to secure contexts; one fix is all the map needs (`components/place-search.js` `locate`). | open |
| MF-42 | **For the maintainer:** centre on the GM's location by itself when the page opens? | Only when **both**: the level has no map position (a level's map position still wins, as before) and the browser **already allows** this page the location (`navigator.permissions.query({name: 'geolocation'})` says `granted`, where the Permissions API exists). Otherwise nothing is asked on open: no permission prompt the GM did not ask for; the first press of *Use my location* asks, and from then on the browser remembers its answer for that address. If the GM moves the map before the fix arrives, the GM's move wins. | A prompt on every open is noise, and a GM who said yes once wants the map where they stand. | open |
| MF-43 | **For the maintainer:** does the page need a `Permissions-Policy` header? | Strictly no: geolocation's default allowlist is `self`, so a top-level page may already ask. It is now said explicitly: the field-map page gets `Permissions-Policy: geolocation=(self)` (`api.py FIELD_MAP_HEADERS`) and **every other console file gets `geolocation=()`** (`SECURITY_HEADERS`, and the phone controller's copy in `app/lib/controller/console_files.dart`), so the GM console, the scoreboard and the poster cannot ask for a location even with an injected script. `test_console.py` checks both headers; the browser test checks the browser's own answer (`document.featurePolicy.allowsFeature('geolocation')`: no on the console, yes on the field-map page). | Least privilege, as the CSP (MF-27): the location is reachable only from the one page that uses it. | open |
| MF-44 | **For the maintainer:** typing a place's name: which service, and its usage policy. | **OpenStreetMap's Nominatim** (`https://nominatim.openstreetmap.org/search?format=jsonv2&q=...&limit=5&accept-language=...`), asked by the GM's browser. Its usage policy (https://operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/nominatim/, read 2026-10-23), and how it is met: **no search as you type** (forbidden there): a request only when the GM presses *Search* or Enter; **at most 1 request a second**: the page waits until a second has passed since the previous answer, one search at a time (the button is disabled while one runs); **results cached on our side**: the same question (same language, spacing and case aside) is answered from the page's memory, never sent twice while the page is open; **identify the application**: browsers cannot set a User-Agent, so the request carries a **Referer** with the page's origin only (`referrerPolicy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` on that request, as for the tiles, MF-21; the page's own `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` stays); **attribution**: shown under the results (*Nominatim do OpenStreetMap; dados ODbL, © OpenStreetMap contributors* with the copyright link); a refusal (403, 429, 503) is said in words, and coordinates and *Use my location* keep working. **Not met, to decide:** (1) the Referer is the controller's address (`http://127.0.0.1:8080/`, `https://10.42.0.1/`...), which identifies a controller but not "Atalaia"; Nominatim's `email=` parameter could name the project, but needs an address the maintainer chooses; (2) the policy asks that an app can be switched off at the OSMF's request **without a software update**: controllers have no phone-home by design, so the only switch is the OSMF blocking the requests (the page then says the search refused); a controller setting to turn place search off or point it at another Nominatim instance (then also the CSP) could be added; (3) the policy says LLM-written code may use the public Nominatim only where the developer made a deliberate, informed decision: this was written by an AI model at the maintainer's request and needs the maintainer's own decision to keep it. A proxy through the controller (the policy's suggestion) was not chosen: the controller needs no internet (MF-22). | The maintainer asked for typing a location; Nominatim is OSM's own geocoder (the map is OSM's), needs no key, sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (checked 2026-10-23), and a GM's few searches while setting up a game are the "directly triggered by the end-user" use the policy allows. | maintainer (2026-10-23): the public Nominatim, decided knowingly after reading its usage policy (including its rule on AI-written code); the requests also carry email=falecom@andersonleite.me (Nominatim's own way to name a heavy user) |
| MF-45 | The Content-Security-Policy (MF-27, amended). | The field-map page's `connect-src` gains `https://nominatim.openstreetmap.org` (`FIELD_MAP_ORIGINS['search']`), that page only; scripts and styles stay `'self'`; every other console file keeps the same-origin policy. `test_console.py` checks the exact policy, that no other page names any of the four origins, and that the page's scripts (now also `components/place-search.js`) name no other. | Least privilege, as MF-27: a fourth named origin, reachable only from the page that needs it. | open |
| MF-46 | The results, and typed coordinates. | "latitude, longitude" in the same box goes straight there at zoom 17, **without a request**, as before. Anything else is a name: up to 5 results (`limit=5`, and cut to 5 in the page), each checked (a result without a valid position is left out), shown as a list of buttons with Nominatim's full name (`display_name`, wrapped at phone width), reachable with Tab and chosen with Enter or a press; choosing one fits the map to the place's `boundingbox` (the closest zoom showing all of it, at most 17), or centres it at 17 without one, says *On the map: ...* and moves the keyboard focus to the map. Names come in the page's language (`accept-language`: `pt-PT,pt`, `en`, `de`, `zh-TW,zh`). Nothing found and an empty box are said in words. | A field is often in a village or a forest: the place's box gets the GM close, the map does the rest. | open |
| MF-47 | Privacy. | The GM's **position never leaves the browser**: it only moves the map (no request, no stored value; the browser test checks that no request carries it, to the controller or anywhere). The **name typed** goes to Nominatim, and the page says so beside the box (*O nome que procuras é enviado à pesquisa Nominatim do OpenStreetMap...*), with only the page's origin as the Referer; coordinates typed there are never sent. Nothing about either reaches the field controller. | The maintainer's rule for the field map: the controller needs no internet, and the GM's whereabouts are no one's business. | open |
| MF-48 | Accessibility, languages, phone width. | The search box is a labelled `type=search` field in a `role=search` form, with the privacy note linked by `aria-describedby`; the results are a labelled list of buttons; statuses are live regions, errors alerts; the focus goes to the map after a choice. Every string in pt-PT (gender-neutral, *tu*), English, German (*du*) and Traditional Chinese (both AI drafts, not reviewed). Checked at 390 px wide with a very long result name: no sideways scroll. | As MF-29. | open |
| MF-49 | Later, and left out. | Not built: search as you type (forbidden by the policy); a "you are here" marker or following the GM's position as they walk (one fix is enough to find the field); reverse geocoding (naming the place the map shows); keeping searches across page loads; a setting to turn place search off or use another Nominatim instance (MF-44 (2)); a `User-Agent`/`email=` identification (MF-44 (1)). | No request for them; the first two policy points wait for the maintainer. | open |
| MF-50 | The maintainer's real use (2026-10-23): `https://overpass-api.de/api/interpreter` answered `502 ... This Overpass API server is currently at capacity ... Retry your request using exponential backoff`. How hard does the page try? | `fetchOsm` (`components/field-render.js`) retries on 429, 502, 503, 504, on a "busy" remark in a 200 answer (`timed out`, `rate_limited`, `too busy`, `capacity`; `out of memory`/`maxsize` stay *too much data*, the area's fault) and on no readable answer (MF-52). Each instance, after a busy answer, rests an **exponential backoff with jitter**: 2 s doubling per try up to 16 s, and a random half of it ("equal jitter", so pages that met the same overload do not come back together); after a **429 at least 30 s** (the main instance's own rule on the OSM wiki) or its `Retry-After` when readable. Meanwhile the next free instance of MF-51 is asked. At most **6 requests and about 60 s** (`RETRY`): no new try starts once the time is used up; then *busy* or *unreachable* is said in words. Only one request at a time, nothing in parallel. | The main instance asks for exactly this ("exponential backoff"); the OSM wiki says it is "overloaded ... do not expect high reliability" and asks for a 30 s pause after a 429. A GM setting up a game will wait a minute, not five. | open |
| MF-51 | **For the maintainer:** which other Overpass instances, and in what order? | After `overpass-api.de`, only **`overpass.private.coffee`** (Private.coffee, Austria; the OSM wiki's list: "Feel free to use our service in any project, there is no rate limit in place", "please notify us in advance if you intend to use our service in a large scale project"; `overpass.kumi.systems` is its former name, not added separately). It sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (checked from here 2026-10-23 with a test query), needs no key and is public; only its origin joins the page's `connect-src` (MF-27). Its data was **three months old** when checked (`timestamp_osm_base` 2026-07-24 on 2026-10-23), so a recent path may be missing: when the data came from it, the page says so and that its data may be a few months old. **Considered and left out, pending the maintainer:** VK Maps' instance (`maps.mail.ru/osm/tools/overpass`, Russia; the wiki: "Feel free to use our services in any project. There are currently no requests limitations"; CORS `*`, current data): it would send the field's area (its box only: no token, no name, no Referer) to a server in Russia, which needs the maintainer's explicit choice (the coordinator's review, 2026-10-23). The instances behind a key or payment (Geofabrik, FairwayMapper, Tracestrack, NextGIS, Overpass.ch, ...) and the regional ones (Switzerland, Britain and Ireland, ...) are not used. **To decide:** whether to add VK's instance (it would add `https://maps.mail.ru/osm/tools/overpass/api/interpreter` to the page's policy), or another one. | The wiki's list of public instances (https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/wiki/Overpass_API, read 2026-10-23); one query per field set-up, after the main one is busy, is the small use these instances allow. | maintainer (2026-10-23): no fallback to VK's Overpass server; overpass.private.coffee stays the only one |
| MF-52 | A busy answer the page cannot read. | A fetch that fails without an answer (`TypeError`: the server is down, a proxy cut it, or an error page **without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`**, which the browser reports as a network error, not as the 502) is taken as **busy or unreachable** and retried like a busy answer (MF-50), unless the browser says it is offline (*No internet*). If no instance ever answered, the last words are *could not be reached ... busy or unreachable; check the internet connection*. Checked 2026-10-23: `overpass-api.de`'s 502 "at capacity" **does** carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, so the browser sees it as a 502 today; the other case is handled because a front proxy's own error pages often do not. | The maintainer asked to handle both; to the GM both mean "try again soon". | open |

A **Cancelar / Cancel** button beside *Draw* ends the wait or the request at once (an `AbortController`), says *Cancelado...* and nothing more is asked. | MF-73 | The candidates MF-72 left (2026-10-23): how each was checked and how the table grew. | Each candidate was checked on 2026-10-23 as MF-68 to MF-70: its **GetCapabilities** read (CRS EPSG:4326 and 3857, MaxWidth/MaxHeight, Fees, AccessConstraints), a WMTS in Web Mercator looked for, then **real requests sent with an `Origin` header** as the page sends them: a **GetMap in EPSG:4326** (about 1024 pixels over a 1.5 km box inside it), a GetMap at its maximum size, a **WMS tile in EPSG:3857** and a **WMTS tile** where there is one, each checked for `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (`*` or the page's own origin, both work for `fetch()`) and for a **real photo** (not white, not black); then its **licence** from the dataset's metadata or the provider's open-data page (commercial use, offline use of a derived image, the exact credit), the need of a **key**, and published **usage limits**. **Added (MF-74 to MF-78):** Belgium by region (Brussels, Flanders, Wallonia), Luxembourg, Switzerland with Liechtenstein, **eleven German Länder** (Bayern, Berlin, Brandenburg, Bremen, Hessen, Mecklenburg-Vorpommern, Nordrhein-Westfalen, Rheinland-Pfalz, Saarland, Sachsen, Thüringen), Poland, Czechia, and IGN France's same source over the **French overseas** departments and collectivities: **22 sources** (four before). **Left out:** MF-80. **Coverage** (MF-66 extended): `tools/ortho_coverage.py` now also reads Natural Earth's **1:10m admin-1 states and provinces** (public domain) for regions: a German **Land** by its ISO 3166-2 code, a **Belgian region** as its five provinces (Brussels-Capital is one); a coverage is a list of parts (countries, optionally inside a box, or states). Same simplification (about 1 km, islands under 5 km² dropped). `ortho-coverage.js` grows from **39 KB to 98 KB** (33 KB gzipped; about 6 500 points), cached by the service worker as before. A region inside another's **outer ring** (Berlin in Brandenburg's, Brussels in Flemish Brabant's: the generator keeps outer rings only) comes **first** in the table, so its own source wins and the surrounding one is the second try at a white picture (MF-67). Which source: as MF-67, the **centroid's** country, region or Land. Code: `wmtsTile` takes a level name and a format (Poland's levels are `EPSG:3857:<zoom>`, Flanders' tiles PNG); `zooms(zmin, zmax, tile, fallback)` uses a WMTS only at the zooms it has (NRW's `00` is zoom 5, ČÚZK's start at 6 and stop at 18) and the WMS per tile at the others; a credit that asks for a year of retrieval uses this page's year (`YEAR`). Every service's exception answer is an XML with HTTP 200: `fetchOrthophoto` already refuses a non-image answer (`ortho_failed`). The browser tests check each new source chosen at a point inside it, its tiles, its credits (map and image), its WMS GetMap in EPSG:4326, the table's WMS, layers and size limits, the WMTS level names, and the policy (MF-79). Probe scripts and capabilities are not kept. | The maintainer's "open source would be nice" (MF-65) and MF-72's list; one source per region where the open imagery is regional (Germany, Belgium), as MF-66 decides by the land the point is in. | open |
