## The Android controller role in slices (PE24, 2026-10-22)

Decision PE24 left the Android controller role's shell for later. This round plans it in slices (`docs/phase-3-android-players/PLAN.md`, "PE24 slices") and builds the first: the game engine and the REST API, checked by shared contract cases against the Pi controller. Slice S2 (2026-10-22, PE24-10 to PE24-15) adds the live stream and the MQTT topics, checked the same way. Slice S3 (2026-10-22, PE24-40 to PE24-44; PE24-16 to PE24-39 left free) keeps the phone's games in files across a restart, with restart steps in the shared cases; they found a bug in both controllers, fixed in the Pi controller too (PE24-42). Slice S4 (2026-10-22, PE24-45 to PE24-53) makes an I/O pod the phone's radio, issues joins and serves Android props on Wi-Fi, with radio, join and prop steps in the shared cases and end-to-end tests with the host pod and host boxes. Slice S5 (2026-10-22, PE24-54 to PE24-60) ports field check-in, player tokens, the replicas-to-chrono list fed by signed readings and player positions, with 7 new shared cases; they found a bug in the Pi controller's poster settings, fixed in both (PE24-58). Slice S6 (2026-10-22, PE24-61 to PE24-69) builds the role's shell and screen: the controller's own isolate under the foreground service, the GM console carried in the APK, HTTPS with a field CA made on the phone, the field's Wi-Fi. Slice S6's console follow-ups (2026-10-22, PE24-70 to PE24-73) give the console its token in the address's fragment and have it hide what a phone controller does not serve yet, from a list of areas the controllers give in `GET /api/v1/whoami`. Slice S7a (2026-10-23, PE24-74 to PE24-79; the maintainer approved pulling it forward from S7 before the field test) brings the field plan and the radio channel with its noise scan to the phone, in a block of 6 new shared cases; PE24-74 to PE24-77 are **for the maintainer** (PE24-77 also for the pod firmware: its IDENT has no `measure` flag). PE24-42, PE24-44, PE24-52, PE24-58, PE24-60, PE24-62, PE24-64, PE24-67 and PE24-70 are **for the maintainer**; PE24-51 and PE24-61 are for the coordinator (tooling outside app/). Slice S7c (2026-10-23, PE24-90 to PE24-99, pulled forward from S7 with the maintainer's approval) brings the boxes over the radio to the phone: re-invites with sealed radio invites, GAME_OVER, the start guard, adoption by field card (written by the phone's NFC) and by pairing code, and firmware updates, with 10 new shared cases and end-to-end tests with the host modem and host boxes; PE24-91 (the I/O pod does not relay field frames yet: a firmware follow-up), PE24-92, PE24-93 and PE24-96 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PE24-1 | How is the Android controller role built? | In seven slices: S1 the game engine and the REST API (built), S2 the live stream and MQTT, S3 keeping games across a restart, S4 the I/O pod as the controller's radio, the Wi-Fi props and joins, S5 check-in, player tokens and positions, S6 the shell and its screens (controller isolate, foreground service, the GM console on the phone, HTTPS, the field's Wi-Fi), S7 the rest after the field test (failover, presets, mode uploads, scenarios, role packages in a game, branding, channel scan, re-invites, adoption, pairing, firmware updates, site sync, ATAK, remote GM). | The Pi controller has about 120 endpoints now; each slice is useful and tested on its own, and S1-S6 are what a small game run from a phone needs. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-2 | **For the maintainer:** may a phone be the field controller of a game, or only a Pi's standby? | Recommended: a phone may be the only controller of a small game with no Pi (S1-S6); it cannot be a Pi's standby, nor a Pi its standby, until failover (CLAIM) is ported in S7. Until then the engine is always in charge (term 1) and only notes TIME and CLAIM from another controller. | A phone on battery has a low ADR-P2-8 priority and its Wi-Fi and the OS's background limits make it a weaker controller than a Pi; failover across the two is its own work and needs the bench. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-3 | **For the maintainer:** is the Android controller part of plan phase G's gate? | Recommended: no. The gate ("one game mixes ESP32 and Android props") runs with the Pi controller (PLAN section 6); the Android controller gets its own bench test (a phone, a pod, a box and a prop) after S6. | Nothing in the field test needs it, and the gate should not wait for six slices. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-4 | How are "the same contract tests as the Python controller" shared? | As JSON cases in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`: requests with a token and body, the status, a pattern for the answer (`$absent`, `$any`, `$contains`; other keys free), values saved for later steps, decrypted box packets (`rx`) and clock steps (`advance_ms` then one tick). `controller/tests/test_api_contract.py` runs them in-process against the Pi controller (the reference: a case is added there first), `app/test/controller/api_contract_test.dart` over HTTP on the loopback address against the Dart engine on the real core. Error texts are not part of the contract; status codes are. 8 cases now, passing on both sides. | One file, two runners: a difference shows as a failing case on one side, and the cases grow with each slice (stream, restart and radio steps later). Rewriting the Pi's 40-odd test files in Dart would not stay in step. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-5 | How does the phone decode a box's EVENT? | With the core's own codec: `atalaia_event_decode` bound in `app/lib/ffi/bindings.dart` (`AtalaiaCore.decodeEvent`), next to the encoder the props already use. | One codec, already checked by the shared event vectors in C and Python; no third EVENT codec in Dart. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-6 | Where does slice S1 answer differently from the Pi? | `GET /api/v1/controller` adds `"host": "android"`; presets do not exist yet (`preset_id` is 404), `team_info` is refused (400), mode uploads, scenarios, joins, check-in, positions and the rest of the Pi's endpoints are 404 until their slice; a game id may name a main mode with modifiers, not a role package or a scenario; integers in bodies are taken as Python's `int()` takes them (a numeric string, a float truncated, never a boolean). | Each is either a later slice or harmless; none is covered by a shared case that the Pi passes. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-7 | Who can reach the phone's API? | In S1 nothing but the phone itself: `ControllerApi.serve` binds the loopback address unless told otherwise, over plain HTTP, with the GM and read tokens compared in constant time. Opening it to the field's Wi-Fi comes with S6, with HTTPS and a field CA made on the phone, as the Pi serves it. | ADR-P3-12 has the broker on localhost by default; the API should not be on a network before it has TLS and a screen that says it is on. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-8 | **For the maintainer:** the GM console on a phone controller. | Recommended: the app ships the GM console's files (`controller/console/src`, AGPL-3.0-or-later) as assets, served unchanged by the phone's API to its own web view and to other browsers, as a separate work under its own licence (to be noted in LICENSING.md). The alternative is GM screens written in Flutter. | One console for both controllers; the console talks only to the API, which is the same. The licence question (AGPL files inside a GPL app's package) is the maintainer's. | maintainer (2026-10-22): the GM console (`controller/console/`) moves to GPL-3.0-or-later with the app-store permission, so the app ships it unchanged; the controller's server code stays AGPL (LICENSING.md); pending the legal review like the rest |
| PE24-9 | Where does the phone keep its games? | In memory in S1 (`MemoryControllerStore`, the Pi store's records and order); in files in the app's private storage in S3, without a SQLite dependency; the game key only in memory unless `persist_game_key` is on, as ADR-P2-4 says for the Pi. | S1 needs no persistence to be tested; the store's interface is the Pi's, so S3 adds a store without touching the engine. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-10 | How are the stream and MQTT checked by the same cases? | New step kinds in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`: `ws_connect` (a token, by `?token=` or header; the hello must match, or the close code), `ws` (every message since the last look must match a list, so nothing may be missing, extra or out of order; the runner sends a marker through the stream to know where "now" is), `ws_send` (ping and pong), `ws_flood` (more messages than a client's queue takes: closed with 4408) and `mqtt` (every topic, QoS, retain flag and JSON payload published since the last look), plus an `mqtt_acl` table checked against `controller/deploy/atalaia.acl` on the Pi side (parsed, with paho's topic matcher) and `MqttAcl.field` on the phone. 5 new cases (13 in all) and 17 ACL checks, passing on both sides. The Pi's Python code is unchanged; only its runner learned the new steps. | The Pi stays the reference and one file describes both; a marker makes "the messages so far" exact on both sides without timeouts. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-11 | **For the maintainer:** the brief asked for the stream's `since`, generation and resync behaviour; what does the Pi do? | The Pi's stream has no `since` or generation: a client that falls more than 256 messages behind is closed with 4408, reconnects and its new hello (the live game, score, nodes, the latest 100 events, the controller's state) is the catch-up; any client resyncs the same way after a drop. The phone does exactly this (case "a stream client that falls behind..."). Recommended: no replay by sequence number for now; if fields on weak Wi-Fi need it, add it to both controllers with a shared case. | The plan's S2 row follows the Pi; inventing a resume protocol on one side only would break the contract. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-12 | **For the maintainer:** who may log in to the phone's MQTT broker? | The Pi's Mosquitto users and ACL (`atalaia.acl`): `gm` reads `field/v1/#` and writes `field/v1/+/cmd/#`, `reader` reads events, score and nodes; anonymous clients are refused (CONNACK 5), as `allow_anonymous false`. On the phone the passwords are the GM token and the read token (the Pi's are set by the operator with `mosquitto_passwd`). The broker takes logins and an ACL only when given them, so its earlier tests are unchanged. | One secret per role for a phone GM to hand out; the alternative is separate MQTT passwords on a settings screen (S6). | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-13 | Where does the phone's broker listen? | On the loopback address by default (it was every address): the field's Wi-Fi only when the shell opens it, with the API, in S6 (PE24-7). | Nothing should listen on a network before the controller's screen says so. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-14 | A slow stream client on the phone. | `dart:io`'s WebSocket gives no back-pressure, so the 4408 rule counts the messages handed to a client that its sender has not yet written (it writes them on the next turn of the event loop): a burst larger than 256 drops the client, as on the Pi, but a client on a slow link is buffered by the socket instead. To revisit in S6, when clients come over Wi-Fi (e.g. a cap on bytes in flight). | Tested and deterministic now; the real risk exists only once the API leaves the loopback address. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-15 | Where does the phone's stream differ from the Pi's? | The hello has no `branding` (team branding is S7) and its `status` has no `modem` or `tx_epoch` (the radio is S4); player tokens are refused with 4401 until S5; no radio or modem `system` messages until S4; an unknown token is accepted and closed with 4401, where the Pi under uvicorn refuses the handshake (HTTP 403, which a browser sees as 1006); a message that is not JSON is ignored, where the Pi ends the connection. None is covered by a shared case the Pi passes. | Later slices, or harmless; 4401 is what the console and the app look for. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-40 | How does the phone keep its games (slice S3)? | `FileControllerStore` (`app/lib/controller/controller_store.dart`): a directory of the app's private storage with `store.json` (`{"format": 1}`; a newer layout is refused), `settings.json`, and per game `games/ID.json` (the record, and the key only as below), `ID.events.jsonl` (the log, one record per line, appended and flushed), `ID.scores.json` and `ID.nodes.json`. Everything is read into memory when the store opens and every change is written through before the call returns; whole files go to a temporary file, flushed and renamed over the old one, so a crash leaves the old file or the new one; a last log line cut short by a crash is dropped (and the file truncated to the last whole line), any other unreadable line is skipped and counted. No SQLite dependency (PE24-9). One controller per directory. | A phone's games are small (one live game, a few thousand events); JSON files need no native library and are easy to inspect, and append-plus-rename gives the crash safety the Pi gets from SQLite's transactions. The store's interface is the Pi's, so the engine did not change for it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-41 | What does a phone controller do after a restart? | What the Pi does (`engine.py` `_restore`), checked by the new restart cases: the live game is taken up again with its term, its clock (saved every 5 s while it runs, and at start, pause and end: a crash loses at most 5 s, as on the Pi), each box's counters as last reported (merged by maximum, so a box's next STATE does not add twice), the GM's adjustments (from the log), each box's name, role, epoch and battery, its link and battery.low as last logged (one that was online counts as offline only `node_offline_s` after the restart), the field script's points of earlier runs and `field.lua` run again (a new run). With `persist_game_key` on (the default, decision OT-7) the key is kept in the game's file until the game ends; with it off the game comes back `key_lost`: only End works (409 for the rest, and a new game waits for it), its boxes are not told (`game_over_frame` is `no_join_key`: the phone has no field join key or radio until S4). A box's owner, phase and firmware come back with its next STATE or HELLO, as on the Pi. | One contract for both controllers: the same restart cases pass on both. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-42 | **For the maintainer (a fix in the Pi controller):** a restarted controller's own events. | The restart cases found that both controllers numbered their own events (GM adjustments, game start, pause and end, field.lua's) from 1 again after a restart, so an event with the same game time and type as an earlier one (a second GM adjustment before the start) had the same `(node, seq, t, type)` key and was dropped from the log as a replay; after the next restart its points were gone too. Fixed in both: the seq goes on from the highest stored for the controller's own node id (`engine.py` `_restore`, `controller_engine.dart` `_restore`), with a shared case ("the GM's score adjustments outlive two restarts") and a Pi unit test (`test_engine.RestartTests.test_own_event_seq_goes_on_after_a_restart`). | A real loss of the GM's points, found by the shared cases; the fix changes no stored data. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-43 | How do the shared cases restart a controller? | A `restart` step: the controller stops (the Pi's `stop()`, the phone's `close()`), its store closes, and a new controller opens the same store (the Pi's SQLite file, the phone's directory); the clock goes on, the open stream connections end, and the MQTT messages go on to the same list. A case may set `persist_game_key` (true when not given) for itself, before and after its restarts. 4 new cases (17 in all): a live game across a restart (clock, score, nodes, the stream's hello, the log), the GM's adjustments across two restarts, `field.lua` run again with its earlier points, and a game without its key that can only be ended. | The Pi's restart tests as cases, in one file for both sides (PE24-4). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-44 | **For the maintainer:** the game key at rest on a phone. | Recommended: as on the Pi, the key is kept (with `persist_game_key`, the default) in the app's private storage, which Android keeps from other apps; not encrypted with the Android Keystore for now. Wrapping it with a Keystore key could come with S6 (the shell), if wanted; a Pi keeps it in a 0600 file beside the field join key. | Same protection as the Pi and decision OT-7's choice; the Keystore needs the Kotlin side, which S6 builds. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-45 | How is an I/O pod the phone controller's radio (slice S4)? | `PodModem` (`app/lib/controller/pod_modem.dart`) does with the pod's RADIO_* frames what the Pi's `radio_modem.py` does with a USB modem (the pod runs the modem's code, protocol/capability-proxy.md): IDENT on every new link, then a PING whose STATUS brings the radio up; a PING every 5 s and down after 3 unanswered (or at once when the link breaks); the game's CONFIGURE and the boxes' peer epochs again on every new link and when the pod says who it is again (it forgot the key); RADIO_RX, RADIO_TX_ACK and RADIO_ERROR to the engine with the modem's status and error names. A pod with no radio chip is never up. The engine's `ControllerModem` interface is the Pi's RadioModem's, so a Bluetooth or USB pod, or a test's modem, are the same to it. | One radio code for the pod and the modem (decision PF2) and one controller code for the radio: what the Pi does, through the frames the pod already has. Checked against the host pod and the host box (PE24-51); not run on a real pod or phone. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-46 | The phone controller's nonce epochs. | As the Pi's store (RFC 0001 Amendment 9): the setting `tx_epochs` keeps `[tag, epoch]` for the last 8 game keys, the tag the epoch-tag HMAC (`epochTag`, checked against `protocol/vectors/epoch_tag.json`); every run in a game (its creation, each restart) takes that key's epoch + 1, stored before the first packet, and a new one when seq wraps (`RadioTransport`, the Pi's ModemTransport). The key's tag is also kept beside the game (`key_tag:ID`) for a later GAME_OVER (S7), and dropped when the game ends. | A nonce never repeats under a key, across restarts of the app, as on the Pi; the shared case "the radio after a restart" checks epochs 1, 2, 3 on both sides. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-47 | How do the shared cases see the radio? | New steps (PE24-4's file): `tx` takes everything the controller asked of its radio since the last look, in order (packets with header and payload by the field names of `protocol/schema/messages.yaml`; the modem's configure without the key, clear, channel and peer epochs), optionally only some `kinds`; `radio_state` takes the modem's link down or up; `tx_ack` is a modem TX_ACK; an `rx` step may ask for an ACK. The Pi's runner wraps its FakeModem, the phone's uses `RecordingModem` (test support). 2 new cases: the game on the modem with ACKs, CMD, TIME beacons, TX failures, a link down and back (503, peer epochs, the idle channel); a new epoch under the same key after each restart. | The Pi stays the reference; the radio's traffic is compared field by field without either side's codecs in the pattern. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-48 | Joins on the phone. | `ControllerEngine.issueJoin`, `POST /api/v1/join` and `POST /api/v1/nodes/{id}/join` (GM token) as the Pi's `issue_join`: the node id given or the lowest free, never one a join was issued for or a box was heard on (BN-18, kept across restarts), kinds node and controller, a role (the only one by default), the game's mode, parameters and box language (join key 15: `console_lang` at creation and `GET/PUT /api/v1/settings/box-language`, as on the Pi), sealed with the field join key, which the engine holds and never shows (the shell gives it, S6; none: 503). Without HTTPS on the phone (S6) the payload has no keys 13 and 14 (`wifi_link: false`). Not yet: scenario node names and the standby's own join (`POST /api/v1/controller/join`, failover, S7). Shared: a `join_key` case setting and a `join_decode` step that opens the payload with it; 1 new case. | A box joins a phone's game exactly as a Pi's; the payload is checked by opening it, not by its bytes (the key is random). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-49 | Android props on the phone's Wi-Fi. | `WS /api/v1/prop` (`app/lib/controller/controller_props.dart`) as the Pi's `prop_link.py`: the challenge, the HMAC proof (the prop client's own `propLinkProof`, checked against new known answers `protocol/vectors/prop_link.json`, made with Python's standard hmac), refusals with 4401, a newer link replacing an older one (4409), hello, state and event encoded with the radio's codecs and handed to the engine as a packet from that node, acks and errors, TIME and commands forwarded (a command for one node to that node only). Events are checked against the schema through the core's own tables (new bindings for `atalaia_event_def` and `atalaia_data_field`, as the simulator's `schema_problems`): the core's encoder alone lets unknown data fields through; a missing priority is the schema's. Plain `ws://` on the loopback address until S6 (PE24-7). Differences, harmless: no 256-message queue limit per link (`dart:io` gives no back-pressure, as PE24-14); a hello missing fields gets defaults where the Pi's encoder refuses it (and may end the link). Shared: `prop_connect`, `prop_send`, `prop`, `prop_closed` steps; 1 new case; the app's own prop client plays against it in `controller_props_test.dart`. | One protocol, the Pi's, and the prop app needs no change to link to a phone. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-50 | A phone controller with no radio. | As a Pi without a modem: each game still gets its transport, so TIME and commands reach the Android props on Wi-Fi, and a command a linked prop took counts as sent (202); one that reached nobody is 503, as before (protocol/prop-link.md). | A small game of Android props needs no pod at all. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-51 | **For the coordinator (tooling outside app/):** the phone controller's end-to-end tests. | `app/test/controller/pod_controller_test.dart` runs the engine on the real core with the host pod (the pod firmware's code) as its radio and host boxes (the Field Box's game and radio code) on a UDP air, as the Pi's `test_box_e2e.py` and `test_pod_e2e.py`: a box joins with the phone's payload, plays, scores, pauses and ends; the phone restarts mid-game and goes on with a new epoch; Hold-All's `field.lua` scores two boxes. For that the host box is now built for the Dart tests too: `tools/docker_app.py` and CI's `flutter-test` job build `firmware/esp32/test/box_host` with mbedtls's static library chosen on the cmake command line (`-DMBEDCRYPTO_LIB=...libmbedcrypto.a`, so it runs in the Flutter image; no firmware file changed) and pass `ATALAIA_BOX_HOST`; `app/tool/check.sh` requires it. | The brief's "host pod/box/modem builds for end-to-end tests where the Pi's tests do the same"; the box host's own CMake is untouched. | coordinator (2026-10-22): accepted |
| PE24-52 | **For the maintainer:** the field's channel choice moves from S4 to S7. | Recommended: `GET /api/v1/radio` and `PUT /api/v1/radio/channel` come with the channel scan in S7, because the console's radio panel shows both and names why a scan cannot run; a phone answering half of it would show a broken panel. In S4 the phone already uses the stored field channel (`radio.channel_hz`, else its configured one) for new games and tunes an idle radio to it when the radio comes up, so S7 only adds the API. | No change to the console for a half panel; nothing in the field test needs a phone to pick a channel. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| PE24-53 | Where the phone's radio answers differ from PE24-15. | Since S4 `GET /api/v1/controller` and the stream's hello have `modem` (the pod's IDENT and its last STATUS, reconnects) and `tx_epoch`, `tx_status` counts the TX_ACKs, and the stream and MQTT get the Pi's `radio.up`, `radio.down`, `tx.failed` and `modem.error` system messages. Still different: no field frames (re-invites, GAME_OVER, pairing: S7), so `game_over_frame` stays `no_join_key`. | PE24-15's radio items are done; the rest are S7's. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-54 | What does slice S5 build on the phone? | Field check-in as the Pi's `checkin.py` (`app/lib/controller/controller_checkin.dart`, `ControllerCheckin`): the endpoints of ADR-P3-13 with the Pi's paths, bodies, checks and status codes (`GET/POST /api/v1/checkin`, `/checkin/token`, `/checkin/me` and its replicas and marshal key, the staff list, manual check-in, changes and removal, the CSV, open and close, the chrono list, `POST /api/v1/chrono/readings`, `/marshals`, the settings and the poster, a player's readings and erasure); player tokens signed with a token key the phone makes per game (decision B8, `app/lib/security/player_token.dart`), their role, team and squad checked against the check-in on every request (an older token keeps the lesser role, `stale`); declarations and the replicas-to-chrono list judged from Ed25519-signed series (`app/lib/chrono/record_signer.dart`, the marshal's registered key), with the Pi's pass, fail, warn, `chrono_valid_for` "day" and "refused today" rules and its `player.checkin`, `chrono.*` events; the rate limits and the retention purge. Player positions as the Pi's `positions.py` (`controller_positions.dart`, `ControllerPositions`): `GET/POST /api/v1/positions`, the reports' checks, the level kept from the player's last report (ML-5), who sees whom through `visibility_engine.dart` (one per game, now also requiring a team on both sides of a grant, as the Pi), `visibility.granted` and `visibility.expired`, a VIP's GPS to `field.lua` as `position.fix` (P4R6), positions deleted when the game ends. Player tokens on the read endpoints (`whoami`, games, score, log, nodes), staff tokens of a game seeing its gm_only data, and on the stream: the player's hello (its own check-in, the positions it may see), staff-only and own messages, positions by who may see them, 4409 after a change of team, squad or role (api.py's `make_route`, `_snapshot`); `pending_declarations` on Start. | The plan's S5 row and the brief: the phone must behave as the Pi on the contract; nothing on the phone is new protocol. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-55 | How do the shared cases check check-in, tokens and positions? | The Pi runner now sets up `Checkin` and `Positions` on every controller as `main.py` does, with no field CA (HTTPS off, as the phone until S6), and both runners give the controller a wall clock at the vectors' new `wall_start` (1800000000, 2027-01-15 08:00 UTC) that moves with the clock, the field's day in UTC; a request or `ws_connect` step may give a raw token (`bearer`, usually a saved player token), and a new `ws_closed` step reads a connection to its close code. The readings are signed in the file with a fixed test key, so both sides verify the same bytes. 7 new cases (28 in all): the poster, open games, a token and what it may read; declarations, the GM's changes, a stale token, removal and leaving, the staff confirmation and the federation number; a marshal's signed readings (refusals by reason, pass, fail, a resend); the player stream (own check-in, names for staff only, 4409); positions (the visibility table, the level kept, a team's view for 30 s, gone at the end); a VIP's extraction in VIP / Payload's `field.lua`; check-ins, tokens, readings and positions across restarts. Passing on both sides. | One file for both controllers (PE24-4); a fixed wall clock makes token expiry and "earlier today" deterministic on both sides. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-56 | Where does the phone keep check-ins, readings and positions? | In the store, as two named documents (`ControllerStore.document` and `putDocument`; `FileControllerStore` writes `docs/checkin.json` and `docs/positions.json`, each replaced whole by rename like the other files, PE24-40): the check-in's records (field identities with the secret's SHA-256, check-ins, declarations, readings with their series and outcome, marshals' keys, the replica registry, the games' token keys) and the players' latest positions, which a restart keeps as the Pi's SQLite does (the shared restart case checks the level kept across a restart). Settings (open, poster, check-in settings) are store settings with the Pi's keys. | No new dependency and the Pi's restart behaviour; a field day's records are small (a few hundred players and readings). Readings are kept a year (as on the Pi): if a phone serves many field days, the check-in document grows and each change rewrites it; a store per record kind can come later if a field needs it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-57 | In what order are positions listed? | By player id, on both controllers: the Pi's `player_positions` now says `ORDER BY player_id` (its primary key's order, which SQLite already gave, so nothing changes), and the phone sorts the same way. | PE24-58 | **For the maintainer (a fix in the Pi controller):** the poster's fields were not checked with HTTPS off. | The shared cases found that `PUT /api/v1/checkin/poster` checked the fields only when the controller had a field CA: with HTTPS off it kept anything, and a `game_id` of `"x"` then closed check-in to every game ("this field's check-in is for another game") with no error anywhere. Fixed in both: the fields are checked as the poster codec checks them, with a placeholder for a missing CA as for a missing address (`checkin.py` `set_poster`, `controller_checkin.dart` `setPoster`); a Pi unit test (`test_checkin_poster.PosterProblemTests.test_fields_are_checked_with_https_off`) and the shared case "check-in: the poster, open games...". | A typo in the console blocked a field's whole check-in; the fix changes no stored data and no valid request. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-59 | Where does the phone's check-in differ from the Pi's? | No HTTPS until S6, so the poster says `https_off` and has no text (the field CA and the addresses come with the shell); the notice texts and the limit template are given by the shell (`CheckinConfig`; the Pi reads files); the field's day is the phone's local time (the Pi's too). Not yet: rosters of registered players from a site server and the outcomes sent back (the site sync, S7; a registration id and ticket sent at check-in are kept with the walk-in, and a scanned ticket is 404 as on a Pi without a roster), a marshal's earlier keys (kept on the Pi for the site sync), positions to ATAK and the nodes' places on the field plan (`/api/v1/games/{id}/positions`, S7 with the field plan, MF-14), map features. `POST /api/v1/chrono/readings` answers asynchronously (the `cryptography` package's Ed25519 is future-based; `ControllerApi.handleAsync`), every other endpoint at once. PE24-15's "player tokens refused with 4401 until S5" is done. | Later slices, or what a phone needs from its shell; none is covered by a shared case the Pi passes. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

The phone's operator is the field's data controller, as for a Pi; the privacy notice is the same. | The same rules as the Pi; a lost phone is the new risk (a Pi stays at the field), which full-disk encryption on current Android phones covers, and a Keystore-wrapped store could come with S6. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended (keys and players' data in app-private storage, the Pi's retention rules); no Keystore wrapping in S6 |
| PE24-61 | **For the coordinator (tooling outside app/):** how do the GM console's files get into the app, and how is it known they are the Pi's? | Copied at build time, never committed: `app/tool/sync_console.dart` (plain `dart:io`, runs before `pub get`) copies `controller/console/src` into `app/assets/console/` and `controller/templates/checkin-notice-{pt,en}.md` into `app/assets/notices/` (both in `.gitignore`); `app/tool/check.sh` runs it in every mode (it now copies `controller/console/src` and `controller/templates` into its work folder too), as do CI's `flutter-integration` job (a new step) and `app/tool/emulator_tests.{sh,ps1}`. Checks: `test/controller/console_bundle_test.dart` compares the assets the test bundle holds (`AssetManifest`) with `../controller/console/src` byte for byte and both ways, so a missing folder in `pubspec.yaml` (Flutter's asset folders are not recursive; five are listed) or a stale copy fails; `check.sh apk` checks that every console file and both notices are in the APK; `sync_console.dart --check` compares without copying. The phone serves the files from memory (the UI isolate reads them and hands them to the controller's isolate), with the Pi's redirects, `index.html` for a folder and security headers. | One console, edited in one place while others change it; a committed copy would go stale with every console change and need a second commit. The price: a plain `flutter test` or `flutter build` in `app/` needs `dart tool/sync_console.dart` first (app/README.md says so); without it, `flutter analyze` warns of the missing asset folders. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-62 | **For the maintainer:** who reaches a phone controller once its shell runs? | HTTP on port 8080 at the **loopback address only** (the app's own web view of the console, never off the phone); with *Aberto na rede Wi-Fi do campo* on (the default, shown on the screen; off: nothing listens on a network, PE24-13), **HTTPS on 8443** with the field CA at every address and the **MQTT broker on 1883** at every address with the Pi's users and ACL (passwords: the GM and read tokens, PE24-12). Unlike a Pi, the phone does **not** answer plain HTTP on the field's network: the poster, join keys 13 and 14 and the console's addresses are all https. MQTT stays plain, as the Pi's Mosquitto `listener 1883`, so its passwords cross the field's Wi-Fi in clear on both (MQTT over TLS on 8883 with the same field CA is a small change for both, if wanted). Changing the opening restarts the controller (the live game goes on, as after a restart). | PE24-7 and PE24-13 said "with HTTPS"; the tokens should not cross the Wi-Fi in clear on a controller that need not be compatible with plain-HTTP scoreboards (a laptop browser accepts the field CA once; players' phones pin it from the poster). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-63 | How is the controller off the UI isolate (constraint 1)? | `ControllerIsolate` (`app/lib/controller/controller_isolate.dart`) spawns one isolate that opens the core itself and runs `ControllerHost` (`controller_host.dart`: store, engine, check-in, positions, API servers, broker, the 100 ms tick, a look at the phone's addresses every 10 s); the UI isolate gets a status map at once, every second and on a game or system message, and sends only stop. An I/O pod's link (the Kotlin USB and BLE plugins' channels belong to the root isolate) stays on the UI isolate and its bytes are relayed through ports to a `RelayPodTransport` the controller's `PodModem` uses (a few hundred bytes a second at the radio's rate). The pod is chosen before the start; a new radio or Wi-Fi opening restarts the controller (stop, start on the same folder; `ControllerShell.restart`), which S3's restart already makes safe. | No platform channels on a background isolate (BackgroundIsolateBinaryMessenger does not take the plugins' event channels reliably); a restart reuses the tested path instead of rebuilding parts of a running controller. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-64 | **For the maintainer:** keeping the controller running on Android. | The shell starts the existing foreground service (`AtalaiaForegroundService`: notification "Atalaia: controlador de campo" with the live game, partial wake lock, Wi-Fi lock in low-latency mode) before the controller's isolate and asks once for the battery exemption, as a prop does (constraint 7). New: while the service runs, the **Flutter engine outlives the activity** (`MainActivity` keeps it in `FlutterEngineCache` and does not destroy it with the activity; the next activity takes it up), so the back button on the home screen or a swipe away no longer ends the controller's isolate or a prop's game loop; the pod's link and the local hotspot are kept too. The service's type stays `connectedDevice` (the pod; Android 14 requires a type, and a server is none of the listed ones; `specialUse` would need Google Play's review). | Without the engine kept, Android's destroying the activity ended every Dart isolate, the controller's too, with the notification still showing; the service's type is what Android allows for the app's own devices. Only a phone can show that it holds through a whole game with the screen off (plan phase G). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-65 | The phone controller's own keys and names. | The **field join key** is the app's own (*Definições*), made there on the first start when none is set (so the settings show it for the boxes' setup); the **field id** of check-in is the app's `field_id` setting (the marshal's, `field` by default as the Pi's); the **GM and read tokens** are made on the first start (`gm-` and `rd-` and 24 base64url characters, from `Random.secure`) and kept in `shell.json` in the controller's folder of the app's private storage (PE24-44, PE24-60), shown hidden on the screen until asked, with a copy button; S6 does not rotate them (forgetting the folder does). The field name for check-in is the poster's, set from the console (`PUT /api/v1/checkin/poster`). | One join key per phone, whichever role it plays; the Pi's defaults where the Pi has them; no new settings screen. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-66 | The GM console in the app's web view on the phone's own controller. | `ControllerConsoleScreen` loads `http://127.0.0.1:8080/console/` (PE24-62), lets the web view go nowhere but that address, and on the first page puts the GM token and the app's language into the console's own storage (`atalaia.token`, `atalaia.lang` of `controller/console/src/ui.js`) when the console has none, so the GM need not type it. **Follow-ups for the console (not changed here):** accept `#token=` as the scoreboard does, so the app need not know its storage keys; the console calls endpoints a phone does not have yet (presets, team presets, the radio panel, boxes and discover, adoption, firmware, ATAK, the field image, levels and map features, scenarios, schedule, mode packages and mode fetch; PE24-6, PE24-52, MF-14): it should hide those panels when the controller says `"host": "android"` (or a capability list) instead of showing their 404s; its CSV export and the poster's print need a computer's browser (the web view has no downloads or printing). These follow-ups were built as PE24-70 to PE24-73 (the app now gives the token in the fragment). | The console is the same files (PE24-8); the log-in uses what the console already reads. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-67 | **For the maintainer:** the field CA made on a phone. | As the Pi's `tls.py`: an ECDSA P-256 CA valid 10 years ("Atalaia field", "<field> field CA"; CA:TRUE, pathlen 0, keyCertSign and cRLSign; subject key id) and a server certificate valid 1 year for `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and the phone's addresses (SAN, serverAuth, authority key id), renewed when the names change or with less than 30 days left, the CA never replaced on its own; SHA-256 of the CA's DER on the poster and in join key 13. Signatures are RFC 6979's deterministic ECDSA with SHA-256 from **pointycastle 4.0.0** (a new dependency: the Legion of the Bouncy Castle's pure-Dart library, MIT; the `cryptography` package has no pure-Dart P-256); the DER is the app's own small encoder (`app/lib/security/der.dart`); a `server.json` beside the certificate records its names and end instead of parsing it back. Keys are PKCS #8 in the app's private storage, not wrapped by the Keystore (PE24-44). Checked by Dart's own TLS (BoringSSL): a client trusting only that CA reaches the phone by address and by name, the app's pinned check-in client does, another CA or an uncovered name is refused. The phone's addresses: its IPv4 interfaces, a hotspot's first, then Wi-Fi, then the rest, mobile data and link-local left out; when they change, a new certificate and the HTTPS listener again on the same port. | The same trust model as the Pi (ADR-P3-9, decision PE2) with no native code; a library for the curve arithmetic rather than the app's own. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-68 | The field's Wi-Fi from the controller's screen. | `FieldWifiPlugin.kt` (`lib/platform/services.dart` `FieldWifiService`): the network the phone is on (its name needs the location permission), the hotspot's state (the sticky `WIFI_AP_STATE_CHANGED` broadcast; "unknown" where a maker leaves it out), buttons to Android's hotspot and Wi-Fi settings (an app cannot turn tethering on), and a **local-only hotspot** (`WifiManager.startLocalOnlyHotspot`, `NEARBY_WIFI_DEVICES` from Android 13, location before; a new manifest permission with `neverForLocation`) whose name and password Android picks anew each time, shown with a Wi-Fi QR code players scan; the screen says not to put it on the poster. Refusals are named (permission, location off, incompatible mode, tethering not allowed, no channel). | A field usually has the GM's phone hotspot or a router; the local hotspot is for a quick game with no other network. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-69 | What only a real phone can check (plan phase G, the Android controller's bench test of PE24-3). | A whole game with the screen off and the phone in a pocket (the wake and Wi-Fi locks, Doze, the maker's battery killers with and without the exemption); the engine kept past the activity (back button, swipe away, the notification bringing the screen back); the foreground service's start on Android 14+ with the `connectedDevice` type; the hotspot's address taken up by the certificate and the poster, the hotspot's state and the network's name on several makers; the local-only hotspot (and its incompatibility with a Wi-Fi connection on some phones); other phones checking in over that HTTPS from the printed poster; a laptop's browser on the console over HTTPS; the console in the system web view (its WebSocket, the log-in); an I/O pod over USB and Bluetooth relayed to the controller, with boxes, and kept linked when Android destroys the activity; a slow client on the field's Wi-Fi (PE24-14's buffering, unchanged in S6); the CPU and battery a game costs. The emulator test (`app/integration_test/controller_test.dart`: a game created, started and ended over the phone's HTTPS from the controller's screen) runs in CI only (no KVM here). | Docker and the emulator have none of these. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-70 | **For the maintainer (an API addition):** how does the GM console know what a controller serves? | A **list of areas** in `GET /api/v1/whoami`: `"features"`, for every token, on both controllers. The Pi's (`api.py` `FEATURES`) names them all: `checkin`, `join`, `box_language`, `positions`, `presets` (presets and saved teams), `branding` (the field's branding, crests, a game's team names), `radio`, `boxes` (free boxes, discover, invites), `adoption`, `firmware`, `atak`, `field` (the field image, its map position, levels, map features, the boxes' places), `scenarios`, `schedule`, `mode_packages` (signed packages, mode uploads, modes sent to boxes) and `failover` (promote, a standby's join, the remote GM); the phone's (`controller_api.dart` `controllerFeatures`) the first four. A controller without the list (an older Pi) serves every area. A new shared contract case checks that both give the four; `test_console.py` and `console_bundle_test.dart` check each side's list against one endpoint per area (served when listed, the router's 404 when not, so the list cannot drift from the routes) and the console's own list (`ui.js` `AREAS`) against the Pi's. | Rejected: `"host": "android"` (already in `GET /api/v1/controller`), which would make the console know what each slice ported and change with every slice; feature detection, which would ask a dozen endpoints at each log-in and still show the 404s in the browser's console. `whoami` is the console's first call, so nothing is asked before it knows. An additive field: older consoles ignore it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-71 | The console's token in the address. | `/console/#token=...&lang=en`, as the scoreboard: the console (`ui.js` `takeHashToken`) takes `token` and `lang` out of the fragment before anything reads it and removes them from the address at once (`history.replaceState`, no new history entry; the rest of the fragment, such as `#field`, kept), then logs in as with a typed token: stored (`atalaia.token`) only once `whoami` accepts it, a wrong one refused with the login page. Never a query string (a fragment is not sent to the controller, so it is in no server log), never logged. The app's `ControllerConsoleScreen` now opens `http://127.0.0.1:8080/console/#token=<GM token>&lang=<the app's language>` (`consoleAddress`) instead of writing the console's storage keys from a script, so it logs in with the GM token each time it is opened. | PE24-66's follow-up: the app need not know the console's storage keys. The stream's `?token=` is unchanged (a browser gives a WebSocket no headers). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-72 | What the console hides on a controller that serves fewer areas. | Tabs: *Campo* (field), *Adotar* (adoption), *Predefinições* (presets or mode packages), *Cenários* (scenarios); an address opening a hidden tab opens the game. Panels: the radio channel's settings and its first-setup reminder, the field's branding, saved teams, team names and crests in the game form and on the game card, the invite dialog, firmware, modes sent to boxes, signed mode packages, the standby join, *Promote*, the ATAK status, the schedule; and the requests behind them (presets, team presets, the boxes' places, map features, the radio). The *Controlador* tab says which parts this controller does not serve yet, so the GM knows they are not lost. The field's branding is fetched after `whoami` when the console has a token (before it only for the login page). Checked in Chromium with `whoami` answering the phone's list: no hidden tab, panel or request, nothing refused, at 390 px in pt-PT. | Hiding is quieter than explaining in each place; one notice where the controller is described is enough. The scoreboard and the poster page still ask for the branding (a 404 on a phone, harmless: they show the default look). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-73 | CSV export and printing in the app's web view. | A short notice under *Imprimir o cartaz* and *Exportar CSV* in Android's web view (the user agent's `; wv)`, `ui.js` `inWebView`): print the poster and save the CSV from a computer's browser. The buttons stay (a laptop on the same controller uses them). | The web view has no downloads or printing (PE24-66); the GM may not know why nothing happens. The same notice shows in the GM role's web view of a Pi's console. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| PE24-74 | **For the maintainer:** what slice S7a pulls forward from S7, and how. | Approved by the maintainer on 2026-10-23 (before the field test): the **field plan** and the **radio channel** on the phone controller, with the Pi's endpoints, bodies, checks, status codes and stream messages. The field plan (`app/lib/controller/controller_field.dart`, `controller_field_api.dart`): a plan per level (PNG, JPEG or WebP, 4 MB; upload, read, delete), the levels (ML-0 to ML-12: on or off, 2 to 10 with names; fewer levels take the plans, own map positions and features of the levels that go, and move the boxes on them to level 0), each plan's map position (GR-1, georef.py written again in Dart with the same arithmetic in the same order: two corners, or 3 to 8 points by least squares, and the Pi's refusals), map features (MF-0 to MF-49: eight types, three shapes, the boundary at most once per level, visibility checked on REST and in the stream's `map_features`, which each connection gets for its own token, player tokens included), the field from a map (MF-26, all or nothing), the boxes' places (`/api/v1/games/{id}/positions`) and the stream's `field_image`, `field_georef`, `field_levels`, `map_features` and `position` messages. The radio channel (`controller_channel.dart`): `GET /api/v1/radio`, `PUT /api/v1/radio/channel`, the scan (`POST`, `GET`, `DELETE /api/v1/radio/scan`) through the I/O pod and `POST /api/v1/radio/random`, with eu868.py's table, channels.py's ranking and suggestion, the `radio` and `radio_scan` messages; no game is created while a scan runs and the radio goes back to the field's channel after it. `whoami`'s `features` (PE24-70) gains `radio` and `field`, so the console shows the *Campo* tab and the radio panel on a phone. Still in S7: the field's features and boxes on ATAK, syncing them with a site server, and the rest of S7's list. | The console's Field tab and radio panel need all of this at once (PE24-52: a half panel shows 404s); the phone now answers them as the Pi does, checked by shared cases. | open |
| PE24-75 | **For the maintainer:** where the phone keeps the field plan. | In the controller's folder in the app's private storage (PE24-44, PE24-60): one store document, `field` (`docs/field.json`: the levels, each level's map position as its points, the plans' type, size and file name, the map features with the next feature id), each plan's bytes in its own file (`field/plan-N.img`, N a serial number; at most 10 plans of 4 MB, so 40 MB), and the boxes' places as the document `field_places`. A new plan is written to a new file and flushed first, and becomes the plan only when the document naming it replaces the old one (the store's rename); the old file is deleted after. That one rename is the commit point of `POST .../from-map` (plan, map position and boundary together, as the Pi's SQLite transaction; a test fails the write and finds the old plan, map position and features, the new file removed at the next start). Files no document names are removed when the controller starts. Without a folder (tests) the plans stay in memory. The boxes' places stay after a game ends, as on the Pi. | No new dependency, the store's own atomic writes, and plans read from disk only when asked (the document stays small). | open |
| PE24-76 | Plans as request bodies on the phone, and the shared cases for images and the scan. | The phone's API now hands the handlers the body's bytes and Content-Type (the routes before had text only) and reads up to 6 MB on `/api/v1/field/...` paths (a 4 MB plan, or the field from a map: its image in base64, about 5.6 MB) and 1 MB elsewhere, as before; a plan over 4 MB is 413, another type 415, an empty one 400, as on the Pi. The shared cases (PE24-4) gain a request's `raw` body (`content_type`, `base64`, zero bytes up to `size`), an answer's `content_type` and `body_base64` (JSON is read only when the answer is JSON), and the scan's steps on the recording modem: `noise` (per channel, the 90th percentile and the LoRa preambles of 10 tries, as the Pi's FakeModem), `hold_measure`, `scan_wait`, MEASUREs in `tx` as `{"kind": "measure", "channel_hz", "dwell_ms"}`, and `tx: "$any"` to empty the list. The cases are in their own block, `field_and_radio` in `controller_api.json` (its own description and cases, run after `cases` by both runners), so slices built at the same time do not edit the same lines: 6 cases (the areas in `whoami`; the plan with its limits, map position, stream and a restart; levels with the boxes' places; map features seen by the GM, read, red, blue and marshal tokens on REST and the stream; the field from a map on two levels; the channel, a game's channel, a scan and a cancelled one). 35 cases in all, passing on both controllers. | The Pi stays the reference and both runners send the same bytes; a scan that the case holds makes its running state testable on both. | open |
| PE24-77 | **For the maintainer (the pod firmware):** the channel scan through the I/O pod; what does the pod lack? | Nothing in the measurement: the pod runs the USB modem's MEASURE (protocol/capability-proxy.md, RADIO_CMD opcode 8 answered by RADIO_NOISE; `pod_task.c` wires the same `radio_task_measure_*` and noise statistics, CAD included), and a whole scan through the host pod passes (`controller_channel_test.dart`). What it lacks is the **flag**: the modem's JSON IDENT says `"radio": "sx1262"` and `"measure": true`, the pod's CBOR IDENT only `has_radio`. The phone therefore takes a pod with a radio as able to measure (`PodModem.canMeasure`); a pod firmware without MEASURE would answer `unknown_cmd`, and the scan would end `failed` with that error instead of being refused at once with `old_firmware`, as on a Pi. Proposed, not built (the firmware is outside this slice): an optional IDENT key 6, `measure` (bool), in capability proxy v1 (readers skip keys 6 to 23, so no new protocol version), and the phone's `old_firmware` when a pod says false. Recorded for the firmware's maintainer. Also noted: the pod sends RADIO_NOISE to the link that last sent a radio frame when no game is configured, which is the phone that asked. | The scan works on every pod built so far; the flag only matters if an older pod firmware appears. | open |
| PE24-78 | The out-of-bounds warning on a phone-controlled field (MF-40). | Works now: the player's app reads `GET /api/v1/field/features` and `GET /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/georef` with its own token from the phone controller and gets the stream's `map_features` for its token, as from a Pi. Checked by `app/test/controller/field_bounds_phone_test.dart`: a player checked in on a phone controller loads the boundary and the map position with its token, a fix 55 m outside warns after the dwell and one inside ends it, and a boundary made for the organisers reaches the player's stream as an empty list. The console's browser test of a phone controller (`test_browser.py`, PE24-72) now expects the *Campo* tab, the radio panel and its first-setup reminder, and no request of the areas still hidden. | MF-40 said the warning would follow the field plan to the phone. | open |
| PE24-79 | Where the phone's field plan and channel differ from the Pi's. | The scan runs on the controller's event loop, not a thread (the same messages in the same order, checked by the shared case); the scan's dwell (60 s) and quiet threshold (-105 dBm) are the Pi's defaults with no setting on the phone; `updated_at` and `set_at` are the phone's clock; the field plan has no ATAK feed and no site sync (S7). Unchanged on both: no image is decoded or resized on upload (only the field from a map checks the first bytes). | Later slices or settings a field has not asked for. | open |
| PE24-90 | What of slice S7 comes before the field test, and how is it built? (S7c, maintainer's approval of 2026-10-23 to pull parts of S7 forward) | **S7c: boxes over the radio on the phone controller**, as the Pi does them: the free boxes heard, DISCOVER, sealed radio invites into the live game before it starts (the join payload made exactly as for a card, cut into one to three INVITE parts paced by their time on air, 150 ms between parts, sent again after 6 s, three times at most) and their answers, the box's HELLO closing an invite, the previous game's boxes, GAME_OVER three times 4 s apart after every game the phone ran ends (by its key's tag, so also after a restart without the key; `game_over_frame`), the start guard (the start check with *Start anyway*, and the firmware guard that `force` never overrides); adoption (field cards, pairing by code); firmware updates. Built as the Pi's modules in Dart: `controller_boxes.dart` (`reinvite.py` and lobby.py's `Inviter`), `controller_pairing.dart` (`pairing.py`), `controller_adoption.dart` (`adoption.py`), `controller_firmware.dart` (`firmware_updates.py`), the routes in `controller_boxes_api.dart` (a part of `controller_api.dart`), and the codecs in `lib/protocol/` (`field_frame.dart`, `pair_frame.dart`, `field_card.dart`, `firmware_release.dart`) and `lib/security/` (`aes_ccm.dart`, `cpace.dart`), each checked against the shared vectors the C and Python codecs check. What differs, on purpose: `fetch_modes` (join key 16) answers 503 as a Pi without mode fetching (sending modes to boxes is mode packages, not S7c); the controller's nonce sender id is drawn from `Random.secure` at every start (RJ-15, as the Pi's `os.urandom`); invites live in memory, as on the Pi. | The maintainer approved pulling these forward; each is the Pi's tested behaviour, so the shared contract cases can hold the phone to it. | proposed |
| PE24-91 | **For the maintainer:** the I/O pod does not relay field or pair frames. | **Recorded as a pod firmware follow-up** (this slice does not touch firmware). What is missing, exactly: (1) `core/include/atalaia/proxy.h`: frame types `ATALAIA_PROXY_RADIO_FIELD_TX` 0x0C (phone to pod), `RADIO_FIELD_RX` 0x0D and `RADIO_FIELD_ACK` 0x0E (pod to phone), the modem's FIELD_TX/RX/ACK (0x09-0x0B) plus 3 as every other radio frame, and `atalaia_proxy_modem_type()` mapping up to 0x0E; (2) `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod.c`: `radio_out()` drops every modem frame above `MODEM_FRAME_NOISE` (0x08), so FIELD_RX and FIELD_ACK never reach the phone (they should go to the link that runs the radio, as RX does), and `on_frame()` hands only RADIO_TX and RADIO_CMD to `handle_radio()`, so a RADIO_FIELD_TX is counted as ignored (it should go to `modem_handle_frame()` as FIELD_TX, with or without a game configured); (3) the pod's IDENT says so: key 6 `field_frames` and key 7 `pair_frames` (booleans; the modem's IDENT `field` and `pair`), in the C and Dart proxy codecs, `protocol/capability-proxy.md` and `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml`; (4) a host pod test (`pod_host`) relaying an announcement and an invite. A FIELD_TX (2 + up to 217 bytes) fits a proxy frame (256); over Bluetooth writes cut to the MTU are put back together as now. The phone's side is built and tested for those types against a scripted pod (`pod_modem.dart`, `pod_codec.dart` keys 6 and 7, `pod_client.dart`); with today's pods it reads no flag, so boxes say `field_frames: false`, GAME_OVER is `no_radio` and pairing refuses (503, "the radio's firmware does not carry pair frames"), as a Pi does with an old modem. | The phone's radio is the pod (PE24-45); the pod runs the modem's code, so the relay is a few lines in `pod.c` and one in `proxy.c`, but it changes the proxy protocol (a maintainer's call) and the firmware (another agent's area). | proposed, for the maintainer; built 2026-10-23 as PE24-100 to PE24-104: the pod relays field and pair frames |
| PE24-92 | **For the maintainer:** a USB radio modem on the phone, besides the pod. | **Built:** `serial_modem.dart` (`SerialModem`) speaks protocol/usb-modem.md to the Pi's own USB radio modem over any byte link, as `radio_modem.py` does (IDENT, PING and STATUS, CONFIGURE and the peer epochs again after the modem says who it is again, RX, TX_ACK, FIELD_TX/RX/ACK, ERROR, and MEASURE for S7a's channel scan); the shell's radio link is probed at start (`probeRadioLink`: both IDENT requests at once, each in its own protocol, which the other ignores; the modem answers JSON, the pod CBOR; "pod" after 1.5 s of silence), so a modem plugged into the phone by USB-OTG (the same USB plugin as a pod's, 921600 8N1) is the controller's radio with field and pair frames today. `ControllerModem` gained `hasFieldFrames`, `hasPairFrames`, `sendField`, `onFieldRx` and `onFieldAck`, no-ops by default. The controller's status says `radio_kind` (`pod` or `modem`) and `field_frames`. | Until the pod relays field frames (PE24-91), the modem is the only radio with which a phone controller re-invites, ends key-lost games and pairs; it is the same board with the modem's firmware. The end-to-end tests run the phone against the host modem and host boxes. Not tried on a phone (an OTG cable to an ESP32-S3; Android's USB permission per device; PE24-69). Over Bluetooth only a pod. | proposed, for the maintainer |
| PE24-93 | **For the maintainer:** the NFC field card on a phone controller. | **The phone writes it with its own NFC**, from the controller's screen (*Cartão de campo*: *Escrever o cartão de campo*), after a dialog saying it is the field's master secret: the screen asks the running controller for the card (`POST /api/v1/adopt/card` with the GM token on the loopback address, so the card is recorded with the other adoptions, `card_made`) and writes its one NDEF record (`application/vnd.atalaia.field`) to the next NTAG215/216 held to the phone, checking first that it decodes to this field's card; NFC off or missing, a locked or small tag and a failed request are said. The console shows the same bytes for any other writer, as on a Pi. Not in the marshal's tag writer. | The phone has the reader the Pi lacks; the GM is the one who holds the master secret, and the controller's screen is the GM's; a marshal never needs it. | proposed, for the maintainer |
| PE24-94 | Pairing by code on the phone. | As `pairing.py`: offers listed for a minute after the last, one run at a time, START/REPLY/KEY/DONE with CPace (`CPACE-X25519-SHA512`) in pure Dart (`cpace.dart`: RFC 7748's ladder on `BigInt`, Elligator 2, SHA-512 from the crypto package; checked against the draft's B.1 and B.1.10 and this project's run), three sends 3 s apart, 10 s a step, the run's secrets wiped on every outcome, KEY's nonce from a sender id drawn per run; listening on 869.525 MHz for five minutes when the field's channel is another and no game is live (`engine.channelLockReason`: a live game or a running scan of S7a; `engine.modemMoved`, called whenever the radio is tuned back to the field's channel: S7a's channel set, its scan starting and ending, the modem coming back). The ladder is not constant-time (`BigInt`). | The Pi's rules and timings; the scalar lives one run and the phone is the GM's, so a timing side channel on it is out of the threat model (adoption.md section 7 lists radio attackers, not someone timing the phone's CPU). | proposed |
| PE24-95 | USB adoption on a phone controller? | **Not offered**: `GET /api/v1/adopt` says `usb: false`, the scan and the adoption answer 503, as a Pi with `[adopt] usb = false`; the console already shows its notice. Android's USB host could reach a Field Box's USB-Serial-JTAG console (the same CDC ACM device as a pod, 303a:1001), but the phone has one USB port (the pod's, or the charger's), the app's USB plugin holds one device at a time and opens it with DTR and RTS set (which may reset a box, as the Pi's adoption avoids), and the line exchange would need its own relay from the UI isolate. The field card (PE24-93) and pairing by code (PE24-94) cover the phone. | Two working ways already; revisit if a field asks to adopt by cable from a phone. | proposed |
| PE24-96 | **For the maintainer:** firmware updates from a phone controller. | As `firmware_updates.py`: releases in the controller's folder of the app's private storage (`firmware/`, at most 4), checked against the **release keys set in the app's Settings** (*Chaves das versões de firmware*, Android only, at most 8, standard base64; the Pi's `[firmware] release_keys`; none: no release is taken) and the image's own descriptors; `CMD update` over the radio only while the live game is ready; served to the boxes with no token over the phone's HTTPS with the S6 field CA (the boxes pin it from join key 13), so only while the controller is open to the field's Wi-Fi (`https`); a box's report checked with the game key; HELLO's version, `node.update_failed` over LoRa, the 600 s timeout, the start guard and the GM's cancel. Uploads take the raw image (up to the 2 MiB OTA slot) on `POST /api/v1/firmware`, from the console in a laptop's browser on the field's Wi-Fi (the app's web view may offer no file picker). | The same trust model as the Pi: the controller only keeps bad releases out of its list; the box decides with its own keys. The keys are a trust anchor the phone's owner types, as a Pi's operator writes `controller.toml`. | proposed, for the maintainer |
| PE24-97 | What does `whoami` say now? (PE24-70) | `features` adds `boxes`, `adoption` and `firmware` (nine areas with S7a's `radio` and `field`); the shared case checks them on both controllers, and `console_bundle_test.dart` that each is routed. The console shows the *Adotar* tab, the invite dialog and the firmware panel on a phone. | Each area is served whole (USB adoption answers as a Pi with it off). | proposed |
| PE24-98 | How are the radio's field and pair frames in the shared contract cases? | New step kinds on both runners: `field_rx` (a box's sealed field frame, its nonce from that device's own counter; `replay`), `pair_rx`, `field_tx` (the frames sent, decoded with the case's join key, `kinds`, `save`), `field_ack`, `pair_box` and `pair_box_done` (an unadopted box's side of a run with its code); requests with raw bodies (`body_hex`, `body_text`), `headers` and `mac_key` (a box's signed report), answers checked by `text` or `bytes_sha256`; case settings `https` and `firmware_keys`. The Pi's runner now sets up `Adoption` (USB off) and `FirmwareUpdates` on every controller, as `main.build` does. 10 new cases (45 in all, after S7a's 35) on both sides: re-invites (an invite and its answer, retries until it fails, a busy box, the previous game, the start guard), GAME_OVER (three times, none without a radio, after a restart without the key), adoption (the card, no USB, without a join key), pairing (a wrong code then the right one, a box that refuses, a run with no answer, offers under one name or id) and firmware (upload, release and image, install, the start guard, report, HELLO, no keys and no HTTPS). | The contract stays the Pi's: every case passes there first; the recording modems keep field frames apart from `tx` so the earlier cases are unchanged. | proposed |
| PE24-99 | Small choices of the port. | AES-128-CCM is the app's own (`aes_ccm.dart`, RFC 3610 over pointycastle's AES block), not pointycastle's `CCMBlockCipher`, so the code read against the RFC is what the vectors check; HKDF-SHA256 likewise (`field_frame.dart`). The engine's hooks are small and additive (`startGuards`, the GAME_OVER in `endGame`, `sendUpdate`, the HELLO to the invites, field frames routed to pairing then the invites, `boxes`/`pairing`/`adoption`/`firmware` set by their constructors as `checkin` is). The request body cap is 2 MiB on `POST /api/v1/firmware` only (1 MiB elsewhere), and handlers now see the raw body and the headers. `previous_game` comes from the store's nodes with a device id, as on the Pi. | Fewer moving parts to review; the same answers as the Pi's. | proposed |


## The Android controller role, slice S7b: presets, mode packages, scenarios, branding (PE24-80 to PE24-89, 2026-10-23)

The maintainer approved (2026-10-23) pulling parts of slice S7 forward before the field test. This part, S7b, gives the phone controller the Pi's presets, mode uploads and signed mode packages, the modes sent to boxes, scenarios (check, compile, install), role packages in a game and team branding, with 11 new shared contract cases passing on both controllers (56 in all, with S7a's and S7c's). S7a (PE24-74 to PE24-79) and S7c (PE24-90 to PE24-99) were built in parallel and merged first; S7b is rebased on them. PE24-80, PE24-82, PE24-84 and PE24-89 are **for the maintainer**; PE24-88 is for the coordinator (tooling outside app/).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PE24-80 | **For the maintainer:** presets, mode uploads and role packages on the phone; the brief asked for presets with GET, POST, PUT and DELETE. | As the Pi, exactly: `GET|POST /api/v1/presets` (a preset is saved under its name, and saving the same name again replaces it and keeps its id; the read token gets them without gm_only parameters); there is no `PUT` or `DELETE /api/v1/presets/{id}` on the Pi, so none on the phone (`PUT|DELETE` are the saved teams', `/api/v1/team-presets/{id}`, served). `GET|POST /api/v1/modes`: the built-in packages and uploads (`app/lib/controller/controller_modes.dart`, the Pi's `modes.py`: the same file names, sizes and manifest checks, the scripts loaded in the core before they are kept, a built-in id refused), kept in the store's `modes` document with a signed package's release and archive; presets in its `presets` document. A game names a main mode, its modifiers and role packages, or a scenario first (`org.club.ridge+org.atalaia.objectives`), composed as the simulator's `compose` (ported): a box's join names only what it runs (`node_mode_id`) with that part's parameters, the start check needs every role of the parts (a scenario's: its boxes' roles), mode commands from every part. A game made from a preset carries its `preset_id`, and `game.start` names the preset. mode.yaml is read with package:yaml (YAML 1.2) where the Pi uses PyYAML (YAML 1.1): an upload with a key given twice is refused on the phone and taken (last one wins) on the Pi; nothing else in a manifest the Pi accepts was found to differ. | The contract is the Pi's API; a preset changed by name covers editing one, and deleting one is a later choice for both controllers if a field asks. | open |
| PE24-81 | Signed mode packages on the phone. | `app/lib/controller/mode_package.dart` is the Dart port of `mode_package.py` (the archive's canonical form, the release and its text, verifying in the Pi's order, the index and the revocation list, a box's report and its MAC), checked against every group of `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json`; Ed25519 by the `cryptography` package's pure-Dart implementation, as the chrono records. `controller_signed_modes.dart` is `signed_modes.py`: `GET /api/v1/mode-packages` (keys, revoked ids, the index's entries and their state), `POST /api/v1/mode-packages` (the archive with the `.release.json`'s fields in the query, never verified by the project), `POST /api/v1/mode-packages/install` (from the index, which is an `https://` URL fetched with the system's trust store, no redirects, 15 s, 1 MB, or a file on the phone with its packages next to it; its `revoked.json` revokes too), each verified against the trusted keys, failing closed, then installed as an upload. | One format, one set of vectors for both languages; the index on a phone without internet is a file as on a Pi. | open |
| PE24-82 | **For the maintainer:** the phone's `[modes]` settings (trusted keys, revoked ids, index, the boxes' keys). | In the app's *Definições*: *Pacotes de modos (controlador de campo)*, Android only (`app/lib/controller/controller_modes_screen.dart`): four fields, the trusted mode keys and the boxes' mode keys (base64, one per line, each checked), revoked key ids (16 hex digits) and the index (an `https://` address or a file); kept in the app's settings (public keys, not secrets) and given to the controller when it starts (`ControllerBoot.modes`), as the Pi reads `controller.toml` at start; a change applies at the next start. `fetch_timeout_s` stays 300 s (not shown). The texts are in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese (the last two drafted, not reviewed, as the rest of those languages). | The Pi's settings are a file the GM edits; a phone has its settings screen. The console could carry them too, but on the Pi they are not on the API, and a key the controller trusts should not be changed by whoever holds the GM token from a laptop. | open |
| PE24-83 | The modes sent to boxes from the phone. | `controller_mode_fetch.dart` is `mode_fetch.py` with the HELLO rule as fixed in 4d0c23d (P5D-14 to P5D-18): a fetch is offered only for signed parts with a box key, over HTTPS (the field CA and addresses of S6); `GET .../{id}/release` and `.../package` without a token only for a part of the live game's mode and only while it is ready; reports with the game key's HMAC; a HELLO ends a fetch only when reports say the box keeps every part it was offered (a HELLO without them sets `hello_seen` and the fetch times out as `report_lost`), `node.modes_failed` taken after `hello_seen` and after a fetch ended in its HELLO; the start guard waits; `DELETE /api/v1/mode-fetch` stops waiting; boxes marked to forget (join key 17). The app's join codec gains keys 16 and 17 (`protocol/join_payload.dart`, checked against join.json's `mode_fetch` vectors), and `POST /api/v1/join` takes `fetch_modes` and `forget_modes` as on the Pi. S7c's radio invites (`controller_boxes.dart`) take `fetch_modes` too and give key 17 to a box marked to forget, with the box's device id and tier, a box that cannot be sent the modes refused alone (`not_sent`); invites show *accepted* or *failed* while a fetch has not ended in its HELLO, and such a box is not counted in the live game, as the Pi's `reinvite.py`. | The same rules, so a box cannot tell which controller it fetches from; the radio's invites are S7c's. | open |
| PE24-84 | **For the maintainer:** the scenario compiler on the phone: port it, run it in the core, or take compiled packages only? | **Ported to Dart** (`app/lib/controller/scenarios/`: the manifest's loader, the validator, the Lua writer, the package with mode.yaml, and the runtime's text), so `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` and `POST /api/v1/scenarios` work on a phone as on a Pi (`controller_scenarios.dart` is `scenario_service.py`: the summary for the console's editor, the load check in the core, the manifest kept beside its package, a scenario a live game runs not replaced, the boxes the GM names in the Join tab, the staff's and the players' views, objective events by visibility). The port gives the same problems for every case of `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` (59) and the same field.lua and mode.yaml, byte for byte, for every case of the new `protocol/vectors/scenario_compile.json` (23 manifests and 34 loader cases, made by `tools/scenario_compile_vectors.py` from the Python compiler; the Pi's tests run its `--check`, so the Python cannot change without the vectors). **Cost:** about 3,800 lines of Dart (1,330 of Python and the 450-line runtime); the Dart reads YAML 1.1 as PyYAML does (`yes`, octal and sexagesimal numbers, merge keys) and writes mode.yaml as PyYAML's emitter does; every change to the compiler or the validator is made twice and the vectors regenerated. Rejected: the compiler in the core's C (none exists: a third language for the same rules, and a YAML parser in the core); compiled packages only (the console's scenario builder would be refused on a phone, and the GM would need a Pi or the site server to compile). Known gaps: a character assigned in a newer Unicode than one side knows may be escaped differently in field.lua; a few rare YAML forms are read by one side and refused by the other (listed in `scenario_manifest.dart`), never read differently. Found on the way, for the Pi (not changed): Python's `$` also matches before a final line feed, so an id or a code ending in a line feed is accepted (the Dart copies it so both agree); a JSON manifest with an emoji written as a surrogate-pair escape makes the Pi's validator raise (a 500); both fixed on both controllers as PE24-106 and PE24-107. | The builder is the GM's way to make scenarios on the field; the shared vectors make two implementations safe to keep, as for mode packages. | changed by the maintainer (2026-10-23): one implementation, in Lua in the core, for both controllers (PE24-109 to PE24-117) |
| PE24-85 | Team branding on the phone. | `controller_branding.dart` is `branding.py` with the engine's team entries: `GET|PUT /api/v1/field/branding` (the name is the poster's, the accent), `GET|PUT|DELETE /api/v1/field/branding/{logo|wallpaper}`, `POST /api/v1/crests`, `GET /api/v1/crests/{id}` (ETag, 304, the Pi's headers), `GET|POST /api/v1/team-presets`, `PUT|DELETE /api/v1/team-presets/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/teams` (no token) and `PUT .../teams/{team}`, `team_info` when a game is made; the stream's hello carries `branding` (PE24-15's last item). Images are checked by `controller_images.dart`, the port of `images.py` (no image library; metadata stripped), which gives the Pi's output byte for byte on 113 cases its checker made (`app/test/controller/fixtures/controller_images.json`) and refuses a decompression bomb before inflating it. Each image is a store document (`img_` and 28 hex digits of its SHA-256) listed in the `images` document; the file store keeps documents in memory, so at most a 4 MB wallpaper, a logo and the crests in use (500 at most, unused ones gone a day after their upload). A phone has no site sync, so its branding is always the GM's (`[sync] branding = "site"` is a Pi's). The API's body limit goes from 1 MB to 4 MB for the wallpaper. | The same pages and apps show the same branding from either controller. | open |
| PE24-86 | How do the shared cases check uploads, packages and boxes' fetches? | New in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` (11 cases whose names start with `S7b`, 56 in all, passing on both controllers): `fixtures.values` saved in every case (the signed `org.example.relay` role package of mode_package.json, its release text and SHA-256, a 16x16 PNG with a text chunk and the id its stripped bytes get, the mode keys); case settings `mode_keys`, `mode_revoked` and `box_keys`, with S7c's `https`; S7c's raw request bodies (`body_hex`, `body_text`, `mac_key`, `headers`) may name their `content_type`; answers matched by S7c's `text` and `bytes_sha256` and by the answer's `headers`; `join_decode` with `fetch_modes` and `forget_modes`.
