Both runners set up scenarios, branding, signed packages and the mode fetch as their controllers do. The cases cover presets, uploads and a role package in a game with its joins, a signed upload and its refusals (no key, a revoked or untrusted key, a bad signature), a fetch from offer to report and the HELLO it waits for, a `report_lost` timeout and stopping the wait, scenarios from check to a game with named boxes and the read token's view, and branding from the field's name to a game's teams. | The phone answers byte for byte where boxes read it (release, archive, crests), and in status and shape elsewhere. | open |
| PE24-87 | Where the phone still differs from the Pi after S7b. | No standby (M6-1: a standby installs the scenario too) nor promotion (failover is later); no site sync of branding or presets; the index's packages over `https://` use the phone's trust store; mode.yaml as YAML 1.2 (PE24-80); the fetch timeout fixed (PE24-82). None is covered by a shared case the Pi passes. | Later slices, or what a phone's role does not need. | open |
| PE24-88 | **For the coordinator (tooling outside app/):** the app's tests read three things from the Pi's folder. | `app/tool/check.sh` now also copies `controller/tests/fixtures` (the branding images the image checker is tested on), `controller/tests/scenarios` (the example scenarios) and `controller/atalaia_controller/scenarios/runtime.lua` (checked to be the app's copy) into its work folder; CI's `flutter-test` runs check.sh, so nothing else changes. `tools/scenario_compile_vectors.py` is new (`--check` from the Pi's unit tests). | The tests compare the phone with the Pi's own files rather than copies that could drift. | open |
| PE24-89 | **For the maintainer (an API addition):** the phone's `features`. | `GET /api/v1/whoami` on the phone now lists `presets`, `branding`, `scenarios` and `mode_packages` with `checkin`, `join`, `box_language`, `positions` and S7a's and S7c's `radio`, `field`, `boxes`, `adoption`, `firmware` (`controllerFeatures`), so the console shows the *Predefinições* and *Cenários* tabs, saved teams, team names and crests, the field's branding and the signed mode packages and modes sent to boxes on a phone (PE24-72). Still hidden: ATAK, the schedule and failover. `console_bundle_test.dart` checks the list against the routes, a new shared case that both controllers list the four. The console's files are unchanged. | The areas are served whole; with S7c's boxes area the invite dialog's *Send missing modes* works on a phone too. | open |
| PE24-106 | The Pi's names that end with a line feed (found by the S7b port, PE24-84). | **Refused, on both controllers.** Python's `re` `$` also matches before a final line feed, so `"org.club.x\n"` was a scenario id, `"hill\n"` an objective id and `"1234\n"` a code (and the parameter name, player role and the Lua writer's bare-key and comment patterns took one too). The patterns in `scenarios/manifest.py` and `scenarios/lua.py` end with `\Z` now; the Dart port, which had copied the quirk with `\n?$` so both agreed, drops it (Dart's `$` never matched there). Three shared cases in `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` (`bad_id` for the id and an objective id, `bad_param` for the code), the golden case of `scenario_compile.json` that compiled them now gives those problems, and a Python test with a parameter name and a player role as well. | A name the box shows or the Lua indexes should be what it seems; nothing valid is refused. | open |
| PE24-107 | Characters past U+FFFF written as surrogate-pair escapes (found by the S7b port, PE24-84). | **A pair is the character it encodes; half of one is a problem in words, on both controllers.** JSON written with only ASCII (Python's `json.dumps` by default) writes an emoji as `"\ud83d\ude00"`; PyYAML reads each escape as its own code point, and the validator raised on encoding them (a 500 from `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check`), while the Dart port's YAML library refused the manifest. Now the Pi's loader joins every pair in the manifest's texts and keys (a key a pair makes equal to another is "given twice") and refuses half of one (`bad_manifest`, "\ud83d is half of a surrogate pair"); a lone surrogate in the text itself is refused by the YAML reader, and the size check no longer raises on it. The validator says `bad_unicode` at the path of a text or key holding half a pair in a map built in code, alone, since such a text cannot be measured. The Dart loader writes each pair as the one `\U` escape it means before package:yaml reads the text (an escape after an escaped backslash is left; escaped line breaks between the halves are skipped, as YAML drops them); it keeps that reading only when no plain, single-quoted or block text held a pair's escape (YAML reads escapes in double-quoted texts only), else it joins each pair where package:yaml stops at it; the Dart validator says `bad_unicode` as the Pi does. Shared cases: three invalid (`scenario.json`: half a pair in a name, a low half first, a key) and one valid (a name and a description written as pairs); in `scenario_compile.json` two manifests that compile (JSON with pairs; YAML with pairs in double-quoted, plain and single-quoted texts, which takes the Dart's slow path), two refused, and seven loader cases. Tests of the API (a pair checks, half of one or a lone surrogate in the request is `ok: false`, not a 500). | What JSON's writers produce must be read; a half cannot be written as UTF-8, so it is refused, with its place when it can be told. | open |
| PE24-108 | A manifest with a date YAML recognises but the calendar has not (`version: 2026-13-45`, `2026-02-30 25:61:00`)? | Refused as `bad_manifest` ("not valid YAML: a date that does not exist") instead of PyYAML's plain ValueError, which was a 500 from `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` (found while fixing PE24-107). The Dart port's reader already refuses such a date (its comment noted the Pi's gap). | Same class of bug as PE24-107: input must never crash the check. | open |

## Phase 5 design refresh after phases 2-4 software (2026-10-22)

The phase 5 design (`docs/phase-5-community/`) was written on 2026-10-18, before phases 2-4 were built. This refresh checks it against the code as built and re-sequences the plan into small plan phases in build order. Each answer is the recommendation used in the refreshed ADR.md (dated amendments) and PLAN.md. P5R-8 and P5R-10 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5R-1 | In which order is phase 5 built? | Twelve small software plan phases, then three hardware ones: 1 the visual mode editor, 2 the editor and the simulator (`field.lua` in the web view, `POST /api/reload`, the Test button), 3 the editor's remaining blocks (geo, GM commands, roles, kinds), 4 mode packages and signing, 5 the sandbox-escape battery, 6 signed packages in the controller, 7 scenario blocks in the console, 8 the registry repository as a template, 9 community processes, 10 German, 11 Traditional Chinese, 12 build guides for the other node types; HW1 carrier PCB, HW2 guide photos, HW3 community content on a real field. First: plan phase 1. | The old phases A-D were large and partly blocked on phases 1-2, which are now done in software; small plan phases are verified from a clean copy and committed alone, as phase 4's were. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-2 | Where does the visual editor live: the simulator's web view, the console's scenario editor, or both? | **Both, for different things.** The mode editor (node and field scripts, `mode.yaml`) is a page of the simulator at `/editor/`, as P5-2 decided; the GM console gets a block view of its existing scenario builder (plan phase 7) that writes the same scenario YAML. | A box runs only the scripts built into its firmware (K10), so blocks made at the field could not reach the boxes: a mode's node script reaches boxes only through `modes/` and a signed firmware release, which is a mode author's job, on a computer, offline, with the simulator. What a GM can install at the field is a scenario (`kind: scenario`, P4R7), and the console already edits scenarios. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-3 | What API do the blocks target, and how are the files laid out? | Today's script API, field scripts included (the field API shipped in phase 2): memory (`state.node{}`, `state.field{}`), events (`on`, `emit` with priority, the event's values), timers (`every`, `after`, kept in a variable to `cancel`), game (`win`, `finish`, `time`, `teams`, stop), score, the box's screen, sound and team display, settings, `rand`, `log`, `//`, and on the controller `field.nodes` (as box ids), `owner`, `common_owner`, `send`, `score`; Blockly's own logic, numbers, text, lists, variables and functions. Geo and GM commands in plan phase 3. One `blocks.js` and one `generator.js` in `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/`, not sixteen files in `editor/`. | The old plan reserved a "Coming soon" field category for an API that now exists; the page has no build step, so fewer files are simpler to load and to review, and the simulator's web server serves its own folder. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-4 | Which Blockly, and how is it shipped? | Blockly **13.3.0** (2026-09-10), vendored unmodified (core, its blocks, its Lua generator, `msg/en.js`, `msg/pt.js`, `media/`) in `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/vendor/blockly/`, checked against npm's integrity and listed with SHA-256 in its README, which a test checks; Apache-2.0 in `REUSE.toml`; never a CDN. The generator is an instance of Blockly's `LuaGenerator`. | 11.x is two majors old; 13 is accessible by keyboard and screen reader by default and drops the jsdom dependency; 13.4.0 was nine days old. Vendoring is how the console ships its libraries, and the editor must work offline; Blockly's Lua generator already handles precedence and its own blocks. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-5 | When does the editor's "Test" (`POST /api/reload`) come? | Plan phase 2, together with `field.lua` in the simulator's web view, which runs node scripts only today. ADR-P5-2's endpoint shape stays. | A reload of a mode with a field script (Hold-All, any scenario) would otherwise play without its controller logic. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-6 | How is a JavaScript generator tested in CI? | In headless Chromium (Playwright) in the GM console's test image: `simulator/editor_tests/` (`run.sh`), `python3 tools/docker_editor.py`, a new CI job `editor`. The generator runs in the page (`window.atalaiaEditor.generate`), so the tested code is the shipped code; the generated modes run in the simulator in the same container. Plain checks (routes, checksums, language keys, examples) run in `tools/check.sh` (`simulator/tests/test_editor_files.py`). | The dev image has no JavaScript runtime and Blockly in Node needs more dependencies; the console's image already has Chromium, Python and the core's build tools. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-7 | How are mode packages signed? | Like firmware releases (`protocol/firmware-update.md`): Ed25519 (RFC 8032) over a text manifest `atalaia-mode-package-v1` with `id`, `version`, `size`, `sha256`, base64 signature; keys made by a `keygen` like `tools/firmware_release.py`'s (encrypted PEM, refused inside the repository); key id = first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the key; `tools/mode_package.py`; shared vectors; revocation by key id. Supersedes ADR-P5-4's signature over the bare hex hash, its SSH-style fingerprint and `registry/tools/keygen.py`/`sign.py`. | One reviewed scheme and one set of tools; binding id and version stops a signature being reused for another mode or an older version. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-8 | **For the maintainer:** may boxes ever load signed community node scripts at run time? | **Not in phase 5.** A registry package reaches the field as a controller package (a scenario or a field-script package, installed through `POST /api/v1/modes` checks); a community mode's node script reaches boxes by being merged into `modes/` and shipped in a signed firmware release. A run-time path would need its own ADR (signed scripts on LittleFS, the flash budget, the review burden). | K10 (confirmed by the maintainer) is what the firmware does; a run-time loader would turn every community script into code on every box, and today the only gate is the sandbox. | maintainer (2026-10-22): yes, but only what a game needs: the GM console sends a box the modes it lacks, as needed (signed packages, nothing else at run time); to plan as its own plan phase. Planned 2026-10-22 as **plan phase 13** (`docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md`, "Plan phase 13: Missing modes from the GM console"; ADR.md "Note 2026-10-22: P5R-8"), design choices P5B-8 to P5B-17 (open; P5B-9 for the maintainer) |
| P5R-9 | When is `atalaia-modes` created, and where do the registry tools live meanwhile? | The tools, schema and tests live in this repository (`tools/mode_package.py`, `registry/`); plan phase 8 prepares the registry repository's files as a template with its CI; the maintainer creates the repository from it. The site server's scenario library stays per field and can import from the registry later. | P5-3 (confirmed: creating a repository changes the maintainer's account); the tooling can be built and tested first. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-10 | **For the maintainer:** which third language first? | **German (de)** first, in every domain (site, console, editor, app, box texts), then **Traditional Chinese (Taiwan, zh-TW)**. | The maintainer chose German and Traditional Chinese next (P5-1, changed 2026-10-21). German needs no new glyphs: the box's fonts and texts take it as a third column; zh-TW needs a CJK subset in the box's fonts (flash budget to measure) and in the app, so it is its own plan phase. A native speaker should review each before release. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended (German first, then Traditional Chinese) |
| P5R-11 | Does the i18n design (YAML everywhere, `i18n/` folders, compile-time firmware headers) still hold? | No: each domain keeps the format it was built with (site YAML, console and editor JSON, app Dart tables, the box's C table chosen per game by the join, modes in `mode.yaml` `i18n:`), each with a parity test; `tools/i18n_check.py` comes with German. AC-8 is met by `mode.yaml` (P4R22). `build_editor_i18n.py` and `build_firmware_i18n.py` are dropped. | Converting working formats would only add tools; the parity checks are what prevent drift. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-12 | Do the review checklist's items still fit? | Item 6 and 11 refer to `mode.yaml`'s `i18n:` instead of an `i18n/` folder; "verified" also needs a valid signature of the release-style manifest by a key that is not revoked. | As built (P4R22) and P5R-7. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-13 | Where does the sandbox-escape battery run? | In this repository's CI, with the core's C tests (next to `test_sandbox` in `core/tests/test_node.c`, under ASan/UBSan) and through the simulator for node and field scripts (plan phase 5); the registry's CI runs the same battery. | Every core change should face the battery, not only registry submissions; the core already tests the basics. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5R-14 | What is left of the build guides? | The Field Box's exists (`hardware/field-box/BUILD.md`, with wiring diagrams); plan phase 12 writes the Satellite's, the I/O pod's and the target addon's in the same manner, rendered on the project page; photos wait for hardware (HW2). | The old plan predates the Field Box build manual. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 1: the visual mode editor (2026-10-22)

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5A-1 | Which blocks, and which are left out for now? | 37 project blocks (memory, events with the inputs each script kind gets, timers, game, score, the box's screen/sound/team display, settings, `rand`, `log`, `//`, five field blocks) plus Blockly's `controls_if`, comparisons, `and`/`or`/`not`, booleans, nothing, the ternary, for-each, numbers, arithmetic, rounding, text, join, list length and creation, variables and functions. Left out: `geo.*`, GM commands, generic `cap(id):method()`, `math.random`-based blocks (the sandbox removes `math.random`), while and repeat loops (a busy loop trips the instruction watchdog), text functions. "Stop here" has no next connection, so nothing can follow a `return`. | What the 14 built-in modes use most; the rest in plan phase 3. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-2 | How does the generated Lua look? | Two SPDX lines from the Mode tab, the mode's name and description as comments, then: the memory (`local s = state.node { ... }` or `local fs = state.field { ... }`), the Blockly variables as one `local a, b` line (a block variable is global to its script, as in Blockly), helpers (`node_ids(role)` for "the boxes with role"), `local function`s ordered so a function comes before its callers (functions that call each other are declared first and defined as `function f()`), then handlers and timers in the order the blocks sit; a blank line between them; 2-space indent; double quotes; block comments as `--` lines; a handler or timer that only calls a function without arguments names it (`on("game.start", show)`). | ADR-P5-1's rules and the hand-written modes' style; a variable model that matches what the blocks show. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-3 | How is a mode saved? | One JSON file, `{"format": "atalaia-mode-editor", "version": 1, "mode": {...the Mode tab...}, "node": <Blockly state>, "field": <Blockly state>}`, downloaded as `<id>.blocks.json` and opened again; the last state is a draft in the browser's storage (best effort). | The blocks are the source; the Lua and the manifest are generated from them every time. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-4 | What does the editor put in `mode.yaml`? | `kind: mode`, one role (its name from the Mode tab, `zone` by default); the inputs the box script handles are `requires` (`team_input`, `keypad`, `nfc`, `hit`, `gps`), the outputs it uses `optional` (`team_display`, `audio`, `display`); settings from "setting ... is a ..." blocks (int with min/max, bool, team, string), English and Portuguese labels; the Portuguese name and description under `i18n: pt`; licence from the seven of ADR-P5-5. Problems: a bad id, no name, a setting used but not declared or declared twice, a default outside its range, a memory value with no starting value, an event value outside a "when" block. | It reproduces the Domination and Hold-All manifests exactly; several roles and other kinds come with plan phase 3. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-5 | Which Content-Security-Policy for `/editor/`? | `default-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'none'`; scripts only from the simulator's origin, no inline script in the page; the simulator's own page keeps its stricter policy. Only known suffixes under the editor's folder are served (no dot files, no `..`). | Blockly injects its stylesheet and sets inline styles; nothing else needs loosening, and the server stays localhost-only. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-6 | The examples differ from the hand-written scripts in two places: is that right? | Yes. Domination's example emits `zone.captured` before it changes the owner (so `previous_team` comes from memory without a variable); Hold-All's field example scores `fs.holder` in its timer instead of a local `team` captured by the closure. Both pass every test of the hand-written modes; the tests are the specification. | Blockly variables are script-wide, so a closure over a per-call local cannot be expressed; the order of an event and a memory write is not observable within one handler. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5A-7 | Where do the editor's tests run? | `simulator/editor_tests/test_editor.py` (15 tests in Chromium: exact Lua for small projects, scripts loading and running in the core, both round trips with the manifests compared to the hand-written ones, the page in both languages, examples, downloads, save and open, the draft, problems pointing at their block) by `simulator/editor_tests/run.sh` in the console's image (`tools/docker_editor.py`, CI job `editor`); `simulator/tests/test_editor_files.py` (6 tests) in `tools/check.sh`. | P5R-6. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 2: the editor and the simulator; plan phase 13 planned (2026-10-22)

Plan phase 2 built the editor's *Test in the simulator* (`docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md`, plan phase 2): P5B-1 to P5B-7. The maintainer's answer to P5R-8 is planned as plan phase 13 (PLAN.md, "Plan phase 13: Missing modes from the GM console"): P5B-8 to P5B-17, not built. P5B-9 is **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5B-1 | `field.lua` in the simulator's web view: what was missing? | Only seeing it. `LiveGame` drives a `Simulation`, which has run a mode's `field.lua` since phase 2 (`serve ../modes/holdall --node ...` scored through the controller's script); the plan and the simulator's README said it did not. The snapshot now carries `controller` (status, error, memory, points, the last 20 commands it sent to boxes, its log) and the page a *Field controller* panel; the README's table is corrected. | Code beats docs; a mode author needs to see what the controller's script does to debug it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-2 | `POST /api/reload`'s body and answer. | ADR-P5-2's body (`mode_yaml`, `node_lua`, `field_lua`, `params`) plus an optional `nodes` (`{name: role}`, 1-16 boxes, names of 1-24 letters, digits, `-` and `_`, never `controller`); any other key, a wrong type or not JSON is `400`; more than 1 000 000 bytes `413` (the controller's package limit, P5-6); the Host check and JSON only as `/api/action` (`403`, `415`). A mode that does not load is `200 {"status": "error", "errors": [...]}`; a loaded one `200 {"status": "ok", "errors": [...errors on game.start], "reloads", "generation", "mode", "nodes"}`. Each error is an object `{message, script, line}` (`script` `node`, `field`, or `mode` for the manifest; `line` from the message's `node.lua:12:`), with `node`, `t` and `type` for errors while running. | ADR-P5-2 had strings like `"line 12: ..."`; the editor needs the script and line to find the block, and the whole message to show. Status codes keep their meaning for requests that are not a mode. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-3 | What does a reload keep and what does it change? | It keeps the server's core, teams, seed and sandbox limits; boxes as given, else the current ones whose role the new mode has, else one box named after its first role; parameter defaults with `params` over them, checked as `serve --param` is. It takes a main mode only (`kind: mode`; modifiers given to `serve` are dropped) with a reverse-domain id; the scripts' chunk names are the mode's id (`org.club.x/node.lua:12`). The scripts load first; on any error the running game is not touched. Then the game starts at once, its clock running (ADR-P5-2's order), and a reset replays the reloaded mode. | What an author tests is the mode they edit; starting at once also brings errors in `game.start` handlers back in the answer. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-4 | Does *Test* open the simulator's page? | No pop-up: the editor shows a status line ("Hold-All Domination is running in the simulator with 3 boxes.") with *Open the game*, a link to `/` in a tab named `atalaia-sim`, which the editor's *Simulator* link now uses too. An open simulator tab picks the new game up by itself (it polls, and a reload changes the game's generation). | The editor page stays where it is with its blocks; a window opened after an awaited request can be blocked as a pop-up; one named tab is reused. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-5 | How do the simulator's errors reach the blocks? | The editor generates each script a second time with Blockly's statement prefix set to a marker line before each statement block; with the markers removed it must equal the real script, and then line *n* belongs to the last marked block before it (`lineBlocks` in `generator.js`; otherwise errors show without a block). Errors on loading come in the reload's answer; errors while the game runs are polled from `GET /api/state` (`script_errors`) each second while the tested mode is the simulator's (its `reloads` count). They are problems ("In the simulator: zone1, zone2: org.club.x/node.lua:8: ...", one per message, naming the boxes) and warnings on their blocks, cleared when the generated files change. | No change to the generated Lua (the source map comes from Blockly's own hook); errors from every box running the same script would otherwise repeat. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-6 | How many boxes does *Test* use? | A *Boxes* field next to the button, 1 to 16, 3 by default, named after the Mode tab's role (`zone1`, `zone2`, ...). | Field scripts such as Hold-All need several boxes; the editor's modes have one role (plan phase 3 adds more). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-7 | Who may reload? | Whoever may already send actions: the server answers only local host names (or `--allow-host` ones) and JSON, so another site can neither post a form nor a script without a CORS preflight the server never grants. No extra token. A reloaded script runs in the core's sandbox like any mode; served on `0.0.0.0` with `--allow-host`, reload is open to that network as actions are. | The simulator is a tool on the author's own machine; a token would add a step and protect nothing the sandbox does not. Alternative if wanted: a `--no-reload` flag. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-8 | Plan phase 13: where do the packages a box gets come from? | Only from its field controller's mode store: a signed package the GM uploads in the console (made with `tools/mode_package.py`, plan phase 4, from the editor's files) or imported from a registry index (plan phase 6; later the site server's library). The editor never sends to a box, and a box never reaches the internet. | One place that the GM controls and that already checks packages (P5-6); the box needs only its controller. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-9 | **For the maintainer:** which keys does a box trust for a mode package? | **Mode keys built into its firmware** (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS`, up to 4, next to the release keys, which may sign modes too); the controller checks the same list (`[modes] box_keys`) only to offer what boxes will take. A community mode reaches boxes once the project signs it (the registry's verified badge becomes that signature). Not now: a field's own key for its club's modes (set at adoption), or trusting whatever the controller vouches for. | The box decides with keys it carries, as for firmware (S19-8): a stolen or wrong controller cannot put code on boxes. The sandbox limits a script; the signature says who vouches for it. A field key would let clubs run their own modes without the project, at the cost of a second trust root per field. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended (mode keys built into the box firmware only) |
| P5B-10 | What does a box check without reading YAML? | P5R-7's signed manifest gets two lines: `atalaia-mode-package-v1`, `id`, `version`, `kind` (`mode`, `modifier`, `role`; a `scenario` is never sent to a box), `min_tier`, `size`, `sha256`. Plan phase 4 builds it so (not built yet, so no format to migrate). | A box has no YAML parser; kind and tier decide whether it may take the package at all, before the download. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-11 | How is a box told, and how does it download? | In the invite dialog, a box that answered *no mode* (field-frame result 3) is invited again with a join carrying a new optional key 16, `fetch_modes`; such a box joins as *fetching*, downloads each part it lacks over the field Wi-Fi with HTTPS pinned to the field CA (join keys 13, 14), `GET /api/v1/mode-packages/<id>/release` then `/package`, checks and installs them, then sends HELLO. Never over LoRa. Firmware without key 16 skips it (keys 16-31 are skipped by every reader) and answers *no mode* as today. | Reuses the join's trust (its HMAC, the field CA, the addresses) and the firmware update's HTTPS path; the radio carries only the small join. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-12 | Where does a box keep them, and how much? | On its `storage` LittleFS partition (2 MiB, with the presets): `/littlefs/modes/<id>/` (`node.lua`, `assets/screens/*.yaml`, the signed release), its preset slot marked *fetched* with version, key id and SHA-256. At most a 256 KiB archive (hashed whole while it streams, only the script and screens kept), 8 fetched packages and 512 KiB in all, and a composed game within the 16 KB preset buffer. A Reduced box uses the same storage, refuses `min_tier=full` and checks a script with its own 64 KB Lua heap. | The partition exists and has room; limits small enough that built-in presets and game state never run out. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-13 | When may a box fetch? | Only between games: while joining, never with a game running or paused (a game starting cancels a fetch); the controller's start guard waits while fetches are on their way (`[modes] fetch_timeout_s`, 300 s; the GM may stop waiting), as for firmware updates. | A box's Wi-Fi and flash work must not disturb a game, and a game must not start with boxes still missing its mode. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-14 | How does a box check and load one, and what becomes of K10? | In order, refusing at the first failure: the release text's exact lines and signature; the id it asked for; the signature with one of its mode keys (`bad_signature`, `no_key`); kind and tier (`tier`); not a built-in id (`builtin`: the firmware owns those); size and SHA-256 while streaming into a temporary file (`too_big`, `bad_hash`); the script loading in a fresh sandbox with its tier's limits and the composed game fitting (`load`); then it is kept. **K10 as amended:** a box runs the scripts built into its firmware and signed mode packages it fetched from its own field controller for a game, checked with keys in its firmware; nothing unsigned, no scenario, nothing over LoRa, no built-in id replaced. | K10 stays the rule for everything but this one checked path (ADR.md, "Note 2026-10-22: P5R-8"). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-15 | Rollback and removal? | A newer version of a fetched id replaces the older one only after passing every check (a failed fetch leaves the box as it was); when space is needed the box drops the fetched package a game used least recently, never one the joined game needs, never a built-in one; the console's *Boxes* tab can make a box forget its fetched modes (join key 17, `forget_modes`); a factory reset forgets them. A revoked key leaves the controller's list at once (it offers nothing signed by it) and the boxes' with a firmware release. | Like the firmware update: nothing is replaced before the new one is known good; a box cannot be filled up; revocation where it can act at once. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-16 | What does the console show, and how does it hear back? | In the invite dialog, next to the boxes that answered *no mode*: the game's parts the controller holds as packages boxes will take, *Send missing modes*, and per box: fetching, installed, refused (`no_wifi`, `unreachable`, `bad_signature`, `no_key`, `tier`, `builtin`, `too_big`, `no_space`, `bad_hash`, `load`, `cancelled`), failed; a part the controller lacks or holds unsigned is named with what to do. The box reports by `POST /api/v1/mode-packages/report` (HMAC with the game key, as the firmware report) or, when HTTPS cannot, by a new critical event `node.modes_failed` (part and reason) over LoRa. The *Boxes* tab lists each box's fetched modes. | The GM decides in the dialog where the problem shows; the report mirrors the firmware update's, which is built and tested. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5B-17 | Where does it go in the build order? | Plan phase 13, built after 6 (it needs 4's packages and signing, 5's sandbox battery and 6's signed packages in the controller); in two parts that can run in parallel: 13a the controller, console and protocol; 13b the box (`mode_fetch.c`, host-tested like `fw_update.c`). On real boxes in HW3. Numbered 13 so the other numbers stay as P5R-1 has them. Android props get the same packages through their Wi-Fi link in a later PE24 slice. | Without 5 a box would run community code no battery has faced; without 4 and 6 there is nothing signed to send. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |


## The field image's map position: any two opposite corners (2026-10-22)

Found on the bench: the maintainer gave a south-west and a north-east corner of a floor plan and the console refused them as "a mirror image".

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GR-1 | Which two corners does the GM give in the *Map position (ATAK)* panel? | Any two opposite corners, labelled *A corner* and *The opposite corner*: before saving, the console puts the northern point at the top (y 0) and the western one on the left (x 0), as on a north-up image, and sends the control points so placed. The field names (`top_left`, `bottom_right`) and the API are unchanged; corners given as before give the same points as before. Two points level in latitude or longitude are still refused by the controller. | The form named the top-left and bottom-right corners and took the points as typed; a GM reading a map app picks whichever two corners are handy, and the swap gave a mirrored transform that the controller (rightly) refused with an error that blamed swapped latitude and longitude. The controller already accepted any opposite pair, so only the page changed. | Built; tested in Chromium with the bench's values |


## Phase 5, plan phases 3-6: the editor's remaining blocks, mode packages and signing, the sandbox battery, signed packages in the controller (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phases 3, 4, 5 and 6. All open; the maintainer's own answers are marked where they apply (P5B-9).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5C-1 | Plan phase 3: which geo blocks, and what do they write? | A *Places* category in both toolboxes: "metres from *lat*, *lon* to *lat*, *lon*" (`geo.distance`), "position *lat*, *lon* is in *zone*" (`geo.in_zone`), a circle (`{ type = "circle", lat, lon, radius_m }`) and an area of corners (`{ type = "polygon", points = { { lat, lon }, ... } }`, a problem below 3 or above 32 corners, `ATALAIA_GEO_POLYGON_MAX`), "the event has a position" (`type(e.lat) == "number" and type(e.lon) == "number"`, since a phone's first `gps.fix` has none); the event values `lat`, `lon`, `acc`, `player`, `role` (and `strength` for `hit.hit`). A zone is a value block, so it can be kept in a variable or memory like VIP / Payload's. | What VIP / Payload's scripts use (ADR-P4-9); the zone tables are the core's own shape, so the core's errors stay meaningful. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-2 | How do GM commands look in blocks? | A declaration block, "GM command *name* for the roles *a, b*" with an English and a Portuguese label, in the boxes' *GM commands* category, writes `commands:` (and `i18n.pt.commands`); "when the mode command *name* arrives" writes `on("cmd.mode", function(e) if e.name ~= "name" then return end ... end)`, one handler per name (the core keeps every handler of an event). The controller's "send the mode command *name* to box *n* with ..." writes `field.send(n, "mode", { name = "...", ... })`, which needs no declaration. Problems: a declared command without its handler, declared twice, or for a role the mode lacks. **Code beats the plan:** PLAN.md said `cmd.<name>` handlers; a box gets `cmd.mode` with the name (RFC 0001 Amendment 5). | The Medic Kit's `refill` and Master and Satellites' `status` are built this way; one block per name reads like the hand-written guards. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-3 | Several roles: a workspace per role or a role block? | Neither: one box script for every role (a script does not know its role, M5-5), and the *Mode* tab lists the roles, a row each: its name, its inputs (by default those the box script reacts to, as before; or its own, chosen by checkbox) and *needed to start* (`needed: false` when off). Optional outputs stay the script's for every role. Problems: no role, a bad or repeated name, a role needing an input the script never reacts to, a role needing none while the script reacts to some (P5R's "a role without its inputs"), and a role named by "the boxes with role" or a GM command that the mode lacks. A project saved before (one `role`) opens as that role. | Master and Satellites (same inputs) and VIP / Payload (checkpoints `team_input`, the payload `gps`) are both expressible; a role workspace would suggest a script per role, which modes do not have. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-4 | Which kinds does the editor make? | Main mode, modifier and role package (`kind: mode`, `modifier`, `role` in `mode.yaml`). Not scenarios: they are built in the GM console (P5R-2, plan phase 7) and have no box script. | As the controller and the simulator take them (P4R7). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-5 | How does the editor know a mode's screens? | The *Mode* tab's *Add screens…* takes `assets/screens/<name>.yaml` files, checked in the page (a template name, at most 4096 bytes, a `template:` line naming the file; the core's own parser checks them with `python -m atalaia_sim test`); they are kept in the saved project as an optional `screens` map (the format stays `atalaia-mode-editor` version 1: older editors ignore the key), shown as preview tabs for download, and offered in the boxes' toolbox as screen blocks next to the box's built-in screens (`neutral`, `captured`, `countdown`, `standby`, `code_entry`, `defused`, `exploded`, `final_score`). A screen block naming neither is a problem. *Test* does not send them: the simulator's view shows a screen's name and data, not its drawing. | The page has no file system access to a mode folder; the files must travel with the blocks to be one project. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-6 | Which boxes does *Test* start for several roles? | *Boxes* in all, one for each role but the last and the rest for the last, at least one per role (Master and Satellites with 3: `master1`, `satellite1`, `satellite2`). | The usual shape: one special box (master, payload) and several of the common one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-7 | The third example: Master and Satellites. Where does it differ from the hand-written scripts? | Four places, all passing the 8 tests of the hand-written mode: the master's status is a boolean and three variables, not a table; `math.floor(e.held)` instead of `math.tointeger(e.held) or 0`; the scoring tick reads `fs.master` instead of a captured local (as P5A-6); the satellites are counted by a function returning one value (the count of all satellites is `#node_ids("satellite")`), and the master's id is `(node_ids("master"))[1]` (Blockly's list item). | Blockly variables are script-wide and its functions return one value; the tests are the specification. Chosen over Last Stand because it uses two roles, its own screen and a mode command. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-8 | Plan phase 4: what is a package's archive? | An **uncompressed POSIX ustar tar** of the mode folder's `mode.yaml`, `node.lua`, `field.lua` and `assets/...` under one root folder named after the id, in one canonical form (regular files only, sorted, mode 0644, owner 0, time 0, no names in the user and group fields, names of at most 100 bytes without the prefix field, two zero blocks at the end and nothing after), at most 1 000 000 bytes and 64 files, UTF-8 text without NUL, no segment empty or starting with a dot. A reader parses each header strictly, then packs what it read again and refuses the archive unless the bytes are equal: nothing is extracted to disk, so no path traversal, links, devices or hidden data. `tests/` stay out of a package (the registry's CI runs them on the submission). Not zip, not gzip, not the controller's JSON map of files. | A box (plan phase 13) must hash the archive while it streams it and keep only `node.lua` and the screens, with no decompressor and a small parser; one canonical form gives one SHA-256 for the same files, so a signature names exactly one archive; Python's `tarfile` and any `tar` read it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-9 | What does the manifest say, and where from? | Exactly P5B-10's lines: `atalaia-mode-package-v1`, `id`, `version`, `kind` (`mode`, `modifier`, `role`, `scenario`), `min_tier` (`reduced`, `full`), `size`, `sha256`, each read by `sign` from the archive's own `mode.yaml` (`kind` `mode` and `min_tier` `reduced` when it says none: scenarios have no tier, and a box checks a script with its own heap anyway). A scenario can be signed (the controller installs signed scenarios, plan phase 6); a box will refuse it. Versions as firmware versions (Semantic Versioning order). | One scheme with the firmware's (P5R-7); the manifest must agree with what the controller installs, so the verifier checks `mode.yaml` against it (`mismatch`). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-10 | In what order are packages checked, and what does each refusal say? | Release fields (`bad_release`), size limit (`too_big`), size and SHA-256 (`bad_hash`), a trusted key's signature over exactly the manifest (`no_key` when the release names a key id not in the trusted list, else `bad_signature`: the named id is only a hint, never trusted), the key not revoked (`revoked`, by id, even while the key is still configured), the archive's canonical form with the id as root (`bad_archive`, `mismatch`), its `mode.yaml` agreeing with the manifest (`mismatch`) and naming an approved licence (`licence`, ADR-P5-5). Cheap checks first; nothing from the archive is parsed before its signature is known good. | Fail closed and say why; parsing an untrusted archive only after the signature narrows what an attacker can feed the parser. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-11 | The registry's index and revocation list? | `index.json` `{schema_version: 1, name?, modes: [{id, name, description?, author?, versions: [{version, kind, min_tier, size, sha256, signature, key_id, license, url, verified}]}]}`; `url` is `https://...` or a path relative to the index (an index in a folder or on a USB stick for a field without internet), never `http://` or `..`; `verified` is the project's review (the badge). `revoked.json` `{schema_version: 1, keys: [{key_id, reason?, date?}]}`. Anything unexpected refuses the whole index (`bad_index`). JSON Schemas in `registry/schema/` for the registry's CI and other tools (checked with examples by `check-jsonschema` in CI's protocol job); the Python check (`parse_index`) is the controller's, with no JSON Schema library. | An index is data from elsewhere: refusing it whole is simpler and safer than guessing; offline fields are common. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-12 | Which keys sign mode packages, and with which tool? | **Mode keys**, made by `tools/mode_package.py keygen` as release keys are (Ed25519, an encrypted PKCS#8 file refused inside the repository, `ATALAIA_MODE_KEY_PASSPHRASE` for scripts), with the same key ids. A controller trusts the keys in its `[modes] keys` (plan phase 6), which may include a release key (P5B-9 says the release keys may sign modes too). `keygen` prints the controller's setting and the firmware's `CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS` line (used from plan phase 13, built into the firmware only, per the maintainer's answer to P5B-9). | A separate key for modes keeps the firmware's signing key out of day-to-day mode reviews; the same format keeps one set of checks. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-13 | How are the package vectors made and checked? | `tools/mode_package_vectors.py` writes `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json` (`--check` in `check.sh` and CI): RFC 8032's TEST 1 and 2 copied from the RFC and checked against the cryptography library before anything is written, TEST 1's key signing the vectors' releases; the canonical archive read back by Python's `tarfile` (an independent reader); 28 archives every reader refuses; changed manifests, whole checks with their result, refused release texts, indexes. Python reads them now (`controller/tests/test_mode_package.py`); the box's C reader comes with plan phase 13b. | Known answers from the RFC where one exists; for the archive, an independent reader instead of our own code checking itself. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-14 | Plan phase 5: where does the sandbox-escape battery live, and what must each case show? | Its own C program, `core/tests/test_sandbox_escape.c` (ctest `sandbox_escape`, also in `check.sh`'s ctest), run alone under ASan and UBSan by a new CI job `sandbox-escape` (and in `sanitizers` with the rest), instead of inside `test_node.c` as PLAN.md said (`test_sandbox` stays there). Each of its 43 cases runs four times: as a box's script with 64 KB (Reduced) and 256 KB (Full) and as a field controller's script with field ops; after loading, an input `go` and five seconds, it must have logged no `escaped` line, the runtime must survive and be destroyed without a crash or leak, and the node must end as the case says (`running` with its `ok` line, `halted` by the sandbox, or any of these). `simulator/tests/test_sandbox_escape.py` runs escapes through the ctypes bindings (with Python field ops giving odd answers and one that raises) and a mode in the simulator whose box trips the watchdog while the other box and the controller's script play on. | Every core change faces the battery (P5R-13); the registry's CI (plan phase 8) can run the same program; four runs per case cover both tiers and both script kinds. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-15 | The battery found two ways to run script code with the instruction watchdog off. How are they closed? | (1) Lua runs `__gc` finalizers with hooks off, so a finalizer with a loop hung the box or controller for good: the sandbox's `setmetatable` refuses a metatable with a `__gc` field (raw lookup, as Lua's own check; a `__gc` added later is never called, as in Lua; scripts cannot make userdata). (2) When the watchdog's own error unwinds through `xpcall`, Lua calls the message handler at the error point with hooks still off, so a looping handler escaped: the sandbox's `xpcall` calls the handler after the protected call has unwound, with the watchdog on. Differences from Lua's: no `__gc` on tables; `xpcall` cannot yield across the call, and the handler runs after unwinding (no traceback is possible anyway without `debug`). No built-in mode used either; the change is in `core/src/sandbox.c`, so boxes, controllers and phones all get it. | A signed community mode (plan phase 13) would otherwise be able to hang a box until it is power-cycled; the fix keeps the API and closes the gap at its source. Alternative: patching Lua's GC and error handling (`lgc.c`, `ldebug.c`), rejected to keep Lua vendored unmodified. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| P5C-16 | What else did the battery change, and what stays a known limit? | The simulator's ctypes field ops (`atalaia_sim/core.py`; the controller runs the same class) now catch a host exception and answer "no nodes", "no owner" or "not sent", logged, instead of letting ctypes hand the core an undefined value (a raising `send` looked like a success). Known limit, unchanged and documented in `core/README.md`: time inside Lua's C library functions (a slow string pattern) is not counted by the watchdog; patterns stay bounded by Lua's own recursion limit and the memory cap. | A host bug must not look like success to a script; the remaining limit is Lua's and needs a time-based watchdog on the box, out of scope here. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-17 | Plan phase 6: how does a signed package reach the controller? | Two ways, both GM-only: an upload (`POST /api/v1/mode-packages`, the archive as the body and the `.release.json`'s fields as query parameters, as firmware releases are uploaded; at most 1 000 000 bytes), and an install from the field's registry index (`POST /api/v1/mode-packages/install {id, version}`; `GET /api/v1/mode-packages` lists the index with each entry's state). Every kind (mode, modifier, role package, scenario) goes on through `ModeRegistry.install`'s own checks; a built-in id is refused. Any install tells the consoles (`{"type": "modes"}` on the stream), now also an unsigned `POST /api/v1/modes`. | PLAN.md named only the index; a club signing its own mode, or a field without the registry, needs the upload too. One install path keeps one set of checks. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-18 | Which keys does the controller trust, and what happens when that changes? | `[modes] keys` (up to 8 base64 Ed25519 public keys; none: nothing signed installs, the console says so) minus `[modes] revoked` (key ids) and the index's `revoked.json`. A package stays installed when its key is later revoked or removed: the modes list shows its `trust` (`trusted`, `revoked`, `untrusted`) and the console marks it; the GM decides whether to replace it. Boxes (plan phase 13) will be offered only packages whose key is trusted then and built into their firmware (P5B-9, maintainer's answer). The controller's mode keys are its own setting, apart from the firmware's release keys (an operator may list a release key in both). | Removing a mode a game may be using would be a surprise in the middle of a field day; showing the trust is enough for packages that only run on the controller, and the boxes' own keys decide for them. | maintainer (2026-10-22): accepted as recommended |
| P5C-19 | Where does the controller read the registry index from? | `[modes] index`: an `https://` URL (Python's default TLS context with the system's trust store, no redirects away from https, 15 s timeout, 1 MB), or the path of an `index.json` with its packages next to it for a field without internet (a USB stick or a copied folder). Packages' `url`s are resolved against the index and must stay under its folder (or under https). `revoked.json` is looked for beside it: missing means none; anything else that does not read whole (the index or the list) refuses the whole index, which then offers nothing. Read on demand, kept 5 minutes, *Read again* in the console. `http://`, `file:` and `ftp:` are refused by the configuration check. | Offline fields are common (P5C-11); failing closed on a broken revocation list keeps a revoked key from slipping through. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-20 | What does the store keep? | Schema 10: `modes.signed_json` (the release's fields, the signer's key id, licence, `verified` and `source`) and `modes.package` (the archive exactly as signed), both NULL for an unsigned upload, which replaces a signed package of the same id signature and all. `GET /api/v1/modes` carries `signed` (key id, `verified`, SHA-256, source, licence, trust). The archive is kept for the boxes (plan phase 13 serves exactly the signed bytes). | One row per package as before; the signed bytes must be kept to be served later, and the files alone could not be re-signed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5C-21 | What does the console show? | A *Signed mode packages* panel in the *Presets* tab (GM only; English and Portuguese): the trusted key ids or a warning that there are none, the upload form (archive and `.release.json`), the installed non-built-in packages with who signed them and *reviewed by the project* or *not reviewed by the project* (every upload, and index entries not marked `verified`), and a warning when the key is revoked or no longer trusted; the index's entries with name, author, version, kind, licence, key, review state and *Install* / *Update to* or why not (`no_key`, `revoked`, `builtin`). Also fixed: a table's screen-reader-only header text made phone-width pages scroll sideways (`.table-wrap` now positions it). | The plan's "the author and an unverified warning", where the GM already picks modes for presets. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |


## Phase 5, plan phase 13a: missing modes from the GM console, the controller, console and protocol side (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 13 (13a; the box, 13b, in parallel) on the design P5B-8 to P5B-17. The wire contract is `protocol/mode-packages.md`, "Fetching missing modes". All open; **for the maintainer**: P5D-3, P5D-6 and P5D-11. P5D-14 to P5D-18 (2026-10-23) fix a bug found on the bench, where the controller took the HELLO of a box saying its fetch failed over LoRa (P5E-8) as success; **for the maintainer**: P5D-14.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5D-1 | How are join keys 16 and 17 encoded? | CBOR `true` when set, left out otherwise; `false`, a number, null or anything else refuses the payload (`body`), so a join has one encoding (as `lang`'s null). The Python codec reads them now; vectors `mode_fetch` in `join.json` (4 valid, 6 invalid, made by hand from `node_domination_zone`'s bytes and tagged with `hmac`). `core/src/join.c` reads them in 13b; the Dart codec keeps skipping them (props are a later PE24 slice), and no contract case was added to `controller_api.json` because the Android controller role serves neither firmware nor mode packages yet. | Same rules as keys 13-15; old readers skip 16-31, so nothing breaks. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-2 | What does a box do with key 16 when it cannot fetch? | Answers *no mode* exactly as today: when the join lacks keys 13 and 14, when no mode key is built in, and of course firmware before 13b. With them, it **accepts** the join and fetches before its HELLO. The console reads a second *no mode* after *Send missing modes* as "its firmware cannot fetch modes: update it" and does not offer the button again. | A join is accepted only when it can succeed; no new field-frame result code, so the C field-frame codec does not change. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-3 | **For the maintainer:** what does a box do after a fetch fails? | It reports (HTTPS, else `node.modes_failed` over LoRa), **leaves the join** and is free again: it announces itself with a new token, the game is not counted among its ended games, and the GM can invite it again into the same game (the same node id, epochs going on). Parts installed before the failure stay. | A box without its mode cannot play; leaving keeps the start guard honest and lets the GM retry or swap the box. Alternative: stay joined and show the failure until the GM acts. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-4 | Who may download a package, and when? | No token, as firmware images: only a `+` part of the **live, ready** game's mode id that the controller **offers** (signed, trusted now, a box kind, at most 256 KiB, signer in `[modes] box_keys`, the archive kept); `404` otherwise, `409` while no game is ready (running, paused, ended or none). Served on both the HTTPS port (what boxes use, pinned to the field CA) and plain HTTP, as firmware. | Every approved licence is open, the box checks everything again, and the window is narrow (between games, this game's parts). A per-box token would need the box to sign each GET with the game key for little gain. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-5 | What is the box's exact check order and reason list? | P5B-14's order made exact: `no_key`, `bad_release` (also another id than asked), `bad_signature` (its mode keys, then release keys), `kind`, `tier`, `builtin`, `too_big`, `no_space`, `download`, `bad_hash`, `bad_archive` (also no `node.lua`), `load` (also a composed game over 16 KB), `storage`, `cancelled`; plus `no_wifi`, `unreachable`, `not_offered` (`404`/`409`). Python reference `mode_package.box_check_release` / `box_check_archive`, 18 shared `box_checks` vectors for 13b's C. | The plan's GM-facing list missed the ones a real HTTP download meets (`not_offered`, `download`, `bad_archive`, `storage`); one list for the report, the event and the console. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-6 | **For the maintainer:** the report's format and the LoRa event's ids. | `atalaia-mode-report-v1` with `game`, `node`, `mode`, `version` (or `-`), `result`, `kept` (every fetched package, `id@version` sorted, `-` for none), HMAC-SHA256 with the game key in `X-Atalaia-Mac`, at most 1024 bytes, one report per part. Event **`node.modes_failed`, id 53, critical, data `mode` (new data key 38) and `reason`** (proposed here; 13b adds them to `events.yaml`, since its generated tables are in `core/`). | Mirrors the firmware report (FU3) and `node.update_failed` (OT-8), which are built and tested; `kept` gives the Boxes tab what each box holds without another request. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-7 | What counts as "fetching" for the start guard? | A fetch `sent` (a join with key 16 made), `fetching` (the invite accepted), `installed` (a part reported, no HELLO yet), each for at most `[modes] fetch_timeout_s` (300 s, 30-3600) since its last news; then `timeout`. `force` does not skip it (as the firmware guard); *Stop waiting* (`DELETE /api/v1/mode-fetch`) does. A node whose fetch has not ended in a HELLO is not counted as joined, even if the controller heard its `node.modes_failed`. The engine's single `start_guard` became a list (`start_guards`). | P5B-13; a box heard only through its failure event must not satisfy a role. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-8 | How does the controller know a box accepted the fetch, without the box telling it over HTTPS? | From the invite's answer: an invite carrying key 16 answered `accepted` is `fetching`; `refused` is `failed` with the box's reason (`no_mode`: P5D-2); no answer is `failed` (`no_answer`). A card or QR join with key 16 stays `sent` until a report or the HELLO. | No new message; the field frame already says it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-9 | Card and QR joins with key 16? | `POST /api/v1/join` and `/nodes/{id}/join` take `fetch_modes: true` (and `forget_modes`), checked as for invites (HTTPS on, every part offered or built in; refused for a standby controller); the Join tab has no checkbox yet. | The plan says a card or QR with key 16 does the same; the console step for it can come when someone needs it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-10 | Which boxes get key 16 from the dialog, and which are refused before sending? | Per box, from its row: the role it is invited as decides the parts (`node_mode_id`); the controller refuses (that box only, status `not_sent`, the others still invited) a role whose parts are not all built in or offered, all built in (`firmware`: update its firmware instead), no `box_keys`, no HTTPS, or a Reduced box (its announcement's tier 1) for a `min_tier=full` part. The dialog shows the parts and their state before the GM presses anything. | The GM sees the reason in the row where the problem is (P5B-16), and no join is sent that cannot work. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-11 | **For the maintainer:** key 17, forgetting fetched modes: when and how? | The GM marks a box (by device id) in the Boxes tab's *Modes fetched by boxes*; its next invite (any) carries key 17, and the mark goes when that box says HELLO in that game. Marks and each box's last `kept` are in the store's settings (`mode_fetch.forget`, `mode_fetch.boxes`, the last 64 boxes); fetches themselves live in memory, as firmware updates. A box forgets before it looks at the join's modes, so key 16 and 17 together give a clean refetch. Card joins never get the mark automatically (the controller does not know which box taps a card). | P5B-15 says "in its next join"; only an invite knows which box it is for. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-12 | What does the console show? | In the invite dialog, under a box that answered *no mode* (or whose fetch failed or timed out, unless its firmware cannot fetch): "Modes this box lacks", each part with its state (*can be sent*, *built in*, *not signed: upload it as a signed package*, *not signed with a key built into the boxes*, *too big for a box*...) and *Send missing modes*, or why it cannot be sent; the row's progress follows the fetch (*fetching its missing modes over Wi-Fi*, *modes installed, joining*, failed with the reason); a notice "The game waits for boxes fetching modes (at most 300 s)" with *Stop waiting*. In the Boxes tab, *Modes fetched by boxes*: the boxes' mode keys, each box's kept packages from its last report, *Forget at its next invite* / *Undo*. English and European Portuguese; tested in Chromium in pt-PT, English and at phone width. | P5B-16. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-13 | Who can forge a box's fetch report? (coordinator's review) | Any box joined to the live game: the report's MAC uses the game key, which every box of the game holds, and the report names its node. Accepted as a known limit: a box inside a game is already trusted with its events; a report only moves that node's fetch state (installed or failed), never installs anything on another box, and the start guard's timeout bounds a false *fetching*. A per-node key would need a key per box in the join; not worth it for this. | Boxes in a game share one key by design (RFC 0001); the downloads themselves are signed and checked on each box. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5D-14 | **For the maintainer:** when does a HELLO end a fetch? (found on the bench, 2026-10-23) | Only when the controller holds HTTPS reports that the box keeps every part it was offered for that join: each reported `installed`, or in the `kept` of a report of this fetch, or (without key 17) of the box's last report before it, for a box that lacked nothing when the join came. A HELLO without them leaves the fetch `fetching` with `hello_seen`: not joined, invite `accepted`, the start guard still waits. Fixed in the controller only; the contract (`protocol/mode-packages.md` section 4, "The HELLO and the event") now says it. | On the bench a box that could not reach the controller over HTTPS followed P5E-8 (HELLO, then `node.modes_failed` unreachable, then left the join): the controller took the HELLO as success (*done*, `installed []`, invite *joined*) and refused the event as "not a fetch it was sent". The box needs the HELLO to send the event (the link's key and epoch), so the controller, not the box, must tell the two HELLOs apart; the reports are what it already has. A firmware change (an event before the HELLO, or a flag in HELLO) would touch the link protocol and every box for nothing the controller cannot see. | open: for the maintainer |
| P5D-15 | When is `node.modes_failed` taken? | For that node and the live game, in any state of a fetch but `failed`: while waiting after its HELLO, after a `timeout` or *stop waiting* (`cancelled`), and even after `done` (every part reported installed, yet the composed game does not load: `load` over LoRa, P5E-10); the node stops counting as joined, the invite shows `failed`, the console's Start is told. A fetch already failed keeps its first reason; a HELLO never ends a failed fetch (that box is leaving the join). | The event is sealed with the game key from that node, as any of its events (P5D-13's limit applies); a failure the box says is news whenever it comes. | open |
| P5D-16 | A HELLO with only some reports (or none) and no event? | Wait: a late report that completes the parts ends the fetch (`done`, joined: the box had got no answer in time, the controller had the report); `node.modes_failed` fails it; neither within `[modes] fetch_timeout_s` (counted from the HELLO): `timeout` with reason **`report_lost`**, not joined, the start guard stops waiting, the GM may *Start anyway* or invite the box again. Not `failed` at once: a box whose last *installed* report got no answer goes on and joins (only failures go over LoRa), so a lost report is not proof of failure. | The box sends `node.modes_failed` right after its HELLO (critical, retried for up to 60 s), so a failing box is told apart within seconds; the rare lost *installed* report costs the GM the timeout and a visible `report_lost`, never a box counted as joined that is not. | open |
| P5D-17 | What do the dialog and the invites show, and can the box be invited again? | `GET .../invites` and `/boxes` show an invite whose box's fetch did not end in its HELLO as `accepted` while the controller waits, `failed` (detail `mode fetch <state>: <reason>`) after, never `joined`; such a node is not "in the game" for the box list or for a new invite, so once the box announces itself again the dialog offers *Send missing modes* again (the same node id, epochs going on). The row says *said HELLO without reporting its modes: waiting for its news*, then the failure. The box's leaving is not heard (it goes quiet on the game's link); its node record stays, not counted as joined. | P5D-3's "the GM can invite it again into the same game" needs the controller not to treat a node it heard once as a box in the game. | open |
| P5D-18 | A failed fetch and a later plain join for the same node? | A join for that node without key 16 or 17 (an invite, a card) ends the fetch made for it before, so its next HELLO joins it as any. Key 17's mark goes on the first HELLO, even one that does not end the fetch (the box reads key 17 first). | Otherwise the old failed fetch would hold the node out of the game forever, now that a HELLO no longer ends a failed fetch. | open |
## Phase 5, plan phase 13b: the Field Box fetching, checking and keeping signed mode packages (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 13 (13b, the box; 13a, the controller, in parallel) against the contract of `protocol/mode-packages.md`, "Fetching missing modes" (13a's), on the design P5B-8 to P5B-17 and 13a's P5D-*. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5E-1 | How is the box's side cut? | Four files in `firmware/esp32/main/`: `mode_package.c` (the release text, its Ed25519 check with Monocypher, a streaming reader of the canonical archive), `mode_store.c` (fetched packages on LittleFS, platform-free over the C library's files), `mode_fetch.c` (the job in the contract's order, platform-free like `fw_update.c`, host-tested with fakes) and `mode_fetch_esp.c` (the task, the keys, the screens). The Wi-Fi and HTTPS pinned to the join's field CA are the firmware update's (`fw_update_esp_net`), with one user at a time (`fw_update_esp_net_claim`). | One HTTPS client and one pinning path to review; the checks run identically on the host (ASan, CI) and the box. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-2 | How does a box read an archive it never holds whole? | Streamed, in pieces of any size: each 512-byte header is checked by building the canonical header of the name and size it read and comparing every byte (which covers the magic, checksum, type, mode, owners, times and prefix field at once), then the order, one root (the id), the allowed names, UTF-8 without NUL (as Python's strict decoder), the zero padding and exactly two zero blocks at the end. All 28 refused archives of the vectors are refused fed whole and in pieces of 1, 3, 100, 511, 512, 513, 4096 bytes. The archive is first streamed into `.new/package.tar` while hashed; nothing in it is parsed until its size and SHA-256 are the signed ones (P5C-10). | A Reduced box has no PSRAM for 256 KiB; parsing only verified bytes keeps unsigned input away from the parser. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-3 | The C join codec and keys 16 and 17? | `core/src/join.c` reads and writes them: `fetch_modes`, `forget_modes`, only `0xf5` (true in its one CBOR form), written after key 15 when true; checked against `join.json`'s `mode_fetch` (4 valid byte-exact round trips, 6 invalid), with 13a's vector and Python codec copied unchanged. A box takes a key-16 join as *fetching* only with keys 13 and 14, a mode key built in (the shell sets `fetch_modes` only then) and no game on the box; otherwise *no mode* as before. Key 17 forgets every fetched package as soon as the join is authentic, except while a game is played or a fetch is on (their scripts and screens are in use). | P5D-1, P5D-2; forgetting under a running game would pull its screens from under it. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-4 | Which keys check a package? | `CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS` (new Kconfig, up to 4, empty by default), then the firmware's release keys, as the contract says; only a mode key makes a box accept a key-16 join. At boot every kept release is checked again with this firmware's keys and must still be the one its slot names; one that does not is dropped (a revocation shipped in a release). `tools/docker_fw.py` sets no mode key, so no bench key ever lands in a Field Box image. | P5B-9 (keys built into the firmware only); the release keys may sign modes too. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-5 | Where and how is a package kept? | `/littlefs/modes/<id>/node.lua` (the preset store's own path), `assets/screens/<name>.yaml`, `release` and `fetched`: the slot (id, version, signer's key id, SHA-256, archive size, last use), one per folder instead of one index file. A package is written into `.new/`, its slot last; the commit renames the older version to `.old`, `.new` to `<id>`, removes `.old` and adds a preset (label the id, no parameters, no time limit). `mode_store_init` finishes a commit a power loss cut between the renames and forgets anything else half done. At most 8 packages and 524288 bytes of archives (`box_limits`); room is made before the download by dropping the package a game used least recently, never one of the joined game's parts nor a built-in one (`no_space` if none). Only missing parts are fetched; a newer version of a kept id would replace it the same way. | The contract allows "an index or NVS"; a slot in its folder is renamed with it, so a slot never names files that are not there. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-6 | Is the fetching join stored? | In RAM only, not in NVS: a box that reboots while fetching is free, and the controller's fetch timeout covers it. It is stored, as any join, once the packages are in and the box joins (HELLO). | A stored join without its mode would fail to rejoin at every boot. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-7 | How does the box tell the contract's transport results apart? | The update's HTTPS operations now say more: `get_text` -1 no answer (`unreachable`), -2 an answer other than 200 (`not_offered`; any status, not only 404 and 409), -3 a 200 longer than 512 bytes (`bad_release`); `open` false with length -2 for an answer other than 200. A download that ends early is `download` when a Content-Length was announced and `bad_hash` without one (as `box_checks`' `truncated`). The firmware update reads only > 0, so it is unchanged. | The contract's codes need the HTTP status; one small change in one place. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-8 | How does a failure reach the controller without HTTPS? | The box brings up the game's link only to say it: HELLO, then `node.modes_failed` (id 53, critical; data `mode`, key 38, and `reason`, added to `protocol/schema/events.yaml` and regenerated, with a vector in `events.yaml`). Its STATE says *waiting*; it leaves once the event is acknowledged (or after 60 s), free again with a new token and an announcement soon, the game not counted among its ended games. A failure reported over HTTPS leaves at once, the same way. | The contract's "before the box leaves the join"; the event needs the link's key and epoch. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-9 | What cancels a fetch? | A GAME_OVER field frame for the game it fetches for, and another game's card or text while fetching (answered *busy*, and the fetch stops: tap again once free); a game picked on the box cannot start meanwhile (the box is busy). A box not yet on the game's link cannot hear its CMD start. Packages already in stay; nothing goes over LoRa for `cancelled`. | The contract's "a game starting while a fetch is on"; the box can only act on what it hears. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-10 | Codes for what the contract does not name? | A firmware update holding the Wi-Fi: `no_wifi`; no memory for the fetch: `storage`; every part in yet the composed game does not start: `load` (over LoRa). No code outside the contract's list is ever sent. | The controller parses only its list (`parse_report`). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-11 | The fetched screens? | `screen_template_set_fetched(mode_store_screen)`: a fetched part's `assets/screens/<name>.yaml` read from storage into one 4 KiB heap buffer, kept for the next call (the renderer parses it at once). The load check parses every kept screen with the core's parser; over 4 KiB or not parsing: `load`. Built-in modes' screens stay in the image only (K10). | The plan's "the box's screens from storage"; no static RAM. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-12 | Memory? | Nothing static for a fetch: the job, its join copy, a 4 KiB buffer, the 16 KB scratch and the template parser's struct are on the heap only while a fetch runs (PSRAM on a Full box), the task has a 12 KB stack; the store's lists are allocated when used. `mode.yaml` is never read (the contract's step 9). | `sdkconfig.defaults`' notes: internal RAM is what the Wi-Fi driver lacks. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-13 | How do the controller's end-to-end tests drive a box? | `box_host --mode-keys HEX [--mode-source DIR]` runs the firmware's own fetch after a key-16 join: from files standing for the controller's answers (`DIR/<id>/release`, `DIR/<id>/package`, a missing file for 404), printing `REPORT ...` and `FETCH result=... part=...`; without a source it has no Wi-Fi, so the LoRa path (`node.modes_failed`) is the one exercised. | The plan's "box_host runs a fetch from a fake controller", without an HTTPS client on the host. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5E-14 | Factory reset? | The firmware has none yet; `mode_store_forget_all` is what one will call, and `@forgetmodes` (serial console, between games) does it now; `@modes` lists the kept packages. | The contract's "key 17 and a factory reset forget all of them". | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 7: scenario blocks in the GM console (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 7 on ADR-P5-1 and P5-2 as amended (P5R-2: the console gets a block view of its scenario builder that writes the same manifest) and the scenario compiler and validator as built (M7-1 to M7-9, M8-1 to M8-9). All open; **for the maintainer**: P5F-2, P5F-4 and P5F-5.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5F-1 | Where is the block view, and what does it edit? | A second view of the *Cenários / Scenarios* tab's one editor: *YAML* and *Blocos / Blocks* buttons (`aria-pressed`) above it; the view used last is remembered in the browser. The YAML text stays the editor's text: the draft, the controller's checks as it changes, *Check*, *Install*, *Example* and *Open* work as before in both views. Each change the GM makes to the blocks writes the YAML again; switching to the blocks reads the current YAML. The mode editor's blocks and generator are not reused (they write Lua; a scenario is data compiled by the controller, M7-2): only Blockly and the approach are shared. | One manifest, one check, one install path (M8-1, M8-7), for the GM and the read token alike; nothing new on the controller beyond P5F-3. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-2 | **For the maintainer:** how does Blockly get into the console, and what happens to its Content-Security-Policy? | Blockly 13.3.0 vendored in `controller/console/src/lib/blockly/`, byte for byte the mode editor's copy (same SHA-256, checked by `controller/tests/test_console.py` and listed in `lib/README.md`; Apache-2.0 in `REUSE.toml`): the core, Blockly's English and Portuguese texts and the 7 media files its stylesheet and icons name, not its own blocks, Lua generator, sounds or text-block images. It is loaded with `<script>` elements (same origin) only when the block view opens.
