The CSP keeps `script-src 'self'` and no `'unsafe-inline'` anywhere: `style-src` allows, besides `'self'`, the SHA-256 of exactly the two stylesheets Blockly writes (its own, with the media path in it, and its renderer's, with the renderer's and theme's names in it; one theme name for light and dark), in `BLOCKLY_STYLES` in `api.py`. The view has no comments (a comment's bubble sets a `style` attribute, which the CSP would refuse); problems are outlines, not warning bubbles (P5F-7). A browser test fails on any other inline style or CSP violation and prints the new hashes when Blockly changes. Not done: `'unsafe-inline'` for styles, as the mode editor has (P5A-5) and PLAN's "CSP adjusted for its styles only" allowed. | The console is reachable by everyone on the field's Wi-Fi and holds the GM token; two hashes keep it as strict as before for everything but those two texts. The cost: a Blockly update must update the hashes (the test says which). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-3 | How does the YAML become blocks without a YAML reader in the browser? | The controller reads it: `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` now also answers `manifest`, the manifest as its reader parsed it, when JSON carries it unchanged (maps with text keys, lists, texts, booleans, null, whole numbers within 2**53, finite numbers), else `null` (a YAML date, a set, a NaN, a number as a key). Additive: nothing else in the answer changed. | M8-7: one reader and one validator, the controller's; a second YAML parser in JavaScript could read a text differently from the compiler. The console needs its controller for checks anyway; a new scenario opens as blocks with no controller. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-4 | **For the maintainer:** what if the YAML has something the blocks cannot hold, and what becomes of its comments? | The blocks refuse rather than change a manifest: an unknown key, a value of the wrong kind, an empty id or name, `null`, a translated label of no parameter, or a value the blocks would write differently (`win: false`, `gm_only: false`, `any: false`, which are the defaults the blocks leave out) is named by its path ("The blocks cannot show these parts as they are: extra") and the YAML view stays. Loading blocks is not an edit, so the YAML text, comments included, stays as it was until the GM changes a block; from then on the blocks write the whole text (comments and hand layout go; the intro says so). | A view that silently rewrote a manifest would change scenarios behind the GM's back; the controller's own checks already explain most of what is refused. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-5 | **For the maintainer:** which blocks? | 30 block types, 29 of them in 8 categories: one *scenario* block (not deletable: name, id, version, the boxes' screen language, squads, and inputs for texts, teams, parameters, boxes and players, objectives); *texts* (description, author, name and description in Portuguese); *team*; *parameter* (name, from, to, default, label and its Portuguese, organisers only); *box* (name, role objective or bomb site, level) and *player* (name, check-in role); *objective* (id, name, type, target, and its rules inside); *who and when* (for team and squad, seen by, after another is completed, opens at minute, closes at minute); *how* (hold seconds, keep it seconds, code, tags with *any card*, defuse holding seconds, explodes after seconds, a destination circle or an area of corners, position accuracy, lives left); *what it gives* (points, the points of a parameter, wins the game, reveals). Holds and defuses are in seconds (the YAML keeps milliseconds). References (an objective's target, *after*, *reveals*, a team, a parameter) are typed names, checked by the controller, not dropdowns. | Every key of the manifest (M7-1) has exactly one place, so any manifest the validator takes can be shown. A dropdown of names would refuse a box or objective the GM has not added yet and would have to change a value on load; the controller's problems point at the block (P5F-7). Seconds are what players say. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-6 | Are any validator rules repeated in the browser? | No (M8-7). The blocks report only what the YAML could not say: a rule given twice in one objective (the first counts), a block in a place that does not take it (Blockly checks only neighbours), blocks left outside the scenario block (a warning), a second scenario block. Errors among these keep *Install* off. | One implementation of the rules. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-7 | How do the controller's problems reach the blocks? | Each value the blocks write records the block it came from (`objectives[1].depends_on[0]` is the second objective's first *after* block); a problem's path goes to the block that wrote the longest part of it, else the objective, else the scenario block. That block gets a red outline, and in the block view each problem in the list has *Mostrar / Show*, which scrolls to the block and gives it the keyboard focus. | As the mode editor's problems on blocks (P5B-5), without the warning bubble the CSP refuses (P5F-2). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-8 | What YAML do the blocks write? | The examples' layout: keys in the documented order, small maps and lists on one line up to 100 characters, a blank line before `nodes`, before `objectives` and between objectives. A text is written without quotes only when PyYAML (YAML 1.1) reads it back as the same text (letters first, then letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `-` and inner spaces, never `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off`, `null`...), else in double quotes with control characters, YAML 1.1's other line breaks and the BOM escaped; numbers as JavaScript writes them, an exponent with a dot (`1.0e-7`, which PyYAML reads as a number). `version` (as text), `lang` and `teams` are always written; `squads` when not 0. | A GM who switches to YAML reads what they would have written; the round trip proves the reader gets the same values back (P5F-9). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-9 | How is "compiles identically" proved? | `test_round_trip_of_every_example_compiles_identically`: the two hand-written manifests (`controller/tests/scenarios/foxhound.yaml`, `multi_objective.yaml`), the console's example and the five valid manifests of `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` (every objective type, squads, parameters, a polygon, warnings, hostile texts) become blocks in the page, are saved and loaded again as Blockly state, and the YAML written is read by the controller's reader: the same package byte for byte (`mode.yaml` and `field.lua`), the same validator problems, the same manifest except the version as text and `lang` and `teams` written out, and the controller's check with the core loads it. Another test installs a scenario edited on the blocks and compares the installed package with the hand-written YAML's. | The task's requirement, with every example the repository has. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-10 | Keyboard, screen readers and languages? | Blockly 13's own keyboard navigation and ARIA labels ("objective, bridge, name, Take the bridge, ..."), with a help line under the view (Tab to the categories, the arrows, Enter to take and put down a block or edit a field, W, T, M, Delete, Ctrl+Z), the workspace as a labelled region; a browser test adds, puts down and deletes a block and renames the scenario with the keyboard only. The YAML view stays a plain, fully accessible text area. The blocks' texts are in the console's language files (`scenario_blocks`, English and European Portuguese: "toda a gente", "autoria", "pessoa", "quem vê", "squad" as players say it); Blockly's menus from its own `msg/pt.js` and `msg/en.js`; a language change rebuilds the blocks with their state kept. | The console's rules (keyboard and labels in every panel; words players use, no gendered forms). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-11 | A phone, and a field without internet? | Under 640 px the categories go above the blocks and the blocks start smaller; the view fits the width (tested at 390 px, no sideways scroll). The service worker precaches Blockly, its texts and media and the two new components with the console's shell (about 760 KB more on its first install), so the view opens when the controller is out of reach; Blockly is parsed only when the view opens. | Offline at the field (the console already precaches its shell); a GM edits scenarios on a phone. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5F-12 | Where are the tests? | `controller/console/tests/test_scenario_blocks.py` (7 browser tests: the round trip, editing and installing in Portuguese then English, the keyboard, the read token on a phone, what the blocks refuse, Blockly's stylesheets against the CSP in light and dark, offline; and a check of the hashes' form), run by `python3 tools/docker_console.py` and CI's `console` job; `controller/tests/test_console.py` (the vendored files, the blocks' texts in both languages with the same `%1` placeholders); `controller/tests/test_scenario_api.py` (the check's `manifest`). | Where the console's other tests run; the mode editor's job (`editor`) is untouched. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |


## Phase 5, plan phases 8 and 9: the registry repository's template and the community processes (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phases 8 and 9 on ADR-P5-3, P5-4 and P5-8 as amended: the template `registry/template/`, `tools/registry_check.py`, the sample submission and its test, `docs/community/`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` "Submitting a community mode", the draft RFC `docs/rfcs/0000-mode-packages-and-registry.md`. All open; **for the maintainer**: P5G-2, P5G-3, P5G-8, P5G-9, P5G-12, P5G-13 and P5G-14 (governance, who holds the keys, moderation, licensing).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5G-1 | What is in the registry repository, and where do packages live? | `index.json`; `keys/authors/<key id>.json` and `keys/project/<key id>.json` (`{key_id, public_key, owner, name?, added?}`, `owner` a GitHub handle); `keys/revoked.json`; `modes/<id>/` with the source of each mode's **newest** version and its `tests/`; `packages/<id>-<version>.tar` with `.release.json` (the author's signature), `.verified.json` (the project's, verified versions only) and `.license` (REUSE). The index names every package by the relative path `packages/<id>-<version>.tar` (an `https://` URL elsewhere is refused). Not GitHub Release assets (ADR-P5-3's first design). | A pull request then holds everything that is reviewed and nothing can change after review; a copy of the published folder is an offline mirror, which the controller already reads (P5C-19); packages are at most 1 MB. Keeping the source with its tests lets the CI prove that what reviewers read is what was signed (P5G-5); older sources stay in Git's history. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-2 | **For the maintainer:** what is the verified badge, technically? | **A second signature.** Every version is signed by its author's key (attribution; `.release.json`). A reviewed version is also signed, over the same archive, by the **project's registry key** (`keys/project/`, `.verified.json`), and the index then lists that signature, its key id and `verified: true`; otherwise it lists the author's. A field that trusts only the project's key gets verified versions only; one that adds authors' keys gets their other versions, marked *not reviewed by the project*; Field Boxes have the project's key built in (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS`, P5B-9), so only verified versions reach boxes. `registry_check.py badge` gives it (`--remove` takes it away); the CI checks it but never sets it. | Plan phase 13 says "the registry's verified badge becomes that signature": a flag in an index is nothing a box can check, a signature is. Keeping the author's signature keeps who made it, and authors need no project key to be listed. One signature per index entry keeps index schema 1 (no RFC needed). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-3 | **For the maintainer:** who holds the project's registry key, and how? | One Ed25519 key made by `tools/mode_package.py keygen`, **separate from the firmware release keys**, held offline by the maintainer (encrypted PKCS#8, a second copy in another place, the passphrase not stored with it), never in CI; its public half in `keys/project/` and in the firmware's `CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS`. Whether a second person holds a copy, or a second project key exists for continuity, is the maintainer's choice (`docs/community/keys.md`). | Reviews happen often; the key that can replace a box's firmware should not be needed for them. Offline signing means a stolen CI or GitHub account cannot give the badge. A second key in the firmware from the start would let a lost key be replaced without waiting for every box to update. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-4 | Where do the registry's checks live, and which version runs? | In this repository: `tools/registry_check.py` (`lint`, `test`, `key`, `add`, `badge`, `site`) on the shared `mode_package.py` and `tools/mode_package.py`; the registry's workflow checks this repository out at `ATALAIA_REF` beside the registry and runs them. `ATALAIA_REF` is `main` in the template; the maintainer pins it to a release tag or commit when creating the repository and moves it in pull requests of its own. | One implementation of the checks, tested here on every change (CI job `registry-template`); a pinned ref means a change here cannot change the registry's verdicts by surprise. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-5 | What does `lint` check, and what stays manual? | Errors: the index and revocation list (as the controller parses them); key files (form, id, owner; no key both an author's and the project's); per version the archive, the author's signature by an unrevoked author key and every package check (P5C-10), the index fields equal to the release, the licence file; for verified ones the project's signature by an unrevoked project key, and the index listing it; a badge file left on an unverified version; checklist items 3 (SPDX headers in `.lua` and `.yaml` naming the package's licence), 5 (the code, outside comments and strings, naming `io`, `os`, `require`, `load`, `loadfile`, `dofile`, `loadstring`, `debug`, `package`, `_G`, `_ENV`, `collectgarbage`, `string.dump` or `:dump(`), 9 (`assets/` at most 512 KiB), 11 (the same keys in every language of `i18n:`, all declared), 4 (`tests/*.yaml` exist); `modes/<id>/` packing to exactly the newest version's archive; files in `packages/` or `modes/` that no version names. Item 4's passing is `test` (the simulator); 1 is the package checks and `test`; 2 and 12 are the package checks; 6, 7, 8 and 10 are manual, in the registry's pull request template. | The amended checklist (P5R-12) as far as a machine can tell; the name scan is a lint on top of the sandbox and its battery (P5C-14), not instead of them, and finds no false alarm in any built-in mode (a test). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-6 | Who owns an id, and which ids are reserved? | An id belongs to the owner of the key that listed it: every version of an id must be signed by keys of one owner (an author may rotate keys under the same owner). Ids starting `org.atalaia.` are refused (the project's built-in modes). On a pull request `lint --base` makes a changed published version an error, and an owner change or a removed version a warning for the reviewer. | Prevents taking over another author's mode with a new key; rotation stays possible; a takeover or a takedown is a maintainer's decision, which the warning puts in front of them. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-7 | How is a key revoked? | A maintainer's pull request adds it to `keys/revoked.json` and, in the same change, removes every version it signed or re-signs it (the author's new key, or a new project key); `lint` refuses a registry that still lists a version depending on a revoked key. Key files are never deleted. A compromised project key is also replaced in the next signed firmware release (boxes drop what it signed at boot, `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 5). Packages a controller already installed stay, marked revoked (P5C-18). | ADR-P5-4's "the author must re-sign"; the CI makes it impossible to forget a version; the revocation list reaches controllers with the index at once, boxes with firmware. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-8 | **For the maintainer:** how is the registry moderated? | `docs/community/moderation.md`: public reports with a *Flag a mode* issue template, private ones for security (`SECURITY.md`); actions from the lightest: ask for a fix, take the badge away, take a version down (index and files; Git history keeps it), revoke the key; security takedowns at once, others after 14 days for the author to answer; every action a pull request that says why; the maintainer decides every case until there are more maintainers; appeals on the issue or privately, answered within 14 days. | A curated registry needs written rules before its first submission; the actions map onto what the files and the CI support. The time limits and who decides are governance, hence the maintainer's. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-9 | **For the maintainer:** the registry repository's licences? | Each mode its own approved licence (headers in every file; a `.license` file beside each archive, written by `add`); the registry's data (`index.json`, `keys/`, the signature files) `CC0-1.0`; its documents and `.github/` `CC-BY-SA-4.0`; its workflow `GPL-3.0-or-later`; `REUSE.toml` in the template says so and `reuse lint` is part of registry-lint (`reuse download --all` fills `LICENSES/`). | Anyone may mirror the index and keys; documents and code as in this repository. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-10 | What does the registry publish? | On a push to `main` that passed the three jobs: `index.json`, `revoked.json` (copied from `keys/` to beside the index, where a controller looks for it), `keys.json` (the project's and authors' public keys with owners and whether revoked), `packages/` and `badges/<id>.svg` (the newest version, *verified* or *not reviewed*), to GitHub Pages; `site` refuses a registry that fails `lint`. | What a controller reads (P5C-19) and what a field needs to configure trust; the SVG is for authors' own pages. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-11 | How is the template tested in this repository? | `registry/tests/test_registry_template.py` (18 tests): the template's workflow shape (the three jobs, publishing after them, `pull_request` only, read-only permissions, checkouts without credentials, the same sandbox battery as `ci.yml`); a registry made from the template with the sample submission `registry/examples/org.example.hill/` and two throwaway keys made at run time, listed then verified, with every `check-` step of the workflow run as written (the JSON Schemas, `lint --base`, REUSE, the mode's tests, the site), the published package verified as a controller does; and the refusals. In `tools/check.sh` (the schema and REUSE steps skipped there for want of the tools) and CI's new `registry-template` job (all of them, `ATALAIA_REQUIRE_REGISTRY_TOOLS=1`). | AC-6 can only be met in the registry repository itself; this proves the files and commands before it exists, without real keys. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-12 | **For the maintainer:** the RFC for the package format? | `docs/rfcs/0000-mode-packages-and-registry.md`, a **draft** recording version 1 as built (archive, manifest, release, checks, index, revocation list, the registry's two-signature rule), with `protocol/mode-packages.md` normative; the maintainer accepts it after the RFC process's two weeks and gives it a number. | ADR-P5-8: format and signing changes need an RFC, so version 1 should be one; it was built before the RFC, so the RFC describes rather than proposes. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-13 | **For the maintainer:** CODEOWNERS and branch protection? | The template's `.github/CODEOWNERS` makes `@vakaobr` the owner of everything and, explicitly, of `keys/project/`, `keys/revoked.json`, `.github/` and `REUSE.toml`; branch protection on `main` (three checks, a code owner's review) is a step in "Creating the repository", for the maintainer, since this work changes no GitHub settings. | The project's key, revocations and the CI are what an attacker would change; until there are more maintainers, the one owner reviews everything. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5G-14 | **For the maintainer:** how fast and by whom are submissions reviewed? | Listing: any maintainer, once the CI passes and the manual items hold. The badge: a maintainer holding the project's key who is not the mode's author, when the tests show the game working and the Lua was read (`docs/community/review.md`). A first answer within 14 days; a pull request waiting 30 days for its author is closed (it can be reopened). The badge belongs to one version. | ADR-P5-8's criteria with numbers on them, so authors know what to expect; the limits are governance, hence the maintainer's. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 10: German (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 10 after the maintainer's answer to P5R-10 (German first), on P5-1, P5-5, P5R-11 and the box language decisions BN-14 to BN-17. The German texts were drafted by an AI model and wait for a native-speaking player's review (P5H-5). All open; P5H-5, P5H-10 and P5H-11 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5H-1 | What does plan phase 10 cover? | German in the five domains PLAN names: the project page (`site/i18n/de.yaml`, pages under `/de/`), the GM console (`controller/console/src/i18n/de.json`, also the scoreboard, poster and field map), the mode editor (`simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/i18n/de.json` and Blockly 13.3.0's `msg/de.js`, vendored with its checksum), the app (`app/lib/l10n/strings_de.dart`, `AppLanguage.de`) and the Field Box's own screens (a third column in `box_text.c`); `tools/i18n_check.py` checking every domain against English (also the site server's pages and the modes' names, for the table); the process for translators in `docs/translations/` (`README.md`, `glossary.md`, `status.yaml`). Not in it: German mode manifests (P5H-7), the site server's pages (P5H-8), the scenarios' screen language (P5H-9). | The plan's list; the rest are other formats or other owners, and each can follow without changing what is built here. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-2 | What happens when English and Portuguese get a text German lacks (every other change adds keys)? | German may lack keys: the console (`ui.js` now falls back on English for every language but English, which falls back on Portuguese), the editor and the app (both already fell back on English) and the box (an empty German cell is English) show English. The parity tests of each domain check only that German has no key English lacks, the same placeholders and no empty text; `tools/i18n_check.py` (in `tools/check.sh`) lists missing German keys as **warnings**, exit 0. A language is held complete only when `status.yaml` says `complete` (en, pt): then a missing key fails; `--strict de` does the same on demand (before calling German complete). The project page stays 100% in every language: `build_site.py` fails on a missing key (P5-5), so a change to the page's texts needs its German too. | Other agents and contributors add English and Portuguese texts every day; a German check that failed CI would block every merge, and English is what a German speaker would see anyway on an older table. The page is the exception the maintainer set (P5-5). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-3 | Does the project page get a German version, and how? | Yes (AC-7): `/de/` with slugs in German (`modi/`, `funk/`, `roadmap/`, `glossar/`), glossary ids `glossar-*`, DE in the language switcher of every page, `hreflang="de"` and the sitemap; `x-default` stays Portuguese. The app's screenshots are German (copied from the app's new German golden images); the social preview (`og-en.jpg`) and the two pictures with English painted in (the night scene, the isometric field, both shown on the German page) stay English, said in their alt texts (`Language.og_image` in `build_site.py`). Links to the repository's English documents say "(auf Englisch)". `build_site.py` no longer fails on the first build of a new language (its address check ran before the new language's diagrams were written). | The plan's "site/i18n/de.yaml and the site's language switcher"; new pictures with German text need an artist, not a translator. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-4 | How do boxes show German, and does it change the protocol? | No protocol change: join key 15 already carries any 2-8 letter code and a box that does not know one shows English (BN-15), so the controller's `box_language` gains `de` (`auto` takes a German console's `de`) and old firmware shows English. The firmware: `BOX_LANG_DE`, `de` or `de-*` (`box_lang_from_code`), a German column for all 118 texts, kept in NVS as Portuguese is; the fonts (`tools/gen_box_fonts.py`, LVGL's own command) gain Ä Ö Ü ä ö ü ß in all four sizes, every existing reference image identical to the pixel; the template text fold keeps them. Mode templates get `lang: "de"` as data and the built-in ones show English for it (they test `lang == 'pt'`). Tests: `test_box_text.c` (codes, every text, OLED lengths, letters in the fonts), `test_screen_template.c` (the fold), `test_box_game.c` (a `de` join gives German, kept across a reboot), 22 new TFT and 15 OLED `_de` reference images (each looked at; no text leaves its screen, `check_bounds`). Words shortened for the OLED's room: *DEFUSE* (players' loanword; ENTSCHÄRFEN does not fit beside the bar), *SCHARF* for arming, *Gedrückt halten*. | PLAN's "a German column in box_text.c (join key 15 accepts de)"; P5R-10's "no new glyphs" was wrong (German needs seven), which the fonts now have. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later; German checked on a real Heltec V4.3 OLED by the maintainer on 2026-10-23 (waiting, neutral, captured): reads well |
| P5H-5 | **For the maintainer:** how are AI-drafted translations marked, and who reviews them? | `docs/translations/status.yaml` lists every language with `status: complete` or `draft`, who drafted it and `reviewed_by:`; German is `draft`, "drafted by an AI model (Claude) on 2026-10-22, not yet reviewed by a native-speaking player". Each German file says so in its header (the YAML, the Dart table, `box_text.c`). A release note names every `draft` language. A native speaker who plays reviews it with the glossary, in the running console, app and box images, then sets `complete` and their name, after which `i18n_check.py` fails on any missing German key. Style: "du" (as German airsoft clubs write), neutral forms where they read naturally (*Spielende*, *Teilnehmende*, *die Orga*, *Spielleitung*), no gender star or colon. | PLAN: "A native speaker reviews before release"; a status in the repository, not a memory, says what is reviewed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-6 | Where do translators start? | `docs/translations/README.md`: where each domain's English file is, its format and placeholders, the rules, and the steps to add or finish a language; `glossary.md`: the project's terms in en, pt-PT and de (people, game, devices, modes); `status.yaml`. `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s old line ("translations in `i18n/` folders") points there. | P5-1's call for translators on the project page needs a place to send them. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-7 | German mode names, descriptions and screens? | Later: no `i18n.de` in the modes' `mode.yaml` yet, so the console and the app show their English names and parameter labels in German (the code already falls back); the built-in modes' own screens (Domination, Bomb, Master and Satellites, Target Windows...) show English on a German box beside the box's German texts (`yaml_domination_neutral_de` pins it). A follow-up adds `i18n.de` to the 14 manifests and `lang == 'de'` texts to the templates (with the app's copies and reference images). | The plan names site, console, editor, app and box texts; the modes' texts are 14 manifests and their templates, a change of its own. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-8 | The site server's pages (field pages, staff pages)? | Portuguese and English as they are; `i18n_check.py` checks their parity (a missing English or Portuguese key fails). German is a follow-up with its own date formats (they are written by hand, S15-9). | Not in the plan's list; their dates have no locale database. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-9 | A scenario's `lang` (the objectives package's screens)? | Stays `pt` or `en` (`scenarios/manifest.py`): the objectives package's templates have no German. | The same follow-up as P5H-7. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-10 | **For the maintainer:** German words to confirm. | *Spielleitung* / *Orga* for the GM and organisers (GM in short labels); *Marshal*; *Spielende* for players and so *Spiel vorbei* (never *Spielende*) for "game over"; *Replik* for a replica (many players just say "Airsoft"); *Zone einnehmen*, *FREI* for a zone nobody holds, *ZONE BESETZT*; *scharf machen* / *schärfen*, *entschärfen*; *Box*, *Feld-Controller*, *GM-Konsole*; *Check-in*, *chronen*; *aufnehmen* for adopting a box, *koppeln* for pairing; *WLAN*; team colours as nouns (*Team Rot*); decimal comma. All in `glossary.md`, marked *(check)* where unsure. | What German players say, as far as research shows; a player should confirm. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5H-11 | **For the maintainer:** German project page before the review? | Published with the rest (the page is the place that calls for translators), saying nowhere that it is unreviewed, as the other languages do not either; alternatively it can wait for the review by leaving `de` out of `LANGUAGES` in `build_site.py` (one line). | AC-7 asks for a third language on the page; the maintainer decides whether an unreviewed one goes public. | maintainer (2026-10-23): publish now, with a notice asking German-speaking players to review |

## Phase 5 leftover: the mode editor makes the package itself (2026-10-22)

Built from the leftover in `docs/phase-5-community/STATUS.md` ("the editor does not yet make a package itself"), on P5C-8 to P5C-13 (the package format) and P5A/P5B/P5C (the editor). The editor's *Make the package* downloads the canonical, unsigned archive of the mode it edits and shows what a mode key will sign and how to sign it. All open; P5I-1 and P5I-3 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5I-1 | **For the maintainer:** where is the archive made: in the browser or by the simulator's server? | **In the browser**: `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/package.js`, a ustar writer of about 60 lines following `pack()` in `controller/atalaia_controller/mode_package.py` field for field (the same names, order, header bytes, padding, limits and refusal codes). Proved byte for byte against the Python packer in Chromium (`simulator/editor_tests/test_editor.py`): the shared vectors' archive and SHA-256 and the manifests of their valid releases (`protocol/vectors/mode_package.json`); every built-in mode folder in `modes/` (14) read as `tools/mode_package.py pack` reads it; the three examples through the page's button; 18 edge cases refused or taken alike (ids, file names, NUL, 64 and 65 files, the 100-byte name at its edge, 1 000 000 bytes at its edge, UTF-8, sort order). A mutation (mode `0640` instead of `0644`) fails 25 subtests. Not done: `POST /api/package` on the simulator importing `mode_package.py`. | The simulator's server is the standard library and PyYAML; `mode_package.py` imports `firmware_release.py`, which needs `cryptography` at import, and would tie the simulator to the controller's package. No new route or POST body on the server. A divergence cannot slip through: every reader (`sign`, the controller, the box) packs what it read again and refuses anything not canonical (P5C-8), so a wrong archive fails closed at signing. Cost: a third implementation of the format (the Python writer, the box's C reader, this writer), pinned by the tests above; the format is version 1 and frozen (the draft RFC, P5G-12). The alternative is one line in the editor and 20 in `web.py` if the maintainer prefers one implementation. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5I-2 | Which files go in the package, under which name, and what does its manifest say? | `mode.yaml`, `node.lua`, `field.lua` only when the controller workspace has blocks (as *Test* sends it, and as a downloaded folder would hold it), and the Mode tab's screens (`assets/screens/<name>.yaml`); never `tests/` (as `pack`). The file is `<id>-<version>.tar`, `pack`'s default name. The manifest's id, version, kind, tier and the licence come from the Mode tab that wrote `mode.yaml`, checked as `manifest_fields` checks `mode.yaml` (id at most 63 bytes, version as a firmware version, an approved licence); the browser test reads the archive's own `mode.yaml` with `manifest_fields` and finds the same. | The package is the mode folder `pack` would read after a download of every file; one name for the same thing in both tools. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5I-3 | **For the maintainer:** how does the author sign: a signing step in the simulator, or the command line? | **The command line, offline, as now.** The panel shows the exact commands with the archive's name: `python3 tools/mode_package.py sign <id>-<version>.tar --key ~/atalaia-keys/modes.pem`, then `verify <archive> <archive>.release.json --keys <your public key>`, and `keygen --out ~/atalaia-keys/modes.pem` for someone without a key (the path `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/community/keys.md` use). No key file is read by the page or the server, nothing is stored in the browser. The browser test runs the shown commands with a throw-away key: `sign` takes the archive and `verify` says OK. | PLAN plan phase 4 and STATUS: "signing stays offline". A key in a page (even from a file picker) would sit in the memory of a page that runs Blockly and talks to a server that may be served to a network (`--allow-host`); the tool already keeps keys encrypted (PKCS#8 with a passphrase) and refuses a key inside the repository. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5I-4 | What does the release text preview show? | The manifest exactly as the key signs it (the seven lines: `atalaia-mode-package-v1`, `id`, `version`, `kind`, `min_tier`, `size`, `sha256`), the archive's size and SHA-256, and a line saying that signing adds `signature=…` after them and writes `<archive>.release.json`, the two files the GM console's *Presets* tab takes. No made-up signature line. SHA-256 is computed in the page by its own code (FIPS 180-4, tested against `hashlib` around the padding's edges, in UTF-8 and at 200 000 bytes). | What the author is about to vouch for, before the key is out; `crypto.subtle` exists only in a secure context, and a simulator served over plain http with `--allow-host` is not one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5I-5 | When does the editor refuse to make a package? | When the generator lists any problem (the simulator's runtime errors do not count: they belong to *Test*), a version that is not `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (with an optional pre-release; the generator itself does not check versions), a licence not approved, an id over 63 characters, more than 1 000 000 bytes or a file it cannot carry. The panel says why in the page's language and nothing downloads. A package shown is of the files it was made from: once they change, the panel goes (as *Test*'s status). | A package with problems would be refused later by `sign` or by the controller's upload checks, after a trip to the command line. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5I-6 | Does the editor's Content-Security-Policy change? | No: `EDITOR_CSP` as P5A-5. The archive downloads through a Blob URL as *Save blocks* already does; the panel's styles are classes in `editor.css`; no new route, request or inline script. The browser tests fail on any request leaving the simulator and on any console error (a CSP violation is one); `test_editor_files.py` checks `package.js` is served as JavaScript and names no network address. | The page stays offline and as strict as before. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 12: build guides for the other node types (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 12 and P5R-14: text guides for the Satellite, the I/O pod and the target addon in the manner of `hardware/field-box/BUILD.md`, rendered on the project page in Portuguese, English and German. Every pin and part was checked against `firmware/esp32/main/Kconfig.projbuild`, the firmware, the mode manifests and the design specification; nothing was invented where nothing is decided. All open; P5J-2, P5J-4, P5J-7, P5J-8, P5J-9 and P5J-11 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5J-1 | What does plan phase 12 make, and where? | `hardware/satellite/BUILD.md`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md` (English, CC-BY-SA-4.0, the Field Box manual's structure and tone, referring to it for every shared step rather than copying it); the folders' READMEs and `hardware/README.md` point at them; a new project page, **build** (`site/src/build.html`), shows safety rules common to every build, each guide shortened (parts, pins, steps, what is not decided, photo placeholders) and a pointer to the Field Box manual, in pt, en and de, linking to the full English guides. | The plan's "text guides ... linked and rendered on the project page"; the repository guides stay the single detailed source (as the Field Box's), so the page carries what a player-builder needs to judge and start, in their language. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-2 | **For the maintainer:** what is a Satellite in hardware? | **The Field Box's board and firmware with fewer parts**: no separate firmware variant. Two team buttons on GPIO 4 and 48 (a third possible on 44), the LED ring on GPIO 35 through the 74AHCT125, the board's own OLED as its only screen, the MAX17048 recommended, one cell; no TFT, keypad, NFC reader. | The roles `satellite` and `master` (and Domination's, Hold-All's and Last Stand's zones) need only `team_input`; the firmware reports a missing PCF8574, PN532 or gauge once and carries on, and an unadopted box shows its pairing code on the OLED (AD-16). A variant would add a fourth image to build, sign and test for no gain. The design specification's "ESP32-S3 LoRa board" is read as the Field Box's board, the only one the firmware supports. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-3 | How does a Satellite without a keypad or NFC reader get set up and into games? | The Wi-Fi setup portal on first start (name, region, admin PIN); adoption by USB cable or by the pairing code on its OLED (no field card); games only as a field controller invites it (it cannot pick a preset by itself); moving to another field by cable with its admin PIN. | What the firmware already does for a box with these parts (BRING_UP.md steps 10a, 10b, 10b2); said plainly so nobody expects a stand-alone satellite. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-4 | **For the maintainer:** the Satellite's piezo buzzer (design specification section 6)? | **Not in the guide as a part**: the firmware makes sound only through the MAX98357A over I2S, so the guide offers the amplifier and a small speaker as optional, or a silent Satellite, and says a buzzer needs a firmware change. | Never document wiring the code does not support. A piezo driver (LEDC tones on a free pin, an `audio` capability without clips) would be a small firmware change if wanted; then the specification and the guide change together. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-5 | The Satellite's power? | The Field Box's power path with **one** protected 18650 (or a 1S pack with BMS): fuse, Schottky diode, covered switch, the same 5 V boost converter (2.5 A or more) for the ring; runtime, current and charge time marked **Verify at the first build**. | The board's socket, charger and gauge are single-cell parts; one design of the power path is easier to check than two. A smaller boost converter for a 12-LED ring may do, but no figure is measured, so the guide does not give one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-6 | What is an I/O pod in hardware? | The Field Box's parts without the TFT (every part optional apart from the board), plus the **enclosure switch** on GPIO 47 to GND, held closed by the lid (`ATALAIA_POD_TAMPER_PIN`, PF8); the log read on GPIO 43 with a 3.3 V USB-UART adapter (the USB-C port carries the frames); the pod's settings in `firmware/esp32/build-pod/sdkconfig`; flashing from `build-pod`. | As the pod firmware is built (ADR-P3-3, `sdkconfig.pod`, `pod_config.h`, BRING_UP.md "The I/O pod"); the separate `sdkconfig` in the build folder is how `tools/docker_fw.py` builds variants. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-7 | **For the maintainer:** the pod's "siren" (design specification section 6)? | The pod's sound is its amplifier and speaker (tones and the mode's clips as tones). A separate siren, relay or strobe (the specification's `output` capability) is **not** in the pod firmware, so the guide lists it under "not decided". | Never document wiring the code does not support; an `output` capability on the pod is a protocol and firmware change of its own. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-8 | **For the maintainer:** a pod on a phone's USB-OTG cable: who powers whom? | **Not decided; to verify at the first build.** On the cable the phone powers the board; with the pod's switch on, the board's charger may then charge the pod's cell from the phone (as a Field Box charges from any USB supply). The guide says so and asks for the current with the switch off and on. | It drains the phone, and phones limit OTG current; whether to fit a diode, a switch or a different charging rule needs a measurement and a choice. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-9 | **For the maintainer:** what can the target addon's guide say? | **What is decided and what is not; no wiring.** Decided (with sources): the parts in outline, the 5-pin M12 port (GND, 5 V, SDA, SCL, interrupt; 1-2 m), the "identify" idea, the `hit` capability (strength 0-255, `arm`, `disarm`), its proxy vectors, Target Windows, the HW2 list. Not decided: the processor; the I2C protocol (address, identify, hit report, arming); the M12 pin order; **the bus voltage** (the box's I2C bus is 3.3 V while the port carries 5 V); **the interrupt line's GPIO: a Field Box has none free** (all eight board-free GPIOs are used, and the GNSS connector keeps 34 and 38-42), so it needs a pin given up, the keypad expander or polling; which ring shows the window; the sensor circuit and the plate. Safety for any target: eye protection, ricochets, only the plate is a target, the field's power limits. The guide says "Do not wire a plate to a Field Box from this page". | The maintainer asked for "what's decided, and mark what's not". The missing GPIO and the bus voltage are real constraints found while checking the pin map; they shape the addon's design and should be decided before anyone builds one. | maintainer (2026-10-23): fix in 3.3 V; built as P5J-15 to P5J-21 |
| P5J-10 | How are the photo placeholders written? | In the guides, an HTML comment `<!-- PHOTO: <file name> \| what it shows -->` (for HW2 to find and replace) followed by the visible note the Field Box manual uses ("Photo to be added after the first build: ..."); on the page, a dashed box (`figure.photo`) with the same words. Never mock-up or generated photos. | The plan's `<!-- PHOTO: ... -->`, plus the visible note readers of the Field Box manual already know. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-11 | **For the maintainer:** the page's name, address and words? | `/montagem/`, `/en/build-guides/`, `/de/bauen/`; a header link *Montagem* / *Build* / *Bauen* after *Roadmap*; *Guias de montagem* (as the Field Box's "manual de montagem" on the page), *caixa satélite*, *módulo de E/S*, *módulo de alvo* (the page's existing words), *célula* for a Li-ion cell, *besouro piezoelétrico*; German *Bauanleitungen*, *Satellit*, *I/O-Pod*, *Ziel-Addon*, *Gehäuseschalter*, drafted by an AI model like the rest of the German (P5H-5); added to `docs/translations/glossary.md`. The full guides stay English, marked "(em inglês)" / "(auf Englisch)". | Words the page already uses, so players see one name for one thing; `build-guides/` in English, not `build/`, which `.gitignore` ignores everywhere (a build folder) and would have left the English page out of every commit; a header link because the page's audience (makers at a club) looks for it there, and at 320 px the header still fits without horizontal scroll. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-12 | Wiring diagrams for the Satellite and the pod? | **Not now.** Both are subsets of the Field Box's two diagrams, which the guides point at, naming what to leave out; a WireViz diagram of their own can follow their first builds (HW2). | A second set of diagrams would have to be rendered, checked and kept in step with the first for no new wire; the pin tables are checked instead (P5J-13). | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-13 | How are the guides' pins kept right? | `tools/check_wiring.py` (in `tools/check.sh` and CI's wiring check) now also reads the pin tables of `hardware/satellite/BUILD.md` and `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` (a row per pin, its Kconfig symbol in backticks) and the page's cells marked `data-kconfig` in `site/src/build.html`: each GPIO must be its symbol's default, never a strapping, GNSS-connector, front-end-module or board-internal pin (the three shared on purpose only for their one use), and every pin a guide must list has a row. | "Every pin checked against the code", and kept so: a pin default that moves fails the check until the guides and the page follow. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5J-14 | (Found while checking the pages at phone width) the German main page scrolled sideways at 320-360 px. | Its hero title kept "Force-on-Force-Plattform" on one line (`nowrap`), 441 px wide; now "Die offene Plattform für <span class="nowrap">Force-on-Force</span>", as its page title already reads. Every page in every language checked at 320, 360 and 1200 px in Chromium: no horizontal scroll. | A phone-width bug of plan phase 10's German, one line to fix. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

## Phase 5, plan phase 12, follow-up: the target addon's port in 3.3 V (2026-10-23)

The maintainer's answer to P5J-9 (2026-10-23): "let's work in a fix, I remember it was decided to use 3.3 instead of 5 V". The earlier decision is **M3** (Field Box build manual, confirmed by the maintainer on 2026-10-21): the I2C modules and the display take their power from the board's 3V3 rail; with it the manual's rule that no module pulls SDA or SCL above 3.3 V (`hardware/field-box/BUILD.md` section 9.2 and its final checklist) and the keypad expander "powered from 3V3, never 5V" (`hardware/field-box/README.md`). No decision had named the M12 port itself: the design specification (section 6, "GND, 5 V, SDA, SCL and an interrupt line"), ADR-P5-6's carrier PCB table and RESEARCH.md still said 5 V, which contradicted M3 for a port that is the same bus. Every document now says 3.3 V on the port, for the supply and for I2C. All open, for the maintainer's review.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5J-15 | What voltage does the M12 addon port carry? | **3.3 V only**, supply and I2C: the board's 3V3 rail, GND, SDA and SCL of the box's existing bus (GPIO 17/18, 100 kHz); no 5 V on any pin. Corrected in `docs/design-spec.md` section 6, ADR-P5-6's carrier PCB table (with a note), `docs/phase-5-community/RESEARCH.md`, `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md` and `README.md`, `hardware/README.md`, and the build page in pt, en, de and zh-tw. | M3: the bus is 3.3 V, and a port that is that bus taken outside must follow the same rule; 5 V on a connector next to SDA and SCL is one bent pin or one addon pull-up away from damaging the board. The maintainer's "3.3 instead of 5 V". | open |
| P5J-16 | How much current may an addon take, and what powers lights or anything bigger? | **At most 25 mA** from the port's 3.3 V (processor and sensor circuit), through a **resettable fuse** (PTC, about 50 mA hold) in the box. *Target Windows* lights the **box's own ring** (its script calls `cap("ring")`), so an addon needs no LEDs; an addon with lights of its own powers them from its **own** protected 1S cell and boost converter, GND joined to the port's, its supply never on the port. This closes P5J-9's "which ring shows the window". | The board's 3.3 V regulator already feeds the board (80-150 mA, peaks 400-500 mA on Wi-Fi or radio transmit), the TFT (40-100 mA), the PN532 (50-150 mA) and the expander and gauge (under 1 mA): about 170-400 mA in a game, peaks near 750 mA (the manual's power budget, estimates); its limit is not documented and is already a "Verify at the first build" item. The LED ring, amplifier and button LEDs are on the 5 V boost, not on 3.3 V. 25 mA is about 3 % of the peak: room for an ATtiny-class part (a few mA) or a slowed RP2040, never for LEDs (one WS2812 takes up to 60 mA). The fuse keeps a short in the cable from browning the box out. | open |
| P5J-17 | The bus's electrical rules over the cable? | I2C stays at **100 kHz** (the firmware's speed, set for the PN532), **at most 2 m** of 5-core M12 cable, **no pull-ups in the addon**. At the first build: the box's pull-ups in parallel measured between 1 and 3.9 kΩ (a 2.2 kΩ pair on the interconnect board if higher), the fuse tried with a short, the addon's current measured; protection on the port (series resistors, diodes) still to choose. | Standard mode allows 1 µs rise and 400 pF; 2 m at about 100 pF/m plus the box's modules is about 300 pF, so the pull-ups must be at most 0.85·R·C ≤ 1 µs → 3.9 kΩ, and at least (3.3 − 0.4) V / 3 mA ≈ 1 kΩ. Pull-ups in the addon would add to the box's and could not be removed from the field. | open |
| P5J-18 | **The interrupt line**: no GPIO is free on a Field Box. Poll, share a line, or free a pin? | **Poll; no interrupt line, no pin change.** The game task reads the addon every **20 ms** (every second 10 ms tick). Numbers: 100 kHz → 90 µs a byte with its ACK; a poll (address + 5 bytes) ≈ **0.56 ms**, ≈ **2.8 %** of the bus; taking a hit adds a 2-byte ACK (0.28 ms). The bus's other users: the keypad scan every 10 ms (9 one-byte transactions ≈ 1.8 ms, ≈ 18 %), the MAX17048 every 10 s (negligible), the PN532 every 300 ms in a game (a few ms of bus), the board OLED's changed pages (up to ≈ 9 ms for a full redraw). **Hit latency**: target 50 ms; got ≈ 10 ms on average and ≤ ≈ 21 ms; while an NFC poll holds the game task (up to ≈ 200 ms when the PN532 is slow, longer while a tag is read) the addon keeps its hits with their age, so none is lost. | An interrupt would be serviced by the same 10 ms game loop, so it saves 10 ms at best. 50 ms is well under what a player notices as feedback and is at most 5 % of the shortest window (`window_s` ≥ 1 s, default 5 s); a BB needs 0.2-0.3 s to fly 20-30 m. Sharing a line: the box services none (the PCF8574's INT is unwired, `ATALAIA_KP_INT_PIN` = -1, and wiring it needs a GPIO too; all eight of its pins are used). Freeing a pin would cost every box its sound (6/15/16) or button 3 (44), and the forbidden pins (strapping, GNSS 34 and 38-42, front end, board-internal) stay untouched; `tools/check_wiring.py` is unchanged. | open |
| P5J-19 | Firmware support for polling? | **A stub driver with host tests**: `firmware/esp32/main/target_addon.c/.h` and `firmware/esp32/test/test_target_addon.c` (40 checks against a model of the addon). A **draft** wire format: the box reads 5 bytes (flags armed / hit / more / overflow and a version nibble; the hit's sequence number; strength 0-255; age in 10 ms units; CRC-8, polynomial 0x07) and writes `ACK seq`, `ARM`, `DISARM`. The addon shows its oldest hit until it is acknowledged, so a lost report is read again and a lost ACK only repeats a sequence number the box takes once; up to 4 reports per poll; `arm` / `disarm` sent again until the report agrees; bad CRC refused; the addon counts as gone after 25 failed reads (0.5 s). Compiled into the Field Box image but **not called**, so the linker drops it: the image stays 0x1bf220. | The polling logic is small and testable now; fixing a draft lets the addon's firmware and the box's be written against the same thing. Not wired because the addon's I2C address and "identify" answer are not decided. Still to do in the firmware: the I2C reads and writes at the address (`hal_esp_i2c`), finding the addon at start and adding `hit` to the box's capabilities, calling the driver from the game task and passing each hit as `hit.hit` with `strength`, routing `cap("hit"):arm()` / `:disarm()` in `command_dispatch.c`, and *Target Windows* as a preset (P4R23). | open |
| P5J-20 | The M12 pin order? | A-coded 5-pin (IEC 61076-2-101), with the usual sensor-cable colours: **1 brown 3.3 V, 2 white SDA, 3 blue GND, 4 black SCL, 5 grey not connected** (free at both ends, never 5 V; a later box with a spare GPIO could make it an interrupt line). | The common sensor pin-out with +V on 1 and 0 V on 3; SDA and SCL on 2 and 4 sit across the connector rather than side by side; keeping pin 5 free leaves the 5-pin connector of the specification. | open |
| P5J-21 | What does the guide say now, and is the addon buildable? | `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md`: the box's side decided (sections 1-4: the port, the power, the first-build checks, the polling with its numbers); **still not buildable**, with what is undecided listed exactly: the processor (within 25 mA) and its firmware; the I2C address (clear of 0x20-0x27, 0x38-0x3F, 0x24, 0x36, 0x3C) and the "identify" answer, the draft report final with the first addon; the connector's parts, the cable's maker and the port's protection; the sensor circuit; the plate; lights on the addon itself. "Do not wire a plate to a Field Box from this page" stays. The build page's target section in four languages says the same in short (new keys `decided.power`, `decided.polling`, `open.checks`; the old `open.voltage`, `open.interrupt`, `open.connector`, `open.ring` gone). | The maintainer's fix makes the box's side buildable on paper; the addon's own design (HW2) is untouched by it. | open |

## Phase 5, plan phase 11: Traditional Chinese, Taiwan (2026-10-22)

Built from `docs/phase-5-community/PLAN.md` plan phase 11 ("as 10 for `zh-TW`, with a CJK subset in the box's fonts sized against the image's free space, and a CJK font for the app and the pages"), on P5R-10 (German first, then Traditional Chinese), P5H-1 to P5H-11 (how German was done) and the box language decisions BN-14 to BN-17. The Chinese texts were drafted by an AI model and wait for a review by a native-speaking player, as German's (P5H-5). All open; P5K-3, P5K-4, P5K-5, P5K-7, P5K-11 and P5K-12 are **for the maintainer**.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P5K-1 | What does plan phase 11 cover? | P5H-1's five domains for Traditional Chinese as written in Taiwan: the project page (`site/i18n/zh-tw.yaml`, pages under `/zh-tw/`), the GM console (`controller/console/src/i18n/zh-tw.json`, also the scoreboard, poster and field map), the mode editor (`simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/i18n/zh-tw.json` and Blockly 13.3.0's own `msg/zh-hant.js`, copied byte for byte from the npm tarball, whose `dist.integrity` matched, SHA-256 `4a952c0c...76de81` in the vendor README), the app (`app/lib/l10n/strings_zh_tw.dart`, `AppLanguage.zhTw`) and the Field Box's own screens (a fourth column in `box_text.c`, 118 texts). As for German (P5H-2): a key Chinese lacks shows in English and `tools/i18n_check.py` only warns; the project page must stay complete. Not in it, as for German: Chinese mode manifests (`i18n.zh-tw`; the modes' own screens stay English on a Chinese box, `yaml_domination_neutral_zh-tw` pins it), the site server's pages, a scenario's `lang`, the printed check-in poster's fixed instructions (Portuguese and English). | The plan's list; the rest are P5H-7 to P5H-9's follow-ups. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-2 | Which language code, and does the protocol change? | **`zh-tw`, one code everywhere**: `status.yaml`, file names (`zh-tw.json`, `zh-tw.yaml`, `strings_zh_tw.dart` with `zhTwStrings`, `BOX_LANG_ZH_TW`; `i18n_check.py` reads them), the console's and editor's `LANGS`, `AppLanguage.code`, `console_lang`, the controller's `BOX_LANGUAGES` (and the app's Dart copy, held equal by `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`), join key 15. Pages and widgets say `zh-Hant-TW` (`<html lang>`, `hreflang`, Flutter's `Locale`). **No protocol change**: key 15 already carries 2-8 letters (BN-15; `zh-tw` is 5) and older boxes show English. The box takes `zh-tw` and `zh-hant` with or without more subtags; plain `zh`, `zh-cn` and `zh-hans` are Simplified Chinese, which it has not: English. The editor gives a browser in zh-TW, zh-HK, zh-MO or zh-Hant the Chinese page; Blockly's file is `zh-hant.js` (`BLOCKLY_MSG` in `editor.js`). | Taiwan's words and punctuation are not Hong Kong's or the mainland's; a code naming the region leaves room for a Simplified Chinese later without renaming anything. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-3 | **For the maintainer:** does Chinese fit the Field Box's flash, and how is a missing letter caught? | **It fits, with the fonts compressed.** The fonts carry exactly the 153 letters (and ， ： ？) of the Chinese box texts, taken by `tools/gen_box_fonts.py` from `box_text.c` itself, in Noto Sans TC 2.004 Medium (OFL-1.1, the weight of the Montserrat Medium beside it; SHA-256 checked by the generator): all of them at 14, 20 and 28 px (the TFT's texts and the whole OLED), and at 48 px only the 17 of the seven texts the TFT draws that big (`AT_48`: defused, exploded, paused, neutral, next game, game N, no limit); the 48 px font falls back on the 28 px one, so a letter it lacks would be drawn smaller, never as an empty box. Measured: the letters cost about 92, 185, 346 and 950 bytes each at 14/20/28/48 px uncompressed, **+117 KB**, which in a first build took the Field Box image from 0x1bee20 to 0x1dc210 (**7 %** of its 2 MB slot free, was 13 %; bench 8 %, modem +46 KB). So the four fonts are now compressed (lv_font_conv's RLE without prefilter, `LV_USE_FONT_COMPRESSED`, lossless: all 254 reference images, Chinese included, identical before and after): Field Box **0x1bf220**, 1 KB more than before Chinese (0x40de0 bytes, **13 %**, free), bench 0x1b92f0 (was 0x1b8f30, 14 % free), modem 0x7f640 (was 0x781e0, +30 KB, 75 % free), pod 0xa3560 (unchanged: no display). Checks: `tools/gen_box_fonts.py --check` (in `tools/check.sh`) fails when a box text uses a letter the fonts lack or the fonts carry one no text uses; `test_box_text.c` checks every text's letters in the 14, 20 and 28 px fonts (and the `AT_48` texts at 48); `test_screens.c` and `test_oled_screens.c` check every letter of every label on every reference screen against **its own** font, no fallback (`test/font_check.h`), which all the existing English, Portuguese and German screens pass too. | The plan's "sized against the image's free space". A subset per size is the cheapest that never draws tofu; compression gives back more than Chinese costs (the 48 px Latin alone shrinks from 110 to 47 KB), for a glyph decoded at each draw (a line buffer of at most 48 bytes from LVGL's own heap; the box redraws a few labels a second at most). Alternatives costed: every letter at 48 px too (+80 KB uncompressed), the OLED in English (P5K-4), the box in English for Chinese. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-4 | **For the maintainer:** is Chinese readable on the 128x64 OLED? | **Kept in Chinese, for the reviewer to judge on a real panel.** The big middle line (28 or 20 px) reads well; the 14 px header and footer, packed to one bit per pixel, lose strokes in dense letters (鍵, 據, 戲; `screens/oled/*_zh-tw.png` are the panel's pixels). FreeType's own monochrome hinting of the same font gives practically the same pixels (compared), and the panel has no room for a taller line. The OLED texts (`TXO_*`) use short, common words (按鍵：選遊戲, 等待主辦, 按住以佔領), 16 columns with a Han letter as two (`test_box_text.c`). If the review finds it unreadable: the OLED shows English for `zh-tw` (`oled_view.c` taking `box_text_in(BOX_LANG_EN, ...)` for that language, one condition), the TFT staying Chinese. | A box without a TFT (the Satellite, a bench board) would otherwise show a Taiwanese field nothing in its language; the big line, the one read from afar, is clear. | maintainer (2026-10-23): checked on a real Heltec V4.3 OLED (waiting, neutral and captured screens in zh-tw): reads fine; Chinese stays on the OLED |
| P5K-5 | **For the maintainer:** what about Chinese the GM types (team, preset, field and objective names)? | **Not in the box's fonts.** A mode's template shows each such letter as `?` (`screen_template_fold_text`, tested with 紅隊); the box's own screens (the game picker's preset names, the status screen's field name) show LVGL's empty box. Every Chinese letter a GM could type would be thousands (5000 common letters at 14 px alone ≈ 400 KB compressed): it does not fit the 2 MB slot. A field in Taiwan names on the boxes what they show in Latin letters, or the colours (teams are 紅隊/藍隊 in the app and console, by colour on the box). Follow-up: the console warns when a name a box shows has letters its fonts lack. The app and console draw every letter. | The flash: the box's own Chinese texts take about 80 KB of it, compressed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-6 | How do lines break, as Chinese has no spaces? | **The box**: its labels are single lines (sized to their text, or cut with "..."), so each Chinese text is written to fit its place; `check_bounds` and `check_overlaps` hold on every `_zh-tw` reference. **The app**: Flutter breaks between Han letters and keeps 。 and ， off a line's start; the theme button "跟隨系統" broke inside the word and is "系統". **The console**: browsers break between Han letters, with `overflow-wrap: anywhere`; the browser test checks 11 tabs at 360 px for sideways scroll. **The page**: `line-break: strict` and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` for `:lang(zh)`, the build drops the spaces YAML folding leaves between Han letters (`unfold_cjk`, Chinese only, keeping the space between Han and Latin or digits), and the diagrams wrap between Han letters, never before a closing or after an opening sign; every page at 320, 360 and 1200 px in Chromium, no sideways scroll. | Each part's own engine, checked where it shows. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-7 | **For the maintainer:** which font draws Chinese in the app? | **Android's own CJK font** (Noto Sans CJK on stock Android), in Taiwan's forms because the app's locale is `zh-Hant-TW`; no font in the APK, which grows by the table only (~30 KB, estimated). Measured alternative: a subset of Noto Sans TC with the app's 544 letters is ~123 KB a weight (95 KB as WOFF2), 250-370 KB for two or three; the whole font 5.7 MB a weight; a subset could not draw the names players type. Golden tests have no system fonts, so `app/test/fonts/` holds two Noto Sans TC subsets (164 KB each, OFL-1.1, `REUSE.toml`) loaded as the theme's fallback family `Noto Sans TC` (on a phone no font has that name, so Android's applies); `cjk_font_test.dart` fails on a letter they lack. The Taiwan forms on a real phone are not yet seen (HW). | No APK growth, every name drawable. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-8 | Fonts and numbers in the console, the editor and the page? | **System fonts only**, under `:lang(zh)`: Latin first as now, then "PingFang TC", "Noto Sans TC", "Noto Sans CJK TC", "Microsoft JhengHei" (the page also "Heiti TC"), so Han is drawn in Taiwan's forms on any machine and other languages look as before; no web font (megabytes, and the page loads nothing from other sites). A **decimal point** for `zh-tw` (869.525 MHz; a comma stays for pt and de: `decimal()` in `ui.js`, `Strings.number`). The console's dates follow the browser's locale, as before. | As German used the system's fonts; Taiwan writes a decimal point. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |
| P5K-9 | How does the project page show Chinese? | `/zh-tw/` with English slugs (`modes/`, `radio/`, `roadmap/`, `build-guides/`, `glossary/`: Han in an address is percent-encoded when shared) and glossary ids as English's (`glossary-*`); **中文** in the switcher (read out as 繁體中文（台灣）), `hreflang="zh-Hant-TW"`, `og:locale` `zh_TW`, `x-default` stays Portuguese; the social preview and the two pictures with English painted in are English's, as German's (alt texts say so); the app screenshots are the app's `_zh-tw` golden images (the modes' screens in them are English, as the alt text says). `build_site.py`: `Language.key` for `page.url.zh_tw`, `{% if lang %}` with a hyphenated code, `unfold_cjk`, an optional `screenshots` language. | P5H-3's way. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

The app's language picker, a segmented button, broke the chosen label one letter a line at 360 px with four languages: it is a row of chips that wraps (`settings_{pt,en,de}` goldens changed for that alone). | Bugs a test or a golden showed. | maintainer (2026-10-23): accepted as recommended, to review later |

Style: 你 (never 您 or 妳), full-width punctuation 「」，。：？, a space between Han and Latin letters or digits (GM 控制台, 3 秒), loanwords Taiwan's players use (GM, Hit, VIP, LoRa, Wi-Fi, App). **Words to confirm**: 主辦 for the GM and organisers (GM in short labels), 裁判 for marshal, 據點 / 佔領 / 中立 for zone / capture / a zone nobody holds, 啟動 / 拆除 for arm / defuse (not the slang 下包), 綁定 for adopting a box, 配對 for pairing, 測速 for chrono, 槍 / 生存槍 for a replica, 劇本 for a scenario, 場主, 韌體, 對抗遊戲 for force-on-force on the page; whether Han and a Latin name take a space in the app's event lines (藍隊佔領了Moinho).
